# varanda — Phase 0 protocol and formats (normative)

R6 wins over R5 wins over R4 wins over R3.

This document is the single normative reference for the Phase 0 prototype. Names, values, paths, shapes, reason strings, messages, flags, constants and stdout lines are exact. Amendments R3, R4, R5 and R6 are folded into the sections they affect; where they conflict, R6 wins over R5, R5 wins over R4, R4 wins over R3 and R3 wins over the base text. Text that R5 or R6 replaced has been removed.

Conventions: CommonJS (`require`), standard style (no semicolons, single quotes, 2-space indent), Node >= 20 built-ins with the `node:` prefix in the CLI, and in `packages/core` only through the `#fs`, `#path` and `#process` package imports (section 8), no TypeScript, no build step. Code, identifiers and comments are in English; user-facing CLI text, help and README are in Brazilian Portuguese. Tests use brittle and run alone with `node apps/cli/test/<file>.test.js`; every network test uses `hyperdht/testnet` through `apps/cli/test/helpers/net.js` (never the public DHT) and binds the DHT to `127.0.0.1` (R5.11). Register teardowns of swarms, stores and seeders with `{ force: true }`.

## 1. Identity and storage

- `hex64` = exactly 64 lowercase hex characters (a 32-byte key).
- Identity = the key of the member's diary Hypercore, shown as hex64. Diary = `store.get({ name: 'diary', valueEncoding: 'json' })` on a Corestore at `<dataDir>/store`. The JSON value encoding is used only for appending; blocks are always read raw and decoded with `decodeBlock` (section 2, R4.1).
- Corestore 7 derives the diary's Ed25519 signing key pair from the store's primary key (generated on the device) and the core key from a one-signer manifest. The core key is the diary's public identifier; it is not byte-equal to the signer's raw public key. Only this device can append.
- TODO(phase2): threshold-key (FROST) recovery of this key.
- Which node uses which diary: the core session (`packages/core/lib/session.js`), the CLI, `varanda-serve` and the app's worklet with node kind `core` (the default, kept for manual configuration and the iOS gate) keep this manifest diary. The browser node and the app's in-app hosted node (the worklet with node kind `web`, `apps/app/worklet/web-node.js`) run the browser session (`packages/web-member/lib/session.js`): a compat Hypercore 10 diary whose key is derived from the member's passkey PRF output ("Web member storage" below). In the app it runs on file storage at `<dataDir>/web-node` (`fileNodeStorage`), and it takes that directory's exclusive `LOCK` before opening anything: a held lock or one that cannot be taken refuses every request with `LOCKED` and nothing is opened. For the `web` kind that lock replaces RocksDB's per-process lock, which guards the takeover race of the app's fixed worklet id (`apps/app/src/client/native.js`).
- A Corestore directory is RocksDB-backed: ONE process at a time (RocksDB takes an OS lock). Short CLI commands open the store, work and close it in a `finally` block; `sync` and `seed` hold it while they run, so local commands must not run concurrently on the same `--data` (they fail with a lock error).

### Data-dir lock (`diretório de dados em uso`)

`openStore(dataDir)` (`packages/core/lib/store.js`) takes an exclusive data-dir lock BEFORE the Corestore (and its RocksDB) is constructed, so a second opener fails fast without touching `<dataDir>/store`: `Error('diretório de dados em uso')` with `err.code = 'LOCKED'` (CLI: exit 1, `erro: diretório de dados em uso`). It applies to every data dir opened through `openStore` (members and seeders); the association dir has no store and no lock. Rules (security fix-up T13c):

1. Acquire: atomic `fs.mkdirSync(<dataDir>/.varanda-lock, { mode: 0o700 })`, then publish the owner file `pid` = `<pid> <32-hex random token>` (written to `pid.<token>.tmp` with flag `wx`, then renamed, so a reader never sees a partial owner) and read it back to confirm ownership. Each `openStore` remembers its own token.
2. The lock path is inspected with `lstat`: anything that is not a real directory (symlink, regular file, ...) fails at once with `code 'LOCK_INVALID'` (`lock inválido (não é um diretório): <path>`); a lock that vanished between the mkdir and the inspection is retried after a short sleep.
3. `EEXIST` with a live pid -> `LOCKED`. This process's own pid counts as live only when the owner token is one this process holds (so a second `openStore` of a dir it already holds is `LOCKED`); our pid with any other token, or with no token, was written by a dead process that had the same pid (a restarted container runs the seeder as pid 1 every time) and is stale at once. A dead pid, or one `process.kill(pid, 0)` refuses (EPERM: another user's process, which cannot own a `0o700` data dir of ours), is stale at once.
4. A missing or garbage owner is stale only when the lock directory's own mtime is at least `LOCK_GRACE_MS` = 10000 ms in the past, or more than that in the future (clock-skew guard). Otherwise it is re-read for up to `LOCK_WAIT_MS` = 1000 ms and the call then fails with `LOCKED`.
5. Takeover runs only while holding `<dataDir>/.varanda-lock.takeover` (an exclusive file holding our token; a later opener removes it once its mtime is 10 s old). The holder re-checks that the lock is the exact stale snapshot it inspected (dev, ino, owner bytes), renames it aside and deletes the aside copy only if it still matches. A moved lock that does not match is renamed back; if that fails it stays at `.varanda-lock.stale-<hex>`, is never deleted, and the call throws `LOCKED`.
6. Release: each store releases only its own token, at most once, after a successful `store.close()`, and synchronously on process `'exit'`. Every removal (release, exit hook, owner-write failure) deletes the lock dir only when the token read back is ours. A failed Corestore close (also on the `ready()` failure path) keeps the lock until exit and rethrows.
7. `<dataDir>/store` must not be a symlink: `openStore` refuses it with `code 'STORE_INVALID'` before any chmod (the Corestore primary key, from which the diary signing key derives, lives there). The data dir itself may be a symlink the user chose.
8. Residual risk: a dead owner's pid reused by a live process of the same user keeps the lock `LOCKED`; recovery is to remove `<dataDir>/.varanda-lock` by hand after checking that no `varanda` process uses the data dir (README).
9. `lockHeld(dataDir)` is the read-only probe of status commands (`seed status`): `true` when the lock is held by the rules above (a live owner as in rule 3, or an owner-less lock younger than `LOCK_GRACE_MS`), `false` when there is no lock or it is stale. It never creates, takes over or removes anything; a lock path that is not a real directory throws `LOCK_INVALID` as on open.

Lock modes: `openStore(dataDir, { lock = 'posix', recover = false, suspend })`.

- `lock: 'posix'` (the default; `openStore(dataDir)` and `openStore(dataDir, undefined)` mean exactly this): rules 1-8 above, including the `process.kill(pid, 0)` probe and the stale takeover.
- `lock: 'worklet'` (the app's Bare worklet, where every worklet shares the app's pid, so a pid proves nothing): `process.kill` is never called. An existing lock directory is `LOCKED` whether this process holds it (its token is in the held set) or it was found on disk, unless `recover: true`, which runs the takeover of rule 5 on the existing lock (held by this process -> still `LOCKED`, also when the data dir is reached through another spelling of its path, a relative path or a symlink alias: the owner token on disk is compared with every token this process holds) and then acquires. The host passes `recover` only on a cold app start or after it terminated and awaited the previous worklet; there is NO same-pid heuristic. A full JS reload in development is the exception the host cannot see (the previous context's worklet may still be alive): the app registers its node worklet under a fixed id so the previous one is terminated, and when a takeover still reaches a live worklet RocksDB's per-process file lock refuses the second open (`worklet-lock.test.js`). Rules 1, 2 (`LOCK_INVALID` unchanged), 6 and 7 apply as is: the owner file is still `<pid> <token>`, the acquisition is remembered by token and released exactly as in posix mode (the `'exit'` hook is registered only when `process.on` is a function).
- Any other `lock` value throws a plain `Error('lock inválido')` before the disk is touched.
- `suspend`: when not `undefined`, Corestore is constructed with `{ suspend }` (corestore 7 suspends on Android only with `suspend: true`), in `openStore` and in `openQuarantine(dataDir, { suspend } = {})`; when `undefined`, Corestore is constructed exactly as before. `runSync({ ..., storeOptions })` forwards `storeOptions` as is to its `openStore` and `openQuarantine` calls (`undefined` = their defaults).

### Member data dir (`--data`, env `VARANDA_DATA`, default `./data`)

- `store/` (Corestore).
- `.varanda-lock/` (the data-dir lock above, present only while a process holds the store; owner file `pid` = `<pid> <token>` inside) and, transiently during a stale-lock takeover, `.varanda-lock.takeover`.
- `quarantine/` (separate Corestore used only by `sync` for block-0 fetches, R5.4): wiped at every `runSync` start, removed at close BEFORE the main store closes (shutdown order of section 6: the data-dir lock is released last); never state.
- `config.json` = `{ "v": 1, "role": "member", "association": "<hex64>" }`, written by `id create`.
- `follows.json` = `{ "v": 1, "follows": ["<hex64>", ...] }`, sorted, unique. Once block 0 exists, `follow` / `unfollow` also append a `follow` record to the diary (section 2, section 7), so the follow set is public and the same on every device of the member; `follows.json` stays the list `sync` and `feed` read.
- `blocklist.json` (B2) = `{ "v": 1, "snapshot": BlocklistSnapshot }`, mode `0o600`, written by `writeBlocklist` (`writeJsonAtomic`, section 1 "Files and replicas") only after a snapshot was verified and accepted (section 3 "Blocklist snapshot"). `writeBlocklist` also writes the same bytes to `blocklist.json.prev` (mode `0o600`). An absent or odd-shaped file reads as no snapshot (`readBlocklist` -> `null`). A torn (not JSON) or missing file reads as the copy when `blocklist.json.prev` holds a well-formed wrapper, else as no snapshot; a caller holding the data-dir lock (`onUnreadable`) also moves a torn file aside to `blocklist.json.ilegivel-<ms>` and puts the copy back as `blocklist.json`. The wrapper is unwrapped before `createBlocklistState` sees it (the state verifies a bare snapshot).

### Seeder data dir

- `store/` (main Corestore). Every core in it, diary or media core (section 5 "Seeder media admission"), is listed in `admitted.json` at every instant (R5.7, R6.1).
- `.varanda-lock/` (the data-dir lock of section 1, held by `seed` while it runs and by `seed list` and `seed drop` while they work, so those two run only with the seeder stopped; `seed key`, `seed cert add` and `seed status` never open the store and take no lock).
- `quarantine/` (separate Corestore for block-0 fetches during admission, R5.4): wiped at every `Seeder.start()` AFTER the main store has been opened (its RocksDB lock protects the running process, R6.2), removed at close BEFORE the main store closes (the data-dir lock is released last, section 5 "Seeder shutdown"); nothing in it is ever state.
- `config.json` = `{ "v": 1, "role": "seeder", "association": "<hex64>" }`, written by `Seeder.ensureConfig` on the first of `seed key`, `seed cert add` or `seed` (R5.2 f).
- `seeder-key.json` = `{ "v": 1, "publicKey": "<hex64>", "secretKey": "<128 lowercase hex>" }`, mode `0o600`: the seeder's Noise key pair, generated by `generateNoiseKeyPair()` (`require('hyperdht').keyPair()`: 32-byte public key, 64-byte secret key) and written by `seed key` through `saveKeyPairFile` (R5.2 a).
- `seeder-cert.json` = the SeederCertificate (section 3) as pretty JSON + LF, written by `seed cert add` (R5.2 e).
- `blocklist.json` (B2): the same wrapper and rules as in the member data dir; the seeder reads it under the data-dir lock at `start()`. A torn or missing file is read from `blocklist.json.prev`; when that leaves no verified list (no copy, or a copy whose signature does not verify) the seeder answers every `announce` of a key it does not already serve with `rejected` `blocklist-pending` until a verified snapshot is adopted (serving without knowing what is blocked could hand out a blocked diary). The hold is decided again at every start: it applies while a torn file kept aside by an earlier start (`blocklist.json.ilegivel-<ms>`, `hasUnreadableBlocklist`) is there and no verified list is, so a restart before a snapshot arrives keeps it. If the torn file cannot be moved aside the start fails (`lista de bloqueio ilegível`).
- `admitted.json` = `{ "v": 1, "admitted": { "<hex64>": Certificate }, "media": { "<hex64>": { "diary": "<hex64>", "seq": <integer >= 1> } } }`: the only state file about WHICH logs are served (R5.7; `blocklist.json` above is the moderation list, not a log list). `admitted` holds the diaries, `media` the media cores, each with the diary it is bound to and the seq of that diary's `media-core` record (section 5 "Seeder media admission"). A file without `media` (written before media cores existed) reads as no media core; a `media` value of any other shape reads as an unusable binding, whose core is purged at the next start (reason `unbound`). The `admitted` entries are the union of the two in-memory maps below (R6.1), and the `media` entries the union of the two media maps (bound and being dropped), written by the same serialized writer in the same write. The group sections of the same file (section 10) are `groups` (writer -> `{ diary, group, seq }`, a root's entry also `by` and, when its header diary bound it with its own `on: true` join for writer = G, `bound: true`, and then also `issuer`, the issuer of the certificate in that creator diary's block 0, kept for when the creator is gone), `revokedCreators` (diary -> the issuer of the certificate in the diary's own block 0, the one judged and never an announced one, whose revocation dropped or refused it; moderation outcomes only, never filled from an announce) and `goneRoots` (root -> `{ creator, issuer? }`: the creator whose block or revocation purged the root and, for a revocation, the issuer; `issuer` is omitted for a block, and an older plain creator string still reads as `{ creator }`) and `groupHeaders` (root -> `{ kind, visibility, join, name }`, the verified header fields of a group root while any core of that group is served or being admitted; `name` only for a public root, else null). `revokedCreators`, `goneRoots` and `groupHeaders` hold up to 8192 entries each (see "Denial bounds" in section 10). All four are omitted when empty, restored at start (a `groupHeaders` entry of a group with no core left is pruned then), and an older file without them or without `bound` or `issuer` reads as before. A `goneRoots` entry is judged on its own data against the CURRENT blocklist and revoked-issuer set, so no other map can evict its evidence.
- R4.7 / R5.7 / R6.1: after `start()`, two in-memory maps are the source of truth: `admitted` (key -> Certificate, keys being served) and `dropping` (key -> Certificate, keys being purged). `admitted.json` is written only from those maps through ONE serialized writer (`persist()`: a single promise queue doing write-tmp-then-rename) as `{ "v": 1, "admitted": { ...entries of admitted, ...entries of dropping } }` — a dropping key stays in the file — never by re-reading the file. Ordering invariant: a key is persisted BEFORE its core is opened in the main store (section 5, R5.5 d) and a dropped key is purged BEFORE its entry leaves the maps and the file (R6.1: `drop` moves it from `admitted` to `dropping`, purges, and only on success deletes it from `dropping` and persists; startup drops purge first, then remove, then persist). Hence every core in the main store is listed in `admitted.json` at every instant, whatever other keys persist concurrently; a key whose purge failed stays in `dropping` and in the file and is re-gated and re-purged at the next start.

### Moderator data dir (B2, `--data <modDir>`, operator machine only)

`config.json` = `{ "v": 1, "role": "moderator", "association": "<hex64>"[, "seeder": "<hex64>"] }` (`seeder`, the Noise key of the seeder the lists are published to, only after `mod init --seeder`), `moderator.json` (key pair, mode `0o600`), `moderator-cert.json` (the association-signed moderator certificate, section 3), `blocklist.json` (`{ "v": 1, "snapshot" }`, mode `0o600`) and `moderation.jsonl` (one line `{ "op", "member" | "issuer", "seq", "date", "source" }` per change, `op` one of `block`, `unblock`, `revoke-issuer`, `unrevoke-issuer`, `source` `painel` when written by `mod apply` and `operacao` for the operator's own commands (a line without `source` reads as `operacao`; `mod apply` uses it to ask a second confirmation before unblocking a key blocked outside the panel); no signature, no secret). Every writing `mod` command holds the data-dir lock (a second one exits 1 `erro: outra operação de moderação em andamento`); a damaged list file (or JSON `null`) is an error and never a restart at `seq` 1. A command that changes the list in a directory without `blocklist.json` is refused (`erro: este diretório não tem lista anterior; ...`) unless it passes `--new-list`, which is only for the association's first list and is itself refused when a list exists (`erro: este diretório já tem uma lista`): a list signed from empty sets by a newer moderator replaces every node's whole list (`acceptSnapshot`) and would silently undo every earlier block and issuer revocation. A moderator rotation therefore copies `blocklist.json` into the new directory and continues from it. The moderator secret never leaves this directory.

### Web member storage (B2)

The browser node keeps the snapshot in the key-value store under `'blocklist'` as `{ v: 1, snapshot }` (`readBlocklist` / `writeBlocklist`, `packages/web-member/lib/node-store.js`); an absent or unparsable value reads as none, an IndexedDB failure throws (fail closed), and a kv `'replicas'` index (`{ v: 1, keys }`) lists the stored replicas so the REVOKED-ISSUER sweep can gate replicas the follow states do not cover (an unfollowed diary or a failed follow). Blocked keys do not need it: they are probed directly in storage and purged.

Media and follows in the same key-value store (`packages/web-member/lib/node-store.js`):

- `'mediaDevice'` = `{ v: 1, nonce: <hex64> }`: a random 32-byte nonce drawn once per device. With the passkey's PRF output it derives this device's media key pair (`packages/web-member/lib/passkey.js`: seed = HKDF-SHA-256 with salt `'varanda/member-media/hkdf/v1'` and info `'varanda/member-media/ed25519/v1'` followed by the nonce); it is not a secret without the PRF output. The media pair lives only in the session's memory next to the identity pair and is zero-filled with it.
- `'mediaCores'` = `{ v: 1, keys: [<hex64>] }`: this device's own media cores (the newest 8 are kept). A media core is stored as the storage core `'media/<hex>'`, its `data` file in lazy mode (below).
- `'follows'` (the follow list), `'followsPending'` = `{ v: 1, ops: [{ key, on, at, sig }] }` (follow changes not yet appended as `follow` records, one per key, newest wins, at most `MAX_INVENTORY - 1`; `sig` = Ed25519 by the identity key over `'varanda-follow-op-v1\n' + key + '\n' + ('1' | '0') + '\n' + at + '\n' + ownHex`, checked before the append: an op that does not verify is dropped, never appended; the drain appends one op per key (the newest `at`) as a `follow` record carrying that same `at`, and skips an op whose key already has a diary `follow` record with `at` at or after the op's, so copies and replays are no-ops; the queue is capped at `MAX_INVENTORY - 1` when read and when written; `follow.add` / `follow.remove` refuse while the identity is locked, `docs/rpc.md` 5.9) and `'followsMigrated'` = `{ v: 1, at }` (set once the person decided this device's follows from before `follow` records existed, through `confirmLegacyFollows`, or once nothing is left to decide; per device; those old follows are never published without that confirmation, and while it is unset and kv `'follows'` is non-empty the sync's fold leaves kv `'follows'` alone).
- `'passkeyEnrol'` = `{ v: 1, rpId, credentialId, publicKey, attestationObject }`: written when a passkey created on this device becomes the identity (`publicKey` = the diary key it derived), so the app can send the creation's attestation object to the account service right after the account link ("Account deletion", passkey enrolment); the handle answers it (`identity.enrolment()`) only while unlocked with that same key, and drops it once the service stored or refused it for good, and at reset. Public data only (the credential id and the passkey's public key). A creation still waiting for its PRF output keeps its attestation object with it in `'passkeyAttempts'`. The native app keeps the same record in a file in its Documents (`apps/app/src/passkeyEnrolStore.native.js`, `publicKey` null until 'concluir').
- `'ageVerified'` = `{ v: 1, proof }`: the age-verified attestation for the own diary key (section 3, "Age attestations"). `'ageSince'` = `{ v: 1, proof }`: the newest age-since attestation (the node never replaces a verifying one with an older-dated one); writing `null` clears either. `'phoneVerified'` = `{ v: 1, proof }`: the phone-verified attestation for the own diary key (section 3, "Phone attestations"). `'phoneSince'` = `{ v: 1, proof }`: the newest phone-since attestation (the node never replaces a verifying one with an older-dated one); writing `null` clears either phone entry. Neither carries a phone number. The kv is writable by the device owner, so a stored proof grants nothing by being there: the node re-verifies it against its configured association (and, for `'ageVerified'` and `'phoneVerified'`, its own key) on every read from storage, and an object that does not verify is ignored.

File storage (the native app's `web` node and Node tests; `fileNodeStorage(dir)`, `packages/web-member/lib/file-storage.js`, same interface as the IndexedDB storage, on `random-access-file`): `<dir>/cores/<hex of the UTF-8 core name>/<file>` holds one file per Hypercore file (`oplog`, `tree`, `bitfield`, `data`, `header`), read and written on demand (`lazy` and `cachePages` are ignored), and `<dir>/kv/<hex of the UTF-8 key>.json` holds each key-value entry, replaced atomically (write `<file>.tmp`, then rename). `<dir>/LOCK` is held with an OS-level exclusive lock (`lock()`, released by `close()` or by the OS when the process dies) because two Hypercore instances on the same files corrupt each other. The lock fails closed: `random-access-file` skips locking silently when `fs-native-extensions` is missing, so `lock()` requires it itself and rejects with `code` `'LOCK_UNAVAILABLE'` when it cannot load; a directory already held rejects with `code` `'LOCKED'` ("diretório de dados em uso").

Lazy storage: `storage.core(name, { lazy: ['data'], cachePages })` opens that file in lazy mode (`LazyPagedFile`, `packages/web-member/lib/node-storage.js`): its 16 KiB pages are loaded on demand into an LRU of `cachePages` decoded pages (default 64), concurrent loads of a page are shared, operations on one page run in issue order, a partial write reads, modifies and writes the page, and `close()` waits for every operation in flight (a persist after close is refused). Media cores use it; diaries keep the whole-file mode (every file of an open diary is held in memory).

The persistent session (`packages/web-member/lib/session-store.js`) is in its own IndexedDB database, `'varanda-session'`, apart from the node storage; the node storage's kv `'signedOut'` marker, written by 'Sair' and by a reset, blocks any resume until the next passkey ceremony: `docs/rpc.md` section 11.

Member recovery: extra passkeys and the recovery code (`packages/web-member/lib/recovery.js`; the in-app worklet reaches it through the IPC controls `identity.recoveryLookup`, `identity.recoveryOpen`, `identity.recoveryWrap` and `identity.recoveryBind`, `docs/rpc.md` 5.91 to 5.94):

- Every member key still derives from the ROOT, the original 32-byte PRF output of the passkey the account was created with (identity, media, DM and group keys as above and in `docs/rpc.md` section 11). An added passkey or a recovery code never derives a key: it only unwraps the root bytes, so the identity, the followers and every derived key stay the same. There is no new diary record, and the node keeps no local cache of the wrapped blobs.
- `WRAP_MARK` = the UTF-8 bytes of `'varanda-wrap-v1\n'` (16 bytes). An added passkey is created with the WebAuthn user handle `user.id` = 16 random bytes followed by `WRAP_MARK` (32 bytes; the random part first, so code that labels a passkey from the leading hex of its user id still shows random hex). A handle is marked only when its LAST 16 bytes equal `WRAP_MARK`. The handle checked is the one the authenticator returned, or, when it returned none, the stored `userId` of the bound passkey record when the credential is the bound one (`identity.status().userId` on the worklet). A credential with a marked handle is NEVER installed directly: its PRF only derives `lookupId` and `wrapKey`, and a marked credential whose blob is missing (404) or does not open fails closed and leaves the node locked. An older client that ignores the mark would derive a NEW identity from that passkey, so marked passkeys need this build or newer.
- Synthetic marked user id: when a wrapped sign-in opened an entry but the authenticator returned no user handle, the device does not know that passkey's real handle, yet its bound record must still say "wrapping", or the next sign-in without a handle would install that passkey's own PRF as a diary key. The in-app device then binds `userId` = 16 zero bytes || `WRAP_MARK` (hex `'00' * 16` + `766172616e64612d777261702d76310a`, `UNKNOWN_WRAP_USER_ID` in `apps/app/src/identity.native.js`), a marked id that no real recovery passkey has (their first 16 bytes are random). The browser node keeps the returned `userId` and adds `wrapped: true` to the bound record instead. `identity.recoveryBind` accepts only a marked `userId` (`docs/rpc.md` 5.94).
- Derivation, with `kindByte` 0x01 for `'passkey'` (secret = the 32-byte PRF output of the extra passkey) and 0x02 for `'code'` (secret = the 20-byte recovery code secret):
  - `lookupId` = HKDF-SHA-256(ikm = secret, salt = utf8(`'varanda/member-recovery/hkdf/v1'`), info = utf8(`'varanda/member-recovery/lookup/v1'`) || kindByte, 32 bytes), as lowercase hex64;
  - `wrapKey` = HKDF-SHA-256(ikm = secret, same salt, info = utf8(`'varanda/member-recovery/wrap/v1'`) || kindByte, 32 bytes).
- Blob: the plaintext is exactly 66 bytes, 0x01 (plaintext version) || kindByte || the member public key (32) || the root (32), sealed with `crypto_secretbox_easy` under `wrapKey` and a fresh random 24-byte nonce. The blob is `{ v: 1, kind, nonce: <hex48>, ciphertext: <hex164> }` (82 bytes: 66 plus the 16-byte MAC), exactly those four keys. Opening checks the shape, the version, that the sealed kind byte equals `blob.kind`, the length, and that the sealed member key equals both the member the service answered for and the public key derived from the unwrapped root; any failure returns nothing and installs nothing.
- Recovery code: `CODE_BYTES` = 20 random bytes (160 bits), written as 36 lowercase Crockford base32 symbols (alphabet `0123456789abcdefghjkmnpqrstvwxyz`): 32 symbols for the secret (MSB first) and 4 symbols for the checksum, the first 20 bits (MSB first) of BLAKE2b-256 (`hypercore-crypto` `hash`) of utf8(`'varanda/recovery-code/v1\n'`) || secret, shown as 9 groups of 4 joined by `-`. Decoding ignores case, spaces and hyphens, reads O as 0 and I and L as 1, refuses any other character or a length other than 36, and refuses a bad checksum before any network request. The code string is the secret: it is shown once and never logged, stored or put in an error.
- Flows. Sign-in with a marked passkey: the node derives `lookupId`, the app fetches the blob (`POST /account/recovery/fetch`), and the node opens it and installs the ROOT bound to that credential (the sealed session holds the root, never the ceremony PRF). Sign-in with a credential that returned no user handle and is not the bound one: the node installs the ceremony PRF directly, as before recovery existed, and looks nothing up at the account service, so no value derived from an ordinary passkey's PRF (the member root) is ever sent to the service and no service answer can turn a passkey into another identity. The original passkey therefore opens the account without internet on any device. An added passkey or the code needs the account service at every new sign-in ceremony (the blob is fetched each time; only a session the device kept avoids it). Limit: a compliant authenticator returns the user handle in a discoverable sign-in, which is the case of a device with no binding. If a non-compliant password manager omits the user handle for an ADDED (marked) passkey on a device where that passkey is not bound, the device cannot see the mark and installs that passkey's own PRF: it opens a separate, empty account there instead of the member's account. Nothing of the real account is affected, and the code or the original passkey still recover it on that device. Code recovery: the code derives `lookupId`, the blob is fetched and opened, and the node holds the root for at most 120 s while the person creates a NEW marked passkey in a user gesture; the root is then installed bound to that new credential (normal bind and sealed session) and wrapped under its PRF, and the app registers that blob with a signed put. A code unlock never installs without a passkey and never saves a session of its own; if the put fails, the device stays bound and signed in and Ajustes offers to register again. If the wrap of the root under the new passkey's PRF fails after the install (or a cancel, lock or sign-out lands in between), the in-app device rolls back, because a device bound to a passkey with no blob at the service could never be opened from another device: it clears the worklet identity (`identity.clear`), deletes the keychain session it just saved, refuses the new passkey as `INSTALL_FAILED` and answers `RECOVERY_BAD` ("enter the code again"). The browser node wraps the root under the new PRF BEFORE it installs, so no wrap can fail after the install there; an install that fails leaves it locked and refuses the new passkey as `INSTALL_FAILED`, and a `cancelRecovery()` that lands after the bind undoes the bind and the sealed session. A recovery passkey creation on the browser node that came without PRF (`{ pending: true }`) is recorded at once in `passkeyAttempts` with the refused code `ADD_PENDING` (`created` true, `cleaned` false, never signalled for deletion): the pending creation itself lives in memory only, so after a reload the entry keeps that passkey excluded from later creations and it is never offered for 'concluir'; a successful 'concluir' (or the bind of 'Trocar') drops the entry. 'Trocar a passkey' adds a marked passkey, registers it, then rebinds this device to it (`identity.recoveryBind` on the worklet, `bindPasskey` on the browser node); the original passkey keeps deriving the same root wherever it exists and cannot be invalidated by the service, so retiring it means deleting it in the password manager.
- Removing or replacing a recovery entry does not rotate the root. The service stops serving that blob, but anyone who fetched it while the entry was live and holds its secret (that passkey's PRF or that code) can still unwrap the root and open the account. This is inherent to the wrapped-root design: every key derives from the root of the original passkey, which no entry change can replace. So a remove, or a new code replacing the previous one, only stops entries on devices that have not yet fetched that blob; it never takes access away from whoever already used the passkey or code, nor from a device still signed in with it. A member who believes an added passkey or the code leaked has no way to cut that holder off except creating a new account (a new identity). The app says so on the code card and in both confirmations.
- Registering, listing and removing entries need an unlocked node and a member linked to the account service. The member Google link is retired: an old build's `POST /account/authorize` answers 410 `versao-antiga`. A new link is a verified phone, and an earlier member's Google sub and e-mail stay. The put carries the blob; the session computes `blobHash` itself with `blobHashOf` (`packages/core/lib/recovery-proof.js`) and signs the recovery statement (section 3, "Reports and operator requests", RPC `recovery.sign`, `docs/rpc.md` 5.90); the service recomputes the hash from the blob it stores.

### Association data dir

- `association.json` = `{ "v": 1, "publicKey": "<hex64>", "secretKey": "<128 lowercase hex>" }`, written with mode `0o600`.
- `members.jsonl` = one JSON line per issued certificate with exactly `{ member, ageBand, method, date }` — never a signature, a secret, a face image or a document.
- `seeders.jsonl` = one JSON line `{ "seeder": hex64, "date": ISO }` + LF per issued seeder certificate (`issueSeederCertificate`, R5.2 d) — never a signature or a secret.

### Files and replicas

- JSON state files are written atomically (`writeJsonAtomic`, `packages/core/lib/store.js`): the content goes to a UNIQUE temporary name in the same directory, `.<base>.<pid>.<6 random bytes hex>.tmp`, opened with `O_WRONLY | O_CREAT | O_EXCL | O_NOFOLLOW` and the target mode (so a planted symlink at any predictable name such as `<file>.tmp` is never followed and never created), fsynced, then `renameSync` over `<file>`; on any failure the temporary file is unlinked in a `finally`. Two concurrent writers (same or different processes) each use their own temporary file, so both succeed and the last rename wins (never `ENOENT` from a shared temp name).
- The account service's directory (`apps/cli/lib/account/store.js`, data dir mode `0o700`) is three kinds of file. `directory.json` is the snapshot `{ v, seq, journal, links, bySub, byUsername, usedNames }` (a file without `seq` is seq 0; `journal` only in `v` 2; `deleted`, a map from a deleted member key to `{ at }`, only once an account was deleted, see "Account deletion"), written through `writeJsonAtomic` with mode `0o600`; every link record must be whole (`sub`, `username` and `email` strings, `usernameChangedAt` and `linkedAt` parseable dates, `cert` and `usernameRecord` objects) or the open fails closed. `directory.journal.<id>` is the journal (never named `*.log`, so log rotation does not match it; `<id>` a random 16-byte hex id): NDJSON whose first line is the header `{ "journal": "<id>" }` (written and fsynced before any change line) and whose later lines are one change each `{ seq, op: 'link' | 'email' | 'rename' | 'delete', member, ... }` (`delete` carries only `at`: the link leaves `links` and `bySub`, the key enters `deleted`, and every name it held stays in `usedNames` held by the deleted key, which a build without `deleted` refuses, so it is a one-way migration like the first page; the hwm counts links plus deleted rows, so a deletion never reads as a restored copy); it is created `O_EXCL|O_NOFOLLOW` and every read-write open calls `fchmod(0o600)` on it. `directory.hwm` is the high-water mark `{ seq, journal, links, names }` (`writeJsonAtomic`, mode `0o600`): the highest committed `seq` with the link and `usedNames` counts at that mark, written under the writer guard after every compaction and at close, and never lowered (a stored mark above the new one makes the store unavailable instead). Each change is one `write` and one `fsync`, and the fsynced append is the commit point; a failed journal `fsync` (or a write that may have put bytes in the file) makes the store unavailable (`indisponivel`) and the line is never adopted. `v` is `2` while the journal may hold lines and `1` after a clean close (`close()`: compaction, the hwm, a complete `v` 1 snapshot without `journal`, then the journal unlinked; a close that fails leaves the store open for a retry and the service logs `close falhou` and exits 1); a build that knows only `v` 1 refuses `v` 2 (`diretório de contas inválido`). A `v` 2 snapshot is bound to its journal: a missing or empty journal, one without a header, or one whose header carries another id fails closed at every open (read-write and read-only), so a deleted, rotated or truncated journal never makes names or Google accounts reusable. A journal file the snapshot does not name is an orphan (a crash between two steps below); readers ignore it, a read-write open removes it after its own snapshot write, only once its mtime is older than `ORPHAN_GRACE_MS` = 40000 ms (the lock's staleness plus a heartbeat) and after reading `directory.json` again (an id it names is skipped) under the writer guard; an orphan with a line past the snapshot's `seq` fails closed unless the named journal holds the identical line at that `seq` (a crash after the fold and before the unlink of a retired journal). A journal file is never truncated or rewritten once another process may hold it: the journal's start and every compaction (after `COMPACT_EVERY` = 256 lines, past `COMPACT_BYTES` = 1048576 bytes, and at every read-write open, which always publishes a fresh journal and a new snapshot naming it, also over a header-only journal) create a NEW file with a new id (header fsynced, data dir fsynced), rewrite the snapshot as `v` 2 naming it at the current `seq`, and only then unlink the previous file; lines a stalled writer landed in the previous file meanwhile are folded into the new one before and right after the unlink (through a read fd kept open across it). Before every append and every snapshot write, and again after each append's `fsync` before the line is acknowledged, the writer checks (`fstat` of its fd, `lstat` of the paths) that the journal is the same file (device, inode, link count above 0) with exactly the size it expects and that `directory.json` is the file (device, inode) it last read or wrote; any difference makes the store unavailable without writing (or without acknowledging the line just written), so another writer's open fences a stalled one and the unlink of its journal (nlink 0) ends it; the `account` service also marks the store unavailable as soon as its lock is lost. The guard and the write stay separate syscalls (no OS file locks): a write landing in the retired file after the new writer's last fold is refused to its client, never acknowledged. A snapshot rename whose data dir `fsync` failed is fsynced again before the next change is acknowledged. Readers resolve the journal by the id the snapshot names, replay the lines with `seq` above the snapshot's, ignore a torn trailing line, and read again (up to `OPEN_RETRIES` = 3 retries) when the snapshot's `v`, `seq` or journal id changed while they read; a gap in `seq` or a line that breaks an invariant fails closed. A read-write open whose snapshot+journal end below the hwm refuses with `diretório de contas restaurado de uma cópia antiga; confira o backup` (code `RESTAURADO`): by `seq` for a snapshot with `seq`, by the link and `usedNames` counts alone for a snapshot without it (written by an older build, whose `seq` restarted; one that passes continues the numbering from the mark's `seq`); a read-only open reports it (`restored`) and still reads. Read-only opens never write, unlink, chmod or create anything. Never rotate or delete a `directory.journal.*`; back up the three files together with the service stopped, but restore only `directory.json` and its `directory.journal.*` from the latest backup and KEEP the volume's existing `directory.hwm` (restore the backup's hwm only if the volume lost it), since overwriting the live mark with the backup's would lower it and the restored-copy refusal could never fire (an operator who deliberately restores an older copy deletes `directory.hwm`, README). A phone link is a link record with `sub` `'telefone:'` plus the member key (lowercase hex64, `phoneSub` in `apps/cli/lib/account/store.js`) and `email` `''`, written only by a phone finish (`store.finish` with `phone: true`). The label passes `SUB_RE` (`packages/core/lib/account-link.js`), `validLink` and `indexes()`, so it needs no new journal op, field or migration: an older build reads the record as an ordinary link. `bySub` stays one sub to one member, including an earlier member's Google sub. Nothing erases that sub or its e-mail. The number is not stored in the directory. `POST /account/finish` (`apps/cli/lib/account/server.js`) is in phone mode when phone verification is on and the pending row is still phase `'reserve'` with no `sub`, on the cookie path and on the app `V` path (`x-varanda-link`). `V` (`x-varanda-link`) is the bearer proof of an app row. The member Google sign-in is retired, so the redeem code `R` (`x-varanda-redeem`) is never minted. `POST /account/authorize` answers 410 `versao-antiga` before any configuration, proof, store check or outbound request, and builds no Google URL: an installed old build reads that 4xx as a definite refusal and does not retry. `GET /account/callback` reads no query, makes no code exchange and no outbound request, logs `{ event: 'callback', reason: 'aposentado' }` and redirects to `/?conta=erro`. `POST /account/authorize`, `GET /account/callback`, `GET /account/pending` and `GET /account/app/conta` answer the same way when the issuer is missing: they never call `configured()`. The `google` option of `createAccountServer` is ignored. The moderator panel (`/account/mod/*`) keeps its own Google login (`googleMod`, redirect `origin + '/account/mod/callback'`). `VARANDA_GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID` and `VARANDA_GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET` stay required because only that panel uses them. With phone verification off the service logs one line, event `cadastro`, which the human line prints as `cadastro de novas contas desligado: verificação de celular não configurada`. The finish statement is verified with `sub` = `phoneSub(member)`. Order after that proof: the finish limit, the age check, the phone check (403 `telefone-pendente` until the member holds a live sealed row), the member added to the server-local linking set (`linking`, a map from member to a count of phone finishes in flight), `await phoneRoutes.settled(member)` (every phone-store call queued before that wait commits or fails; `phones.settled`), the phone check again, then `store.finish({ signature, phone: true })`. The count is dropped in a `finally` (a count above 1 is only decremented). The linked check the phone routes read, on each request, is `store.status(m).linked || linking.has(m)`. A record of the same number for another member queued before this member entered `linking` can still end the row: `settled` lets it land and the second phone check answers `telefone-pendente` with no link. A record queued after the member is in `linking` sees the member as linked and answers `telefone-em-uso`. While the member is in `linking`, `POST /account/start` for that member answers 400 `pedido-invalido` before it touches the store, an invite hold or an app row, and with invites on the row is marked as finishing before the wait. With invites on, `finishInvited` resolves the subject with `memberForSub(row.sub ?? phoneSub(row.member))`, because a phone row has no `sub` yet. An invite code is spent only when the link commits; an unverified member keeps the pending row and the code (403 `telefone-pendente`). `GET /account/pending` answers 404 `expirado` for every caller (a cookie, an app `V`, a redeem code or none) and reads and writes nothing. `GET /account/app/conta` answers 404 `expirado` with or without a query, never echoes the query and never logs it. A finish that is not a phone finish answers 404 `expirado` once the body has a signature and a pending row, and before that signature is verified: phone verification off, a row past phase `'reserve'`, or a row that already holds a `sub` (a subject left by the retired Google callback, which is never finished). A finish with no issuer still answers 503 `indisponivel`. `store.finish` with `phone: true` accepts only a live row in phase `'reserve'` with no `sub`. `account phone --username <nome>` (`lookupPhone` in `apps/cli/lib/commands/account.js`) is read-only and prints at most one JSON line for the name's single holder (`nameHolder`, current or past; a leading `@` is stripped). `kind` is `membro`, `excluido` or `pagina`. A member line is `{ username, key, kind, current, deletedAt, phone }`, and `phone` is the rows `--member` would print (an empty list when there are none). A page prints `owner` and no number, and reads no phone key; a moved page also prints `movedTo`. A page with no `pages.json` row fails with `página sem registro em pages.json`. An unknown name fails with `nome não encontrado`.
- `<account dataDir>/usage.json` (`apps/cli/lib/account/usage-stats.js`) = `{ v: 1, days: [{ day, members, admitted, activeDay, activeWeek, blocks, bytes, mediaBytes, topicPosts, connections, external }] }`. `day` is the Brasília calendar day (fixed UTC−3; no DST since 2019) of the sample. The account service passes `localDay` from `packages/core/lib/activity.js` (`DAY_OFFSET_MS`, `dayStart`, `localDay`) as `dayOf`, so a history row and the seeder's `activeDay` start the day at the same instant. Each count is a non-negative safe integer or `null` (unknown). Rows are strictly increasing by `day`, one per day, and only the newest `MAX_DAYS` = 400 are kept. The file is replaced whole through `writeJsonAtomic` with mode `0o600`, and only while the account store still allows writes. A path that is not a regular file, or a file whose JSON is not exactly that shape (a day out of order included), is never replaced: it is logged once as `{ event: 'usage', reason: 'invalido' }`, `history` in the stats view is `null`, and the operator can inspect it. A failed write is logged once as `{ event: 'usage', reason: 'falhou' }` until a later write succeeds. `PEAK_FIELDS` = `activeDay`, `activeWeek`, `connections`, `external` keep the day's maximum; `members`, `admitted`, `blocks`, `bytes`, `mediaBytes` and `topicPosts` keep the latest value; a `null` sample never erases a stored count. A sample whose day is earlier than the last row (the clock stepped back) is ignored. The background sample runs `FIRST_SAMPLE_MS` = 60000 ms after start and then every `SAMPLE_MS` = 900000 ms (15 min). Deleting the file only loses the history (the next sample starts a new one). The account service logs `{ event: 'usage', reason: 'ligada' }` when `VARANDA_STATS_EMAILS` turns the feature on and `{ event: 'usage', reason: 'desligada' }` when it is off. Those lines carry no e-mail, key, username or address.
- `GET /account/mod/api/stats` (`apps/cli/lib/account/mod-routes.js`) needs a panel session whose e-mail is in `VARANDA_STATS_EMAILS`, and that list must be a subset of `VARANDA_MODERATOR_EMAILS`: `deploy.sh` refuses a list that is not, and so does the account service at start (`VARANDA_STATS_EMAILS inválido`, exit 1); the route also keeps only addresses that are moderators. Anyone else with a session gets 403 `{ "error": "nao-autorizado" }`; a request with no session stays the existing 401 `{ "error": "nao-autorizado" }`. The body is exactly `{ at, members: { total, max }, seeder, signups: { weeks, months }, history }`. `at` is the view time as a canonical ISO string. `members.total` is the number of linked accounts and `members.max` is the member cap or `null`. `seeder` is `null` until a read of `GET <VARANDA_SEEDER_HEALTH>/health` succeeds (default `http://127.0.0.1:8788`; loopback `http` with an explicit port only, and the URL is read only while stats are on); otherwise `{ at, uptimeSeconds, admitted, activeDay, activeWeek, blocks, bytes, mediaBytes, topicPosts, connections, external }`, each count a non-negative safe integer or `null` (an older seeder without `activeDay` / `activeWeek` yields `null` for those two). A panel view triggers at most one such read per `FRESH_MS` = 300000 ms (5 min), and concurrent views share the in-flight sample. `signups.weeks` is 12 `{ start, count }` buckets, the weeks ending with the one that contains today, `start` the Monday of that week as `YYYY-MM-DD`; `signups.months` is 12 `{ month, count, total }` buckets, the calendar months ending with the current one, `month` as `YYYY-MM` and `total` the cumulative sign-ups through that month. Both bucket by the Brasília calendar day (fixed UTC−3; no DST since 2019) of `linkedAt` (`localDay`). The sign-up counts are exact: there is no small-cell suppression, because `linkedAt` is the `date` on the member's public certificate. `history` is the `usage.json` rows, oldest first, or `null` when the file is invalid. The body has no report, pending or blocked counts, and never a key, an e-mail, a sub, a username or an address. `GET /account/mod/api/me` carries `stats: true` only for a stats admin; the key is omitted for everyone else, so the unauthenticated body `deploy.sh` probes (`{"error":"nao-autorizado"}\n`, HTTP 401) is unchanged.
- `<account dataDir>/recovery.json` (`apps/cli/lib/account/recovery-store.js`) holds the member recovery entries (section 1, "Web member storage (B2)"). It is ONE sidecar file like `terms.json`, mode `0o600`, replaced whole through `writeJsonAtomic`, and never part of `directory.json`, `directory.journal.*` or `directory.hwm`, so older and newer builds keep opening the same data dir. The shape is exactly `{ v: 1, seq, entries, removed }`: `seq` is a non-negative safe integer, the store-wide generation counter; `entries` maps a `lookupId` (hex64) to `{ member, kind, nonce, ciphertext, createdAt, gen }` and `removed` maps a `lookupId` to the tombstone `{ member, removedAt, gen }`; an id is never both, and a `gen` above `seq` makes the file malformed. Opening never creates, mkdirs or chmods anything (a missing file means no entry yet). Validation is total: a file the module would not have written is never rewritten and every recovery call then fails with `indisponivel` until an operator repairs it, while every other route of the service stays up. Every write first re-reads the file and asks the service lock before and after it. Generations are ONE store-wide monotonic sequence, never per id: every put, every remove and every tombstone the normalisation writes takes `gen` = ++`seq`, and a merge takes `seq` = max(memory, disk) and keeps, per id, whichever of row or tombstone has the higher `gen` (ties keep memory), so a remove beats the stale row it buried and a later re-put beats the tombstone, whatever the clocks say. Of two live `'code'` rows of one member after a merge, only the strictly higher `gen` stays and the others become tombstones under fresh gens. Rows tied at the highest `gen` (a stale writer that lost its lock and its replacement both took `seq` + 1) are all kept live: neither write is newer, and choosing by `lookupId` could delete the code that was acknowledged; the next code put buries them all. Tombstone pruning: a tombstone whose `removedAt` is older than 30 days goes; then each member keeps at most 32, dropping the lowest gens first, but the cap never drops a tombstone younger than 10 minutes (`STALE_WINDOW_MS`, which also covers a `removedAt` ahead of this clock) nor the member's newest one, so a member may briefly hold more than 32. A copy of the file older than a pruned tombstone may resurrect that row (accepted: a stale writer's rename lands within one lock period, far inside that window). Reads: after an uncertain write (a failed write, or the lock refused after the rename) `list` and `fetch` first re-read and merge the file, or answer `indisponivel`, never from stale memory. Caps per member: 4 live `'passkey'` rows (a 5th answers 409 `{ "error": "limite-recuperacao" }`) and 1 live `'code'` row (a new code replaces the previous one, whose id becomes a tombstone, so the old code stops opening the account on any device that has not already used it; see "Web member storage (B2)" for why it never revokes past use); tombstones never count. A `lookupId` owned by another member answers `pedido-invalido`. `account export` and `account status` do not include the file. Logs carry only `{ event: 'recovery', reason }`, never a `lookupId`, a blob, a member key or an address.
- Recovery routes (`apps/cli/lib/account/recovery-routes.js`), HTTPS from the app to the account service, never from the swarm; POST only, same origin check and 4096-byte body cap as the other account POSTs; without a usable store every one answers 503 `indisponivel`:
  - `POST /account/recovery/start` `{ member }` -> `{ nonce }` for a linked member (single use, 10 minutes). The nonce is stateless, like the report nonces: base64url (54 characters) of an 8-byte expiry, 16 random bytes and a 16-byte HMAC-SHA256 under a per-process secret over `'varanda-recovery-nonce-v1'` and the member, so start stores nothing and a restart expires every outstanding nonce. Only the canonical spelling is accepted. Expiry and pruning read a clock that never steps back (the highest wall-clock value seen), so a wall clock corrected backwards cannot revive a spent nonce. A step back of more than one TTL instead draws a new secret, which voids every outstanding nonce, clears the spent set and lets TTLs follow the wall clock again. There is no per-member quota on start (anyone may name any public key, so such a quota would let a stranger lock a member out of removing a leaked code); only the global bucket bounds it.
  - `POST /account/recovery/put` `{ member, nonce, signature, kind, lookupId, blob }`, `POST /account/recovery/remove` `{ member, nonce, signature, kind, lookupId }` and `POST /account/recovery/list` `{ member, nonce, signature }`. Bodies: put -> `{ createdAt, changed }`, remove -> `{ changed }` (`changed` false for a no-op: the entry was already there, or already gone), list -> `{ entries: [{ lookupId, kind, createdAt }] }` (never the nonce or ciphertext). For each: the nonce must be one this process minted for that member, live and not yet spent; then the statement is verified with the service's association and, for put, `blobHash` recomputed from the validated blob; a per-member window (10 per 600 s) is charged only after the proof, then the nonce is recorded as spent until its own expiry (a refused proof spends nothing, so the spent set grows only with validly signed requests), then the link is checked, then the store. A put or remove that fails after a possible write (answered 503 `resultado-incerto`: a lost lock or a failed write) is an unknown outcome: the app keeps its unknown-outcome handling and never retries by itself; listing settles it.
  - `POST /account/recovery/fetch` `{ lookupId }` -> `{ member, kind, blob }` or 404 `{ "error": "nao-encontrado", "recovery": true }`. The `recovery: true` mark tells this route's miss from any other 404 (an unknown route of an older service, a proxy page): clients signing in with a marked passkey or entering a code read only the marked body as "no entry" and treat every other 404 as the service being unavailable (a sign-in that cannot ask stays locked; a passkey without the mark is never looked up). The `lookupId` travels only in a POST body, never in a path or query string. A malformed id answers `pedido-invalido` without touching the store, and neither unknown nor malformed ids grow any state. It is charged on the shared lookup window and the global bucket only, with no per-IP limit (the service never reads the client address).
  - Recovery statement (`packages/core/lib/recovery-proof.js`): the UTF-8 bytes of `'varanda-recovery-v1'`, action (`'put'`, `'remove'` or `'list'`), nonce, member, association, kind, `lookupId`, `blobHash` joined by LF with no trailing LF, signed by the member's diary key. `kind` and `lookupId` are `''` for list; `blobHash` is `''` except for put, where it is the lowercase hex BLAKE2b-256 (`hypercore-crypto` `hash`) of utf8(kind + LF + `lookupId` + LF + `blob.nonce` + LF + `blob.ciphertext`). The action is signed, so a put never verifies as a remove or a list.
- `<account dataDir>/phone.json` (`apps/cli/lib/account/phone-store.js`) holds the verified phone numbers. It exists only once a first number was verified with `VARANDA_PHONE_KEY` set; it is ONE sidecar file, mode `0o600`, replaced whole through `writeJsonAtomic`, and never part of the directory files. Shape: `{ v: 1, check, since, rows, released }`. `check` is the lowercase hex HMAC-SHA256 that ties the file to its key: two subkeys are derived from the 32-byte `VARANDA_PHONE_KEY` (HMAC-SHA256 under the labels `'varanda-phone-seal-v1'` and `'varanda-phone-index-v1'`) and `check` is the index subkey's HMAC of `'varanda-phone-check-v1'`, so a file written under another key fails closed instead of being rewritten. `since` is the canonical ISO time of the first verification. Each row is exactly `{ member, index, sealed, verifiedAt, endedAt, end, burnedAt }`: `member` the diary key (hex64); `index` the lowercase hex HMAC of the E.164 number under the index subkey (uniqueness and burns compare it, never the number); `sealed` `{ iv, tag, data }` (canonical base64, AES-256-GCM under the seal subkey with the AAD `'varanda-phone-v1\n' + member`, so a seal moved to another member's row never opens) or `null` once only a burn is left; `verifiedAt` canonical ISO; `end` `null` (live) or one of `'trocado'`, `'excluida'`, `'substituido'`, `'liberado'`, with `endedAt` set exactly when `end` is; `burnedAt` canonical ISO or `null` (the number of an owner blocked by the moderation desk stays taken while the block lasts). At most one live row per member and per index. `released` maps a member to the time of a court-ordered release (`account phone --release`). Retention: an ended or burned row keeps its seal 180 days, then a burn keeps only the index and an ended row goes; a live row of a key that never linked goes after 7 days (`apps/cli/lib/account/phone-routes.js` sweeps at start and every hour). Validation is total: a file the store would not have written (unknown key, bad shape, another `check`) is never rewritten, and `send`, `check`, `status` and, when the service has an issuer, `since` then answer 503 `indisponivel`, while `policy` answers 200 `{ required: true }`, `start` answers 200 `{ nonce }` and `since` without an issuer answers 200 `{ since: null }` (none of these three reads the store). The number is in no certificate, swarm message, log line, moderation view or `account export`; only the operator command `account phone --member <hex64>` (no HTTP route) prints it, also reached by name with `account phone --username <nome>`, which prints at most one line `{ username, key, kind, current, deletedAt, phone }` for the name's single holder (`nameHolder`, current or past; a page prints `owner` and no number).
- Phone routes (`apps/cli/lib/account/phone-routes.js`), HTTPS from the app to the account service, never from the swarm. The POSTs have the same origin check as the other account POSTs and a 4096-byte body cap. The feature is on when the service has both a phone client and a phone store (`VARANDA_PHONE_KEY` set), even when the store could not open: then the gate stays shut and the routes answer `indisponivel`.
  - `GET /account/phone/policy` -> exactly `{ required }` (also with the feature off).
  - `GET /account/phone/since` -> `{ since: null | PhoneSince }` (section 3 "Phone attestations"): `null` while the feature is off, without an issuer or before a first verification; an unreadable store answers 503 `indisponivel`, never `null`.
  - `POST /account/phone/start` `{ member }` -> `{ nonce }` (single use, 10 minutes, in memory). It reads no state; with the feature off it answers 503 `indisponivel`.
  - `POST /account/phone/send` `{ member, nonce, signature, phone }` (signature: the phone-verification request for `'send'`, section 3 "Reports and operator requests"; `phone` a Brazilian mobile in E.164, `+55` and 11 digits; a `channel` field is refused) -> `{ state: 'enviado' }` once the provider was asked to text a 6-digit code by SMS, or `{ state: 'verificado', proof? }` when that number is already the member's. It needs the member's age band (`idade-pendente` otherwise) and, for a key without a link, room in the directory (`lista-de-espera`). It answers the same for a free, a taken and a burned number.
  - `POST /account/phone/check` `{ member, nonce, signature, code }` (`code` 6 digits) -> `{ state: 'verificado', proof? }`, where `proof` is a phone-verified attestation dated the row's `verifiedAt` when the service has an issuer. Only after a correct code is the number checked against the others (`telefone-em-uso`).
  - `POST /account/phone/status` `{ member, nonce, signature }` -> `{ state: 'verificado', proof? }` or `{ state: 'nenhum' }`.
  - Errors (`{ error }`): 400 `pedido-invalido`, `assinatura-invalida`, `telefone-invalido`, `codigo-invalido`; 403 `idade-pendente`; 409 `telefone-em-uso`, `telefone-recusado` (landline, VoIP or disposable line), `conta-excluida`; 410 `codigo-expirado` (no code pending, the code expired or the attempts are used up); 429 `limite-telefone` (5 sends per member and 3 per number per day, 60 s between sends, 10 checks and 30 status reads per member per 10 minutes); 503 `lista-de-espera`, `indisponivel` (feature off, store unreadable, daily ceiling, or the provider refused the request) and `resultado-incerto` (a send or check whose outcome is unknown; the client never repeats it on its own); the nonce errors are `expirado` and `assinatura-invalida` as for the age routes.
  - The gate: with the feature on, `POST /account/finish`, `/account/rename/finish` and `/account/invites/finish`, and after the signature and the linked check the push finish for `on`, the seen finish for `write` and a page create by the member (not by a delegate), answer 403 `telefone-pendente` until the member holds a live sealed row. `POST /account/authorize` is not on this gate: the member Google sign-in is retired and that route answers 410 `versao-antiga` before any check. Push `off`, seen `read` and `remove`, terms, deletion, recovery and passkeys are never gated. For Feira codes a seller (author or page owner) with no live row counts as not linked, so the route answers 404 `nao-encontrado` as for an unlinked owner. The number never appears in a certificate or a swarm message; the phone-verified attestation carries only the member key and a date.
- Followed logs (replicas) are `store.get({ key: b4a.from(hex, 'hex'), valueEncoding: 'json' })` (`openReplica`); they are never writable. Their blocks are read only with `core.get(i, { raw: true, ... })` followed by `decodeBlock` (R4.1), never through the JSON value encoding.
- A not-yet-accepted log is NEVER opened in the main Corestore; its block 0 is fetched through the quarantine store (section 4, R5.4). An empty core must not exist in the main store.
- R5.9 a: replicas and the diary keep hypercore's DEFAULT `allowFork: true`; the project never passes `allowFork: false` (with it a rewrite is silently ignored and undetectable through public API; with the default the rewrite is applied and surfaces as the `'truncate'` event and `core.fork !== 0`).
- Every purge in the project goes through `purgeReplica(store, hex)` or through `quarantineGet0` (R5.4 b, c).

### Trust root

Every node trusts exactly ONE association public key, persisted in `config.json` and immutable afterwards. `id create` and the first seeder command (`seed key`, `seed cert add` or `seed`, through `Seeder.ensureConfig`) require it (`--association <hex64>` or env `VARANDA_ASSOCIATION`; seeder USAGE `informe --association ou VARANDA_ASSOCIATION`). On every later command `--association`/`VARANDA_ASSOCIATION` is optional and, when given, must equal `config.json`'s value, else error `associação diferente da configurada`. A node never infers its trust root from a certificate it received.

## 2. Log records (JSON blocks; seq = block index, 0-based)

- Block 0 (genesis) MUST be `{ "type": "member-cert", "cert": Certificate }` with `cert.member` equal to the diary key; no other block may have this type; a diary without block 0 cannot receive other records.
- `{ "type": "profile", "name": <string 1..80 chars>, "bio": <string 0..280 chars>, "at": <ISO-8601 UTC string> }`, optionally with a fifth key `"avatar": AvatarDescriptor` (below; never `null`: a profile without a photo is the plain 4-key record). An `avatar` value that is not an AvatarDescriptor is reason `media-invalid`, checked right after the key set.
- `{ "type": "post", "text": <string 1..1000 chars>, "at": <ISO>, "replyTo": null | { "author": "<hex64>", "seq": <integer >= 1> } }`. A post with `replyTo` is a reply to block `seq` of `author` (`seq >= 1` because block 0 is never a post). A post may carry a fifth key, `"media": [MediaDescriptor, ...]` (1 to `MAX_MEDIA_PER_POST` = 10 descriptors, below); with `media` the text may be empty (`0..1000` chars, a photo-only post), without it the text is `1..1000` chars. A `media` value that is not such a list (a sparse array included: its holes are invalid) is reason `media-invalid`.
- `{ "type": "username", ... }` (seq >= 1 only; at seq 0 it is `genesis-required`): the member's @username, signed by the association's issuer (section 3, "Username record"). `validateRecord` checks its shape only; readers take the name from `latestUsername` (`packages/core/lib/username.js`), which also verifies the signatures. Nodes from before this type treat it as an unknown type (`malformed`) and skip it like any invalid block, without rejecting the diary.
- `{ "type": "media-core", "key": "<hex64>", "at": <ISO> }` (seq >= 1): declares a media core of this diary (section 2 "Media cores"). Exactly these 3 keys, else `malformed`; a non-canonical `at` is `date-invalid`.
- `{ "type": "follow", "key": "<hex64>", "on": <boolean>, "at": <ISO> }` (seq >= 1): the member starts (`on: true`) or stops (`on: false`) following `key`. Exactly these 4 keys, else `malformed`; a non-canonical `at` is `date-invalid`. The follow set of a diary is `followSet(entries, ownHex)` (`packages/core/lib/media.js`): the valid `follow` records replayed in seq order, the last one per key wins, the diary's own key ignored, the result sorted. Follows are therefore public: anyone holding the diary can read whom the member follows.
- `{ "type": "dm-key", "box": "<hex64>", "gen": <integer 1..4294967295>, "at": <ISO> }` (seq >= 1): the member's direct message box key (X25519 public key, section 11), a key advertisement only. Exactly these 4 keys (`DM_KEY_KEYS`), else `malformed`; `box` is lowercase hex64 and not a weak box key (`isWeakBoxKey`, `packages/core/lib/group.js`), else `malformed`; `gen` is an integer in `1..4294967295` (`MAX_DM_KEY_GEN`, the uint32be of the key derivation), else `malformed`; a non-canonical `at` is `date-invalid`. Published once per identity; readers take the valid `dm-key` with the highest `gen`, ties by the highest seq, and a rotation appends `gen + 1`. It is never a message, an accept, a block or a peer key, and it makes no push candidate and no notice row. Old clients skip it as an unknown type.
- Página records (section 12; `packages/core/lib/records.js`, `validateRecord` never throws; all of them are seq >= 1, and an unknown key set or a bad field is `malformed`, a non-canonical `at` or `exp` too). Hex fields are lowercase; `page`, `owner`, `to`, `from`, `actor`, `diary` and every admin key are hex64; `gen` is an integer in `1..4294967295` (`>= 2` for `page-close` and `page-accept`); `nonce` matches `NONCE_RE` of `packages/core/lib/account-link.js`; `admins` is 0..16 hex64 keys sorted ascending, unique and never equal to `owner`. Every signature is Ed25519, 128 lowercase hex, over the UTF-8 payload `prefix LF field LF field ...` (no trailing LF, `null` written as the 4 characters `null`, `admins` as the keys joined with `,`, empty for none). There is NO `page-invite` record: an invite is a private service statement (section 12).
  - `page-roster`: exact keys `type, page, gen, owner, admins, prev, at, nonce, association, issuer, issuerDate, issuerSignature, signature, countersignature`; `prev` is `null` or an integer >= 1 (the seq of the previous-gen roster). `signature` is the owner's over `varanda-page-roster-v1`, `page, gen, owner, admins, prev, at, nonce, association`.
  - `page-accept`: exact keys `type, page, gen, rosterSignature, at, nonce, actor, signature`; `actor` (the invitee) signs `varanda-page-accept-v1`, `page, gen, rosterSignature, at, nonce, actor`. No issuer.
  - `page-offer`: exact keys `type, page, to, exp, gen, at, nonce, actor, signature`; `to` and `exp` are both `null` (a cancel) or both set; `actor` (the owner) signs `varanda-page-offer-v1`, `page, to, exp, gen, at, nonce, actor`. No issuer.
  - `page-close`: exact keys `type, page, gen, at, nonce, actor, association, issuer, issuerDate, issuerSignature, signature, countersignature`; `actor` (the owner) signs `varanda-page-close-v1`, `page, gen, at, nonce, actor, association`.
  - `page-moved`: exact keys `type, page, to, gen, at, nonce, actor, association, issuer, issuerDate, issuerSignature, signature, countersignature`; `to !== page`; `actor` (the owner) signs `varanda-page-moved-v1`, `page, to, gen, at, nonce, actor, association`.
  - `page-origin`: exact keys `type, page, from, fromSeq, gen, owner, admins, at, nonce, actor, association, issuer, issuerDate, issuerSignature, signature, countersignature`; `from !== page`, `fromSeq` is an integer >= 1, `actor` must equal `owner` and signs `varanda-page-origin-v1`, `page, from, fromSeq, gen, owner, admins, at, nonce, actor, association`.
  - `page-link`: exact keys `type, page, diary, actor, at, nonce, association, issuer, issuerDate, issuerSignature, signature`, `diary !== page`, `actor !== diary` and `actor !== page`; it is signed by the ISSUER alone (like a username record) over `varanda-page-link-v1`, `page, diary, actor, at, nonce, association`.
  - `page-delegate`: exact keys `type, actor, at`, written by a core node into its own manifest diary (section 12, "Nó core: delegado"); the diary's Hypercore signature authenticates it, so it carries no signature of its own.
  - The delegation trio `association`, `issuer`, `issuerDate`, `issuerSignature` is copied from the issuer certificate exactly as `createUsernameRecord` does (section 3): `issuerSignature` is the ASSOCIATION's signature over the issuer delegation, not a signature over the page record. The four countersigned types (`page-roster`, `page-origin`, `page-close`, `page-moved`) carry `countersignature`, the ISSUER's signature over the issuer payload: the issuer prefix (`varanda-page-roster-issuer-v1`, `varanda-page-origin-issuer-v1`, `varanda-page-close-issuer-v1`, `varanda-page-moved-issuer-v1`), then the same fields in the same order, then `signature`. Verification of a countersigned record returns the first failing reason: `malformed`, `page-mismatch` (`page` is not the log key being read), `untrusted-association`, `bad-issuer-signature` (the delegation, then `countersignature`), `bad-signature`, and `revoked-issuer` last. Old readers skip every `page-*` type as `malformed`, so a page is a person to them.
- `{ "type": "group-join", "group": "<hex64>", "writer": "<hex64>", "on": <boolean>, "at": <ISO> }` (seq >= 1): the member declares (`on: true`) or removes from seeder indexes (`on: false`) a writer core of a group (section 10). Exactly these 5 keys, or these 5 plus an optional sixth key `rejoin` (`null` or exactly `{ writer: <lowercase hex64>, seq: <safe integer >= 1> }`, section 10 "Leave sets and stale leaves"), else `malformed`; `group` and `writer` are lowercase hex64 (`writer` equals `group` for the group's creator, whose root core is also the first writer); a non-canonical `at` is `date-invalid`. Builder `groupJoinRecord({ group, writer, on, rejoin, at })`; `groupJoinHistory` items are `{ writer, on, seq, rejoin }`. For indexes, `groupJoins(entries)` (`packages/core/lib/group.js`) keeps the highest-seq valid join per `writer` only when `on: true`. The fold instead receives the full history from `groupJoinHistory(entries, group)`; binding and membership follow section 10. Joins are public, like follows. To apps, CLIs and seeders from before groups, `group-join` is an unknown type and is skipped like any invalid block, never rejecting the diary.
- `{ "type": "group-cut", "group": "<hex64>", "writer": "<hex64>", "cut": <integer >= 0>, "at": <ISO> }` (seq >= 1): the diary vouches that the records of its writer core `writer` in group `group` at seq >= `cut` carry no authority (section 10, "Creator marks on group roots"). Exactly these 5 keys, else `malformed`; `group` and `writer` are lowercase hex64 and `cut` a safe integer >= 0; a non-canonical `at` is `date-invalid`. Builder `groupCutRecord({ group, writer, cut, at })`. It is not a `group-join` and changes no binding; the fold receives a diary's marks for one group from `groupCutHistory(entries, group)` as `[{ writer, cut, seq }]`, plus `{ gap: true }` when the entries as read skip a seq. Marks are public, like joins. Old clients treat it as an unknown type and skip it.
- `{ "type": "group-confirm", "group": "<hex64>", "writer": "<hex64>", "seq": <integer >= 1>, "digest": "<hex64>", "at": <ISO> }` (seq >= 1): the diary confirms exactly the `group-leave` at (`writer`, `seq`) in group `group` whose digest is `digest` (section 10, "Confirmed leaves"). Exactly these 6 keys, else `malformed`; `group`, `writer` and `digest` are lowercase hex64 and `seq` a safe integer >= 1; a non-canonical `at` is `date-invalid`. Builder `groupConfirmRecord({ group, writer, seq, digest, at })`. `leaveDigest(record)` (`packages/core/lib/group.js`) is the lowercase hex BLAKE2b-256 (32 bytes) of `JSON.stringify({ type: 'group-leave', seen, cuts, at })` in that key order, refs as `{ writer, seq }`, of the validated leave (null for anything that is not a valid `group-leave`), so it never depends on how the block travelled. The fold receives a diary's confirmations for one group from `groupConfirmHistory(entries, group)` as `[{ writer, seq, digest, dseq }]`, `dseq` being the confirmation's own diary seq. Public, like joins; old clients skip it as an unknown type.
- `{ "type": "like", "to": { "author": "<hex64>", "seq": <integer >= 1> }, "on": <boolean>, "at": <ISO> }` and `{ "type": "repost", "to": ..., "on": ..., "at": ... }` (seq >= 1): the member likes / reposts (`on: true`) or takes back the like / repost (`on: false`) of block `seq` of `author`. Exactly these 4 keys, and `to` exactly `{ author: lowercase hex64, seq: safe integer >= 1 }` (`validTarget`), else `malformed`; a non-canonical `at` is `date-invalid`. Builders `likeRecord({ to, on, at })` / `repostRecord({ to, on, at })`. The state of a diary is `engagementState(entries)` (`packages/core/lib/engagement.js`, pure, never throws): the valid `like` / `repost` records replayed in seq order, the last operation per (type, target ref) wins (the rule of `followSet`), giving `{ liked: Set<ref>, reposted: Map<ref, { seq, at }> }` (`seq` / `at` of the effective `on` record). Liking or reposting one's own post is allowed and counted. Likes and reposts are public, like follows.
- Depoimentos (`packages/core/lib/records.js`, `packages/core/lib/testimonials.js`; both seq >= 1, else `genesis-required`). A depoimento is a sealed commitment: the writer's diary says only that it wrote a depoimento to someone, and the text becomes public only when the recipient shows it.
  - `{ "type": "testimonial", "to": "<hex64>", "gen": <integer 1..4294967295>, "hash": "<hex64>", "box": "<base64>", "at": <ISO> }` (`TESTIMONIAL_KEYS`, builder `testimonialRecord({ to, gen, hash, box, at })`), in the WRITER's diary. `to` is the recipient's diary key and `gen` the generation of the recipient's `dm-key` record (section 11, "Key: the `dm-key` record and its derivation") whose `box` key sealed it (`MAX_DM_KEY_GEN` bounds it). `box` is canonical base64 (standard alphabet, padded, equal to its own re-encoding) of `crypto_box_seal(plain, box)` where `plain` is the UTF-8 bytes of the canonical JSON `{"text":<JSON string>,"nonce":"<64 hex>"}`: exactly these two keys in this order, `JSON.stringify` escaping, no whitespace (`canonicalPlain`). `nonce` is 32 random bytes as 64 lowercase hex, fresh for every depoimento. `hash` is the lowercase hex sha256 of `utf8(from + nonce + text)` (`commitment(from, nonce, text)`), `from` the WRITER's diary key as 64 lowercase hex, then the nonce, so the commitment hides a short or guessable text and binds the block to its writer: a copy of `box` and `hash` in another member's diary still opens for the recipient but never matches under that member's key, so it is never listed or shown as theirs. The writer key is not in the sealed plaintext (the box bound below has no room for it); whoever opens a box checks the commitment with the key of the diary that holds the block. Nothing in the record is readable by anyone but the recipient: the public learns only that the writer wrote a sealed depoimento to `to`, when, and roughly how long it is (the box length).
  - `{ "type": "testimonial-show", "from": "<hex64>", "seq": <integer >= 1>, "text": <string 1..400 chars>, "nonce": "<hex64>", "at": <ISO> }` (`TESTIMONIAL_SHOW_KEYS`, builder `testimonialShowRecord({ from, seq, text, nonce, at })`), in the RECIPIENT's (owner's) diary: the owner chose to show the depoimento of block `seq` of `from` on the profile, and copies its opened `text` and `nonce`. This record is public, like a post.
  - Field rules (`check`, reason `malformed` unless said otherwise): exact key sets; `to`, `from`, `hash` and `nonce` lowercase hex64; `gen` a safe integer 1..`MAX_DM_KEY_GEN`; `seq` a safe integer >= 1; `text` 1..`MAX_TESTIMONIAL` = 400 characters counted like post text (`charLength`), with the forbidden characters of the Rules below (LF allowed) and no lone UTF-16 surrogate (a lone surrogate would reach the hash as U+FFFD, so two texts could share one commitment), all checked by `validTestimonialText`; `box` canonical base64 whose decoded length is `MIN_TESTIMONIAL_SEALED` = 135 to `MAX_TESTIMONIAL_SEALED` = 1734 bytes and at most `MAX_TESTIMONIAL_BOX` = 2312 characters. The bound is the worst case: the JSON frame and nonce are 86 bytes, a valid character takes at most 4 bytes in the JSON (a non-BMP character; a quote, a backslash or LF escapes to 2), so `MAX_TESTIMONIAL_PLAIN` = 86 + 4 x 400 = 1686, plus the 48 bytes of `crypto_box_SEALBYTES`. A non-canonical `at` is `date-invalid`.
  - Opening (`openOffer(box, boxKeyPair)`, the recipient's node only, with the box key pair of the `dm-key` generation `gen`): returns `{ text, nonce }` or `null`, never throws; the caller then requires `commitment(author, nonce, text) === hash` with `author` the diary that holds the block. It refuses a non-canonical or out-of-bounds box, a weak box key, a failed open, a plaintext that is not exactly the two keys, a text or nonce that fails the field rules, and a plaintext that is not byte for byte `canonicalPlain(text, nonce)` (a reordered or re-escaped JSON). The opened buffer is zero-filled.
