Sealing (`sealOffer({ from, text, boxPk })`, `from` the writer's own key) refuses a `from` that is not lowercase hex64, an invalid text and a weak box key; errors never carry the text or key material.
  - Verification rule (`verifyShow(show, writerRecord, ownerHex)`, never throws): a reader shows a `testimonial-show` of owner O only when block `show.seq` of `show.from` is present, valid, not tombstoned, a `testimonial`, with `to === O`, `show.from !== O`, and `sha256(utf8(show.from + show.nonce + show.text)) === writerRecord.hash`. Anything else hides the show: the web node lists it under `invalid` (docs/rpc.md, 5.100). The commitment is what keeps an owner from putting words in the writer's mouth: only a text the writer sealed can match the hash.
  - Withdrawal: the writer revokes with a tombstone (`delete`) of its `testimonial`; verification then fails everywhere the tombstone reached, so the owner's show is hidden without any action of the owner. The owner removes the depoimento from the profile with a tombstone of its `testimonial-show`. Declining ("Agora não") is a per-device hide in the app and writes no record. `shownRows(entries)` folds the owner's diary to one candidate per writer: the newest show by seq decides, and a tombstoned newest show hides that writer; each candidate is then checked with `verifyShow`.
  - Old readers: to apps, CLIs and seeders from before depoimentos, `testimonial` and `testimonial-show` are unknown types, skipped as invalid (`malformed`) and never renumbered; the diary is never rejected for them. Such a reader shows no depoimento at all, the shown text included.
- Momentos (`packages/core/lib/records.js`, `packages/core/lib/moments.js`; seq >= 1, else `genesis-required`). A momento is one photo and a short text that readers show for 24 hours after `at` and then hide; the record itself stays in the diary until its author deletes it.
  - `{ "type": "moment", "text": <string 0..200 chars>, "at": <ISO>, "media": MomentMedia }` (`MOMENT_KEYS`, builder `momentRecord({ text, media, at })`). Exact key set, else `malformed`. `text` is 0..`MAX_MOMENT_TEXT` = 200 characters counted like post text (`charLength`, code points), with the forbidden characters of the Rules below (LF allowed) and no lone UTF-16 surrogate (`validMomentText`), else `text-length`. A non-canonical `at` is `date-invalid`.
  - MomentMedia (`validMomentMedia`, else `media-invalid`) is exactly ONE image item in the DM media item shape (`isDmMediaItem` in `packages/rpc/lib/methods.js`), a plain object with exactly the keys `id, key, nonce, chunks, bytes, sha256, mime, w, h, ms` (`MOMENT_MEDIA_KEYS`): the first six are a blob descriptor of section 13 that `validateBlobDescriptor` (`packages/core/lib/blob.js`) accepts (`id`, `key`, `sha256` lowercase hex64, `nonce` lowercase hex48, `bytes` the ciphertext size, 17..`257 * 32768`, `chunks === ceil(bytes / 32768)`); `mime` is one of `MEDIA_MIMES` (`image/jpeg`, `image/png`, `image/webp`, `image/gif`; never audio); `w` and `h` are integers 1..`MAX_MEDIA_DIMENSION` = 16384; `ms === 0`. The photo is not in a media core: it is a blob of the anonymous blob pool (section 13), and its `key` and `nonce` are PUBLIC in the record on purpose, so anyone who reads the diary can fetch the blob by `id` and decrypt it. The reader's `fetchBlob` checks the ciphertext size and `sha256` against the descriptor, opens it with the public key and nonce, and checks the image bounds for the `mime` before showing it; any failure, like a swept blob, shows as "foto indisponível".
  - Reader rule (`foldMoments`, `ownPast` in `packages/core/lib/moments.js`, pure): a live (not tombstoned) moment shows while `Date.parse(at) + MOMENT_TTL_MS > now` (`MOMENT_TTL_MS = 86400000`, 24 h) and is hidden once `at + 24 h <= now`; one dated more than `MOMENT_SKEW_MS` (10 minutes, a clock tolerance) ahead is hidden until its time comes, so no date buys more than 24 h plus that tolerance on screen (posts allow `MAX_FUTURE_MS`, 24 h, which would let a forward-dated moment show for almost 48 h). This is a reader rule only: nothing in the record expires, and a reader that ignores it can keep showing the moment. Per author newest first, at most `MAX_MOMENTS_PER_AUTHOR` = 4; authors by their newest moment, at most `MAX_MOMENT_AUTHORS` = 32. The author's own expired, live moments are listed newest first, at most `MAX_PAST_MOMENTS` = 16, so they can still be deleted. These caps keep the worst-case `moments.read` answer under `MAX_MESSAGE` (docs/rpc.md 5.113). The feed's page cut applies to moments too (`liveLogCopies` in `packages/core/lib/feed.js`): a verified `page-close` or `page-moved` ends a page's moments, and their photos, exactly as it ends its posts.
  - Retention: seeders drop the blob bytes within 7 days of the upload (`BLOB_RETENTION_MS`, section 13), never renewed, so the photo of an old moment becomes unavailable (NOT_FOUND `foto indisponível`) even before a tombstone. The `moment` record (text, descriptor, key and nonce included) stays in the diary until the author deletes it with a `delete` tombstone; deletion clears the record bytes like any deleted block, and the blob is not cleared on demand (it expires by itself). Copies other people made (a saved photo, a screenshot, a replica that ignores the reader rule) cannot be recalled: hiding is not deleting.
  - Old readers: to apps, CLIs and seeders from before momentos, `moment` is an unknown type, skipped as invalid (`malformed`) and never renumbered; the diary is never rejected for it.
- A quote is a `post` with a fifth key `"quote": { "author": "<hex64>", "seq": <integer >= 1> }` (the quoted post; the same `validTarget` shape, never `null`), optionally with `media` as a sixth key. A quote's `replyTo` MUST be `null` (else reason `quote-with-reply`), a `quote` value that is not a valid target is `quote-invalid`, and its text is always `1..1000` chars, with or without `media`. A plain post never carries `quote`. `postRecord({ text, replyTo, at, media, quote })` adds `quote` (copied as `{ author, seq }`) only when given.
- A post or a quote may carry a link preview instead of media: `"preview": LinkCard` (section 2, "Link cards", with a public thumbnail) as its fifth key (`type`, `text`, `at`, `replyTo`, `preview`, `POST_PREVIEW_KEYS`) or, for a quote, its sixth (`type`, `text`, `at`, `replyTo`, `quote`, `preview`, `QUOTE_PREVIEW_KEYS`). A post never carries both `media` and `preview`: no key set has both (`malformed`). The text stays `1..1000` chars (a preview is not a photo-only post). A `preview` value that is not a valid LinkCard, `null` included, is reason `preview-invalid`. The thumbnail (`image`, or `null`) is a MediaDescriptor with `mime` `'image/jpeg'` and `bytes` at most `MAX_CARD_IMAGE_BYTES` = 32768 (so `blocks` 1), in a declared media core like any post photo (`validPreviewImage`). `postRecord({ ..., preview })` adds `preview` only when given and not `null`, copying exactly the card keys and the descriptor keys.
- `{ "type": "delete", "seq": <integer >= 1>, "at": <ISO> }` (seq >= 1, the tombstone), optionally with a fourth key `"media": [{ "core": "<hex64>", "start": <integer>, "blocks": <integer> }, ...]`: the author withdraws block `seq` of their OWN diary. Exactly these 3 keys or these 4 keys (each range exactly `core`, `start`, `blocks`), else `malformed`; there is no `v`, `author` or `kind` field. `seq` must be a safe integer with `1 <= seq <` the tombstone's own block seq (reason `delete-invalid`, so it can only point at an earlier block and never at block 0); a non-canonical `at` is `date-invalid`; `media` must be a non-empty list of at most `MAX_DELETE_RANGES` = 4 ranges (the limit from before the post cap rose to 10, kept so old readers still accept every tombstone), each with a hex64 `core`, `start` in `1..MAX_MEDIA_BLOCKS - 1`, `blocks` in `1..MAX_MEDIA_ITEM_BLOCKS` and `start + blocks <= MAX_MEDIA_BLOCKS`, else `media-invalid`. Builder `deleteRecord({ seq, at, media })`; `deleteRecords({ seq, at, media })` returns that one record when there are at most `MAX_DELETE_RANGES` ranges, else one record per chunk of `MAX_DELETE_RANGES` ranges, all with the same `seq` and `at`.

MediaDescriptor (`validDescriptor`, `packages/core/lib/records.js`; duplicated in `packages/rpc/lib/methods.js`, which cannot import the core) has exactly the keys `core` (hex64, the media core), `mime` (one of `MEDIA_MIMES` = `'image/jpeg'`, `'image/png'`, `'image/webp'`, `'image/gif'`), `start` (integer `1..MAX_MEDIA_BLOCKS - 1`), `blocks` (integer `1..MAX_MEDIA_ITEM_BLOCKS`), `bytes` (safe integer with `(blocks - 1) * MEDIA_BLOCK_BYTES < bytes <= blocks * MEDIA_BLOCK_BYTES`), `sha256` (hex64, the SHA-256 of the item's bytes), `width` and `height` (integers `1..MAX_MEDIA_DIMENSION`), and `start + blocks <= MAX_MEDIA_BLOCKS`. The descriptor is key-agnostic: that `core` is not the diary's own key is checked by the readers and writers. Media is images only (no video): section 8 "Residual risks".

AudioDescriptor (`validAudioDescriptor`, `packages/core/lib/records.js`) is a voice note: exactly the MediaDescriptor keys plus `ms` (`AUDIO_DESCRIPTOR_KEYS`), with `mime` exactly `AUDIO_MIME` = `'audio/mp4'`, `width` and `height` both `0`, `ms` an integer `1..MAX_AUDIO_MS` = 120000, `bytes` at most `MAX_AUDIO_BYTES` = 1572864, and `core`, `start`, `blocks`, `bytes` and `sha256` under the MediaDescriptor rules. It is valid only as the single item of a group post (section 10, "Group post media"); a diary post's `media` stays MediaDescriptors, still images only. No other audio mime and no video mime is accepted anywhere.

The bytes of a voice note are checked by `audioBounds(bytes)` (`packages/rpc/lib/audio-bounds.js`, exported by `@varanda/rpc`), since the mime alone is not trusted: it walks the ISO BMFF boxes (at most 16384 boxes, every size inside its parent, 64-bit sizes read, size 0 running to the end of the parent) and returns `{ ms }` only when the first box is `ftyp` with a major or compatible brand among `M4A `, `isom`, `iso2` to `iso9`, `mp41`, `mp42`, `dash`, there is exactly one `moov` with exactly one `mvhd` and exactly one `trak`, whose `mdia`/`hdlr` handler type is `soun` (a `vide` or any other track refuses the file), whose `minf` has no `vmhd`, whose `dinf`/`dref` entries are all self-contained `url ` entries, and whose `stsd` holds exactly one `mp4a` or `Opus` sample entry; the duration comes from the samples: the `stts` total (no zero counts or deltas, at most 20000 samples, matched by exactly one `stsz`/`stz2`), plus, for a fragmented file (an `mvex` in `moov`, what browser recorders write), the `trun` sample durations of contiguous fragments (each `tfdt` equal to the running total, every `traf` naming the one track); it must round to `1..120000` ms, and every `mvhd`, `mdhd`, `tkhd` and `mehd` duration that is given, and the edit list (at most one leading empty edit within the tolerance and one media edit at rate 1), must agree with it within the tolerance. `ctts`, `trun` composition offsets, `sidx`, and fragments in a file without `mvex` refuse the file. Otherwise `null`; it never throws. A writer checks its own recording with `audioBounds(bytes, { metadata: true })`, which skips `udta` and `meta` boxes unparsed and does not compare the `mvhd`, `tkhd`, `mdhd` and `mehd` durations (recorders write them wrong: Chrome's MediaRecorder puts milliseconds in an `mdhd` whose timescale is 48000), and stores the copy `stripAudioMetadata(bytes)` returns: creation and modification times zeroed, each of those durations that is given (not 0, not all ones) rewritten from the samples (`null` when the new value does not fit a version 0 field), every `udta` and `meta` turned into a zero-filled `free` box of the same size, and the result must pass the strict `audioBounds`. A declared `ms` (descriptor, private item or DM item) must be within `AUDIO_MS_TOLERANCE` = 1000 ms of the container's: writers store the container's value and refuse a declared one further away, and readers refuse an item whose `ms` does not match its bytes.

AvatarDescriptor (`validAvatar`, `packages/core/lib/records.js`; duplicated as `isAvatar` in `packages/rpc/lib/methods.js`) is a MediaDescriptor with `mime` in `AVATAR_MIMES` = `'image/jpeg'`, `'image/webp'`, `width` = `height` = `AVATAR_SIDE` = 512, `blocks` <= `MAX_AVATAR_BLOCKS` = 8 and `bytes` <= `MAX_AVATAR_BYTES` = 262144 (`8 * MEDIA_BLOCK_BYTES`). It never throws. The browser node also checks the stored bytes with `stillImageSize(bytes, mime)` (`packages/core/lib/still-image.js`): it reads the pixel size from the JPEG frame header or the WebP container without decoding pixels or reading metadata, and returns `null` for anything else and for truncated or malformed input. JPEG: the first SOFn (`C0`..`CF` except `C4`, `C8`, `CC`) before any SOS or EOI, whose length `L` is at least 11 and equals `8 + 3 * Nf` with `1 <= Nf <= 4`. WebP: the RIFF size must be at least 4 and fit in the bytes (bytes after the RIFF end are ignored), every chunk must fit inside it, and the whole RIFF body is walked: any `ANIM` or `ANMF` chunk, the `VP8X` animation flag, a second `VP8X`, a `VP8X` after the image chunk, a second image chunk (`VP8 ` or `VP8L`) or a bad image chunk signature gives `null`; other chunks are skipped. With a `VP8X`, its canvas must equal the image chunk's frame. An avatar must read as exactly 512x512. The hosted app checks fetched avatar bytes with the same algorithm (`apps/app/src/media/avatar.js`).

Example records:

```json
{ "type": "member-cert", "cert": { "v": 1, "member": "<hex64>", "ageBand": "18+", "method": "stub", "date": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z", "association": "<hex64>", "signature": "<128 hex>" } }
{ "type": "profile", "name": "Ana", "bio": "", "at": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z" }
{ "type": "profile", "name": "Ana", "bio": "", "at": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z", "avatar": { "core": "<hex64>", "mime": "image/webp", "start": 4, "blocks": 2, "bytes": 41230, "sha256": "<hex64>", "width": 512, "height": 512 } }
{ "type": "post", "text": "olá", "at": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z", "replyTo": null }
{ "type": "post", "text": "concordo", "at": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z", "replyTo": { "author": "<hex64>", "seq": 1 } }
{ "type": "media-core", "key": "<hex64>", "at": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z" }
{ "type": "post", "text": "", "at": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z", "replyTo": null, "media": [{ "core": "<hex64>", "mime": "image/webp", "start": 1, "blocks": 3, "bytes": 81234, "sha256": "<hex64>", "width": 2048, "height": 1536 }] }
{ "type": "follow", "key": "<hex64>", "on": true, "at": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z" }
{ "type": "group-join", "group": "<hex64>", "writer": "<hex64>", "on": true, "at": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z" }
{ "type": "like", "to": { "author": "<hex64>", "seq": 1 }, "on": true, "at": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z" }
{ "type": "repost", "to": { "author": "<hex64>", "seq": 1 }, "on": false, "at": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z" }
{ "type": "testimonial", "to": "<hex64>", "gen": 1, "hash": "<hex64>", "box": "<base64>", "at": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z" }
{ "type": "testimonial-show", "from": "<hex64>", "seq": 4, "text": "pessoa incrível", "nonce": "<hex64>", "at": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z" }
{ "type": "moment", "text": "fim de tarde", "at": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z", "media": { "id": "<hex64>", "key": "<hex64>", "nonce": "<hex48>", "chunks": 2, "bytes": 41246, "sha256": "<hex64>", "mime": "image/webp", "w": 1080, "h": 1350, "ms": 0 } }
{ "type": "post", "text": "olha isso", "at": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z", "replyTo": null, "quote": { "author": "<hex64>", "seq": 1 } }
{ "type": "delete", "seq": 1, "at": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z" }
{ "type": "delete", "seq": 7, "at": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z", "media": [{ "core": "<hex64>", "start": 1, "blocks": 3 }] }
{ "type": "page-roster", "page": "<hex64>", "gen": 1, "owner": "<hex64>", "admins": ["<hex64>"], "prev": null, "at": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z", "nonce": "<nonce>", "association": "<hex64>", "issuer": "<hex64>", "issuerDate": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z", "issuerSignature": "<128 hex>", "signature": "<128 hex>", "countersignature": "<128 hex>" }
{ "type": "page-accept", "page": "<hex64>", "gen": 2, "rosterSignature": "<128 hex>", "at": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z", "nonce": "<nonce>", "actor": "<hex64>", "signature": "<128 hex>" }
{ "type": "page-offer", "page": "<hex64>", "to": "<hex64>", "exp": "2026-01-08T00:00:00.000Z", "gen": 3, "at": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z", "nonce": "<nonce>", "actor": "<hex64>", "signature": "<128 hex>" }
{ "type": "page-close", "page": "<hex64>", "gen": 4, "at": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z", "nonce": "<nonce>", "actor": "<hex64>", "association": "<hex64>", "issuer": "<hex64>", "issuerDate": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z", "issuerSignature": "<128 hex>", "signature": "<128 hex>", "countersignature": "<128 hex>" }
{ "type": "page-moved", "page": "<hex64>", "to": "<hex64>", "gen": 4, "at": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z", "nonce": "<nonce>", "actor": "<hex64>", "association": "<hex64>", "issuer": "<hex64>", "issuerDate": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z", "issuerSignature": "<128 hex>", "signature": "<128 hex>", "countersignature": "<128 hex>" }
{ "type": "page-origin", "page": "<hex64>", "from": "<hex64>", "fromSeq": 7, "gen": 4, "owner": "<hex64>", "admins": [], "at": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z", "nonce": "<nonce>", "actor": "<hex64>", "association": "<hex64>", "issuer": "<hex64>", "issuerDate": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z", "issuerSignature": "<128 hex>", "signature": "<128 hex>", "countersignature": "<128 hex>" }
{ "type": "page-link", "page": "<hex64>", "diary": "<hex64>", "actor": "<hex64>", "at": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z", "nonce": "<nonce>", "association": "<hex64>", "issuer": "<hex64>", "issuerDate": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z", "issuerSignature": "<128 hex>", "signature": "<128 hex>" }
{ "type": "page-delegate", "actor": "<hex64>", "at": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z" }
```

Rules:

- Forbidden characters (reason `control-chars`, security audit S5) in `name`, `bio` and `text`: every code point below 0x20 (LF, 0x0a, is allowed in `bio` and `text` only, never in `name`), DEL (0x7f), the C1 controls (0x80-0x9f), the line and paragraph separators U+2028 and U+2029, and the bidi embedding/override/isolate controls U+202A-U+202E and U+2066-U+2069.
- Exact keys (S3): a `profile` has exactly `type`, `name`, `bio`, `at`, or exactly `type`, `name`, `bio`, `at`, `avatar` (`profileRecord` adds `avatar` only when given and not `null`), and a `post` exactly `type`, `text`, `at`, `replyTo` (`postRecord` always writes `replyTo`, `null` when absent), or those four plus `media` (`postRecord` adds it only when given), or a quote's `type`, `text`, `at`, `replyTo`, `quote` (plus `media`), or the post or quote sets with `preview` in place of `media`; a `delete` exactly `type`, `seq`, `at`, or those three plus `media`; any other key set is `malformed`.
- Storage limits (S3, constants in `packages/core/lib/records.js`): `MAX_BLOCK_BYTES = 16384` (`decodeBlock` returns `null` for a longer buffer, so an oversized block 0 is `not-in-log` and an oversized record is skipped), `MAX_LOG_BLOCKS = 100000` and `MAX_LOG_BYTES = 67108864` (64 MiB) per log (sections 5 and 6, reason `quota`). Media cores have their own limits (below); their bytes never count against the diary's.
- Old readers (apps, CLIs and seeders from before media and follow records): `media-core` and `follow` are unknown types and a post with `media` has an unknown key set, so all three are invalid records there (`malformed`). They are skipped like any invalid block and never renumbered; the diary itself is never rejected for them. Such a reader shows no text-only fallback for a media post: the whole post is skipped. The same holds for the engagement records: to apps, CLIs and seeders from before them, `like` and `repost` are unknown types and a quote has an unknown key set, so they are skipped as invalid (a quote is not shown at all, not even its text) and the diary is never rejected. Likewise a post with `preview` has an unknown key set there and is skipped whole, as a post with `media` is.
- Old readers and profile photos: to apps, CLIs and seeders from before the avatar, a profile with `avatar` has an unknown key set and is skipped as invalid (`malformed`), never rejecting the diary. So a writer that leaves an avatar in effect appends the COMPATIBILITY PAIR in ONE append: first the plain 4-key profile, then the same `name`, `bio` and `at` with `avatar` (`profileRecords({ name, bio, avatar, at })` returns `[plain]` without an avatar, `[plain, withAvatar]` with one). An old reader's newest valid profile is then the plain block with the same name and bio; a new reader's is the 5-key block. Removing the photo is one plain record. Readers never depend on the pair: `latestProfile` takes the highest valid seq and returns `{ name, bio, avatar }`, `avatar` a fresh copy or `null`.
- Old readers and the 10-photo cap: apps, CLIs, seeders and account services from before `MAX_MEDIA_PER_POST` rose from 4 to 10 treat a `post` (a reply and a quote included) with more than 4 descriptors as `media-invalid` and skip the whole block, with no text fallback and without rejecting the diary. An old seeder does not count or serve those media ranges, and an old account service refuses such a page post. The `delete` record did not change (at most `MAX_DELETE_RANGES` = 4 ranges each), so those readers still apply the deletion of such a post. Listings, group posts and direct messages keep their limit of 4 and are unchanged. There is no compatibility pair. Rollout order: seeders and the account service first, then the web bundle, then the app, then the CLI.
- Mentions (`@nome`): a mention is a token in `text`, found by `findMentions` of `@varanda/rpc` (`packages/rpc/lib/mentions.js`), and a `post` record gains NO key. This is why: a reader from before mentions skips a post whose key set it does not know, so a `mentions` key would hide the whole post from every old app, CLI and seeder, as media did. The grammar: `@` followed by a run of `[A-Za-z0-9_]`, lower-cased, that matches the username rule of section 3 (`^[a-z][a-z0-9_]{2,19}$`) and is not the 6-hex fallback handle. The boundaries fail closed, because a mention must notify only the name the text visibly shows: invisible characters (soft hyphen, zero-width and bidi marks, variation selectors, tag characters and the like) are looked past, and the visible neighbour on each side must be one of an allowlist of separators (ASCII punctuation and spaces, Latin-1 and general punctuation, symbols, CJK punctuation, emoji); a letter, mark or digit of any script, or another `@`, next to the token blocks it. An unlisted neighbour only costs a missed link, never a wrong notice. At most `MENTION_MAX = 8` distinct names count per post (the first eight). Nothing in a record says a name was resolved: the mention is a claim about the text, and the reader decides who it names (below, "Notices channel v2").
- Deletion (`packages/core/lib/deletion.js`, `applyDeletes`, `deletedSeqs`, `deletedRanges`). Deletion is a diary feature: group and community cores are read without it (section 10, "Group cores and deletion"). READER RULE: a valid `delete` record at block b deletes block `seq` of the same diary, WHATEVER the target's type and whether or not this replica holds the target block, and a deleted block has no effect in any fold (feed, profile, follows, engagement, media cores, indexes). To make the result the same on every replica that holds the same blocks, tombstones are evaluated from the HIGHEST block down: a valid tombstone at b is effective only if b is not already in the deleted set, and an effective tombstone adds its target to the set. So a tombstone that a later effective tombstone deleted has no effect; an author who tombstones a tombstone undoes their own deletion. That is a self-only residual: honest writers never do it, and it cannot touch another author's diary because a tombstone counts only in the diary that holds it. `applyDeletes(entries)` keeps index alignment: a deleted entry becomes `{ seq, record: null, deleted: true }`, and `log.read` (docs/rpc.md 5.12) reports its slot as `invalid` with reason `'deleted'`. WRITER RULE: the writers (`post.delete` and `profile.delete`, docs/rpc.md 5.33 and 5.34) emit a tombstone only for the author's own valid `post` (a reply and a quote included) or `profile` block, and refuse block 0, a tombstone and every other type. A compatibility pair (the plain profile at n immediately followed by the profile with `avatar` at n + 1, equal `name`, `bio` and `at`) is ONE logical version: both blocks are deleted in ONE append, two tombstones. The tombstone carries `media` ranges only for the target's descriptors (a post's `media`, a profile's `avatar`) whose interval no surviving own post or profile overlaps and that lie in a media core the diary declared; a hand-made range that a surviving record overlaps, or in an undeclared core, frees nothing (`deletedRanges`). A target with more than `MAX_DELETE_RANGES` freed ranges (a post with more than 4 photos) gets several tombstones of the same `seq` and `at`, each with at most `MAX_DELETE_RANGES` ranges (`deleteRecords`). A writer SHOULD put them in ONE append; a page close (`apps/cli/lib/account/pages-writer.js`) batches its records by at most 8 and keeps the tombstones of one target in one append. Readers union the ranges of every effective tombstone of a target: tombstones of one `seq` never target each other, so each is effective and `deletedRanges` frees the ranges of all of them, and a reader that holds only some of them frees only those ranges. Deleting what is already deleted writes nothing.
- Deletion and old readers: to apps, CLIs and seeders from before the tombstone, `delete` is an unknown type, so they skip the block as `malformed` and keep showing the item. The diary itself is never rejected for it. A reader from before the byte clearing below also stops at the first block it cannot read (`readAll` reads `0..contiguousLength-1`, `packages/core/lib/store.js`), so it sees an author's diary cut at the first cleared block. Rollout order: seeders first, then the web bundle, then the app, then the CLI; clearing stays off (`VARANDA_NO_CLEAR`, below) until the official readers are upgraded.
- Clearing deleted bytes (`clearDeleted`, `downloadExcept`, `presentState`, `presentLength`, `clearRanges` in `packages/core/lib/deletion.js`). Every official replica (the author's own store, a follower, the seeder, the browser node) discards with `core.clear(seq, seq + 1)` exactly the blocks the READER fold hides, taken from the same highest-first evaluation as above (never from a seeder's monotone index flag), so a tombstone of a tombstone never makes a replica discard what readers still show. Never cleared: block 0 and every valid tombstone (all replicas need the tombstones to compute the same fold, and a tombstone that a later one deleted is effective again if that later one is itself deleted). A block is cleared only when it was read back and is not a valid tombstone; a read error or a missing block is never cleared. The media ranges of an effective tombstone (`deletedRanges`) are cleared in the media core they name, but only the ranges no surviving own post or profile overlaps and only in a core the diary declared; the seeder clears them from per-block counters of the declared cores, and the browser clears the `deletedRanges` of its own diary in the media cores it holds that name the diary, so a block a surviving record still shows stays. `core.clear` is not a truncate: `fork` stays 0 and `watchFork` is untouched, the length and the signed tree do not change, and the block is a hole that can be neither read nor served.
- Present length. A clear pulls `contiguousLength` back, so a hole would hold every later block out of the readable prefix, and hypercore 10 (the browser engine, 10.38.2) does not keep the lowered value across close and reopen after a clear. The present length replaces `contiguousLength` as the end of a diary: it is the first block this replica does not hold that no valid LOCAL tombstone targets, or the core length when there is none, and it is walked with `has(i)`, never read from `contiguousLength`. A tombstone above a hole the replica never had is learned all the same (every local block below the core length is read, holes included), so a fresh replica does not wait for a block nobody serves any more; the price is that a replica very sparse above an untargeted hole reads those blocks too. Wherever this document names `contiguousLength` for the length of a diary in sections 5 to 7 (hello inventories, the gate, the scans, the sync goals), the present length is meant. The value is also used for the readable prefix of `readAll`, the `downloaded` count of a sync result, the seeder's scans, stats and admitted lengths. A target of a tombstone that was itself deleted still counts as a deleted hole: its bytes may be cleared everywhere, and stopping there would hide every later block forever; such a revived seq reads as absent. The advertised (`have`, hello), goal and `downloaded` lengths stay the present length on every node, also on the browser node while it downloads a followed diary newest-first (section 6, "Browser node download order"): blocks stored above the present length are never advertised.
- Downloads and re-clearing. A replica never asks a peer for a block its fold deletes: it opens one `linear` range per gap between deleted seqs, the last one open-ended, and replaces them whenever the deleted set changes (a single `{ start: 0, end: -1 }` range would fetch the cleared block back). A block that arrives before its tombstone (a fresh replica, or a peer that was behind) is stored first and cleared as soon as the tombstone is known; a block that still arrives after a range was destroyed (a request already answered on the wire) is cleared again on its `'download'` event. A deleted block that is revived by a later tombstone is fetched again from a peer that holds it. A group binding diary (section 10) follows the same model as a followed diary on both nodes: its deleted blocks are cleared and never requested again, also from stale peers (including by the browser's `diary.peek`, which skips the seqs the binding diary knows deleted), and a tombstone that arrives later clears its target.
- Kill switch. `VARANDA_NO_CLEAR=1` (or the `noClear` option of the seeder, the sync, the member session and the browser session) keeps the bytes: tombstones still hide the items everywhere and the present length still passes deleted holes, but nothing is cleared and the whole range is downloaded. At sync startup a replica that is no longer followed is purged (`sweepUnfollowed`, `packages/core/lib/sync.js`), and one listed past the follow limit is cleared but not synced; the kill switch turns both off.
- Feira records (section 15 says what they mean; rules in `packages/rpc/lib/feira.js`, builders and `validateRecord` in `packages/core/lib/records.js`; all of them are seq >= 1, `validateRecord` never throws, an unknown key set or a bad field is `malformed` unless a reason below names it). Four shapes, with exactly these keys in this order (the builders emit it):
  - `listing`: `{ "type": "listing", "kind": "produto" | "servico" | "procura", "title", "text", "price", "unit", "cat", "place", "at" }`, optionally followed by `"media": [MediaDescriptor, ...]` (1 to `MAX_LISTING_MEDIA` = 4 descriptors, `feira.MAX_LISTING_MEDIA` of `@varanda/rpc`, the media rules of section 2, else `media-invalid`). Example: `{"type":"listing","kind":"produto","title":"Bicicleta aro 26","text":"Pouco usada.","price":35000,"unit":null,"cat":"esporte","place":{"uf":"SP","city":"São Paulo","bairro":null},"at":"2026-11-07T12:00:00.000Z"}`.
  - `listing-edit`: `{ "type": "listing-edit", "seq", "title", "text", "price", "unit", "cat", "place", "at" }` (+ optional `media`). It carries no `kind`: the kind lives in the listing it edits. `seq` is an integer with `1 <= seq < own seq` (`target-invalid` otherwise); whether it names a live listing of this diary is the fold's check. An edit is a FULL snapshot of the content, never a patch, and `media` absent means no photos.
  - `listing-status`: `{ "type": "listing-status", "seq", "status": "disponivel" | "reservado" | "vendido", "at" }`, `seq` as for an edit; an unknown status is `status-invalid`.
  - `listing-rate`: `{ "type": "listing-rate", "to": { "author": "<hex64>", "seq": <integer >= 1> }, "offer": "<hex32>", "consent": "<hex128>", "stars": <integer 1..5>, "text": <string 0..280>, "at" }` (`stars-invalid`, `text-length`; a bad `to` is `target-invalid`, a bad `offer` or `consent` `malformed`). Its `to` is another member's listing, and the record means nothing without the author's consent (section 15, "Ratings").
- Feira fields. `title` is 1..80 code points (`MAX_LISTING_TITLE`) and `text` 0..`MAX_TEXT` (1000), both without the forbidden controls of a post (the title also without LF) and well formed (`name-length`, `text-length`, `control-chars`). `price` is `null` ("A combinar") or an integer of cents in `0..100000000` (`MAX_PRICE_CENTS`; 0 is "Grátis"), else `price-invalid`. `cat` is one of `casa`, `roupa`, `eletronicos`, `moveis`, `infantil`, `esporte`, `veiculos`, `comida`, `aulas`, `beleza`, `reparos`, `outros` (`category-invalid`). `place` is exactly `{ "uf", "city", "bairro" }`, all three keys present: `uf` one of the 27 federative units, `city` 2..40 code points that also give a city key (`cityKey`: NFKC, no format characters, accents dropped, lowercase, runs of anything else as one `-`, 2..48 characters, so "São Paulo", "sao  paulo" and "SAO-PAULO" are one city) and `bairro` `null` or 2..40 code points (`place-invalid`). `at` must be canonical (`date-invalid`).
- Feira units and procura. `unit` is `null` or one of `hora`, `dia`, `mes`, `servico`, `a-partir`; an unknown value is `unit-invalid`, and so is a combination `unitAllowed(kind, price, unit)` refuses: a free or `null` price has `unit` `null`; a `procura` always `null` (its price is a ceiling, shown "Até R$ ..."); a `produto` `null` or `a-partir`; a `servico` any unit. A listing-edit has no kind of its own, so `validateRecord` checks its unit as for a `servico` (the widest) and the FOLD checks it again against the root's real kind, skipping an edit that fails. A `procura` is a request ("procuro"): it never has `reservado` (the fold ignores that status) and its `vendido` is shown as "Encontrado". Labels (`priceLabel`, `statusLabel`) are integer math over the cents, never floats.
- Feira and old readers: to apps, CLIs and seeders from before the Feira, the four types are unknown, so they skip the blocks as `malformed` and show nothing of a listing (no text-only fallback); the diary is never rejected for them. Rollout order: seeders, web bundle, app (section 15).
- Feed rendering (S5): `formatFeedItem` indents EVERY line of the text with two spaces, so no line of a post can start at column 0 and pass for a `[date]` header.
- `at` = `new Date().toISOString()` at write time and must be canonical, checked with `isCanonicalIso(at)` (below, R4.3); it is untrusted presentation data (ordering only).
- Readers skip blocks that are not valid records and never renumber. Reference string: `'<hex64>:<seq>'`.
- Logs are append-only. No Phase 0 writer ever truncates (clearing deleted bytes, above, is not a truncate: it leaves the length, the tree and `fork` as they were), so `fork !== 0` on any log means its history was rewritten by the key holder (R5.9 a); such a log is dropped everywhere with reason `'fork'` (sections 5, 6, 7).
- Future-dated posts (R5.10): a valid post whose `Date.parse(at) - now > MAX_FUTURE_MS` (`86400000`, 24 h) is SKIPPED by readers (excluded from the feed and reported as future), not clamped. Clamping would give the post a different position on every reader and every run (non-deterministic across devices) and still let one author pin the page bottom by republishing; skipping keeps `(at, author, seq)` ordering deterministic for a given clock, and the post reappears by itself once the reader's clock reaches `at - 24h`. The 24 h margin tolerates ordinary clock skew; past-dated posts are harmless (they sink).
- Momentos (24 h reader rule, section 2 "Momentos"): not `MAX_FUTURE_MS` but the tighter `MOMENT_SKEW_MS` (10 minutes) hides a `moment` dated ahead of the reader's clock until its time comes, and `MOMENT_TTL_MS` (`86400000`) hides one once `Date.parse(at) + 24 h <= now`. Both are reader rules on the reader's clock: the record is not changed, and a forward date buys at most the 10-minute tolerance of extra time on screen.
- Moderation is no longer a TODO: the signed blocklist and issuer revocation of section 3 (B2) keep diaries out.

### Link cards (`packages/core/lib/link-card.js`)

A link card is what the sender's device read once from a page it linked: `LinkCard = { "url", "host", "title", "desc", "image" }`, exactly these keys (`CARD_KEYS`). Diary posts carry one as `preview` (above), direct messages and group posts as `card` (sections 11 and 10). `validLinkCard(card, validImage)` never throws; each place passes its own thumbnail rule.

- `url`: at most `MAX_CARD_URL` = 300 characters, printable ASCII from the RFC 3986 set only (no space, quote, backslash, angle bracket or control), starting with exactly `https://`. Any other scheme (`http:`, `javascript:`, `data:`, `ftp:`, `file:`, an uppercase `HTTPS:`) is refused. The authority has no credentials (no `@` before the first `/`, `?` or `#`), no IP literal (no `[` or `]`, and the last label starts with a letter, so no IPv4), and an optional port `1..65535`. The hostname is lowercase LDH labels (1 to 63 characters, no leading or trailing hyphen), at least two of them, at most 253 characters; an internationalized name travels as punycode.
- `host`: exactly the hostname of `url` (lowercase, without the port).
- `title`: `0..MAX_CARD_TITLE` = 80 code points; `desc`: `0..MAX_CARD_DESC` = 160 code points. Both are well-formed strings (no lone surrogate) with the post text character rule (section 2, S5: no control character except LF, no U+2028, U+2029 or bidi controls).
- `image`: `null` or a thumbnail descriptor, a re-encoded JPEG of at most `MAX_CARD_IMAGE_BYTES` = 32768 bytes (the composer re-encodes it; every validator bounds the descriptor's `bytes`). Its shape depends on where the card travels: a MediaDescriptor in a diary post or a public group post, a DM media item in a direct message, a private item in a private group's sealed inner. Never an address on the web.
- One rule for every producer: the URL, host and text rules and the caps above live in `@varanda/rpc` (`lib/link-card.js`: `cardUrlHost`, `cardTextLength`, `isCardForbiddenChar`, `cardText`, `MAX_CARD_URL`, `MAX_CARD_TITLE`, `MAX_CARD_DESC`, `MAX_CARD_IMAGE_BYTES`), and core validates with them. The account service (section 12, "Service answers: link preview") and the app (`apps/app/src/preview.js`) check the normalized target URL with `cardUrlHost` before any fetch, so a device never fetches an IP literal or a single-label name, and both read the page with the one reader `parseOg` of `@varanda/rpc` (`lib/og-parse.js`, section 12, "Service answers: link preview", "Reading"), which builds `title` and `desc` with `cardText`, so every card they build passes `validLinkCard` and the app and the service build the same card from the same page. The fragment is part of `url` everywhere. Both refuse an `og:image` URL whose host `cardUrlHost` refuses. The native app never follows a redirect, for the page or its `og:image`: it asks with `redirect: 'manual'` and drops any answer the transport marks as redirected or whose URL (normalized) is not the one asked, since a device cannot check where a name resolves and a public link could send it to an intranet page the sender's network reaches. So on the phone a link that redirects (a shortener, for example) gets no preview, and a redirected image no thumbnail. For the same reason the native app fetches an `og:image` only from the page's host or a subdomain of it (`sameSiteImage`), so a page cannot point the device at another host, an intranet one for instance; an image on another domain (a separate CDN) gives the card no thumbnail on the phone. It reads the body as a stream (`expo/fetch`), cancelled at the cap; a transport without a stream gives no preview. On the web the account service follows up to 3 redirects, each address checked (section 12, "Service answers: link preview").
- **Residual risk.** The card is what the sender's device (or, on the web, the association's service fetching on its behalf) read once when the message was written. Readers never contact the site to show it, so a card says nothing about the page now, and the sender can put any title, description and picture on any URL: the card is a claim by the sender, like the text. Apps show `host` next to the card and open only `url`.

### Media cores (`packages/core/lib/media.js`)

A media core is a Hypercore separate from the diary, signed by its own key. Block 0 is the media header, a JSON block with exactly `{ "type": "media-header", "v": 1, "diary": "<hex64>", "at": <canonical ISO> }` and at most `MAX_HEADER_BYTES = 16384` bytes (`validateMediaHeader` -> `null` or `'malformed'` | `'date-invalid'` | `'too-large'`; `mediaHeader({ diary, at })` builds one and throws a `TypeError` on invalid input). Blocks 1.. are raw item bytes: an item of `bytes` bytes is split into `MEDIA_BLOCK_BYTES` = 32768-byte blocks (the last one shorter), at the `start` and `blocks` its descriptor names. Limits (`packages/core/lib/records.js`): `MAX_MEDIA_BLOCKS = 8192` blocks per media core (header included) and `MAX_MEDIA_BYTES = 268435456` (256 MiB) per media core; `MAX_MEDIA_ITEM_BLOCKS = 256` blocks (8 MiB) per item; `MAX_MEDIA_PER_POST = 10`; `MAX_MEDIA_DIMENSION = 16384`.

- A diary declares a media core with a `media-core` record. Only the latest `MAX_MEDIA_CORES = 4` declarations count: `latestMediaCores(entries, diaryHex, n = 4)` -> `[{ key, seq }]`, newest first, deduplicated by key (the newest seq kept), the diary's own key never a media core.
- The binding is two-way: a media core belongs to diary D only while D declares it among its latest 4 AND its header names D. Either side alone binds nothing (a diary cannot claim someone else's core, a core cannot attach itself to a diary that never declared it).
- Only the browser node writes media cores, one per device (`docs/rpc.md` section 11). The core node and `varanda-serve` refuse a post with `media` (section 9) and only show that a post has photos.
- A profile avatar is an item in this device's media core like a post photo: written only by the browser node, public and permanent. Removing it (a plain profile) only stops it being displayed; the bytes stay in the core. It becomes unavailable to readers once its core is no longer among the diary's latest `MAX_MEDIA_CORES` = 4 declarations. A writer keeps an avatar item that lives in another device's core by repeating its descriptor (`docs/rpc.md` 5.6).
- Readers fetch items over HTTP from the seeder (section 7 "Seeder media route"), never over the admission channel.
- A v2 `group-post` (section 10, "Group post media") points into the media cores of its writer's diary the same way: a public group with these descriptors, a private group with private items whose bytes are secretbox ciphertext. Old readers skip such a post whole, with no text fallback.
- The other exports never throw: `validateDescriptor(d)` -> `null | 'media-invalid'`, `followSet`, `hasFollowRecords(entries)` and `isOwnMediaCore(descriptor, ownCores)` (a valid descriptor whose `core` is in `ownCores`, an array or a `Set`).

### Canonical timestamps never throw (R4.3)

`isCanonicalIso(s)` = `typeof s === 'string' && Number.isFinite(Date.parse(s)) && new Date(s).toISOString() === s`, evaluated inside try/catch (false on any throw: `new Date(invalid).toISOString()` throws `RangeError`). It is used for certificate `date` fields and record `at` fields. `validateRecord` NEVER throws for any input (including hostile strings, getters and non-objects); tests cover `'2026-13-01T00:00:00.000Z'` and non-string values.

### Safe block decoding (R4.1, supersedes the R3 a wording)

`packages/core/lib/records.js` exports `decodeBlock(buf)` -> a plain object or `null`. It returns `null` when `buf` is not a Buffer/Uint8Array, when `JSON.parse(b4a.toString(buf, 'utf8'))` throws, or when the result is not a plain object (`null`, array, string, number).

No code path in the project reads a diary block through the JSON value encoding: every read of a block that may come from another device is `core.get(i, { raw: true, ... })` followed by `decodeBlock`. (Hypercore 11 throws `DECODING_ERROR` from a JSON-encoded `get` on bad bytes, after the block is already stored; raw reads avoid that.)

`readAll(core)` (`packages/core/lib/store.js`) reads every block `0..present-1` (the present length of section 2 "Clearing deleted bytes"; `core.contiguousLength` before byte clearing) with `core.get(i, { wait: false, raw: true })` + `decodeBlock`, and maps a `null` result to `{ seq: i, record: null }` (kept, never renumbered; `validateRecord(null, seq)` returns `'malformed'`). `readAll` never throws `DECODING_ERROR` and never stops early.

## 3. Certificates

### Membership certificate

```json
{
  "v": 1,
  "member": "<hex64, the diary key>",
  "ageBand": "18+",
  "method": "stub",
  "date": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z",
  "association": "<hex64>",
  "signature": "<128 lowercase hex>"
}
```

Certificate fields (no other keys):

- `v`: `1`.
- `member`: hex64 (diary key).
- `ageBand`: string matching `/^[a-z0-9+-]{1,32}$/` (suggested values `'13-15'`, `'16-17'`, `'18+'`; default `'18+'`).
- `method`: string matching `/^[a-z0-9-]{1,32}$/` (`'stub'` in phase 0).
- `date`: canonical ISO-8601 UTC with milliseconds, checked with `isCanonicalIso(date)` (section 2, R4.3).
- `association`: hex64 (Ed25519 public key from hypercore-crypto `keyPair()`).
- `signature`: 128 lowercase hex (hypercore-crypto `sign(payload, associationSecretKey)`).

`payload` = UTF-8 bytes of the six strings `'varanda-cert-v1'`, `member`, `ageBand`, `method`, `date`, `association` joined by a single LF character (no trailing LF), hex always lowercase. Never sign `JSON.stringify` output.

`verifyCert(cert, associationHex)` returns `{ ok: true }` or `{ ok: false, reason }` with the FIRST failing reason in this order:

1. `'malformed'` (shape above, `v === 1`, no unknown keys).
2. `'untrusted-association'` (`cert.association !== associationHex` lowercase).
3. `'bad-signature'` (hypercore-crypto `verify(payload, signature, association)` is false).

`certForKey(cert, keyHex, associationHex)` inserts `'member-mismatch'` (`cert.member !== keyHex`) after `'malformed'`. Callers add `'no-cert'` (block 0 absent) and `'not-in-log'` (block 0 present but not a valid member-cert for that key).

R4.3: `verifyCert`, `certForKey` and `validateCertShape` NEVER throw for any input (including hostile strings such as a `date` of `'2026-13-01T00:00:00.000Z'`, getters and non-objects); an input that would make a check throw yields `'malformed'`. Tests cover `'2026-13-01T00:00:00.000Z'` and non-string values.

The v1 format above stays valid and unchanged (same keys, payload and verdicts); an object with the v1 key set and `v !== 1`, or with any extra key, is `'malformed'`. `validateCertShape`, `verifyCert` and `certForKey` accept a v1 OR a v2 certificate (below) and branch on the key set and `v`; `genesisCert` and the block-0 gate (section 5) therefore accept both. A v2 certificate is valid only as block 0.

### Issuer certificate

The association delegates the issuing of v2 member certificates and username records to an online issuer key, so the association secret stays offline.

```json
{
  "v": 1,
  "issuer": "<hex64, the issuer public key>",
  "date": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z",
  "association": "<hex64>",
  "signature": "<128 lowercase hex>"
}
```

Exactly these 5 keys: `v` (`1`), `issuer` (lowercase hex64, `issuer !== association`), `date` (canonical ISO, `isCanonicalIso`), `association` (lowercase hex64), `signature` (128 lowercase hex, `sign(payload, associationSecretKey)`). Anything else is `'malformed'`.

`payload` = UTF-8 bytes of `'varanda-issuer-v1'`, `issuer`, `date`, `association` joined by a single LF (no trailing LF, hex lowercase). The prefix differs from every other payload, so no other signature of the association is ever a valid issuer signature.

- `issuerCertPayload(cert)` -> Buffer.
- `createIssuerCert({ issuer, date = new Date().toISOString() }, associationKeyPair)` -> IssuerCertificate; TypeError unless `issuer` is hex64 in any case (stored lowercase) and differs from the association key, `date` is canonical and the key pair is valid.
- `validateIssuerCertShape(cert)` -> `null | 'malformed'`.
- `verifyIssuerCert(cert, associationHex)` -> `{ ok: true } | { ok: false, reason }` with reasons in order `'malformed'`, `'untrusted-association'`, `'bad-signature'`. Never throws.

### Membership certificate v2

```json
{
  "v": 2,
  "member": "<hex64, the diary key>",
  "ageBand": "unchecked",
  "method": "google",
  "date": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z",
  "association": "<hex64>",
  "issuer": "<hex64>",
  "issuerDate": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z",
  "issuerSignature": "<128 lowercase hex>",
  "signature": "<128 lowercase hex>"
}
```

Exactly these 10 keys (written in this order by `createIssuedCert`): `v` (`2`), `member` (lowercase hex64), `ageBand` (exactly `'unchecked'`), `method` (exactly `'google'`), `date` (canonical ISO), `association`, `issuer` (lowercase hex64, `issuer !== association`), `issuerDate` (canonical ISO), `issuerSignature` (128 lowercase hex), `signature` (128 lowercase hex). Anything else is `'malformed'`. A v2 certificate asserts NO age: `'unchecked'` records that the issuer only linked an account (the fixed method `'google'`, not a Google subject, and the same value for a phone link); no age band is claimed. The member Google sign-in is retired: an old build's `POST /account/authorize` answers 410 `versao-antiga` (the directory, section 1). The @username is not part of the certificate (it is the `username` record below, so it can change). The age band verified by the account service (hosted web, Didit) is NOT in the certificate: it stays in the account service's `age.json` sidecar (`docs/design.md`), so the v2 shape and the P2P admission rules are unchanged.

`payload` = UTF-8 bytes of `'varanda-cert-v2'`, `member`, `ageBand`, `method`, `date`, `association`, `issuer`, `issuerDate`, `issuerSignature` joined by a single LF (no trailing LF, hex lowercase). `signature` = `sign(payload, issuerSecretKey)`.

`verifyCert(cert, associationHex)` for a v2 certificate returns the FIRST failing reason in this order:

1. `'malformed'` (shape above).
2. `'untrusted-association'` (`cert.association !== associationHex` lowercase).
3. `'bad-issuer-signature'`: the embedded delegation `{ v: 1, issuer, date: issuerDate, association, signature: issuerSignature }` fails `verifyIssuerCert(delegation, association)`.
4. `'bad-signature'` (`verify(payload, signature, issuer)` is false).
5. `'revoked-issuer'` (B2, issuer revocation): only when the caller passes `{ revokedIssuers }` (a `Set` of lowercase hex64 issuer keys, normally `revokedIssuerSet` of the node's current blocklist snapshot, below) and it has `cert.issuer`. Checked last, so the reason is only claimed for a certificate the revoked issuer really signed.

`certForKey` inserts `'member-mismatch'` after `'malformed'`, as for v1. The full signatures are `verifyCert(cert, associationHex, { revokedIssuers } = {})` and `certForKey(cert, keyHex, associationHex, { revokedIssuers } = {})`; without the option (or with a value that has no `has` method) the verdicts are the B1 ones. A v1 certificate is signed by the association itself and is never affected by `revokedIssuers`.

- `certPayloadV2(cert)` -> Buffer.
- `createIssuedCert({ member, date = new Date().toISOString() }, issuerKeyPair, issuerCert)` -> v2 Certificate; TypeError unless `member` is hex64, `date` is canonical, `issuerCert` is a valid issuer certificate that verifies for its own association and `hex(issuerKeyPair.publicKey) === issuerCert.issuer`.

### Username record

A diary record (section 2, seq >= 1) that gives the member an @username:

```json
{
  "type": "username",
  "username": "ana_maria",
  "member": "<hex64, the diary key>",
  "date": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z",
  "association": "<hex64>",
  "issuer": "<hex64>",
  "issuerDate": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z",
  "issuerSignature": "<128 lowercase hex>",
  "signature": "<128 lowercase hex>"
}
```

Exactly these 9 keys: `type` (`'username'`), `username` (matches `USERNAME_RE = /^[a-z][a-z0-9_]{2,19}$/` and NOT `/^[0-9a-f]{6}$/`: a 6-hex name cannot be told apart from the fallback handle `'@' + key.slice(0, 6)`; `isUsername(s)`), `member` (lowercase hex64), `date` (canonical ISO), `association`, `issuer` (lowercase hex64, `issuer !== association`), `issuerDate` (canonical ISO), `issuerSignature`, `signature` (128 lowercase hex). Anything else is `'malformed'`.

`payload` = UTF-8 bytes of `'varanda-username-v1'`, `username`, `member`, `date`, `association`, `issuer`, `issuerDate`, `issuerSignature` joined by a single LF (no trailing LF, hex lowercase). `signature` = `sign(payload, issuerSecretKey)`.

- `usernameRecordPayload(record)` -> Buffer.
- `createUsernameRecord({ member, username, date = new Date().toISOString() }, issuerKeyPair, issuerCert)` -> record; same TypeError rules as `createIssuedCert`, plus `isUsername(username)`.
- `validateUsernameRecordShape(record)` -> `null | 'malformed'`.
- `verifyUsernameRecord(record, memberHex, associationHex, { revokedIssuers } = {})` -> `{ ok: true } | { ok: false, reason }`, first failing reason in order `'malformed'`, `'member-mismatch'` (`record.member !== normalizeHex(memberHex)`), `'untrusted-association'`, `'bad-issuer-signature'`, `'bad-signature'`, `'revoked-issuer'` (as for a v2 certificate). Never throws.
- The @username of a diary is `latestUsername(entries, logKey, association, { revokedIssuers } = {})`; a candidate signed by a revoked issuer fails verification like any other invalid record (so the result is `null`, no fallback). The candidate is the HIGHEST-seq shape-valid `username` record whose `member` equals the log key; only that one record is verified (`verifyUsernameRecord` with `memberHex` = the log key and the association). If it verifies its `username` is the result, otherwise `null`: there is no fallback to an earlier record. A record naming another member (copied from someone else's diary) is never a candidate; it is dropped by the shape/member filter at no crypto cost. Rationale: only the diary's owner can append to it and `username.add` verifies a record before appending, so a bad newest record can only come from the owner's own modified client and only hides the owner's own name; in exchange a followed peer cannot make readers pay one verification per junk record it appends: the cost is at most one candidate verification per log per feed build.

### Age attestations

Two statements the account service signs with its issuer key (the same key pair and issuer certificate that sign v2 member certificates). Both carry the issuer delegation inline exactly like a v2 member certificate or a username record and are verified with the same chain: association match, `verifyIssuerCert` on the embedded delegation, then the issuer's Ed25519 signature over the payload, then (optionally) revocation. Neither is a diary record, a swarm message or a block: they travel over HTTPS from the account service to the member's own client and are kept only in that client's node storage (kv `'ageVerified'` and `'ageSince'`, "Web member storage" in section 1). Neither carries an age band, a birth date, a method or a provider id. Module: `packages/core/lib/age-proof.js` (pure: b4a, hypercore-crypto and `./cert` only).

**Age-verified attestation** (one member):

```json
{
  "type": "age-verified",
  "member": "<hex64, the diary key>",
  "date": "2026-10-30T12:00:00.000Z",
  "association": "<hex64>",
  "issuer": "<hex64>",
  "issuerDate": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z",
  "issuerSignature": "<128 lowercase hex>",
  "signature": "<128 lowercase hex>"
}
```

Exactly these 8 keys (written in this order by `createAgeVerified`): `type` (`'age-verified'`), `member` (lowercase hex64), `date` (canonical ISO: `new Date(s).toISOString() === s`; the date the account service recorded the age band, so the attestation for one member is stable across requests), `association`, `issuer` (lowercase hex64, `issuer !== association`), `issuerDate` (canonical ISO), `issuerSignature`, `signature` (128 lowercase hex). Anything else is `'malformed'`. The serialized JSON is at most `MAX_AGE_PROOF_BYTES` = 1024 UTF-8 bytes (a real one is about 620).

`payload` = UTF-8 bytes of `'varanda-age-verified-v1'`, `member`, `date`, `association`, `issuer`, `issuerDate`, `issuerSignature` joined by a single LF (no trailing LF, hex lowercase). `signature` = `sign(payload, issuerSecretKey)`.

It states: "the account service of `association` has an age band on record for `member` since `date`". It never says which band.

**Age-since attestation** (one installation):

```json
{
  "type": "age-since",
  "since": "2026-10-29T00:00:00.000Z",
  "date": "2026-11-04T10:00:00.000Z",
  "association": "<hex64>",
  "issuer": "<hex64>",
  "issuerDate": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z",
  "issuerSignature": "<128 lowercase hex>",
  "signature": "<128 lowercase hex>"
}
```

Exactly these 8 keys (written in this order by `createAgeSince`): `type` (`'age-since'`), `since` (canonical ISO, never null: "no since" is the unsigned answer `{ since: null }`), `date` (canonical ISO, when the service signed it), then the same delegation fields and rules as above. Same byte cap.

`payload` = UTF-8 bytes of `'varanda-age-since-v1'`, `since`, `date`, `association`, `issuer`, `issuerDate`, `issuerSignature` joined by a single LF (no trailing LF, hex lowercase). `signature` = `sign(payload, issuerSecretKey)`.

It states: "no member certificate dated from `since` to `date` (both included) was issued without an age band on record". It vouches only for member certificates dated in `[since, date]`: a verifier holding a member certificate accepted by `certForKey` whose `since <= date(cert) <= date(attestation)` may treat the account as age-checked, and a certificate dated after the attestation's `date` is not covered, so a saved older attestation cannot vouch for certificates issued after the check was later turned off. The account service re-signs it on each request with a fresh `date` (at most 60 s old) and never with a `since` earlier than the last time the check was turned on. A node keeps the newest: it refuses an age-since whose `date` is earlier than the one it already holds and that still verifies (`age.proof`, rpc 5.83).

The prefixes are new: `'varanda-age-v1'` is the member's own age-verification request (`packages/core/lib/age.js`, "Reports and operator requests" below) and `'varanda-phone-v1'` is the member's own phone-verification request (`packages/core/lib/phone.js`, same section), so neither attestation can be read as either request, as each other, as a phone attestation (`'varanda-phone-verified-v1'` or `'varanda-phone-since-v1'`), as a v2 member certificate or as a username record, and vice versa.

- `AGE_VERIFIED_PREFIX` = `'varanda-age-verified-v1'`, `AGE_SINCE_PREFIX` = `'varanda-age-since-v1'`, `MAX_AGE_PROOF_BYTES` = 1024.
- `PHONE_VERIFIED_PREFIX` = `'varanda-phone-verified-v1'`, `PHONE_SINCE_PREFIX` = `'varanda-phone-since-v1'`, `MAX_PHONE_PROOF_BYTES` = 1024 (`packages/core/lib/phone-proof.js`, "Phone attestations" below).
- `ageVerifiedPayload(att)`, `ageSincePayload(att)` -> Buffer; TypeError when a signed field is malformed.
- `createAgeVerified({ member, date }, issuerKeyPair, issuerCert)` and `createAgeSince({ since, date = new Date().toISOString() }, issuerKeyPair, issuerCert)` -> attestation; same TypeError rules as `createIssuedCert` (`checkIssuer`), plus `member` hex64 and `date` / `since` canonical.
- `validateAgeVerifiedShape(att)`, `validateAgeSinceShape(att)` -> `null | 'malformed'` (exact keys, byte cap, lowercase hex, canonical ISO, `type`, `issuer !== association`).
- `verifyAgeVerified(att, memberHex, associationHex, { revokedIssuers } = {})` -> `{ ok: true } | { ok: false, reason }`, first failing reason in order `'malformed'`, `'member-mismatch'` (`att.member !== normalizeHex(memberHex)`), `'untrusted-association'`, `'bad-issuer-signature'`, `'bad-signature'`, `'revoked-issuer'` (as for a v2 certificate). Never throws.
- `verifyAgeSince(att, associationHex, { revokedIssuers } = {})` -> the same without `'member-mismatch'`. Never throws.

Neither attestation is revocable or expires: a band is never removed from the account service. `revokedIssuers` is accepted for parity with the other issuer-signed objects.

### Phone attestations

Two statements the account service signs with its issuer key (the same key pair and issuer certificate that sign v2 member certificates). Both carry the issuer delegation inline exactly like a v2 member certificate, a username record or an age attestation, and are verified with the same chain: association match, `verifyIssuerCert` on the embedded delegation, then the issuer's Ed25519 signature over the payload, then (optionally) revocation. Neither is a diary record, a swarm message or a block: they travel over HTTPS from the account service to the member's own client and are kept only in that client's node storage (kv `'phoneVerified'` and `'phoneSince'`, "Web member storage" in section 1). Neither carries a phone number, a mask, an index or a provider id. Module: `packages/core/lib/phone-proof.js` (pure: b4a, hypercore-crypto and `./cert` only).

**Phone-verified attestation** (one member):

```json
{
  "type": "phone-verified",
  "member": "<hex64, the diary key>",
  "date": "2026-11-11T12:00:00.000Z",
  "association": "<hex64>",
  "issuer": "<hex64>",
  "issuerDate": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z",
  "issuerSignature": "<128 lowercase hex>",
  "signature": "<128 lowercase hex>"
}
```

Exactly these 8 keys (written in this order by `createPhoneVerified`): `type` (`'phone-verified'`), `member` (lowercase hex64), `date` (canonical ISO: `new Date(s).toISOString() === s`; the date the account service recorded that the member had a verified phone), `association`, `issuer` (lowercase hex64, `issuer !== association`), `issuerDate` (canonical ISO), `issuerSignature`, `signature` (128 lowercase hex). Anything else is `'malformed'`. The serialized JSON is at most `MAX_PHONE_PROOF_BYTES` = 1024 UTF-8 bytes (a real one is about 620).

`payload` = UTF-8 bytes of `'varanda-phone-verified-v1'`, `member`, `date`, `association`, `issuer`, `issuerDate`, `issuerSignature` joined by a single LF (no trailing LF, hex lowercase). `signature` = `sign(payload, issuerSecretKey)`.

It states: "the account service of `association` had a verified phone on record for `member` at `date`". It never says the number.

**Phone-since attestation** (one association):

```json
{
  "type": "phone-since",
  "since": "2026-11-10T00:00:00.000Z",
  "date": "2026-11-11T10:00:00.000Z",
  "association": "<hex64>",
  "issuer": "<hex64>",
  "issuerDate": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z",
  "issuerSignature": "<128 lowercase hex>",
  "signature": "<128 lowercase hex>"
}
```

Exactly these 8 keys (written in this order by `createPhoneSince`): `type` (`'phone-since'`), `since` (canonical ISO), `date` (canonical ISO, when the service signed it), then the same delegation fields and rules as above. There is no `member` key. Same byte cap.

`payload` = UTF-8 bytes of `'varanda-phone-since-v1'`, `since`, `date`, `association`, `issuer`, `issuerDate`, `issuerSignature` joined by a single LF (no trailing LF, hex lowercase). `signature` = `sign(payload, issuerSecretKey)`.

It states: "from `since`, the association requires a verified phone". It carries no phone number. It never vouches for a certificate: it only marks that the association requires a verified phone. A saved attestation does not say that any member certificate was issued with a phone on record.

The prefixes separate these attestations from each other, from the age attestations, from the member's `'varanda-age-v1'` and `'varanda-phone-v1'` requests, from a v2 member certificate and from a username record, and vice versa.

- `PHONE_VERIFIED_PREFIX` = `'varanda-phone-verified-v1'`, `PHONE_SINCE_PREFIX` = `'varanda-phone-since-v1'`, `MAX_PHONE_PROOF_BYTES` = 1024.
- `phoneVerifiedPayload(att)`, `phoneSincePayload(att)` -> Buffer; TypeError when a signed field is malformed.
- `createPhoneVerified({ member, date }, issuerKeyPair, issuerCert)` and `createPhoneSince({ since, date = new Date().toISOString() }, issuerKeyPair, issuerCert)` -> attestation; same TypeError rules as `createIssuedCert` (`checkIssuer`), plus `member` hex64 and `date` / `since` canonical.
- `validatePhoneVerifiedShape(att)`, `validatePhoneSinceShape(att)` -> `null | 'malformed'` (exact keys, byte cap right after the key set, lowercase hex, canonical ISO, `type`, `issuer !== association`).
- `verifyPhoneVerified(att, memberHex, associationHex, { revokedIssuers } = {})` -> `{ ok: true } | { ok: false, reason }`, first failing reason in order `'malformed'`, `'member-mismatch'` (`att.member !== normalizeHex(memberHex)`), `'untrusted-association'`, `'bad-issuer-signature'`, `'bad-signature'`, `'revoked-issuer'` (as for a v2 certificate). Never throws.
- `verifyPhoneSince(att, associationHex, { revokedIssuers } = {})` -> the same without `'member-mismatch'`. Never throws.

`revokedIssuers` is checked the same way as for a v2 certificate. A stored object that does not verify is ignored (`phone.proof`, rpc 5.116).

### Moderator certificate (B2)

The association certifies a moderator key that signs the blocklist snapshots below, so the association secret stays offline.

```json
{
  "v": 1,
  "moderator": "<hex64, the moderator public key>",
  "date": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z",
  "association": "<hex64>",
  "signature": "<128 lowercase hex>"
}
```

Exactly these 5 keys: `v` (`1`), `moderator` (lowercase hex64, `moderator !== association`, else `'malformed'`), `date` (canonical ISO), `association` (lowercase hex64), `signature` (128 lowercase hex, `sign(payload, associationSecretKey)`).

`payload` = UTF-8 bytes of `'varanda-moderator-v1'`, `moderator`, `date`, `association` joined by a single LF (no trailing LF, hex lowercase). The prefix differs from every other payload, so an issuer delegation, a seeder certificate or a member certificate is never a moderator certificate: an issuer key cannot moderate.

- `moderatorCertPayload(cert)` -> Buffer.
- `createModeratorCert({ moderator, date = new Date().toISOString() }, associationKeyPair)` -> ModeratorCertificate; TypeError unless `moderator` is hex64 in any case (stored lowercase) and differs from the association key, `date` is canonical and the key pair is valid.
- `validateModeratorCertShape(cert)` -> `null | 'malformed'`.
- `verifyModeratorCert(cert, associationHex)` -> `{ ok: true } | { ok: false, reason }` with reasons in order `'malformed'`, `'untrusted-association'`, `'bad-signature'`. Never throws.

### Blocklist snapshot (B2, `packages/core/lib/blocklist.js`)

The moderator signs numbered snapshots of the blocked diary keys and the revoked issuer keys; every node keeps the newest snapshot it accepted and enforces it. The module is pure (`b4a`, `hypercore-crypto`, `./cert`; no `#fs` of its own, no network).

```json
{
  "v": 1,
  "seq": 1,
  "date": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z",
  "blocked": ["<hex64>", "..."],
  "revokedIssuers": ["<hex64>", "..."],
  "moderator": "<hex64>",
  "moderatorDate": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z",
  "moderatorSignature": "<128 lowercase hex>",
  "signature": "<128 lowercase hex>"
}
```

Exactly these 9 keys, in this order: `v` (`1`), `seq` (safe integer >= 1), `date` (canonical ISO), `blocked` (array of at most `MAX_BLOCKED = 4096` (= `MAX_INVENTORY`, section 5) lowercase hex64 keys, strictly increasing, i.e. sorted lexicographically and distinct), `revokedIssuers` (the same rule with at most `MAX_REVOKED_ISSUERS = 64` issuer keys), `moderator` (lowercase hex64), `moderatorDate` (canonical ISO), `moderatorSignature` and `signature` (128 lowercase hex). Anything else (including uppercase, unsorted, duplicate or too many keys) is `'malformed'`. `moderator`, `moderatorDate` and `moderatorSignature` are the moderator certificate's `moderator`, `date` and `signature`; the association is not repeated: the verifier supplies it.

`payload` = UTF-8 bytes of `'varanda-blocklist-v1'`, `seq` in decimal, `date`, the `blocked` keys joined by `','` (`''` when empty), the `revokedIssuers` keys joined by `','` (`''` when empty), `moderator`, `moderatorDate`, `moderatorSignature` joined by a single LF (no trailing LF). `signature` = `sign(payload, moderatorSecretKey)`.

Size: `MAX_SNAPSHOT_BYTES = 393216`; the JSON of the largest valid snapshot (4096 blocked keys, 64 revoked issuers, expanded-year dates, `seq = Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER`) is under it (`packages/core/test/blocklist.test.js`), and far under the seeder's `MAX_EXTERNAL_FRAME` (section 4).

- `snapshotPayload(snapshot)` -> Buffer; `validateSnapshotShape(snapshot)` -> `null | 'malformed'`.
- `createSnapshot({ seq, date = new Date().toISOString(), blocked = [], revokedIssuers = [] }, moderatorKeyPair, moderatorCert)` -> snapshot. Keys are accepted in any case, order and with repeats; they are stored lowercase, sorted and distinct. TypeError for a bad `seq`/`date`/key, more than the limit, a bad key pair, or a moderator certificate that is malformed, does not verify for its own association or names another key.
- `verifySnapshot(snapshot, associationHex)` -> `{ ok: true } | { ok: false, reason }`, the FIRST failing reason in order: `'malformed'` (shape, or `associationHex` not hex64); `'bad-moderator-signature'` (the embedded `{ v: 1, moderator, date: moderatorDate, association: associationHex, signature: moderatorSignature }` fails `verifyModeratorCert`: a moderator of another association, a changed `moderatorDate`, an issuer or seeder key presenting its own certificate's signature); `'bad-signature'` (`verify(payload, signature, moderator)` is false). Because the association comes from the verifier, `'untrusted-association'` cannot occur here (a foreign moderator is `'bad-moderator-signature'`). The snapshot is copied once and every check runs on the copy, so getters cannot change it between checks.
- `compareSnapshots(a, b)` -> `-1 | 0 | 1`: the total order on (`moderatorDate` as a time, then `seq`). `a`/`b` may be snapshots, `have` values `{ moderatorDate, seq, sig }` (`moderatorDate` `''`, `seq` `0` and `sig` `''` when the node holds none; `sig` is ignored by the order) or `null` (= none). It ignores the moderator; dates are compared as times, not strings (expanded years).
- `acceptSnapshot(previous, next, associationHex)` -> `{ ok: true } | { ok: false, reason }`: `next` must pass `verifySnapshot` (else its reason); `previous` (the node's own stored snapshot, trusted) `null` -> accept; identical `seq`, `moderator` and `signature` -> `'same'` (a no-op, not an error); accept when `next.moderatorDate` is later than `previous.moderatorDate` (a newer association-certified moderator replaces an older one and its `seq` restarts), or when the moderator and `moderatorDate` are the same and `next.seq > previous.seq`; everything else is `'stale'`, including a different moderator with the same `moderatorDate` (the first one stored wins) and the same `seq` with another signature.
- `blockedSet(snapshot)` / `revokedIssuerSet(snapshot)` -> `Set` (empty for `null`); `isBlocked(set, key)` -> boolean (any case). `revokedIssuerSet` is what `verifyCert`, `certForKey`, `verifyUsernameRecord` and `latestUsername` take as `{ revokedIssuers }`.
- `createBlocklistState({ association, load, save })` -> `{ ready(), current(), blocked(), revokedIssuers(), offer(snapshot) }`. `load()` and `save(snapshot)` may be sync or async; a loaded snapshot that does not verify for `association` is ignored. `offer` runs in ONE promise queue per state: inside the queue it re-runs `acceptSnapshot` against the then-current snapshot, awaits `save` and only then swaps, and resolves `{ adopted, reason, added, removed, revokedAdded, revokedRemoved }` (`reason` `null` when adopted; the four `Set`s are the blocked and revoked keys that changed, empty when not adopted). A failing `save` rejects that offer and keeps the previous snapshot; a failing `load` rejects `ready()` and every offer (fail closed).
- `verifySnapshot`, `acceptSnapshot`, `compareSnapshots`, `validateSnapshotShape`, `blockedSet`, `revokedIssuerSet` and `isBlocked` never throw.

### Blocklist channel (`'varanda/blocklist/1'`, `packages/core/lib/blocklist-net.js`)

Carries the snapshot above between nodes on the SAME protomux that replication and the admission channel use (`socket.noiseStream.userData`). Messages are UTF-8 JSON (compact-encoding `c.buffer`), at most `MAX_BLOCKLIST_MESSAGE = 393216` bytes (the same bound as `MAX_SNAPSHOT_BYTES`):

- `have`: exactly `{ "type": "have", "moderatorDate": <canonical ISO, or '' when the node holds none>, "seq": <safe integer >= 0>, "sig": <the first 32 lowercase hex characters (`SIG_HEX_LENGTH`) of the held snapshot's `signature`, or '' when none> }`: the ordering key of the snapshot the sender holds (`compareSnapshots`, above) plus a prefix that identifies its contents. `moderatorDate` `''` requires `sig` `''`; `sig` takes no part in the order. It lets the operator's publisher tell "the seeder holds exactly this list" from "the seeder holds another list with the same (`moderatorDate`, `seq`)" (after restoring an old backup of the moderation directory): the first is a delivery, the second a conflict (`{ delivered: false, conflict: true }`, the command exits 1). A `have` over `MAX_HAVE_BYTES = 256` bytes is a bad message.
- `snapshot`: exactly `{ "type": "snapshot", "snapshot": BlocklistSnapshot }`.

Rules (constants exported by `blocklist-net.js`):

- **Open and order.** Every node calls `attachBlocklist` right after `store.replicate(socket)` and BEFORE the admission `hello` (seeder `_attachPeer`, member `runSync` connection handler, web link), and sends a `have` on open. It registers `mux.pair({ protocol })` AND opens its own channel, because protomux pairs an incoming open only when this side has already opened or registered the protocol (`node_modules/protomux/index.js:729-745`); whichever side opens first is paired.
- **Credit.** A `have` is a credit for exactly ONE snapshot from the receiver of that `have`. A node sends a snapshot only to a peer whose `have` is older than its own and spends the credit by sending; the receiver answers every processed verdict except a peer-fault one (adopted, `'same'`, `'stale'`) with a new `have`. A snapshot that arrives with no credit left is one bad message and is never offered, so publishes bunched together after a suspension (a PWA in the background) never exceed the credits granted and arrival time is never judged.
- **One snapshot per 10 s.** `MIN_SNAPSHOT_INTERVAL_MS = 10000`: one pending slot per connection, offered to `state.offer` at most once per interval and never while another offer of that connection is in flight (a newer credited arrival replaces the held one). A local `offer` failure (storage) keeps the snapshot and retries it with exponential backoff up to `MAX_RETRY_DELAY_MS = 300000` for as long as the connection lives. The node's own sends are spaced a quarter longer than the receiver's minimum.
- **5 bad messages.** `MAX_BAD_BLOCKLIST = 5`: the fifth bad message (unparsable, wrong shape, oversized, an uncredited snapshot, or a snapshot whose verdict is `'malformed'`, `'bad-signature'`, `'bad-moderator-signature'` or `'untrusted-association'`) destroys the socket (peer-dropped reason `'blocklist'`). `'same'` and `'stale'` never count.
- **Message rate.** A per-connection token bucket over every received message, checked before `JSON.parse`: `MESSAGE_BURST = 512`, refilled at `MESSAGE_RATE = 2` per second from the monotonic clock; a peer out of tokens is dropped (reason `'message-rate'`).
- **Publishing and delivery** (`apps/cli/lib/mod-publish.js`). `mod` publishes ONLY to the association's seeder, dialed by its Noise public key (`--seeder`, or `seeder` stored by `mod init --seeder`) with `swarm.joinPeer`; it never joins the public association topic and never listens, and any connection from another key is destroyed before a channel is opened. The publisher is a throwaway peer: a temporary data dir, an empty corestore and an ephemeral Noise key (never the moderator or the association key). Delivery is confirmed only on the seeder's connection and needs BOTH, in either order: its admission `hello` verified as role `'seeder'` with a seeder certificate of this association for the very Noise key of the connection (`seederCertForKey`), AND its blocklist `have` equal to the snapshot's (`moderatorDate`, `seq`), which the channel sends only after the snapshot was adopted and stored. A `have` from any unverified peer never counts (anyone can echo the publisher's own `have`). The publisher re-dials every 2 s (hyperswarm gives up on a peer after a few attempts), times out after `--timeout-ms` (default 30000) leaving the signed list in `blocklist.json`, and `mod publish` re-sends the stored list unchanged. From the seeder the nodes fan the snapshot out. Who can see the operator machine's address: the seeder and the DHT nodes that route the lookup and the connection to the seeder's key, not arbitrary peers of the association.
- **Adoption and fan-out.** A snapshot is adopted only through `state.offer` (above: one promise queue, `acceptSnapshot` re-run inside it, `save` before the swap). After an adoption the node calls `announce()` on every OTHER open blocklist handle, so the snapshot travels on without the moderator.
- `hello` is UNCHANGED: a node does not advertise its snapshot there.

**Old nodes (from before B2).** They have no `pair` for the protocol, so protomux rejects our open (`_onopensession` -> `_requestSession` -> `_rejectSession`, `node_modules/protomux/index.js:705-757`, `:794-805`) and our side closes its local channel (`_onrejectsession`, `:759-779`). Unlike the admission channel, that close never destroys the socket: `attachBlocklist`'s `supported` resolves `false` and replication and admission continue as before. Nothing else changes for them, and they do NOT enforce the list.

### Notices channel (`'varanda/notices/1'`, `packages/core/lib/notices-net.js`, `packages/core/lib/notices.js`)

Tells a member who started following them, so they can follow back. It is private to the followed member: the seeder answers only for the diary key the connection proved it owns, and no message carries a count, a total or a length (pages are cursors, never tallies). It travels on the SAME protomux as replication and the admission channel (`socket.noiseStream.userData`). Messages are UTF-8 JSON (compact-encoding `c.buffer`), exact keys, lower-case hex, at most `MAX_NOTICES_MESSAGE = 16384` bytes (checked on the raw buffer before `JSON.parse`). The channel protocol is `NOTICES_PROTOCOL = 'varanda/notices/1'`.

- `challenge`: seeder to member, once, on open: exactly `{ "type": "challenge", "nonce": <64 hex> }` (32 random bytes).
- `proof`: member to seeder, once: exactly `{ "type": "proof", "key": <64 hex, the diary key>, "sig": <128 hex> }`.
