- `page`: member to seeder, one at a time: exactly `{ "type": "page", "after": null | { "at": <canonical ISO>, "key": <64 hex> }, "limit": <integer 1..64> }`.
- `items`: seeder to member, the answer to a `page`: exactly `{ "type": "items", "items": [{ "key": <64 hex>, "seq": <integer >= 1>, "at": <canonical ISO> }], "next": null | { "at", "key" }, "incomplete": <boolean> }`, at most 64 items. `key` is the follower's diary key, `seq` the block of their signed `follow` record and `at` that record's `at`. Order: newest `at` first (by `Date.parse`), then key ascending; the cursor is exclusive. A page is 30 items by default and 64 at most (`NOTICES_LIMIT_DEFAULT = 30`, `NOTICES_LIMIT_MAX = 64`).

**Proof.** The payload is `'varanda-notices-v1\n'` (`NOTICE_PROOF_PREFIX`) + nonce + `'\n'` + the member's own Noise public key (hex) + `'\n'` + the diary key (hex), as UTF-8. The member signs it with the diary key as an Ed25519 key (the diary key IS the Ed25519 public key of a browser member). The seeder verifies it against the `remotePublicKey` of THIS connection and its own nonce, so a proof cannot be replayed on another connection nor relayed. One proof per channel: a second or a failed proof is a bad message. After a valid proof every page is the followers of the bound key, whatever the member asks; a page before a valid proof, or a second page while one is unanswered, is a bad message.

**Why a proof is required.** The Noise key of a sync link is ephemeral, so it names nobody. The `have` of a hello lists the diaries a node FOLLOWS and is not ownership of anything. And who follows you is not published by your own diary (a follow lives in the follower's diary). Without a proof the seeder could be asked for anyone's followers.

**Bad messages.** A frame over 16384 bytes, a JSON or shape failure, a page before the proof, a failed or second proof, a second page in flight, a second challenge, unsolicited `items` and any message of the other role. More than `MAX_BAD_NOTICES = 5` bad messages destroys the socket (peer-dropped reason `'notices'`). Message rate: the per-connection token bucket of the blocklist channel (`MESSAGE_BURST = 512`, `MESSAGE_RATE = 2` per second), checked before `JSON.parse`; a peer out of tokens is dropped (reason `'message-rate'`).

**Member handle and timeout.** A member handle is single use per channel. When its channel closes for any reason (rejected open, seeder close, drop, socket close) the handle is finished: the in-flight request rejects, `requestPage` rejects with `'notices channel closed'` and the next caller attaches a fresh channel. One page request is in flight at a time, with a `NOTICES_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS = 10000` timer; the timeout is terminal for the CHANNEL (it rejects the request and closes the channel, never the socket), so a late reply cannot be mistaken for the answer to a later request.

**The seeder's index** (`createNoticeIndex`, in memory only).
- Built by the diary scan (`_runScan`): every valid `follow` block folds into a per-author map `target -> { seq, at, on }`; the highest `seq` wins per author and target, and the scan's ascending-seq order is an API guarantee (a record at or below the author's high-water mark is ignored). A self-follow is never a notice. Pages list the authors whose last record for the target has `on: true`.
- Caps while folding: at most `MAX_FOLLOW_TARGETS_PER_AUTHOR = 4096` targets per author and `NOTICES_MAX_EDGES = 262144` fold entries in total. Beyond a cap a new target is skipped and every page says `incomplete: true`.
- Member predicate: the index is built with `isMember(key)` (the seeder's admitted or served diary keys; a throwing check means not a member). A follow of a non-member key is nobody's notice: each author stores at most `MAX_NONMEMBER_TARGETS_PER_AUTHOR = 64` new non-member targets, and further ones are skipped WITHOUT marking the author truncated (pages stay complete), so a few diaries following random keys can neither fill `NOTICES_MAX_EDGES` for every member nor make every page incomplete. Stored non-member entries count toward both caps above; the count is reset when the author is removed or re-served. A follow of a key admitted later is picked up on that author's next serve or on restart.
- Commit: an author's fold is invisible to pages until the scan reached the signed length (present length >= `core.length`); then it replaces the author's published edges. A scan that stops short, a failed or missing block, a served-diary `append` and every (re)serve mark the author pending, and any pending author makes every page `incomplete: true`.
- Rebuilt from block 1 on every serve and at start (`removeAuthor` then `markPending`); dropping a diary removes its author. Nothing is persisted: `close()` replaces the index. `stats()` is unchanged; the channel adds only the log events `notices-page-invalid`, `notices-page-failed` and `peer-dropped` (reason `'notices'` or `'message-rate'`), none of which carries a follower key or an index size.
- Pages skip the target itself and blocked authors (`_isBlocked`; a throwing check hides the row). A blocked target gets an empty page with `incomplete: false`; a target that is not (yet) served or not gated, or a seeder that is closing, gets what the index holds with `incomplete: true`, never a final empty answer.

**Compatibility.** The seeder attaches the channel in `_attachPeer` right after the blocklist channel and before its hello. A peer that never opens it (an older node) stays connected and replicates: `supported` resolves `false` and the socket is never destroyed for it. The admission bytes (hello, announce) are UNCHANGED.

**Residual.** The seeder is not trusted for the facts: the member re-verifies every row (next section). A seeder can withhold an edge, or a later unfollow that falls outside the verified tail window, but cannot forge a follow.

### Notices channel v2 (`'varanda/notices/2'`, `packages/core/lib/notices-v2.js`, `packages/core/lib/post-notices.js`)

Tells a member who followed them, replied to them, quoted them or mentioned them. It has the same design as v1 (challenge, proof, page, items; private to the proved key; no count, total or length; the same `MAX_NOTICES_MESSAGE = 16384`, `MAX_BAD_NOTICES = 5`, message rate and `NOTICES_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS = 10000`, the same single-use member handle). The channel protocol is `NOTICES_V2_PROTOCOL = 'varanda/notices/2'`. What differs:

- **Proof.** The prefix is `'varanda-notices-v2\n'` (`NOTICE_V2_PROOF_PREFIX`), so a signature made for one channel never verifies on the other; the rest of the payload is v1's (nonce, the member's Noise public key, the diary key).
- `page`: exactly `{ "type": "page", "after": null | { "at": <canonical ISO>, "key": <64 hex>, "seq": <integer >= 1> }, "limit": <integer 1..64> }`.
- `items`: exactly `{ "type": "items", "items": [{ "key": <64 hex>, "seq": <integer >= 1>, "at": <canonical ISO>, "kind": "follow" | "reply" | "quote" | "mention" }], "next": null | { "at", "key", "seq" }, "incomplete": <boolean> }`, at most 64 items. `key` is the author's diary key and `seq` the block of their signed `follow` record (kind `follow`) or of their signed `post` (the other three). `kind` is only a hint: `NOTICE_KINDS` is `['follow', 'reply', 'quote', 'mention']` and any other value makes the message invalid, but the browser never trusts it (next section).
- **Order.** Newest `at` first (by `Date.parse`), then `key` ascending, then `seq` descending (one author may have several notices at one instant, so the cursor carries `seq`); the cursor is exclusive.
- **Two channels, one socket.** The seeder attaches both channels in `_attachPeer`, right after the blocklist channel and BEFORE its hello, in the slots `_varandaNotices` (v1) and `_varandaNoticesV2` (v2), so neither disturbs the other. v1 is UNCHANGED and stays follow-only; v2 also carries the follows (the same rows `createNoticeIndex` serves), so a v2 reader needs no v1 page. The log events are `notices-v2-page-invalid` and `notices-v2-page-failed`, and the drop reason `'notices-v2'`. A seeder from before v2 does not pair the channel: `supported` resolves `false` and nothing else changes.

**The seeder's post index** (`createPostNoticeIndex`, in memory only, separate from the follow index on purpose, so a mention spammer can neither exhaust the follow edges nor mark follow pages incomplete).
- Built by the diary scan (`_runScan`): every valid `post` and `username` block is folded there (not in `_runIndex`, whose queue pauses at capacity and would leave mention targets unknown). A post yields at most one row per post and target, with the priority reply > quote > mention: a reply to the target's key, a quote of it, or a mention of a name. A reply or quote of the author's own post, and a mention of a name the author verifiably holds (a self-mention), are nobody's notice and take no capacity. At most `MENTION_MAX = 8` names per post.
- Names. A mention row is stored by the folded name token, not by owner. The index keeps, per author, the newest `MAX_HELD_USERNAMES = 64` username records of its own (the window of `heldUsernames`, `packages/core/lib/username.js`), verified once at settle with `verifyUsernameRecord` against the association (the revoked issuers are applied at page time), so a renamed member keeps every verified name of its window: the account service never gives a name once held to another member, and the old name still notifies the same person. A page for the target reads the rows of the target's own key plus the rows of every name it verifiably holds whose issuer is not revoked (a revoked issuer's names stop matching at once).
- Caps. Per author and target (a key, or a name token) the newest `MAX_POST_NOTICES_PER_PAIR = 32` rows survive; per author the newest `MAX_POST_NOTICES_PER_AUTHOR = 1024`. Both evict the author's own lowest seq, a post whole (never half a notice), and are not incompleteness: they only forget the old notices of one noisy author. Rows whose target is not known to be anyone (a key `isMember` rejects, a name no verified record holds yet) are counted as distinct pairs and capped at `MAX_UNRESOLVED_TOKENS_PER_AUTHOR = 64` per author, and further ones are skipped silently, like the non-member follow targets of v1. The global cap `POST_NOTICES_MAX_EDGES = 262144` refuses the new row and marks that author truncated; it never evicts another author's rows. Authors are bounded by the seeder's `maxServed` (`POST_NOTICES_MAX_AUTHORS = 4096` is the default of the index itself, the seeder's default `MAX_INVENTORY`).
- `incomplete: true` when any author is pending (the scan has not reached its signed length), truncated by the global cap, or refused at the author bound, when the follow index says so, or when the target is not (yet) served and gated or the seeder is closing. A refusal is sticky: posts folded while an author was refused are gone, so the author stays refused (and pages stay incomplete) until `removeAuthor`, which a re-serve calls before a fresh fold from seq 1. A refusal the bounded set cannot record keeps every page incomplete until the index is rebuilt (restart).
- Pages skip the target itself and blocked authors (a throwing check hides the row), and hide a row dated more than 24 h in the future (`MAX_FUTURE_MS = 86400000`; hidden, not deleted, so it shows when the clock catches up). Rows are published at fold: a later block never cancels a post (only an unfollow cancels a follow). A blocked target gets an empty page with `incomplete: false`. Nothing is persisted and `close()` replaces the index.

**Known limit.** A self-mention folded before the author's own username record settles is stored as an unresolved mention. It can still evict that author's older rows inside the author's own budget; it never touches another author's rows. Once the name is verified the row is dropped (`dropOwnMentions`).

**Residual.** As in v1, the seeder is not trusted for the facts: it can withhold a row, never forge one.

### Notices channel v3 (`'varanda/notices/3'`, `packages/core/lib/notices-v3.js`, `packages/core/lib/engagement-index.js`)

Tells a member who followed them, replied to them, quoted them, mentioned them, or liked or reposted a post of theirs. It has the same design as v2 (challenge, proof, page, items; private to the proved key; no count, total or length; the same `MAX_NOTICES_MESSAGE = 16384`, `MAX_BAD_NOTICES = 5`, message rate and `NOTICES_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS = 10000`, the same single-use member handle, the same cursor `{ at, key, seq }`). The channel protocol is `NOTICES_V3_PROTOCOL = 'varanda/notices/3'`. v1 and v2 are unchanged. What differs:

- **Proof.** The prefix is `'varanda-notices-v3\n'` (`NOTICE_V3_PROOF_PREFIX`), so a signature made for one channel never verifies on another; the rest of the payload is v1's (nonce, the member's Noise public key, the diary key).
- `items`: exactly `{ "type": "items", "items": [{ "key": <64 hex>, "seq": <integer >= 1>, "at": <canonical ISO>, "kind": "follow" | "reply" | "quote" | "mention" | "like" | "repost" }], "next": null | { "at", "key", "seq" }, "incomplete": <boolean> }`, at most 64 items. `NOTICE_V3_KINDS` is `['follow', 'reply', 'quote', 'mention', 'like', 'repost']`. Any other value makes the whole message invalid. `kind` is only a hint.
- **Like and repost items.** `key` is the engager's diary key and `seq` the block of their signed `like` or `repost` record with `on: true`. The post is that record's `to`, not a field of the item. The browser re-reads the signed block (Notices link).
- **Why v2 does not grow.** An unknown v2 kind invalidates the whole `items` message (`NOTICE_KINDS` stays `['follow', 'reply', 'quote', 'mention']`; `validItem` fails the message on the first unknown kind), so an old client rejects a whole v2 page that carries one like. A failed items build is sent as an empty incomplete page: `itemsMessage` returns null when the result would not validate, and `answer` in `notices-net.js` then sends `emptyPage()` (`{ "type": "items", "items": [], "next": null, "incomplete": true }`). Likes and reposts therefore travel only on v3. v2 pages stay follow, reply, quote and mention.
- **Three channels, one socket.** The seeder attaches all three in `_attachPeer`, after the blocklist channel and BEFORE its hello, in the slots `_varandaNotices` (v1), `_varandaNoticesV2` (v2) and `_varandaNoticesV3` (v3). v3 reuses `projectItemV2` and `projectCursorV2`. The log events are `notices-v3-page-invalid` and `notices-v3-page-failed`, and the drop reason is `'notices-v3'`. A seeder from before v3 does not pair the channel: `supported` resolves `false` and the socket stays up. v3 pages are `pageV3`: the v2 rows plus the engagement rows, sorted together, so a cursor never skips a row of another kind. A blocked target gets an empty page with `incomplete: false`.

**Where the like and repost rows come from** (`noticeRows` on the engagement index, in memory only, served only to the proved key).
- A row is `{ key, seq, at, kind }` for an engager whose last operation on one of the member's indexed posts is still `on: true`, newest `at` first, then key ascending, then `seq` descending. A self-like and a self-repost (the engager is the post's author) are absent. An unlike or an unrepost (`on: false`, the last operation wins) removes the row. A tombstone of the like or repost record (`removeRecord`) removes it. A deleted target post (`removePost`, and a ref in `deletedRefs`) removes every incoming row, and a like of a post the index does not hold is not a row.
- An edge dated more than 24 h ahead (`MAX_FUTURE_MS = 86400000`) is hidden, not removed, and shows once the clock catches up. An edge whose `at` is before 1970, and one dated exactly `1970-01-01T00:00:00.000Z`, is stored as `atMs` 0 by `atMillis` and skipped.
- Caps forget older rows and return no count: `NOTICE_ROWS_PER_ENGAGER = 64` per engager first, so one engager cannot crowd out the others, then `NOTICE_ROWS_MAX = 4096` overall. The visibility check and the future rule run before the caps, so a hidden engager takes no slot.
- `incomplete: true` for a v3 page when `engagementIncomplete` is set, and for the same reasons as v2. `engagementIncomplete` is set when an engager was held out by `_searchHeld` or when `_indexFlags().complete` is false (a block refused at an index cap pauses that diary's cursor, so it counts there; `stats().saturated` alone does not mark the page, since it turns true at the cap before anything is refused). A closing seeder, or a target not yet served or not gated, also marks the page incomplete.

**Push window.** `PUSH_MAX_AGE_MS` (6 h) stays on the push relay only. This channel does not apply it: a like or repost older than 6 h is still a row when the index holds it.

**Residual.** As in v1, the seeder is not trusted for the facts: it can withhold a row, never forge one.

### Push relay (loopback, `packages/core/lib/push-events.js`, `apps/cli/lib/push-relay.js`)

Hosted Web Push for the PWA. It is not the notices channels above. Those feed the in-app Avisos list: v1 follows, v2 follows plus replies, quotes and mentions, and v3 the same plus likes and reposts of the member's posts. The 6 h window (`PUSH_MAX_AGE_MS`) applies only on this relay. This relay sends only `follow`, `reply`, `like` and `repost` (`PUSH_KINDS`). A quote, a mention and growth of a followed diary are not push events. The in-app list does not travel this path. The relay is not a swarm channel and not a diary block. The seeder never sees a subscription.

**Candidates** (`pushCandidate`, `packages/core/lib/push-events.js`). On-records only. A `follow` with `on: true`. A `post` whose `replyTo` names another member's post. A `like` or `repost` with `on: true`. Never self: the author is not the recipient. The recipient must be a member (`isMember`; a throw counts as not a member). The author and the recipient must not be blocked (`isBlocked`; a throw counts as blocked). A quote, a retraction (`on: false`), a self-target and any other record type give null. `PUSH_MAX_AGE_MS` is `6 * 3600 * 1000` (6 h) and is suppression only, against the author-supplied `record.at`. A record older than 6 h is not a candidate. A future-dated record is not suppressed by age. The cursor, not the age, is what stops a replay.

**Cursor.** Each served diary has `pushNext`, set once in `_serve` (`packages/core/lib/seeder.js`) and only ever advanced. At serve time `pushFrom` defaults to `core.length`, the stored signed length. First admission passes `pushFrom: Math.max(core.length, res.length)`, because the main replica may still be empty while `res.length` is the signed length the quarantine verified, and that history must not become a push. Startup re-serves stored diaries with `{ regate: false }` and no `pushFrom`, so a restart, a re-admission and an index rebuild stay silent: an engagement-index rebuild resets `indexed` (the topic cursor `topicIndexed` is never rebuilt wholesale), never `pushNext`. The cursor is in memory and is not persisted. `onPush` is not a test hook. It is called synchronously, never awaited, and a throw is ignored. The cursor still advances, so a block is pushed at most once (at-most-once: a push is a hint, and the in-app notices stay the record). Without `onPush` the scan does not run.

Blocks the seeder did not yet hold, written while it was offline and replicated after it restarts, are growth past the cursor. They are notified only when `record.at` is younger than 6 h. History already stored at serve time stays silent on purpose. That silence is not the same rule as a drop after the cursor has moved (below).

**Target and name.** A follow skips the target check. A reply, like or repost is emitted only when `_pushTargetIsPost` reads the recipient's replica without waiting and finds a valid `post` at the named seq. The name is `_pushActorName`: the newest profile name the index holds, passed through `pushName` (leading `@` and invisible characters stripped, then `displayText`, at most 64 code points), or null.

**Event.** Exact keys `{ v: 1, kind, actor, recipient, seq, ref, name }`. `v` is 1. `kind` is one of `PUSH_KINDS`. `actor` and `recipient` are 64 lowercase hex and differ. `seq` is an integer >= 1, the block in the actor's diary. `ref` is null for a follow, otherwise a ref whose author is the recipient. `name` is null or a display-safe string of 1 to 64 code points (`validatePushEvent`).

**Transport.** `apps/cli/lib/commands/seed.js` builds a relay only when `VARANDA_PUSH_TOKEN` is set, and then sets `onPush` to `relay.send`. Without the token there is no relay, the seeder skips push, and the log line is `{ event: 'push', reason: 'desligado' }`. With it, the line is `{ event: 'push', reason: 'ligado' }`. Nothing else about the event is logged. `createPushRelay` (`apps/cli/lib/push-relay.js`) POSTs the JSON to `/push-event` on the account service. `VARANDA_ACCOUNT_HTTP` defaults to `http://127.0.0.1:8790` and must be loopback HTTP with an explicit port (`127.0.0.1`, `localhost` or `[::1]`). The header is `Authorization: Bearer` plus the token, 64 lowercase hex. There is no retry. A redirect is an error. The timeout is 5000 ms. The queue holds `maxPending` 256 events. A full queue increments `full` and drops the event. Dedupe keeps `dedupeMax` 4096 keys of `actor:seq`.

The account service (`handleRelay` in `apps/cli/lib/account/push-routes.js`) hashes the bearer with sha256 and compares it to the stored digest with `crypto.timingSafeEqual`. A missing token (`relayTokenHash` null), a bad or missing bearer, an `Origin` header, or a method other than POST is a generic 404. The body is not read. Caddy's `handle /account/*` does not proxy `/push-event`. `apps/cli/test/caddyfile.test.js` checks that `push-event` and `push-vapid` never appear outside comments. A valid event is answered 202 before delivery, so the answer never says whether a subscription exists.

**What is lost, and what is not replayed.** Once a candidate is emitted, the cursor has moved. If the account service is down, the relay does not retry and the event is lost. If the seeder queue is full (`maxPending` 256) or the account queue is full (`MAX_QUEUE` 512), the event is dropped and not replayed. Events that occur while either process is down are not replayed as history. The 6 h rule above is the only look-back, and only for blocks that arrive as growth after the seeder returns.

**Account routes** (`apps/cli/lib/account/push-routes.js`), HTTPS from the hosted page to the account service, never from the swarm:

- `GET /account/push/key` answers `{ key }` (the VAPID public key) or 503 when there is no key or the store cannot be used.
- `POST /account/push/start` takes `{ member, action }` with `action` `on` or `off` and answers `{ nonce }` (base64url of 32 bytes). The nonce lives 600 s (`NONCE_TTL_MS`) and at most 1000 nonces are kept (`MAX_NONCES`).
- `POST /account/push/finish` spends the nonce first, then checks the signature. `on` also needs the current terms accepted (`TERMS_VERSION` 4, or `termos-pendentes`) and an allowlisted endpoint. An endpoint already stored under another member moves only with the same `p256dh` and `auth` (the same browser subscription under a new identity); other keys answer `pedido-invalido`, so knowing an endpoint URL never takes someone else's notices. The per-member window is 10 finishes per 600 s.
- `POST /account/push/check` takes `{ member, endpoint, p256dh, auth }`, all required and validated as in finish, and answers `{ registered }`. It needs no proof. `registered` is true only when the member's stored row has that exact endpoint and equal `p256dh` and `auth` (compared in constant time once decoded). The browser vendor's push service knows every endpoint it issued but never sees these keys, so it cannot ask which member owns one of its devices. The window is 30 checks per 600 s per member and endpoint hash, so checks with a guessed endpoint never drain the window of the real one.

**Canonical endpoint.** An endpoint is a canonical printable-ASCII `https` URL, length 1 to 2048. `isEndpoint` (`packages/core/lib/push-proof.js`) requires code points 0x21 through 0x7e, no backslash (0x5c), no `#` and no `@` in the authority. The WHATWG parser strips tab, CR and LF, reads `\` as `/`, and turns a lone surrogate into U+FFFD, so those spellings must not hash as a second string for one request. `isPushEndpoint` (`packages/rpc/lib/methods.js`, the `push.sign` check) adds `url.protocol === 'https:'`, an empty username, password and hash, and `url.href === v`, which refuses an explicit `:443`, an uppercase host and dot segments. `endpointParam` on the finish and check routes requires `validEndpoint`, `new URL(value).href === value` and a successful `endpointHash`.

**Statement** `varanda-push-v1` (`PREFIX` in `push-proof.js`). The member's diary key signs these fields joined by LF, with no trailing LF: nonce, member, association, action (`on` or `off`), `endpointHash` (lowercase hex BLAKE2b-256 of the UTF-8 endpoint), p256dh (unpadded base64url of 65 bytes starting with 0x04), auth (unpadded base64url of 16 bytes). The endpoint URL is not in the statement, only its hash. The bytes go over HTTPS to the account service only. They are never a swarm message and never a diary block. RPC `push.sign` is browser-only (`docs/rpc.md` 5.31). The core node, the CLI and `varanda-serve` answer USAGE `assinatura disponível só no navegador`.

**Subscription and delivery.** The account service stores `endpoint`, `p256dh`, `auth` and `createdAt` in `push.json`, mode 0600, on the `account-data` volume. At most `PUSH_MAX_PER_MEMBER` = 4 rows per member, and `MAX_MEMBERS` = 100000 members. `account export` adds `pushDevices`, a count, never the endpoint. `isAllowedEndpoint` (`apps/cli/lib/account/web-push.js`) allows the hosts `fcm.googleapis.com`, `updates.push.services.mozilla.com` and `web.push.apple.com`, and the suffixes `.push.apple.com`, `.push.services.mozilla.com` and `.notify.windows.com`. https only, no userinfo, no hash, port empty or 443, no IPv4 and no bracket host. The message is RFC 8291 `aes128gcm` (VAPID, RFC 8292). TTL `PUSH_TTL_S` = 86400. The `Topic` header, sent in clear, is an HMAC-SHA256 of `actor:seq` under a random secret of the account process, never a bare hash of those public values. The vendor sees ciphertext and the device address only. Every push plaintext is padded to `PUSH_PAD_TO` = 1024 bytes (RFC 8188 padding: the 0x02 delimiter, then zero octets), so its length shows neither the kind nor the name. Delivery is immediate, so the push service can still correlate a push's timing with a public diary record (a documented limit). The sentence is `pushBody` (`apps/cli/lib/account/push-text.js`): title `Varanda`, and "{name} começou a seguir você", "respondeu ao seu post", "curtiu seu post" or "republicou seu post". With no display name the verified @nome is used, written without `@`; with neither, "Alguém". The fallback, when the payload is not valid, is `Você tem um aviso novo na Varanda`. `PUSH_BODY_MAX` is 120 code points. No quote and no post text.

Delivery keeps a seen set of `actor:seq`, FIFO, at most `MAX_SEEN` 10000. The cooldown is `keyedWindow(1, 600 * 1000)` per actor, recipient, kind and ref, and logs `descartado`; for a reply the key also carries the reply's seq, so every reply is its own notice and only a like, repost or follow repeated inside 600 s is dropped. The cooldown runs before any budget, and the budgets are charged only when the recipient has a subscription, so a repeat never spends them. Then the per actor and recipient window is `keyedWindow(10, 3600 * 1000)`, 10 per hour, so one actor cannot use up a recipient's budget, and the recipient cap is `keyedWindow(60, 3600 * 1000)`, 60 per hour; both log `{ event: 'push', reason: 'teto' }`. The queue is `MAX_QUEUE` 512 with `MAX_IN_FLIGHT` 8. Overflow increments `dropped` and is lost. A vendor status 404 or 410 (`gone`) removes the row. Logs are only `{ event, reason }`.

**Worker.** The page asks the active service worker whether it speaks push: one message `{ type: 'varanda-push-ping' }` (`apps/app/src/pushClient.web.js`). `apps/app/scripts/sw.js` answers `{ source: 'varanda-sw', push: 1 }` only for that exact one-key message. An older worker does not answer. Until every window of the old release is closed, turning notices on stays blocked, with `PUSH_RELOAD` ("Feche todas as janelas do Varanda e abra de novo para ligar os avisos."); turning them off stays allowed. There is no `pushsubscriptionchange` handler. On iOS the sheet reads `getPushState` when it opens, so the tap's first await is `pushManager.subscribe()` (Safari spends the user activation on any earlier await).

**Invite drop notice (account-originated).** With `VARANDA_INVITE_DROPS=1` the account service itself sends one more payload, `{ v: 1, kind: 'invite-drop' }`, exactly two keys, to every stored subscription of one member when a weekly invite opens (`notifyInvite` in `apps/cli/lib/account/push-routes.js`, driven by `apps/cli/lib/account/invite-drops.js`). It carries no name, no text and no member key: the title ("Chegou um convite pra sua varanda") and the body live in `apps/app/scripts/sw.js` (tag `varanda-invite-drop`), so a payload can never put words of its own on the lock screen, and a worker that predates it shows the generic fallback. A click opens `/?aviso=convite` (or, with a window already focused, posts `{ type: 'varanda-invite-open' }`). It does not come from the seeder: `PUSH_KINDS` and the relay (`/push-event`, `validatePushEvent`) never emit or accept it. It goes only to members who accepted the current terms version, to all of a member's devices or to none, and its jobs take at most a quarter of the queue (`INVITE_QUEUE_MAX`) and only while the queue is under half full. Each job carries a deadline, the end of the day's window at 21:00 Brasília: past it the job is dropped unsent, and before it the TTL is the seconds left, never above the usual one. That shorter TTL is visible to the browser's push service (the payload is padded like every notice, the TTL header is not), so that service can tell an invite notice from a social one; it learns nothing else, and the trade keeps a phone that is offline at 20:40 from showing the notice at night.

### Reports and operator requests (B3; not part of the swarm)

A report is neither a diary record nor a message on any swarm channel: it travels over HTTPS from the hosted web page to the account service (`POST /account/report`, one signed statement per report) and stays there; no node, seeder or follower ever receives it. The blocklist snapshot bytes, its payload and the channel `'varanda/blocklist/1'` are unchanged, and the snapshot still carries no reason, no report and no e-mail. Sixteen kinds of signed statement exist (a report has the variants below), all domain-separated by a prefix that no other signature of this protocol uses and built from LF-joined fields with no trailing LF:

- **Report** (`packages/core/lib/report.js`, signed by the reporter's diary key, RPC `report.sign`, `docs/rpc.md` 5.20): `'varanda-report-v1'`, nonce, member, association, target, seq (decimal, `''` for an account report), reason, `digest(text)`, `digest(quote)`, where `digest` is the lowercase hex BLAKE2b-256 of the UTF-8 bytes. `text` (at most 280 code points) and `quote` (at most `MAX_TEXT`, only with a seq) must be well formed (`isWellFormed()`: a lone surrogate and U+FFFD share their UTF-8 bytes, so the digest would not bind the exact string). `quote` is what the reporter supplied, and the report itself never verifies it against the diary (the account service does not open the seeder's RocksDB, section 1).
- **Listing report** (`packages/core/lib/report.js` `signListingReport`, signed by the reporter's diary key, RPC `report.sign` with `kind: 'listing'`, `docs/rpc.md` 5.20): `'varanda-listing-report-v1'`, then exactly the eight lines of the report v1 statement: nonce, member, association, target, seq, reason, `digest(text)`, `digest(quote)`. `target` is the seller's diary key and `seq` (required, never `''`) the listing record, so a listing report is never an account report. It carries no writer, group or anexo: the listing sits on the seeder, where the moderator reads it. The prefix is its own, so a post report never verifies as a listing report and back, and the account service can route the two apart; `verifyListingReport` never throws. The `quote` is the reporter's word only, as for a post.
- **Operator request** (`packages/core/lib/mod-request.js`, signed by the association-certified moderator key, `mod apply`): `'varanda-mod-request-v1'`, origin, association, moderator, nonce, op (`'pull'` or `'ack'`), hex BLAKE2b-256 of the exact body bytes. The origin is in the statement because the account service never sees the real Host header (the Caddyfile rewrites it), so a signature for one deployment cannot replay against another. The `pull` body is exactly `{}`; the `ack` body is the compact JSON `{"changeIds":[...sorted...],"snapshot":{...the 9 snapshot keys in order...}}`, which the server rebuilds from what it parsed. Both nonces are base64url, 43 to 128 characters.
- **Appeal** (`packages/core/lib/appeal.js`, signed by a blocked member's diary key, RPC `appeal.sign`, `docs/rpc.md` 5.99): `'varanda-appeal-v1'`, nonce, member, association, `digest(text)`, with no date (the account service records its own) and no target: an appeal is always about the signer's own key. `text` is at most `MAX_TEXT` code points and may be empty. Sent over HTTPS to the account service only (`POST /account/appeal/start`, then `POST /account/appeal`) by a linked member (a phone link, or an earlier Google link that stays; the member Google sign-in is retired and an old build gets 410 `versao-antiga`); the service answers `nao-bloqueado` (403) unless the key is in the published list or has a pending block, and `limite-recursos` (429) for a second appeal inside 30 days. A retry of the open appeal answers the same row (`duplicate: true`). The panel lists it under Recursos for moderators other than the one who decided the block (and never the appellant); accepting appends an `unblock` change like the panel's Desbloquear, which leaves the list only when the operator's `mod apply` signs and the operator confirms it (an unblock never goes out with `--yes`); denying only closes the case. The snapshot, its payload and the swarm channels do not change.
- **Terms acceptance** (`packages/core/lib/terms.js`, signed by the member's diary key, RPC `terms.sign`, `docs/rpc.md` 5.29): `'varanda-terms-v1'`, nonce, member, association, version (decimal), with no date (the account service records its own). Sent over HTTPS to `POST /account/terms/start` and `POST /account/terms/finish` only for a linked member, and kept only in the account service's `terms.json`; never on a swarm channel, never in the blocklist snapshot, and it carries no e-mail.
- **Signup-invite request** (`packages/core/lib/invite.js`, signed by the member's diary key, RPC `invite.sign`, `docs/rpc.md` 5.30): `'varanda-invite-v1'`, action (`'issue'` or `'summary'`), nonce, member, association, with no date (the account service records its own). Sent over HTTPS to the account service only (`POST /account/invites/start`, `POST /account/invites/finish` and `POST /account/invites/summary`) by a linked member asking for one signup invite (`issue`) or reading their own weekly allowance (`summary`, which mints nothing and answers `{ mode, perWeek, opened, used, remaining, resetsAt }`; the action is a signed line, so a signature for one never verifies as the other); the account service keeps only a SHA-256 of the issued code in its `invites.json`, the statement never travels on a swarm channel, and it carries no e-mail, name, `sub` or key of the invited person.
- **Invite card** (`packages/core/lib/invite-card.js`, signed by the member's diary key, RPC `inviteCard.sign`, `docs/rpc.md` 5.101): `'varanda-invite-card-v1'`, code, design, expires (decimal), nameDigest, messageDigest, member, association, where the digests are the lowercase hex BLAKE2b-256 of the UTF-8 bytes of the name and of the message, always computed by the signer. It is the greeting a member attaches to a signup invite link: it travels only in the link's URL fragment (`#c1.` plus base64url), never to the account service and never on a swarm channel, and it carries no e-mail. A reader checks it with `inviteCard.verify` (5.102); it proves only that this key wrote this greeting for this code and association until `expires`.
- **Push opt-in** (`packages/core/lib/push-proof.js`, signed by the member's diary key, RPC `push.sign`, `docs/rpc.md` 5.31): `'varanda-push-v1'`. The fields, the canonical endpoint and the routes are under "Push relay" above. HTTPS to the account service only, never a swarm channel and never a diary block.
- **Age-verification request** (`packages/core/lib/age.js`, signed by the member's diary key, RPC `age.sign`, `docs/rpc.md` 5.32): `'varanda-age-v1'`, action (`'session'` or `'result'`), nonce, member, association, with no date (the account service records its own). Sent over HTTPS to the account service only (`POST /account/age/session` and `POST /account/age/result`) by a hosted member asking to start an age verification or to read its result. It proves only that the member asked, never an age: the account service keeps only the age band (with the method and its own date) in its `age.json`, never a birth date, a document or a provider session, and the statement never travels on a swarm channel.
- **Phone-verification request** (`packages/core/lib/phone.js`, signed by the member's diary key, RPC `phone.sign`, `docs/rpc.md` 5.115): `'varanda-phone-v1'`, action (`'send'`, `'check'` or `'status'`), nonce, member, association, with no date (the account service records its own). Sent over HTTPS to the account service only by a hosted member asking to send a code, check a code, or read the status of that verification. It proves only that the member asked: the statement carries no phone number and no code, and it is never a swarm message, a diary block or a certificate field.
- **Age-verified attestation** (`packages/core/lib/age-proof.js`, signed by the account service's issuer key, not by a member): `'varanda-age-verified-v1'`, member, date, association, issuer, issuerDate, issuerSignature. Format and verification under "Age attestations" (section 3). Sent over HTTPS by the account service to the member's own client only, kept only in that client's node storage, never a diary block and never a swarm message; it carries no age band.
- **Age-since attestation** (`packages/core/lib/age-proof.js`, signed by the account service's issuer key, not by a member): `'varanda-age-since-v1'`, since, date, association, issuer, issuerDate, issuerSignature. Format and verification under "Age attestations" (section 3). Same transport and storage as the age-verified attestation.
- **Phone-verified attestation** (`packages/core/lib/phone-proof.js`, signed by the account service's issuer key, not by a member): `'varanda-phone-verified-v1'`, member, date, association, issuer, issuerDate, issuerSignature. Format and verification under "Phone attestations" (section 3). Sent over HTTPS by the account service to the member's own client only, kept only in that client's node storage, never a diary block and never a swarm message; it carries no phone number.
- **Phone-since attestation** (`packages/core/lib/phone-proof.js`, signed by the account service's issuer key, not by a member): `'varanda-phone-since-v1'`, since, date, association, issuer, issuerDate, issuerSignature. Format and verification under "Phone attestations" (section 3). Same transport and storage as the phone-verified attestation. It never vouches for a certificate: it only marks that the association requires a verified phone, and it carries no phone number.
- **Private message report** (`packages/core/lib/report.js` `signDmReport`, signed by the reporter's diary key, RPC `dm.reportSign`, `docs/rpc.md` 5.81): `'varanda-dm-report-v1'`, nonce, member, association, target, reason, `digest(text)`, `digest(quote)`. The quote is the plaintext of the chosen message and `target` the sender's diary key; nothing binds the sender's signature, so the quote is the reporter's word only (section 11, "Reports").
- **Recovery statement** (`packages/core/lib/recovery-proof.js`, signed by the member's diary key, RPC `recovery.sign`, `docs/rpc.md` 5.90): `'varanda-recovery-v1'`, action (`'put'`, `'remove'` or `'list'`), nonce, member, association, kind, lookupId, blobHash, with no date (the account service records its own). Fields and routes under "Files and replicas" (section 1). HTTPS to the account service only (`/account/recovery/*`), never a swarm channel and never a diary block; it carries no secret, no e-mail and never the blob itself.
- **Report with an anexo** (an image the reporter attaches, `packages/core/lib/report.js`): one more line, the lowercase hex SHA-256 of the attachment bytes, after `digest(quote)`, under a prefix of its own, so every older statement still verifies and none crosses another. A private message signs `'varanda-dm-report-v2'`, nonce, member, association, target, reason, `digest(text)`, `digest(quote)`, anexo (RPC `dm.reportSign` with `anexo`), and its `quote` may then be `''` (a media-only message). A group message signs `'varanda-report-v3'`, the eleven `'varanda-report-v2'` fields, anexo (RPC `report.sign` with `writer`, `group` and `anexo`). A diary post or an account report never carries an anexo. The node signs the digest it is given and never sees the bytes; the account service hashes the bytes it receives and verifies the statement over that digest, so another image fails as `assinatura-invalida`. Without an anexo the v1 and v2 statements are signed unchanged.

When AI triage is on, the account service reads the author's signed post from the local seeder, not from `quote` and not over any swarm channel: `GET /moderation-post/<D>/<seq>` on the seeder's HTTP listener (section 7, "Seeder moderation-post route"), with `Authorization: Bearer` and `VARANDA_TRIAGE_TOKEN`. The answer is the raw blocks (block 0 and the reported block, base64). The account service re-validates them: the block-0 certificate, including issuers revoked by the list; that the requested block is a post; and that the text is well-formed UTF-16. There is no new swarm channel. The blocklist snapshot bytes, its payload and the channel `'varanda/blocklist/1'` stay unchanged.

A listing report (kind `listing`) is routed by the account service apart from every other kind. `POST /account/report` accepts `kind: 'listing'` with `seq` required and no `writer`, `group`, `peer` or anexo (`pedido-invalido`), verifies the statement with `verifyListingReport` (`assinatura-invalida` otherwise) and stores a row of kind `listing` in `moderation.json` (a v2 row, the version unchanged: an older binary fails closed on a file holding one). An open report of the same kind with the same reporter, target and seq is a duplicate, so a post and a listing that share a seq never merge. A listing report is human only: AI triage never reads the listing, and the worker marks the row terminal with the code `anuncio` (and a report whose target turns out not to be a post with `nao-e-publicacao`), so no retry and no suggestion ever exists for it. The panel lists it as `anúncio`, with a filter chip, a link to the public page (`<origin>/f/<D>/<seq>`) and a `Ver anúncio` action that asks `GET /account/mod/api/reports/<id>/anuncio`, which reads the seeder's folded CURRENT version of the listing through the token-gated `GET /moderation-listing/<D>/<seq>` (exactly `{ title, text, priceLabel, city, uf, kind, status }`, each a string or null; `{ ausente: true }` when gone; `{ erro }` when the seeder cannot answer) and shows it as text only, marked "versão atual; o anúncio pode ter mudado". Both decisions exist for it, `descartar` or `bloquear`, and `bloquear` blocks the WHOLE diary of the target (the panel says so), never one listing: it follows the rules of an account block without a group (the local seeder must confirm a served diary). The view is never read by `decide`.

The server answers `pull` with exactly `{ changes: [{ id, op, member, at, actor }], published: { snapshot } }` (`actor` is `'ia'`, `'titular'` for an Excluir conta request below, or `'pessoa'`; at most 1000 changes, oldest first). An operator CLI from before `actor` rejects that shape, and one from before `'titular'` rejects a pull that holds one, so the operator program is updated together with the server. The server takes the signed snapshot back in `ack`; what the server holds as "published" is a mirror it verified (`verifySnapshot`, the moderator certificate and `acceptSnapshot`), not a second source of truth. Nodes learn a block only through the snapshot the operator publishes to the seeder, with the propagation rules above.

### Account deletion (Excluir conta; account service, not part of the swarm)

A member deletes the account from the app (`apps/cli/lib/account/delete-routes.js`). Nothing new travels on a swarm channel and the blocklist snapshot bytes are unchanged: the diary leaves the network through an ordinary block.

- `POST /account/delete/start` `{ member }` -> `{ nonce, username, passkeys, scheduled }`: no proof, a linked member only. A deleted key answers 409 `conta-excluida`, an unlinked one 403 `nao-vinculado`, and a member who owns a page that is neither closed nor moved answers 409 `paginas-ativas` (closing a page needs the owner's signed `page-close`, section 12). The nonce is 32 random bytes, base64url, single-use, in memory for 600 s; its 32 bytes are also the WebAuthn challenge of the passkey check. `passkeys` lists the credential ids whose public keys the service holds for the member ("Passkey enrolment" below), `scheduled` the date of a deletion already waiting, or `null`.
- `POST /account/delete/finish` `{ member, nonce, signature, assertion?, wait? }`: `signature` is the account-link statement (`packages/core/lib/account-link.js`, RPC `account.sign`, `docs/rpc.md` 5.18) at phase `'delete'`: `'varanda-account-link-v1'`, `'delete'`, nonce, member, the member's current @nome (the one `start` returned, which the person typed), association and `''`, joined by LF. It is charged on a per-member window after it verified. A rename since `start` answers `expirado`. Then:
  - `assertion` = `{ credentialId, clientDataJSON, authenticatorData, signature }` (base64url, each at most 2048 characters) of a passkey whose key the service holds: it is verified before anything changes (`apps/cli/lib/account/webauthn.js` `verifyAssertion`): `clientDataJSON` parses as a JSON object with `type` `'webauthn.get'`, `challenge` equal to the nonce, `origin` one of the allowed origins, no `crossOrigin` other than `false` and no `topOrigin`; the authenticator data carries no attested credential, its rpIdHash is SHA-256 of the rpId and UP and UV are set; the signature over `authenticatorData || SHA-256(clientDataJSON)` verifies under the stored key (ES256 with a DER signature, or RS256 PKCS#1 v1.5); and the signature counter, when the stored or the new one is not 0, is greater than the stored one (synced passkeys always report 0). Any failure answers 403 `passkey-invalida` (the log carries only the reason token, `passkey-<motivo>`) and changes nothing; success runs the deletion now (steps 1 to 3 below) and answers `{ deleted }`. The rpId is the host of the service's public origin (`VARANDA_PUBLIC_ORIGIN`, the installation hostname the app uses as rp.id); the allowed origins are that origin (the web, the PWA and the iOS app) and, when `VARANDA_ANDROID_CERT_SHA256` is set (the value `assetlinks.json` publishes), `android:apk-key-hash:` followed by the base64url of that SHA-256 (the Android app).
  - Otherwise, `wait: true` schedules the deletion: `{ scheduled }` answers the date, 7 days ahead (`WAIT_MS`). This is the path of every account linked before enrolment existed, of a creation whose platform gave no attestation object, and of a person whose device passkey is not the stored one (an extra passkey, a passkey lost). Asking again keeps the first date; a schedule write that failed answers 503 `resultado-incerto` (it may have landed, and the app reads the status). A push notice `{ v: 1, kind: 'delete-scheduled' }` goes to the member's devices when notices are on (the worker owns its text: "Pedido de exclusão da sua conta"), and `GET /account/status` answers the date to every device (below). The hourly deletion sweep runs each deletion whose date came through steps 1 to 3; a member who meanwhile came to own a live page is skipped (logged `agendada-paginas-ativas`) and retried at the next sweep. An `assertion` of a stored passkey still deletes at once while a deletion waits.
  - Neither: 403 `passkey-invalida`.
  The deletion, now or at its date, runs in this order: (1) a `block` change of the member's diary with `by: 'titular'` and no report is queued in `moderation.json` (`requestDeletion`; a key already in the published list, or with an owner block already pending, adds nothing; a key blocked only through someone else's pending change, a moderator's or the AI's, still gets its own owner block, so the panel can never drop the deletion and `mod apply --yes` never holds it as an AI block), so a refusal here (`indisponivel` without a moderation desk, `lotado` when the list is full) deletes nothing; (2) the directory commits the journal line `{ op: 'delete', member, at }` ("Files and replicas"); (3) the other stores are purged (`apps/cli/lib/account/deletion.js`). A failure after (1) answers 503 `resultado-incerto`. The answer is `{ deleted: 'pendente' | 'concluida' }`; a finish for a key already deleted answers the same, so a retry after an unknown outcome ends well.
- `GET /account/status?member=` answers a deleted key `{ linked: false, deleted }`, `deleted` being `'concluida'` once the published snapshot the desk mirrors holds the key (the operator's `mod apply` signed it and the seeder received it) and `'pendente'` before; never the date. A linked member with a deletion waiting gets the usual answer plus `scheduledDeletion` (its ISO date); member keys are public, so anyone can read that a deletion is waiting, as anyone can read `deleted` afterwards.
- `POST /account/delete/cancel/start` `{ member }` -> `{ nonce, username, scheduled }` (a linked member, the same nonce table, marked as a cancel nonce) and `POST /account/delete/cancel/finish` `{ member, nonce, signature }` -> `{ cancelled }`: the account-link statement at phase `'cancel'` over that nonce and the current @nome. A delete nonce is never a cancel nonce and the two statements never verify for each other. `cancelled` is `false` when nothing was waiting.
- Passkey enrolment (`apps/cli/lib/account/passkey-routes.js`): right after the account link, the app sends the creation's attestation object of the passkey that created the identity. `POST /account/passkey/start` `{ member }` -> `{ nonce }` (a linked member; single-use, in memory, 600 s) and `POST /account/passkey/add` `{ member, nonce, signature, credentialId, attestationObject }` -> `{ added }`: `signature` is the account-link statement at phase `'passkey'` over the nonce and the current @nome, with `sub` = the lowercase hex SHA-256 of the attestation object's bytes; it is charged per member after it verified. The key is read from the attestation object (`keyFromAttestation`: a CBOR map of exactly `fmt`, `attStmt` and `authData`, at most 2048 bytes; the authenticator data's rpIdHash, the attested credential id equal to `credentialId`, UP, and a COSE key that is EC2 P-256 with ES256 or RSA of 2048 to 4096 bits with RS256, imported by node:crypto); the attestation statement is not checked (creations use attestation `'none'`). It is accepted only while the member has no stored key AND `linkedAt` is at most 1 hour old (`LINK_WINDOW_MS`): that is the only moment a key is trusted on first use. Refusals: 403 `passkey-invalida` (the attestation does not parse or match), 409 `passkey-registrada` (another key is stored; a stored key is never replaced), 409 `vinculo-antigo` (the window passed). The same credential again answers `{ added: false }`.
- `<account dataDir>/passkeys.json` (`apps/cli/lib/account/passkey-store.js`) holds the stored keys and the waiting deletions. One sidecar like `recovery.json`, mode `0o600`, replaced whole through `writeJsonAtomic`, never part of `directory.json` or its journal. The shape is exactly `{ v: 1, seq, members, removed }`: `members` maps a member key to `{ keys: [{ credentialId, alg, spki, signCount, addedAt }], hold: null | { at, runAt }, gen }` (at most 4 keys, `alg` -7 or -257, `spki` the lowercase hex DER SubjectPublicKeyInfo); `removed` maps a deleted member to `{ removedAt, gen }`, pruned after 30 days. Every change to a member takes `gen = ++seq` for the whole record, and a merge keeps, per member, the record or tombstone with the higher gen (ties keep memory), so a stale copy can never bring back a cancelled deletion. Validation is total, as for `recovery.json`; an unusable file makes Excluir conta and the enrolment answer 503 `indisponivel`.
- The panel shows a `titular` change as asked by the person. `POST pending/<id>/cancel` of it, or of any other change of a deleted key, answers 409 `conta-excluida`, and so does `POST unblock` of a deleted key or of a key with an owner block pending (the store refuses that too, also in the window where the directory write failed). Only the operator's own `mod unblock`, at a terminal, can undo it. `mod apply` prints such a block as `bloquear <key> (exclusão pedida pela pessoa)`, and `--yes` signs it like any human block.
- What the service deletes: the link (sub, e-mail, certificate, records, invite id), `terms.json`, `age.json`, `push.json`, `recovery.json` (each entry leaves the store's usual tombstone with the public key, 30 days), the invites the member issued (`invites.json`), the surprise-invite marks (`invite-drops.json`), the encrypted seen blob (`seen/<member>`, through the store's compare-and-swap; a conflict waits for the next sweep) and the stored passkey keys with any waiting deletion (`passkeys.json`, a 30-day tombstone with the public key). What stays: the deleted row (public key and date) and every @nome the key held, burned (`usedNames`); reports and decisions in `moderation.json` (retention open, issue #38); the access logs, if they apply. The deletion sweep runs after every request, at every start and every hour, so a store that could not be purged at once (unavailable, a failed write, or rows a stale writer's union merge brought back) is purged later; logs carry counts only.
- A page delegate (section 12) whose diary was deleted is refused (`nao-vinculado`) before the block reaches it. The age routes refuse a deleted key (`POST /account/age/session` and `/result` answer 409 `conta-excluida`; a provider session opened before the deletion is closed there), so it never pays for, nor shows anything to, the provider and never gets a band again. Pages where the member was only an admin keep the key in their roster until the owner removes it (that needs the owner's signed roster); the service refuses every action of the deleted key at once.
- On the device, after a deletion that ran now, the app wipes the account as `identity.reset()` does and keeps only `{ v: 1, member, at }` (public key and date) to show, at Entrar, when the deletion completed. A scheduled deletion wipes nothing: every signed-in device shows the date with a way to cancel, and once it runs each device meets the notice below. Another device still signed in with the same passkey meets a notice when it reads `/account/status`, and erases the account there. A diary key cannot be revoked (the passkey derives it again), so the account service refuses that key for good: `start` answers `conta-excluida` and the directory never links it, reserves it or makes it a page.
- Limits: a key is trusted on first use, at sign-up: someone who controls the person's device during the hour after the link could enrol a forged authenticator instead. An unlocked session holds the diary key, so it can always schedule a deletion; the person then sees it on every signed-in device (and in a push, when notices are on) and can cancel it for 7 days, but a person who opens no device in that time loses the account. Extra passkeys are not enrolled, so a person whose only stored passkey is gone waits the 7 days. iOS may give no attestation object at creation (`rawAttestationObject` is optional), and the iOS origin is expected to be `https://<rpId>`: both are device checks (`docs/verification.md` section 116). The list holds at most 4096 keys (`MAX_BLOCKED`), and every deletion keeps one; copies on modified apps and devices that ignore the list are not reached; `directoryFloor` (`apps/cli/lib/account/age-check-state.js`) is read from the live links only, so after a restart it no longer counts a deleted member who had no age band, which matters only until the block reaches that diary.

### Enforcement: the `'blocked'` and `'revoked-issuer'` reasons at every gate

The node's current snapshot gives two sets, `blockedSet` (diary keys) and `revokedIssuerSet` (issuer keys). Both are applied wherever a key or its block-0 certificate is judged; the reasons are `'blocked'` and `'revoked-issuer'` (reason master list, section 5). Both are moderation outcomes, not misbehaviour: a peer re-announcing such a diary is not counted as a bad announce.

- **Seeder.** `start()` loads the snapshot under the lock; a lost list holds new admissions (section 1). Step 3 of startup (re-gate of every listed log) takes a blocked key down the fork path (rejected, purged, un-listed) and a revoked issuer through the local verdict. `announce` for a blocked key is answered `rejected` / `'blocked'` before anything is queued or fetched, and a block 0 from a revoked issuer is `'revoked-issuer'`. Admissions in flight re-check the list after each await (quarantine fetch, gate hook, `persist`, `openReplica`): a key found blocked before persisting is rejected; after `openReplica` it is closed, purged, un-listed and rejected. On adoption `_drop(key, 'blocked')` runs for every served blocked key, a moderation generation counter makes any gate verdict computed before the adoption re-run, and a newly revoked issuer re-runs the local gate on every served replica (a block 0 that cannot be read drops the diary). `stats()` has `blocked` (size of the blocked set) and `blocklistSeq` (`0` without a snapshot); `Seeder.status` has `blocklist: { seq, date } | null` and `seed status` prints `lista de bloqueio: nenhuma` or `lista de bloqueio: seq <n> de <data>`.
- **Member (`runSync`, `member.js`).** At startup and after every adoption, every stored replica of a blocked key other than the own key is purged whether or not it is followed (a failed follow entry or a quota failure does not keep it), and when issuers are revoked every stored core whose block 0 carries a revoked issuer is purged. The follow entry becomes skipped with reason `'blocked'` / `'revoked-issuer'` and stays in `follows.json`, so an unblock needs no new follow. A blocked key is never advertised, fetched or served, and its block-0 verdict is `'blocked'`. `log.read` of another blocked author -> NOT_FOUND `sem dados locais para este diário`; `follow.add` of a blocked key -> USAGE `autor bloqueado`; `cert.add` refuses a certificate from a revoked issuer; the feed skips both reasons. The own diary is never purged: the node warns once (`aviso: sua identidade está bloqueada pela associação`, `aviso: o emissor do seu certificado foi revogado pela associação`).
- **Web member.** The same rules over its stored replicas. It attaches the channel on its one seeder link before admission but ignores incoming snapshots until the seeder's `hello` verified the link, and at verification announces its own so the seeder re-sends anything newer; a regate answering `'blocked'` or `'revoked-issuer'` is a moderation skip (purge), not a divergent block 0. Offline, the PWA enforces the snapshot stored in its kv; it receives a newer one only through the seeder.
- **`seed drop`** is unchanged: a drop is not a block (section 7).

### Operator response to a stolen issuer key

The issuer key lives in the account service's volume on a public host. If it may have leaked: (1) `mod revoke-issuer <old issuer hex>` publishes a snapshot whose `revokedIssuers` holds it; every up-to-date node then refuses the v2 certificates and `username` records it signed, purges and skips those diaries, and the seeder drops them; (2) create a new key with `account key`, certify it with `assoc certify-issuer`, `account cert add`, restart the service; (3) the affected members link again (their old v2 certificates stop working). Re-issuing the old certificates is not implemented (a later PR may add it). Individual member certificates are NOT revocable: block the diary key instead (`mod block`).

### Golden vectors (`packages/core/test/cert.test.js`, `packages/core/test/blocklist.test.js`)

Keys from hypercore-crypto `keyPair(seed)`: association seed = 32 bytes `0x01`, issuer seed = 32 bytes `0x02`; member = `'ab'` x 32; issuer `date` = `2026-10-24T00:00:00.000Z`; member certificate and username record `date` = `2026-10-24T12:00:00.000Z`; username `ana_maria`. Ed25519 signatures are deterministic.

- association = `8a88e3dd7409f195fd52db2d3cba5d72ca6709bf1d94121bf3748801b40f6f5c`
- issuer = `8139770ea87d175f56a35466c34c7ecccb8d8a91b4ee37a25df60f5b8fc9b394`
- issuer certificate `signature` (= `issuerSignature` below) = `7e06f2e675e84ea405dba956c8bb54f3a2e070c4bfb2803a067f1b9215fae89a76b4cb2a8b1137c1fec106e14cf25a390a58f1e7a0757d762c7cb1c9d9faf50d`
- member certificate v2 `signature` = `3e9041b0a15d3ab5066697e6998be04a0a6599dee997649c51ff7420a80238742b258fffc06c29861c65a18bbad04b792b6bb31d9621f2b530319c40d6777605`
- username record `signature` = `43d469145321e09697cbd68cad520105d594941d173f236c35ca6e9460f2a67d4541990b253f3a0c4544900cb72bf15b8e490b17604081826c88ba4967c42500`
- moderator (seed = 32 bytes `0x03`) = `ed4928c628d1c2c6eae90338905995612959273a5c63f93636c14614ac8737d1`; moderator certificate `date` = `2026-10-24T00:00:00.000Z`, `signature` (= `moderatorSignature` below) = `00c028052fd43e7518b5825298baf5ef2d194670421b6422c4211f3b1d3f9a2839b6cec54e628c416c8367191a69628bc2e3db5d4263781df11de781fbe9920d`
- blocklist snapshot `seq` = `1`, `date` = `2026-10-24T12:00:00.000Z`, `blocked` = [`'ab'` x 32, `'cd'` x 32], `revokedIssuers` = [the issuer above], signed by that moderator: `signature` = `b9b1a5e572534604be3a2708153777b74e6df23e054f22ae874dd3d13ddf80a7bc761491a9529a36514bd6ec2c5f11b069c78065420be2eafc40cf37b9e7960f`

Size: the largest legal v2 certificate (two 27-character expanded-year dates) is under `MAX_CERT_BYTES = 4096` (`packages/rpc/lib/limits.js`) and its block 0, like the largest username record, is under `MAX_BLOCK_BYTES = 16384` (`packages/core/test/records.test.js`).

### Seeder certificate (R5.2)

```json
{
  "v": 1,
  "seeder": "<hex64 Noise public key>",
  "date": "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z",
  "association": "<hex64>",
  "signature": "<128 lowercase hex>"
}
```

`SeederCertificate` has exactly these 5 keys: `v` (`1`), `seeder` (hex64, the seeder's Noise static public key from `seeder-key.json`), `date` (canonical ISO, `isCanonicalIso`), `association` (hex64), `signature` (128 lowercase hex, hypercore-crypto `sign(payload, associationSecretKey)`).

`payload` = UTF-8 bytes of `'varanda-seeder-v1'`, `seeder`, `date`, `association` joined by a single LF (no trailing LF, hex lowercase). The prefix differs from `'varanda-cert-v1'`, so a member signature is never a valid seeder signature and vice versa.

- `seederCertPayload(cert)` -> Buffer.
- `createSeederCert({ seeder, date = new Date().toISOString() }, associationKeyPair)` -> SeederCertificate; TypeError unless `seeder` is hex64 in any case (stored lowercase), `date` is canonical and the secretKey is 64 bytes.
- `validateSeederCertShape(cert)` -> `null | 'malformed'`.
- `verifySeederCert(cert, associationHex)` -> `{ ok: true } | { ok: false, reason }` with reasons in order `'malformed'`, `'untrusted-association'`, `'bad-signature'`.
- `seederCertForKey(cert, noiseHex, associationHex)` inserts `'seeder-mismatch'` (`cert.seeder !== normalizeHex(noiseHex)`) after `'malformed'`.
- All four never throw (R4.3).

The association issues it with `assoc certify-seeder` (`issueSeederCertificate`, which appends the `seeders.jsonl` line). Members verify it against the Noise-authenticated `socket.remotePublicKey` of the connection that carried it (section 6, R5.3 c).

No expiry in phase 0 for any certificate (member v1 and v2, issuer, moderator, seeder) or username record. Revocation (B2) exists only for issuer keys, through the blocklist snapshot's `revokedIssuers` (a stolen issuer key could otherwise mint v2 certificates and usernames forever; an expiry alone would not help, since a stolen key can back-date); diaries are kept out through its `blocked` list. Member v1 certificates, seeder and moderator certificates have no revocation (a compromised moderator is replaced by certifying a new one with a later `date`). The age check is a stub: the operator of `assoc certify` asserts the age band (TODO phase 1: real age-verification vendor). The association stores only (public key, age band, method, date) for members and (seeder key, date) for seeders.

## 4. Network

- Rendezvous topic = hypercore-crypto `hash(b4a.from('varanda/rendezvous/v1:' + associationHex, 'utf8'))`, 32 bytes. Every member and seeder of an association joins it with `{ server: true, client: true }`.
- Members ALSO join their diary's `discoveryKey` and every followed key's `discoveryKey`; seeders ALSO join every admitted replica's `discoveryKey` and every bound media core's `discoveryKey` (at startup and immediately after each admission). Media core keys never travel on the admission channel (section 5 "Seeder media admission").
- For topics joined before the first flush: `await discovery.flushed()` for each, then `await swarm.flush()`.
- Hyperswarm keeps one connection per peer pair regardless of how many topics match.

### Bootstrap and DHT binding (R5.11)

`--bootstrap host:port` (repeatable) or env `VARANDA_BOOTSTRAP` (comma-separated `host:port`). Unset = the public DHT (manual use only). A malformed value is a usage error (`bootstrap inválido <entry>`) and never falls back to the public DHT. Any `--bootstrap` flag wins over `VARANDA_BOOTSTRAP`. A bootstrap source that is PRESENT but yields no entry (an empty string, only commas or spaces, e.g. `VARANDA_BOOTSTRAP=` or `--bootstrap ''`) is also a usage error, `bootstrap inválido (vazio)`: silently falling back to the public DHT would move private-network traffic onto the internet; only an absent flag and an unset variable mean "public DHT". `bootstrap` is an array of `'host:port'` strings (HyperDHT also accepts the `{ host, port }` objects that `hyperdht/testnet` returns).

`createSwarm({ bootstrap, keyPair, host } = {})`: `const dhtOpts = bootstrap ? { bootstrap, port: [0, 0] } : {}`; `if (host) { dhtOpts.host = host; if (host === '127.0.0.1' || host === '::1') dhtOpts.firewalled = false }`; `return new Hyperswarm({ keyPair, dht: new HyperDHT(dhtOpts) })` (`keyPair` undefined lets hyperswarm generate one; the seeder passes its loaded `seeder-key.json` pair). With a private bootstrap the DHT therefore binds an ephemeral port (hyperdht rewrites `port: 0` to 49737; the range `[0, 0]` has no candidates and falls through to `bind(0)`); without a bootstrap (public DHT, manual use) hyperdht defaults stay. `firewalled: false` is set ONLY when the DHT host is a loopback address (tests, the demo and same-machine manual runs): there every node is directly reachable, and with hyperdht's default firewall probing a client-initiated connection between two processes that both bound ephemeral ports opened but carried no data until the other side reconnected (observed with hyperdht 6.34 on loopback; the demo's `sync --once` then waited on the seeder's re-discovery and took ~80 s instead of ~10 s). A private bootstrap on any other host says nothing about reachability (a peer behind NAT would fail to connect if forced unfirewalled), so hyperdht's own detection stays on. `--dht-host <ip>` / `VARANDA_DHT_HOST` is passed as `host` to `runSync` and `Seeder`. Every test and the demo use `host: '127.0.0.1'` / `--dht-host 127.0.0.1`. `swarm.destroy()` also destroys the DHT instance passed to it; never destroy it separately.

### Re-discovery (`startRediscovery`)

Hyperswarm refreshes a discovery only every 10 minutes, so two swarms whose first join of a topic happens at the same moment (each looks up before the other's announce is stored) would otherwise not find each other for that long. `startRediscovery(swarm, topics, { intervalMs, hasPeer, log })` (`packages/core/lib/net.js`) -> `stop()`: every `intervalMs` it calls `discovery.refresh()` (announce + lookup) on each joined topic for which `hasPeer(topic)` is false; errors are logged as `{ event: 'rediscovery-error', topic, error }` and never thrown. `hasPeer` must reflect CURRENT live connections, so re-discovery resumes when the last peer of a topic disconnects: `runSync` counts open sockets per topic (from `info.topics` of client-side connections, incremented on connect and decremented on the socket's `'close'`) plus connected peers whose `remoteHave` holds the topic's key, and for the rendezvous topic the number of open sockets (`intervalMs` 1000 in `--once`, 10000 long-running); the seeder uses `swarm.connections.size > 0` for the rendezvous (10000 ms).

### findingPeers

`const done = store.findingPeers()` before joining; call `done()` exactly once in a `finally` block when `swarm.flush()` settles (success or failure).

### On EVERY connection, in this order

1. `store.replicate(socket)`: the main Corestore creates the Protomux muxer on `socket.noiseStream.userData` (what `Hypercore.getProtocolMuxer(socket)` returns) and attaches every open core; cores opened later are attached when they start downloading, and cores stored on disk are attached when the remote asks for them by discovery key. This is why a not-yet-accepted log is never opened in the main store (quarantine below).
1b. `attachBlocklist(socket, ...)` from `packages/core/lib/blocklist-net.js` (B2, section 3 "Blocklist channel"), immediately after `store.replicate` and before the admission channel, so whichever side opens the channel first is paired.
2. `attachAdmission(socket, ...)` from `packages/core/lib/net.js` (implemented in `packages/core/lib/admission.js`), which takes the SAME muxer via `Hypercore.getProtocolMuxer(socket)` (a hyperswarm socket is its own noiseStream); never construct a second muxer.
3. Both sides open the admission channel immediately and send `hello` after `onopen` (followed by the overflow `have` messages of section 5, R4.4). The open carries the capability bits of section 5 "Capabilities"; a seeder with discovery attaches `'varanda/discovery/1'` (section 5 "Discovery channel") before its admission channel, right after the groups channel.

### External transports (`Seeder#acceptStream`)

- `seeder.acceptStream(rawStream, { handshakeTimeoutMs = 10000, onRefuse })` -> `NoiseSecretStream | null` lets the seeder accept a raw duplex from any transport that is not the swarm (phase 0 spike: a WebSocket). The stream is wrapped as a Noise responder (`new NoiseSecretStream(false, rawStream, { keyPair })`) keyed with the `seeder-key.json` pair, so the remote public key a member sees is the seeder's Noise key and the member's seeder verification (section 6: `seederCertForKey(remoteCert, hex(remotePublicKey), association)`) is unchanged.
- After the Noise handshake (`'connect'`) the connection follows "On EVERY connection, in this order" above exactly (`store.replicate`, then `attachAdmission` with role `seeder`); nothing runs before the handshake completes, because the peer key is snapshotted when the admission channel is attached. A handshake that yields no remote public key is destroyed.
- Two independent connection caps, each checked synchronously on its own set: swarm sockets are bounded by `seeder.maxConnections` (default `MAX_CONNECTIONS = 64`; the swarm path passes `limit = maxConnections` to `refuseOverConnectionLimit`, counting `swarm.connections` only), and external sockets, in-flight handshakes included, by `seeder.maxExternal` (default `Math.max(1, Math.floor(maxConnections / 2))`, 32 with the defaults; constructor option `maxExternal`, which must be an integer >= 1, else `TypeError`). External sockets never reduce the swarm limit and swarm sockets never reduce the external one. `acceptStream` refuses (logs `{ event: 'peer-dropped', peerKey: null, reason: 'connection-limit' }` and returns `null`; it destroys the raw stream, unless the caller passed `onRefuse`: then it calls `onRefuse({ reason: 'connection-limit' })` and leaves the socket to the caller, which the WebSocket entry closes with 1013, section 7 "Seeder WebSocket entry") when external sockets already reach `maxExternal`, BEFORE the handshake; the check and the insertion into the external set are synchronous, so no race lets an external socket pass the cap. `Seeder#stats()` reports `connections` (swarm plus external), `swarm`, `external`, `maxConnections` and `maxExternal` (section 7).
- External idle deadline: once the handshake completes, an external socket is destroyed after `EXTERNAL_IDLE_MS = 30000` without any incoming data (secret-stream `setTimeout`, refreshed by every received chunk; constructor option `externalIdleMs`, tests only). A live peer sends Hypercore keep-alives every 5 s, so only a silent peer is dropped and its slot freed.
- The handshake must complete within 10 s (`handshakeTimeoutMs`, an absolute deadline not refreshed by incoming bytes); a stream that closes or fails before the handshake is destroyed at once.
- Frame guard (external streams only). `@hyperswarm/secret-stream` 6.9.2 frames the raw bytes as a 3-byte little-endian length plus body and allocates every declared length (up to 16 MiB) as soon as the first body byte arrives, before the Noise handshake completes. On the external path a streamx `Duplex` proxy (`FrameGuard`, `packages/core/lib/frame-guard.js`, used by `seeder.js`) sits between the raw stream and the secret stream, parses that framing on the incoming bytes before forwarding them, and destroys both streams, without forwarding the offending chunk (not even its well-formed frames before the offending one), logging `{ event: 'peer-dropped', peerKey, reason: 'frame-limit' }` (`peerKey` `null` before the handshake, the remote key after it; never an address), when: (a) one of the first two frames, the Noise XX handshake frames, declares more than `MAX_HANDSHAKE_FRAME` = 1024 bytes (measured: msg1 32 bytes, msg3 64 bytes), so everything up to and including the second frame is at most `MAX_HANDSHAKE_BYTES` = 2 x (3 + 1024) = 2054 bytes, a derived bound rather than a separate budget; (b) any later frame declares more than `MAX_EXTERNAL_FRAME` = 8 MiB + 64 KiB, whether or not the secret stream has emitted `'connect'` yet. Nothing is held back: anyone completes a Noise XX handshake with an arbitrary key pair for free, so the bytes after msg3 are exposed to the post-handshake bound in any case, and judging them by a stricter pre-`'connect'` rule would buy no security while refusing valid members (a coalescing transport delivers a member's first post-handshake writes, its stream-id header and one channel open per replicated core, ~43 KiB for 500 cores, in the same chunk as msg3; acceptance never depends on chunk boundaries). The post-handshake limit is a code bound, not a measured one (the largest frame measured while a diary of near-16 KiB blocks replicated was 16 477 bytes): hypercore answers its queue of block requests with the muxer corked and protomux flushes a corked batch only at `MAX_BATCH` = 8 MiB, so a legitimate member can send a frame of up to 8 MiB plus one message, and a tighter limit would break replication. Writes pass through unchanged; close, destroy and error propagate both ways. Nothing is buffered by the guard. Swarm connections keep secret-stream's own behaviour: there, post-handshake frames remain up to 16 MiB (a residual risk for the later hardening PR). The guard lives in `packages/core/lib/frame-guard.js` and depends on streamx only, so a browser bundle reuses it (kept by `test/browser-safe.test.js`). Option `handshakeFrames` says how many leading frames are handshake frames: 2 for the Noise XX responder (the seeder: msg1 and msg3), 1 for the initiator (a browser client: it receives only msg2); `maxHandshakeFrame` and `maxFrame` set the two bounds; the frame memory budget below is an injected option and defaults to unlimited for a client, and the browser's guards pass `deadlines: false` (no frame deadline or throughput check: a paused consumer on the client must never read as a dripping seeder and cut an honest link; the size bounds are what protect the browser). Options are validated, and an `onLimit` callback that throws can no longer skip the destroy.
- Charged frames (external streams only). Size alone does not bound memory: a peer may declare a large frame and send its body one byte at a time while secret-stream keeps the whole declared length allocated. Only frames that can amplify memory are charged to the frame memory budget below: a frame is charged when it declares more than `SMALL_FRAME_BYTES` = 64 KiB AND is still incomplete at the end of the chunk being parsed. A frame whose body arrives completely within the chunk is never charged, and neither is a frame of at most 64 KiB split across chunks (bounded per socket and of the same order as the bytes a client really sends): the Noise handshake frames, keep-alives and ordinary replication messages are therefore never charged. A charged frame reserves its whole declared length (secret-stream allocates it on the first body byte), once, before the chunk is forwarded. Constructor option `frameSmallBytes` (tests only; default `SMALL_FRAME_BYTES`).
- Frame deadlines (external streams only). When a frame header with a non-zero length `len` leaves the frame incomplete at the end of a chunk, the guard arms ONE timer of `frameDeadlineFor(len)` = `Math.min(FRAME_DEADLINE_CAP_MS, FRAME_DEADLINE_BASE_MS + Math.ceil(len / FRAME_MIN_BYTES_PER_S) * 1000)` ms, with `FRAME_DEADLINE_CAP_MS = 120000`, `FRAME_DEADLINE_BASE_MS = 10000` and `FRAME_MIN_BYTES_PER_S = 65536` (11 s for a frame up to 64 KiB, 120 s for `MAX_EXTERNAL_FRAME`). Body bytes never refresh it; it is cleared only when the frame completes or the guard is destroyed. For an uncharged frame this is the only deadline. A charged frame keeps it as the outer bound and is also checked every `FRAME_DEADLINE_BASE_MS`: at the k-th check its body bytes received since the header must be at least `(k - 1) x FRAME_DEADLINE_BASE_MS x FRAME_MIN_BYTES_PER_S / 1000`, i.e. the first check (10 s) is grace and from then on the frame must keep up with 64 KiB/s on average, so a drip is dropped at the second check (20 s) instead of at the 120 s cap. Either expiry destroys both streams and logs `{ event: 'peer-dropped', peerKey, reason: 'frame-deadline' }`. Timers are unref'd. Constructor options `frameDeadlineBaseMs` and `frameDeadlineCapMs` (tests only). The socket idle deadline and the 10 s handshake deadline above are unchanged.
- Frame memory budget and eviction (external streams only).
