The seeder keeps one counter of the bytes reserved by charged frames across all external guards, bounded by `maxFrameMemory` (constructor option, default `MAX_FRAME_MEMORY` = 64 MiB), and the set of guards holding a reservation. A reservation is held until its frame completes or the guard is destroyed or closed, never released per body byte, and released exactly once. When a reservation does not fit, the seeder evicts, among the guards holding a reservation for at least `FRAME_EVICT_MIN_AGE_MS` = 1 s, the one with the lowest progress (body bytes received / declared length): it is released at once, both of its streams are destroyed and `{ event: 'peer-dropped', peerKey, reason: 'frame-budget' }` is logged for it; then the reservation is retried once. Only if it still does not fit (or no guard is old enough) is the requester refused: its chunk is not forwarded, both streams are destroyed and `frame-budget` is logged. Because uncharged frames never touch the budget and a stalled declaration loses to a frame that is making progress, the shared budget cannot be used to refuse new members' handshakes or to drop active members: an attacker filling it only gets its own stalled sockets evicted or dropped by the throughput check. Constructor option `frameEvictAgeMs` (tests only; default `FRAME_EVICT_MIN_AGE_MS`). The budget covers declared-but-incomplete frames, the amplified vector; it does NOT cover the bytes the `ws` library buffers for a partial WebSocket message before `FrameGuard` (`apps/cli/lib/seed-ws.js`): those are bytes the client actually sent (not amplified), at most `MAX_PAYLOAD` per socket, and the 30 s idle deadline drops a socket that stops sending them. Sizing rule: worst-case receive memory ≈ `maxExternal` x `MAX_PAYLOAD` + `maxFrameMemory` (plus at most 64 KiB of uncharged frame per socket).
- Frame guard drop reasons: `frame-limit` (a declared size over its limit), `frame-deadline` (a frame not completed within `frameDeadlineFor(len)`, or a charged frame below the minimum throughput), `frame-budget` (a charged frame that does not fit in the budget even after one eviction, or a stalled charged frame evicted to make room); always `{ event: 'peer-dropped', peerKey, reason }`, `peerKey` `null` before the handshake and the remote key after it, never an address. `MAX_HANDSHAKE_FRAME`, `MAX_HANDSHAKE_BYTES`, `MAX_EXTERNAL_FRAME`, `MAX_FRAME_MEMORY`, `SMALL_FRAME_BYTES`, `FRAME_DEADLINE_BASE_MS`, `FRAME_DEADLINE_CAP_MS`, `FRAME_MIN_BYTES_PER_S` and `frameDeadlineFor` are exported from `seeder.js` for the tests.
- Streams arriving before `start()` completes (or after `close()` began) are destroyed and `null` is returned: nothing replicates before the store exists.
- The socket never joins the DHT and its address is never logged. `close()` destroys every external socket first, before the swarm.
- The CLI exposes this entry as `ws://<host>:<port>/seeder` with `seed --ws-port` (section 7 "Seeder WebSocket entry").
- Capabilities and discovery (section 5 "Capabilities", "Discovery channel") work on an external socket exactly as on a swarm socket: the seeder attaches the discovery channel and offers `CAP_DISCOVERY` in its admission open after the handshake, so a member over the WebSocket that sends `CAP_NO_INVENTORY` gets the lean hello and pages the inventory instead of receiving up to `MAX_INVENTORY` advertisements. Nothing changes before the handshake: the `maxExternal` check, the 1013 refusal of `seed-ws` (`onRefuse`), the frame guard and its limits, the idle and handshake deadlines are the same, and the discovery channel takes no socket of its own.

### Quarantine: no uploads during admission (R5.4)

- Why: the main Corestore serves ANY core present in its storage to ANY peer that asks by discovery key (`_attachMaybe`), `active: false` does not change that, and hypercore 11 has no upload switch (`uploading: true` hardcoded, `_handleRequest` answers from storage). Therefore a not-yet-accepted log is NEVER opened in the main Corestore.
- Mechanism: block 0 is fetched through a separate quarantine Corestore at `<dataDir>/quarantine` whose cores are attached ONLY to the socket(s) of the peer(s) that offered the key, with `core.replicate(socket)` at the hypercore level. This registers only the per-core `pair({ protocol: 'hypercore/alpha', id: discoveryKey })` and opens one unique channel on that muxer; it never calls `Corestore.replicate(socket)`, so the main store's protocol-wide `pair` handler on that socket is untouched (a second `Corestore.replicate` on the same socket would replace it — protomux `pair` is a Map.set).
- A third peer asking for the key reaches the main store's handler, which finds nothing (quarantine has its own storage and core tracker) and rejects the channel; the quarantine channel exists only on the offering peer's socket.
- `startQuarantineFetch(qstore, keyHex, sockets, timeoutMs, opts?)` -> `{ promise, addSocket, attachedCount }` (section 8) always purges the quarantine core whatever the outcome; it holds at most `MAX_FETCH_ATTACHMENTS = MAX_CONNECTIONS` (64; `opts.maxAttachments` for tests) open sockets AT ONCE, drops a socket as soon as it closes (its slot is free at once; there is no lifetime limit, so reconnecting identities can neither pin closed sockets nor lock an honest advertiser out), refuses a socket that is destroyed or being destroyed, and replicates a socket added before the quarantine core is ready only once it is ready and only if it is still open; `attachedCount()` is test-only; `quarantineGet0` is the same fetch awaited directly. Late advertisers: while a fetch for K is in flight, `addSocket(socket)` attaches one more peer (one that advertised K with length >= 1 after the fetch started) to the SAME quarantine core, so a working peer that arrives later can still supply block 0 before the deadline; the member sync uses it (section 6, R5.8 b), and so does the seeder for its queued announcers and waiters (section 5, "Announce limits, capacity and the shared gate fetch"). The quarantine directory is wiped at every `Seeder.start()` and `runSync` start and removed at close; nothing in it is ever state.
- Lock before wiping (R6.2): `Seeder.start()` and `runSync` open the main store FIRST (`store = await openStore(dataDir)`, which takes the RocksDB lock and fails fast when another process holds that data dir) and only THEN call `qstore = await openQuarantine(dataDir)`. A second process on the same data dir therefore fails on the lock before touching `<dataDir>/quarantine`, so it can never wipe the quarantine of a running `seed` or `sync`.
- Lock released last (shutdown): the quarantine store is closed and its directory removed, and every in-flight fetch and purge has settled, BEFORE the main store closes; `store.close()` (which releases the data-dir lock) is the LAST step of `Seeder.close()` and of the `runSync` cleanup. A process that takes the lock the instant it is released (and wipes/recreates `<dataDir>/quarantine` at its start) therefore never has its quarantine removed under it by the previous owner's cleanup.

### Environment summary

`VARANDA_DATA`, `VARANDA_ASSOCIATION`, `VARANDA_BOOTSTRAP`, `VARANDA_DHT_HOST`, `VARANDA_WS_PORT`, `VARANDA_WS_HOST`, `VARANDA_WS_ORIGIN`.

## 5. Admission channel (`'varanda/admission/1'`)

`mux.createChannel({ protocol: 'varanda/admission/1', onopen, onclose })`; `null` means the channel already exists on this socket (reuse the cached handle). ONE message type is added with encoding `c.buffer` (compact-encoding); the payload is UTF-8 JSON. A payload over 16384 bytes, empty, unparsable, or failing the shape rules (`validateMessage`) is ignored (counted in `badMessages`) and never dispatched. Send only after `onopen` (queue earlier sends).

`Peer.opened` resolves `true` on `onopen`; `Peer.hello` resolves with the first VALID hello. When the channel closes before it opened, `opened` resolves `false`; when it closes before a valid hello arrived, `hello` resolves `null`. Neither promise ever rejects, so a caller awaiting them treats `false`/`null` as "peer gone" (the seeder's `peer` log line is skipped on a `null` hello; `runSync` tracks hello through `onmessage` instead). After close, `send()` returns `false` and the queue is dropped; `onclose(peer)` runs exactly once.

### Messages

- `hello`: `{ "type": "hello", "v": 1, "role": "member" | "seeder", "count": <integer >= have.length>, "have": [{ "key": "<hex64>", "length": <integer >= 0> }], "cert"?: SeederCertificate }` — first message after open. See "Bounded inventory" below for `count` and `have`. R5.3 a: `cert` MUST be present and a plain object when `role === 'seeder'` and MUST be absent when `role === 'member'`; `validateMessage` enforces both (a seeder hello without `cert`, or a member hello with one, is counted in `badMessages` and never dispatched). The 16384-byte payload limit is unchanged.
- `have`: `{ "type": "have", "key": "<hex64>", "length": <integer >= 0> }`: sent (1) for each inventory entry that did not fit in `hello`, (2) whenever the present length of a served core grows, and (3) by a member when a followed key changes state: becoming pending (length 0, interest only) or passing the gate (length = the present length). The present length (section 2 "Clearing deleted bytes") replaces `contiguousLength` as the VALUE of `length` in `hello`, `have` and `admitted`; the message shapes are unchanged, and for a diary with nothing deleted the two are equal.
- `announce`: `{ "type": "announce", "key": "<hex64>", "cert": Certificate }` — member -> seeder: replicate this diary, here is its certificate; sent once per connection for the member's own key when its block 0 is a valid member-cert for the trusted association. Members ignore incoming `announce`. `announce` stays unauthenticated by design.
- `admitted`: `{ "type": "admitted", "key": "<hex64>", "length": <integer >= 0> }`: seeder -> member after admission (idempotent; `length` = the seeder's present length, section 2).
- `rejected`: `{ "type": "rejected", "key": "<hex64>", "reason": <string matching /^[a-z0-9-]{1,64}$/> }` — R5.1 a: may be sent by ANY role, to any peer. Meaning: "the sender will not keep or serve `key`". Reason master list (R5.9 f, S3): `malformed` | `member-mismatch` | `untrusted-association` | `bad-issuer-signature` (a v2 certificate whose embedded issuer certificate fails, section 3) | `bad-signature` | `not-in-log` | `timeout` | `fork` | `no-cert` | `admission-failed` | `quota` | `seeder-full` (review item 4a: the seeder already serves `MAX_INVENTORY` logs) | `blocked` (B2: the key is in the node's current blocklist snapshot, section 3) | `revoked-issuer` (B2: a v2 certificate whose issuer is in the snapshot's `revokedIssuers`, section 3) | `blocklist-pending` (the seeder lost its blocklist and holds new admissions until a verified snapshot arrives, section 1; not a moderation outcome, so nothing is purged). S4: a `reason` outside that charset (free text, control characters) is a bad message, never dispatched; every reason the seeder or a member can send is a token of the master list. Defence in depth: `apps/cli/lib/commands/sync.js` escapes C0/C1 controls, DEL, U+2028/U+2029 and the bidi controls as `\u{XX}` in every string it prints that came from a peer or a log (`safe`).
- `admitted` (S2) may only come from a peer whose hello role is `seeder`: an `admitted` from any other role (or before the hello) is a bad message and never touches `remoteHave`. (`rejected` stays allowed from any role: members send it as a withdrawal, R5.1 a.)
  - Members send it (broadcast to every open admission channel) when (1) a followed key's remote gate FAILS with a non-timeout reason (section 6, R5.8), (2) a fork is detected on a followed key, reason `'fork'` (R5.9), and (3) the local re-gate of a replica opened after a remote pass fails (section 6, `bloco 0 divergente`).
  - Seeders send it to the announcer on admission failure (including `'admission-failed'`: the gate passed but persisting or opening the replica threw, section 5 d) and broadcast it on every runtime drop (R5.5, R5.9).
  - Nobody sends `rejected` for `'timeout'` except a seeder answering an announce; nobody sends `'admission-failed'` except a seeder answering an announce.

Example `hello` from a member with its own diary (3 blocks), one followed key that passed the gate and one whose gate is pending:

```json
{ "type": "hello", "v": 1, "role": "member", "count": 3, "have": [{ "key": "<own hex64>", "length": 3 }, { "key": "<followed hex64>", "length": 5 }, { "key": "<pending hex64>", "length": 0 }] }
```

A seeder `hello` additionally carries `"cert": { "v": 1, "seeder": "<hex64>", "date": "...", "association": "<hex64>", "signature": "<128 hex>" }`.

### Latest-wins advertisements (R5.1 b)

`Peer.remoteHave` (packages/core/lib/net.js) holds the LATEST length advertised per key, never a max: a valid `hello` REPLACES the map with its `have` entries (plus the overflow `have` messages); `have` and `admitted` do `remoteHave.set(key, length)`; a valid `rejected` does `remoteHave.delete(key)` BEFORE `onmessage` is called.

### Bounded inventory (R4.4)

- The inventory of a peer is every key it serves or wants. Member: its own key, every followed key whose gate PASSED (length = the present length) and every followed key whose gate is still PENDING with length 0 (R4.5). Seeder: every admitted key (length = the present length).
- `count` = total number of keys in the sender's inventory (integer >= `have.length`); `have` = the first at most 100 entries. The remaining entries are sent immediately after `hello` as individual `have` messages, in order.
- `validateMessage` rejects a `hello` whose `have` has more than 100 entries or whose `count < have.length`.
- The Peer gains `remoteCount` (from `hello`) and `inventoryComplete`: true once `remoteHave` holds `remoteCount` distinct keys, or 5000 ms after its `hello`, whichever comes first; the timeout is logged.
- Hello before inventory completion: the valid `hello` is dispatched to `onmessage` BEFORE the completion check for that hello runs, so `oninventory` (and the completion it reports) always observes the caller's per-peer state already updated from that hello (`remoteRole`, `remoteCert`, the member's `verifiedSeeder`). A hello with an empty inventory therefore reaches `onmessage` first and `oninventory` second, never the other way round.
- Overflow advertisements before inventory completion (review item 2): the same rule holds for `have` and `admitted`: `remoteHave.set(key, length)`, then `onmessage(msg)`, THEN the completion check. When the advertisement that completes the inventory is the last overflow `have`, the caller has already recorded it (in `runSync`, the claim of section 6) by the time `oninventory` fires, so `--once` can never resolve on an inventory whose last advertisement it has not seen.
- No replacement hello (S6): our side sends exactly one `hello` per connection (in `onopen`); a second valid `hello` on the same connection is a protocol violation and the peer is dropped (`duplicate-hello`), so a peer can never keep its inventory incomplete by re-sending hello.

- Lean hello (`packages/core/lib/admission.js`, option `lean`): a seeder that offers `CAP_DISCOVERY` and opens with a member whose open carries `CAP_NO_INVENTORY` sends exactly `{ "type": "hello", "v": 1, "role": "seeder", "count": 0, "have": [], "cert": SeederCertificate }`, a valid v1 hello with an empty inventory, and NO `have` message on that connection afterwards: the overflow entries are never sent, `have` messages queued before the open are dropped, and growth broadcasts to that peer are dropped (`admitted` and `rejected` are still sent). The member learns the inventory from the discovery channel instead. Only a seeder sends a lean hello: a member's own inventory is never shortened, and a seeder without `CAP_DISCOVERY` (an older one, or one started with `discovery: false`) ignores the bit and sends the full inventory. A member that does not send the bit (every member from before capabilities) gets the full inventory exactly as above.

### Capabilities (`packages/core/lib/admission.js`, re-exported by `packages/core/lib/net.js`)

- The admission channel's open handshake carries one compact-encoding `uint`, the sender's capability bits; with no bits set (caps 0) the open carries no bytes at all, so a peer without capabilities opens the channel byte for byte as before. Decoding is tolerant: an open with no bytes (every peer from before capabilities) or with bytes that do not parse reads as caps 0, and unknown bits are ignored, so either side may be older than the other. `attachAdmission` takes the option `caps` (a non-negative safe integer, else `TypeError`, default 0) and exposes the peer's bits as `Peer.remoteCaps`.
- `CAP_NO_INVENTORY = 1`: sent by a member that wants no inventory from the seeder (the lean hello of "Bounded inventory" above).
- `CAP_DISCOVERY = 2`: sent by a seeder that answers `'varanda/discovery/1'` and sends lean hellos. The seeder sends it unless it was started with `discovery: false` (constructor option, default true, no CLI flag: tests and rollback), in which case it behaves exactly like a seeder from before discovery: no bit, no lean hello, no discovery channel (a member's discovery open is rejected).
- The capabilities change nothing else: the message shapes and limits of this section, `maxServed`, `maxExternal`, the frame limits and the hello of a peer that sends no bit are unchanged.

### Discovery channel (`'varanda/discovery/1'`, `packages/core/lib/discovery-net.js`, `packages/core/lib/seeder.js`)

A member pages through a seeder's inventory (which diaries it holds and their present lengths) instead of receiving it whole in the hello. The channel travels on the SAME protomux as replication (`socket.noiseStream.userData`); the seeder attaches it on every connection (swarm or external), right after the groups channel and before the admission `hello`, only when started with discovery (default). Messages are UTF-8 JSON in one `c.buffer` message type, exact keys, lowercase hex, 1..`MAX_DISCOVERY_MESSAGE = 16384` bytes (checked on the raw buffer before `JSON.parse`). All numbers are safe integers; messages, cursors and items are plain objects.

- `page` request, member to seeder: exactly `{ "type": "page", "id": <integer >= 1>, "after": null | Cursor, "limit": <integer 1..MAX_DISCOVERY_LIMIT> }` or the same with `"view": <32 lowercase hex>` as a fifth key. `MAX_DISCOVERY_LIMIT = 64`. `id` is chosen by the member (one more than its previous request) and echoed back. A Cursor is exactly `{ "t": <integer >= 0>, "h": <64 lowercase hex> }`.
- `page` answer, seeder to member: exactly `{ "type": "page", "id": <the request id>, "view": <32 lowercase hex>, "items": [{ "key": <64 hex>, "length": <integer 0..MAX_LOG_BLOCKS> }], "next": null | Cursor, "total": <integer 0..MAX_INVENTORY> }`, at most `limit` (and at most `MAX_DISCOVERY_LIMIT`) items, each with exactly the two shown keys and distinct keys; or exactly `{ "type": "page", "id": <the request id>, "stale": true }`. A request that names a view gets an answer from that view or `stale`, never a page of another view (the seeder checks it before sending, the member counts any other view as a bad message). `length` is the seeder's present length (section 2), an unsigned hint as in the hello: the member verifies every diary itself before showing anything from it.
- **One answer per request.** The seeder answers every valid request at once, synchronously, from `Seeder#discoveryPage({ after, limit, view })`. A lookup that throws, or returns something the member would count as bad, is logged (`discovery-page-failed`, `discovery-page-invalid`) and answered `stale`. The seeder sends nothing unsolicited.
- **Bad messages.** An empty or oversized frame, a JSON or shape failure, a message of the other role, an answer nobody asked for, or one whose `id`, item count or view does not match the request. More than `MAX_BAD_DISCOVERY = 5` destroys the socket (peer-dropped reason `'discovery'`).
- **Seeder limits.** Every inbound message costs one token of a bucket of `DISCOVERY_BURST = 8` tokens refilled at `DISCOVERY_RATE = 4` per second (monotonic clock), taken before `JSON.parse`; an empty bucket drops the peer (reason `'message-rate'`). At most `MAX_DISCOVERY_REQUESTS = 160` valid requests are answered per connection; the next one drops the peer (reason `'discovery-request-cap'`).
- **Member handle.** `attachDiscovery(socket, { role: 'member' })` returns `page({ after, limit, view })` and `read({ limit, maxPages })`. One request in flight at a time, each sent no sooner than `DISCOVERY_PACE_MS = 250` after the previous one (the refill rate, so an honest member never empties the bucket), and the member stops itself at `MAX_DISCOVERY_REQUESTS`. A request times out `DISCOVERY_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS = 5000` after it was sent (a request asked before the channel is open waits for the open at most that long, and then gets the full timeout from its send): the request rejects and the channel closes, never the socket, so a late answer cannot satisfy a later request. `read` follows `next` and the view from a first page for at most `maxPages` pages, never more than `MAX_PAGES_PER_READ = 8`; a stale answer ends the read with what it had (`stale: true`, `complete: false`) and the next read starts over from a first page. A peer from before discovery rejects the open: `supported` resolves `false` and the socket stays connected.
- **Eligible diaries.** A served diary is offered only while it is gated, admitted, not dropped, not in the blocklist snapshot (section 3) and has at least one block past its certificate (present length >= 2). Eligibility is checked when a view is built AND again for every item of every answer, so a diary blocked or dropped after its view was built is skipped (the page then holds the next eligible diaries of the same view). A diary held by a pending re-gate after an issuer revocation is skipped by the answers given while it is held, but it is not left out of a view built meanwhile: the hold covers every gated diary at once and is released as each verdict clears it, so it would otherwise empty a whole view. A skipped diary is not visited again by that traversal; a later traversal from a first page offers it again once it is eligible.
- **Order.** By `recentAt` descending, then `h` ascending. `recentAt` is the time of the newest post or repost in effect (`on: true`, not deleted) the seeder's index scan (section 5 "Seeder engagement index") has read in that diary: `Date.parse(at)` of the record, but never later than the seeder's clock when it read it, so a date in the future counts as the moment it was read; 0 until a post is read. The seeder keeps the times of the diary's newest `DISCOVERY_RECENT_KEPT = 8` posts and reposts in effect: a deletion of one of them, or an undo (`on: false`) of a kept repost, drops its time and `recentAt` falls back to the newest one left (0 when none is), so a cursor never carries the time of something taken back. `h` is `hash(bootSalt, key)` in lowercase hex (`hypercore-crypto` `hash`, BLAKE2b-256 over the salt and the key's 32 bytes), with a random 32-byte `bootSalt` per seeder process, so ties are not broken by key order and a cursor means nothing after a restart.
- **Frozen views.** Pages are cut from a view: the eligible keys sorted once, at most `DISCOVERY_VIEW_MAX_KEYS = 4096` (never more than `MAX_INVENTORY`), named by a random view id (16 random bytes in hex, `newViewId()`). A request without `view` uses the newest view; when that view was last compared at least `DISCOVERY_REBUILD_MS = 30000` (30 s) ago, or holds no key, the seeder first sorts the eligible diaries again and builds a new view only if the keys or their `recentAt` differ from the newest view (an unchanged order keeps the view and its id). So a diary that publishes moves to the front of a first page within about 30 s of the index scan reading it. A request with `view` uses that view while it is kept. The seeder keeps views for `DISCOVERY_VIEW_TTL_MS = 300000` (5 minutes) from when each was built, at most `DISCOVERY_VIEWS_KEPT = 10` (the TTL divided by the rebuild interval, so a view is not evicted by newer ones before its TTL however busy the seeder is; only the empty-view rebuild of a seeder that just started can build views faster); a request naming a view that is no longer kept (expired, evicted, or from before a restart) is answered `stale`. So a first page (`after: null`, no view) always comes from the newest view, and a traversal that carries its view sees every diary of that view at most once and in that view's order, even when a diary publishes between two pages.
- **Cursor.** Stateless: `after` is the `{ t, h }` of the last item of the previous page, and an answer continues with the first item of the view strictly after it in view order. `next` is the `{ t, h }` of the last item of the answer, or `null` when the view has no item after it. `total` is the number of keys in the view, eligible or not when the answer is cut.
- **Trust.** The seeder could already omit a diary from its inventory; with pages it also chooses the order in which candidates are offered, and the member cannot check that order (`recentAt` is the seeder's reading, not a signed value). Every post, name and length the member shows is still read from the diary and verified on the device, as with the hello inventory, and a member that does not ask for the lean hello keeps the full inventory.

### Per-connection limits (security audit S1, constants in `packages/core/lib/net.js`)

- `MAX_INVENTORY = 4096`: a `hello` with `count > MAX_INVENTORY` is invalid; a `have`/`admitted` for a key not yet in `remoteHave` while `remoteHave.size >= MAX_INVENTORY` drops the peer (`inventory-limit`), so the map never exceeds the cap. Every advertised `length` must be `<= MAX_LOG_BLOCKS` (`isLength`), else the message is invalid.
- `MAX_BAD_MESSAGES = 16`: a connection whose `badMessages` exceeds it (the 17th bad message) is dropped (`bad-messages`).
- `HELLO_TIMEOUT_MS = 10000`: a peer that has not sent a valid `hello` within it (counted from `attachAdmission`, which opens the channel at once) is dropped (`hello-timeout`); injectable as `attachAdmission(socket, { helloTimeoutMs })` for tests.
- `MAX_CONNECTIONS = 64`: `createSwarm` passes `maxPeers: MAX_CONNECTIONS` to Hyperswarm (which bounds the connections it initiates; hyperswarm 4.17 stores `maxServerConnections` but never reads it), and `refuseOverConnectionLimit(swarm, socket, { limit, log })` runs on EVERY `'connection'` of the seeder and of `runSync` BEFORE `store.replicate(socket)`: when the swarm already holds `limit` other open connections the socket is destroyed (`connection-limit`). `Seeder` and `runSync` take `maxConnections` (tests only). The seeder's external sockets have their own cap, `maxExternal` (section 4), and never count against this one.
- Senders never advertise more than the receivers accept (review item 4): `attachAdmission` throws `TypeError('inventory over MAX_INVENTORY')` for a `have` list longer than `MAX_INVENTORY`, and the callers keep their inventories under it: (a) the seeder refuses an admission with `rejected { key, reason: 'seeder-full' }` while it already serves `MAX_INVENTORY` logs (`Seeder` option `maxServed`, tests only; an already admitted key is still answered `admitted`); (b) `follow` refuses a new key when `follows.json` already holds `MAX_INVENTORY - 1` keys (own diary + follows = `MAX_INVENTORY`): stderr `erro: limite de diários seguidos atingido`, exit 1, `follows.json` unchanged (an already followed key stays idempotent, exit 0); (c) `runSync` with a hand-edited `follows.json` over the limit uses only its first `MAX_INVENTORY - 1` entries and writes `aviso: limite de diários seguidos excedido: só os primeiros <n> serão sincronizados` through `log` (stderr) (`runSync` option `maxFollows`, tests only).
- `announce` flood (review item 1, `packages/core/lib/seeder.js`): a peer that would hold more than `MAX_INVENTORY` distinct keys outstanding in the seeder's announce queues (running, queued or waiting, section 5) is dropped (`announce-limit`; `Seeder` option `maxAnnounces`, tests only).
- Every drop logs exactly one event `{ event: 'peer-dropped', peerKey, reason }` with `reason` in `inventory-limit` | `bad-messages` | `hello-timeout` | `connection-limit` | `duplicate-hello` | `announce-limit`, plus, on the seeder's external streams only, `frame-limit` | `frame-deadline` | `frame-budget` (section 4) (the seeder prints it through its `log`; `runSync` emits it through `onEvent` and writes `aviso: par descartado (<peerKey8>): <reason>` through `log`). Destroying the socket closes the channel, so `onclose(peer)` runs as for any disconnect.

### Message rate (security fix cycle 3)

- Every valid admission message (hello included) costs one token from a per-connection bucket: `MESSAGE_BURST = MAX_INVENTORY + 64` tokens, refilled at `MESSAGE_RATE = 100`/s (`packages/core/lib/net.js`, injectable as `messageBurst` / `messageRate`). An empty bucket drops the peer (`peer-dropped`, reason `message-rate`).
- Senders pace themselves below that limit (the same burst minus 1/16, half the rate); queued `have` and `rejected` messages are merged per key (the rule is below; order is kept against other messages for that key). The initial inventory (hello plus overflow `have` messages) goes out at once and fits the receiver's burst.
- A member processes a `have` only for its own key or a followed key, and only when that peer's advertised length changed; the `have` event fires only on a change and `withdrawn` at most once per peer.
- Once a closed peer's `onclose` has run, its advertised inventory (`remoteHave`, and in member sync `lastAdv` / `emittedHave`) is released, so a socket still referenced elsewhere does not pin it.
- Closing the admission channel, before or after hello, destroys the socket: replication and the connection slot end with it (no slot can be held without a hello).
- Messages sent before the channel opens share the same paced, per-key-merged send backlog; it is flushed after the initial inventory and capped at `MAX_SEND_BACKLOG = 2 * MAX_INVENTORY + 64` entries (exceeding it drops the peer: `peer-dropped`, reason `send-backlog`). Queued sends merge per key: at most one `have` (latest length) and one `rejected` (latest reason, in the position of the earliest one queued), in their relative order, never across an `admitted` or `announce` for that key; `hello`, `admitted` and `announce` are never merged or reordered.
- The rate buckets (per-connection messages and the seeder's announces) use a monotonic clock (`lib/clock.js` `now()`: `performance.now()`, or the process hrtime on a runtime without `performance`), so a wall-clock step never drops honest peers.

### Interest is advertised without data (R4.5)

A member lists in `hello`/`have` its own key, every followed key whose gate PASSED (length = the present length) and every followed key whose gate is still PENDING with length 0 (interest only). A pending key has no core in the main store (R5.4, R5.8 a), so nothing can be served for it. Followed keys whose gate FAILED are excluded. Advertising a pending key with length 0 never makes the sender serve data for it.

### The block-0 gate (R4.2, R5.4; identical in the seeder and in member sync)

- Verdict on a raw block (inline in packages/core/lib/seeder.js and packages/core/lib/sync.js, identical): `block0 = decodeBlock(raw)`; reason `'not-in-log'` when `block0 === null` or `validateRecord(block0, 0) !== null`; else `certForKey(block0.cert, keyHex, associationHex)` -> `null` when ok, otherwise its reason.
- REMOTE gate (key not yet accepted): `r = await quarantineGet0(qstore, keyHex, sockets, timeoutMs)` through the quarantine store only (section 4). `r.error === 'timeout'` (any throw of the fetch) -> timeout outcome; otherwise the verdict on `r.raw`. The quarantine core is always purged by `quarantineGet0`; nothing is opened in the main store on this path.
- LOCAL gate (block 0 already in the main store): `core.get(0, { wait: false, raw: true })` + the same verdict; any error thrown by `get` or by the checks counts as `'not-in-log'` (never let it skip the purge). On failure the main-store core is purged through `purgeReplica(store, keyHex)`.

### Seeder admission algorithm (R5.5) for `announce { key, cert }` from peer P

R4.7 as bounded by review item 1: step (a) runs synchronously when the `announce` arrives, BEFORE anything is queued, so an invalid announce is answered at once and never costs memory. A valid one enters the key's queue: at most ONE admission RUNS per key and at most ONE more is QUEUED (it runs when the running one settles, normally answering through (b)); any further announce for the key only adds its peer to the queued admission's waiter set (one slot per peer; the queued announcer itself is answered by its own run) and is answered with that admission's outcome (admitted -> `admitted { key, length }`, rejection -> `rejected` with the same reason; nothing when the seeder is closing). So a flood of announces behind a slow gate keeps the queue at one running + one queued + one waiter slot per peer, and the key is admitted once. Per peer, the outstanding keys (running, queued or waiting) are bounded by `MAX_INVENTORY`: the announce that would exceed it drops the peer (`peer-dropped`, `announce-limit`, section 5 limits). An announce is not bound to the diary signer: anyone may ask the seeder to keep a member's log, but only a log whose block 0 passes the gate is ever kept.

(a) `key` not hex64 or `certForKey(cert, key, association)` fails -> `rejected` with that reason.

(a') `key` is a bound media core, or one being dropped or admitted as a media core -> `rejected { key, reason: 'not-in-log' }` (its block 0 is a media header, never a certificate; refusing here keeps the two admission paths from fetching the same key at once).

(b) `key` in the in-memory `admitted` map -> `admitted { key, length: <present length> }`. An announce for a key in `dropping` (R6.1) waits on that key's per-key chain (the drop in progress) and is then handled as a fresh attempt.

(b') Inventory cap (review item 4a): when the seeder already serves `MAX_INVENTORY` logs -> `rejected { key, reason: 'seeder-full' }` and log `{ event: 'rejected', key, reason: 'seeder-full' }`, before any fetch (a seeder serving more could no longer send a valid `hello`).

(c) `r = await quarantineGet0(qstore, key, [P.socket], 15000)` (constant `GATE_TIMEOUT_MS = 15000`); if a test `gateHook` is configured, `await gateHook(key)` runs here. Then (R6.3): `r.error` -> `rejected { key, reason: 'timeout' }` and log `{ event: 'rejected', key, reason: 'timeout' }`; else compute the verdict (block-0 gate above); a NON-NULL verdict -> `rejected { key, reason: verdict }` and log `{ event: 'rejected', key, reason }`; a NULL verdict -> continue with step (d). `rejected` is never sent with a null reason. Nothing is ever opened in the main store on the rejection paths.

(c') Log quota at the gate (S3): the quarantine fetch also returns the signed `length` and `byteLength` the quarantine core learned with block 0 (the upgrade proof); `length > MAX_LOG_BLOCKS || byteLength > MAX_LOG_BYTES` -> `rejected { key, reason: 'quota' }` and log `{ event: 'rejected', key, reason: 'quota' }`, nothing opened in the main store (`Seeder` takes `maxLogBlocks` / `maxLogBytes`, tests only).

(d) Admit (null verdict): `admitted.set(key, cert)`; `await persist()` (R5.7, BEFORE the main store opens the key); `core = await openReplica(store, key)`. Rollback: if `persist()` throws (e.g. `ENOSPC`) or `openReplica` throws, the key is removed from `admitted`, `persist()` runs again best-effort (its failure is ignored), the seeder logs `{ event: 'rejected', key, reason: 'admission-failed' }` and replies `rejected { key, reason: 'admission-failed' }`; nothing is served and a later `announce` for the key runs the full gate (c) again. S7: when the failure happened in the open step and the storage already holds the core (`store.storage.hasCore(discoveryKey)`; corestore would serve a stored core), the replica is purged (`purgeReplica`) BEFORE the key leaves `admitted`; a purge failure is fatal like every other (`{ event: 'fatal', key, reason: 'purge-failed' }`, the seeder closes, the key stays listed and the next `start()` re-gates and purges it, `no-cert`). Otherwise: `watchFork(core, ...)` (R5.9); live `core.download({ start: 0, end: -1 })`; `swarm.join(core.discoveryKey, { server: true, client: true })`; `watchContiguous(core, ...)` -> broadcast `have { key, length }` + log `{ event: 'have', key, length }`; re-gate on arrival: the first time `contiguousLength >= 1` after this open, run the LOCAL gate: failure -> `drop(key, reason)`; reply `admitted { key, length: <present length> }` (0 at this point; the `have` messages follow) and log `{ event: 'admitted', key, length }`. Review item 6: the quota (S3) is checked once when the replica starts being served, BEFORE the join; a resumed main-store core already over the quota is dropped right there (`drop(key, 'quota')`: `rejected` broadcast, purge) and the admission stops: no `swarm.join` of its topic, no `admitted` reply, no `admitted` log.

(d') Log quota on growth (S3): for every served replica the seeder listens to `'append'` (registered before the growth watcher) and checks again on every growth: `core.length > MAX_LOG_BLOCKS || core.byteLength > MAX_LOG_BYTES` -> `drop(key, 'quota')` through the path (e) below (admitted.json updated, purge failure fatal), before any block past the limit is advertised.

(e) `drop(key, reason)` (runtime, R6.1): move the entry from `admitted` to `dropping` (the key stays persisted: `persist()` writes both maps); stop its watchers and destroy its download range; broadcast `rejected { key, reason }` to every open channel; log `{ event: 'rejected', key, reason }`; `await purgeReplica(store, key)`; on success delete it from `dropping` and `await persist()`. On a purge throw the key STAYS in `dropping` (and in the file): log `{ event: 'fatal', key, reason: 'purge-failed' }` and close the seeder; the next `start()` re-gates it (it fails the local gate or is empty/forked) and purges it. A concurrent `persist()` for another key (admission or drop) can never write a snapshot without the dropping key. Forks (R5.9 c): `watchFork`'s `onFork` -> `drop(key, 'fork')`.

### Announce limits, capacity and the shared gate fetch (security fix cycle 2)

- Per-peer announce rate: a token bucket of `ANNOUNCE_BURST = 20` announces refilled at `ANNOUNCE_RATE = 10`/s (injectable `announceRate` / `announceBurst`), checked before any signature is verified. A peer over it is dropped (`peer-dropped`, reason `announce-rate`).
- A peer whose announced certificate (or key) fails `MAX_BAD_ANNOUNCES = 16` times is dropped (`peer-dropped`, reason `bad-announces`); once dropped, its later announces cost no verify.
- Capacity: an admission reserves a slot before its gate; when served + reserved >= the cap (`maxServed`, default `MAX_INVENTORY`) the answer is `rejected { reason: 'seeder-full' }`, and the cap is checked again before the key is admitted.
- At start, valid listed logs beyond the cap (admitted.json order) are purged and removed from admitted.json before the swarm starts (event `warning`, reason `seeder-full`). A hello never carries more than `MAX_INVENTORY` entries.
- The gate uses `startQuarantineFetch`: every queued announcer and waiter of a key is attached (`addSocket`) to the block-0 fetch in flight, so any honest announcer can supply block 0.
- Waiters only receive an outcome that does not depend on another announcer's socket: `admitted`, or a block-0 certificate rejection other than `not-in-log`. After any other outcome (`timeout`, `not-in-log`, `quota`, `admission-failed`, `seeder-full`), a still-connected waiter runs its own gate; a queued announcer that left hands its turn to a connected waiter.

### Seeder media admission (`packages/core/lib/seeder.js`)

A media core K is kept only under the two-way binding of section 2 "Media cores", and with the same invariants as a diary: listed in `admitted.json` before it is opened in the main store, purged before it leaves the file, a purge failure fatal.

- **Scan.** For every admitted, gated diary D the seeder reads D's own blocks for `media-core` records (a cursor from block 1, raw reads with `{ wait: false }`, `decodeBlock` + `validRecordCopy`), never trusting a peer's word. Scans run one at a time across all diaries, at most one queued per diary, in the background: the first pass starts after `ready`, never inside `start()`, and each growth or gate pass of D queues another. The result is D's latest set (`latestMediaCores`, 4 cores).
- **Reconcile.** A bound core that fell out of D's latest set is dropped (`unbound`); a changed declaration seq is persisted; nothing new is admitted for a blocked D; each wanted core not yet bound, dropping or in flight is admitted. A declaration whose block 0 failed the header gate is retried only when re-declared at a newer seq; every other failure is retried on D's next growth or when a new peer connects.
- **Admission of K for D**, in order: (1) still wanted (D served, gated, not blocked, K in its latest set, K not a diary admitted, dropping or being gated) and a media slot free (bound + reserved < `maxMedia`, else `media-rejected` `media-full`); (2) a quarantine fetch of block 0 attached to the sockets of ALL connected peers (nothing on the admission channel says who holds K; no peer -> `no-peers`), `GATE_TIMEOUT_MS`, and every peer that connects meanwhile is attached to it (`addSocket`); a timeout -> `timeout`; (3) verdict: block 0 a valid media header (`header-invalid` otherwise, an oversized block included) naming D (`wrong-diary` otherwise); the fetched signed `length` / `byteLength` within `MAX_MEDIA_BLOCKS` / `MAX_MEDIA_BYTES` (`quota`); (4) `admitted.json.media[K] = { diary: D, seq }` persisted BEFORE the open (the same `_persist` as the diaries); a persist or open failure rolls back exactly as in (d) above (`admission-failed`, a stored core purged first, a purge failure fatal); (5) right after the open: still wanted (`unbound`), `fork === 0` (`fork`), within the quota (`quota`), else closed, purged and un-listed; (6) served: live download, fork watcher, quota on every `'append'`, `swarm.join(discoveryKey)`, log `{ event: 'media-admitted', key, diary, length }`.
- **Local re-gate.** Once block 0 is local in the main store it must be byte-identical to the block the quarantine fetched (else `fork`) and a valid header naming D; only then does `mediaRead` serve K. A startup survivor whose stored block 0 passed the gate is served at once.
- **Purge rules.** K is dropped (runtime order of (e): the binding stays listed while it is purged, a purge failure is fatal and keeps it listed for the next start) when D is dropped for any reason (`blocked`, `fork`, `quota`, a revoked issuer, a failed local gate), when K falls out of D's latest 4 (`unbound`), when K forks (`fork`), when it grows over its quota (`quota`) or when its re-gate fails. Each drop logs `{ event: 'media-rejected', key, diary, reason }`.
- **Never on the admission channel.** No `announce`, no `have`, no new message type for media keys: a new type would be a bad message for every peer from before this change (they disconnect on it), and members read every `have` key as a diary. The swarm serves K's discovery key like any other admitted core.
- Limits: `maxMedia` (constructor option, default `maxServed`) bound media cores; per core `MAX_MEDIA_BLOCKS` / `MAX_MEDIA_BYTES` (options `maxMediaBlocks` / `maxMediaBytes`, tests only).
- `mediaRead(diaryHex, coreHex, start, blocks)` -> `null | { bytes, blocks }`: non-null only when K is bound to D, D is admitted and not blocked, K passed its re-gate, `start >= 1`, `start + blocks <= core.length` and EVERY block of the range is local. `bytes` is the exact total; `blocks` is an async iterator yielding each block read with `{ wait: false }`. A read never waits on the network; a block gone meanwhile makes the iterator reject.

### Seeder engagement index (Índice de engajamento; `packages/core/lib/engagement-index.js`, `packages/core/lib/seeder.js`)

The seeder already holds every admitted diary, so it answers what a member's device cannot see alone: who replied to, quoted, liked and reposted a post, and which posts a member wrote. The index only says WHERE TO LOOK; the content is always read and verified from the diaries themselves (section 7 "Seeder index route").

- **In memory, rebuilt at start.** Nothing is persisted. `createEngagementIndex({ maxRecords })` is pure (no I/O, no timers, no method throws). It keeps, per target ref, every reply edge and every quote edge ordered by `(Date.parse(at), author, seq)` (a pre-1970 `at` clamped to 0), and the engagers currently "on" for likes and reposts (a map engager -> edge (atMs, engager, seq) of the effective on record, the last operation per (engager, target) wins, so counts are distinct engagers, plus an ascending array per target and kind only when it has 2 or more engagers, so a page is O(log n + page) reads); per author, the timeline (post seqs, replies and quotes included, plus the seqs of the repost records currently on) and the highest seq of a `profile` record and of a `username` record whose `member` is the author (hints, not verified here). `profileSeq` is the newest VALID profile for the seeder's own `validRecordCopy`: a seeder from before the avatar skips the 5-key block and points at the plain block of the compatibility pair (section 2), so hinted reads (`diary.peek` with `profileSeq`) show the name and bio but no avatar until the seeder is upgraded.
- **Its own scan.** For every admitted, gated diary the seeder reads the diary's own blocks from an index cursor (`entry.indexed`, from block 1, raw reads with `{ wait: false }`, `decodeBlock` + `validRecordCopy`) and hands each valid `post`, `like`, `repost`, `profile` or `username` record to the index. The cursor and its queue (`_indexQueue`: one diary at a time, at most one queued per diary) are separate from the media scan of section 5 "Seeder media admission", so indexing never delays media admission. The first pass starts after `ready`, never inside `start()`; each growth or gate pass of a diary queues another.
- **Bounded, never skipping.** Three caps bound memory, all constructor options (not CLI flags): `indexMaxRecords` (default `DEFAULT_MAX_RECORDS` = 1,000,000 records), `indexMaxTargets` (default `DEFAULT_MAX_TARGETS` = 500,000 distinct targets; target structures are allocated lazily) and `indexPerAuthorMax` (default `DEFAULT_PER_AUTHOR_MAX` = 50,000 records per diary, so a few diaries cannot starve the rest). When a record would exceed a cap, `add` refuses and that diary's cursor PAUSES at the refused block (`indexPaused`): nothing is skipped, the record stays pending. Whenever capacity is freed (an unlike or unrepost that empties a target, a dropped diary, which also frees the targets of its own posts) the index signals it and every paused diary resumes (once per tick). A block read that fails is retried with backoff (2 s, 10 s, 30 s, then every 60 s) without blocking other diaries. Reads cost O(log n + page): per-target counters are maintained on add/remove and the reply and engager cursors are found by binary search.
- **Honesty flags.** Every answer carries `indexing` (true until the first pass over every diary served at start finished; the set of those diaries is fixed before anything is served, in step 4 of startup) and `complete` (false while `indexing`, while the seeder is closing, or while any served, gated diary's cursor is paused or behind its contiguous length). Nothing is silent.
- **Moderation.** A diary leaves the index the moment its drop starts, on every runtime drop path (`Seeder#_drop`: `blocked` on blocklist adoption included, `fork`, `quota`, a revoked issuer, a failed local gate), before the purge starts: its edges, engager entries, posts and hints are removed and its records freed from the cap, together with the engagements other diaries aimed at its posts. If that diary is admitted again in the same process (an unblock and a new announce, or a new announce after a fork or quota drop), the index is rebuilt from every served diary (all cursors back to 1; `indexing: true` until the rebuild finishes; several re-admissions coalesce into one rebuild) so those engagements come back. At read time every author is filtered again (`_indexVisible`: served, gated, not dropped, admitted, not blocked), so blocked, dropped or ungated authors never appear in a list, a count or a hint.
- **Reads** (back the route of section 7): `indexPost(authorHex, seq, cursor)`, `indexCounts(refs)`, `indexMember(authorHex, before)` and `indexEngagers(kind, authorHex, seq, cursor)` -> a plain object or `null` (anything unknown). `noticeRows(author, now, visible)` is the new read, and it backs notices channel v3 only (not an HTTP route): likes and reposts aimed at the author's indexed posts, newest first, with no count (section 3, "Notices channel v3").
- `stats()` adds `indexRecords` (records held) and `indexSaturated` (true at the cap).
- **Trust model.** Refs, seq hints, counts, completeness, the order and the omissions of a liker or reposter list, its total, whether a listed on record is still the engager's latest operation, and which record is the "latest" profile or username are claims of the seeder. A member node shows only posts, names and bios it fetched from the author's diary and verified (`diary.peek`, `docs/rpc.md`); a lying index can hide or invent counts and refs, never content.

### Seeder name index (Índice de nomes; `packages/core/lib/name-index.js`, `packages/core/lib/search-text.js`, `packages/core/lib/seeder.js`)

The seeder also answers "who is called this?" so a member can find an account by name or @nome and follow it. Like the engagement index, it only says WHERE TO LOOK; the name and @nome are read and verified from the diary (section 7 "Seeder index route").

- **In memory, fed by the existing index scan.** Nothing is persisted. The index scan of "Seeder engagement index" hands the newest `profile` record and the newest same-member `username` record of each diary to the name index; there is no second scan. Copied records (`member` different from the diary) are ignored. The `username` signature and the issuer delegation are verified once per pass, with no fallback to an older `username` record; a pending candidate keeps only the `username` schema fields. The same scan also hands the name index every `page-roster`, `page-close` and `page-moved` record (they never reach the engagement index). A shape-valid `page-roster` whose `page` is the diary's own key sets a boolean `page` hint for that author; a shape-valid `page-close` or `page-moved` whose `page` is the diary's own key sets `closed` or `moved` (with `page`) and forgets that author's display name and @nome, so search stops matching them (a moved page's @nome answers only on the new key, whose own `username` record indexes it). The hint is set only for a record that verifies for that log key (countersigned by the issuer, revoked issuers applied: the seeder's `verifyPage`), so an unsigned copy cannot label a person "Página" or hide a name; it holds three booleans inside the author's existing entry, and the app still confirms a page with `page.read`.
- **Moderation.** An author leaves the index synchronously when its drop starts (same paths as the engagement index) and the whole index is cleared on rebuild. Revocation is checked at every query: an @nome whose issuer is revoked stops matching without a rescan (the display name can still match), and a diary whose re-gate after a newly revoked issuer is still pending (`_onBlocklistAdopted` re-checks block 0's certificate asynchronously) is held out of results until that verdict clears it or the diary is dropped; the certificate an announcement carried is never used for this. At read time the author is filtered again (`_indexVisible`: served, gated, not dropped, admitted, not blocked).
- **Cap, never skipping.** At most `maxServed` authors (default 4096). A refusal at the cap pauses the scan (`complete: false`); nothing is skipped.
- **What it holds.** Folded tokens and the verified @nome only; never the raw name, the bio, the signature (after verification), the e-mail or the account subject.
- **Fold (`search-text.js`).** The first 160 UTF-16 units / 80 code points, NFKD, combining marks and Default_Ignorable characters dropped, `A-Z` lowercased, a small table (`ß`->`ss`, `æ`->`ae`, `œ`->`oe`, `ø`->`o`, `ł`->`l`, `đ` / `ð`->`d`, `þ`->`th`, `ı`->`i`, `ħ`->`h`, `ŧ`->`t`, `ŋ`->`n`, `ĸ`->`k`), every other character a separator. A name keeps at most 16 tokens of 20 characters. Names in other scripts are found only by @nome.
- **Query and order.** 1..4 typed words folded the same way, joined by `-`, at least 2 characters in total (so `@a_b` is searchable as `a-b`). Order: 0 exact @nome (the words joined by `_`), 1 @nome equal ignoring `_`, 2 @nome starting with the text, 3 every typed word starts a word of the name or of the @nome; ties by member key; at most 20 results; no cursor.
- **Trust model.** Order, omissions, completeness and which profile / username record is the newest are claims of the seeder, which may point at an older authentic record (a stale name; the same trust model as the member profile screen, `docs/rpc.md` `diary.peek`). A lying index can hide a member or list a wrong key, never forge a name or an @nome the app shows.
### Seeder topic index (Índice de assuntos; `packages/core/lib/topic-index.js`, `packages/core/lib/seeder.js`)

The seeder also keeps WHICH public posts contain WHICH whole words, so the Espiadinha can search by topic over every admitted diary (issue #81). Like the engagement index it only says WHERE TO LOOK: it holds no text, name or bio, only hints `<author>:<seq>`; the node reads and verifies the post itself (section 7 "Seeder index route").

- **Matching contract.** Text is folded (NFD, combining marks U+0300-U+036F stripped, then `toLowerCase`) and split on anything outside `[a-z0-9]` (`#` and `+` are separators like any other character); the tokens are the runs of 2..32 characters (`MIN_TOKEN`, `MAX_TOKEN`). `Céu`, `#Céu` and `CÉU!` all give `ceu`; `Céus` gives `ceus` and does NOT match `ceu`. A query is 1..8 (`MAX_QUERY_TOKENS`) distinct tokens and a post matches when it contains EVERY one of them (AND, whole tokens). There is no prefix or substring matching, no positions, no phrase query and no ranking. Letters outside `a-z` after folding (non-Latin scripts) produce no tokens and are not searchable. The app drops words beyond the eighth and says so.
- **In memory, rebuilt at start.** Nothing is persisted. `createTopicIndex({ maxPosts, perAuthorMax, maxTerms, maxPostings, perAuthorPostingsMax, perAuthorTermsMax })` is pure (no I/O, no timers, no method throws). Only `post` records (replies and quotes included) with text are indexed, each as one entry `{ atMs, author, seq, tokens }` shared by every posting list it is in; EVERY token of a post is indexed. A post whose `seq` is above `MAX_SEQ` = 999,999,999,999,999 (15 digits, the largest seq the route accepts) is refused like a cap. Posting lists are append-only with a lazy sort on the first query that touches a dirty list.
- **Its own scan.** For every admitted, gated diary the seeder reads the diary's blocks from a SEARCH cursor (`entry.topicIndexed`, from block 1, raw reads with `{ wait: false }`, `decodeBlock` + `validRecordCopy`) on its own queue (`_topicQueue`: one diary at a time, at most one queued per diary), its own startup set (`_topicStartup`) and its own flags (`_topicFlags`). It is separate from the engagement scan and from the media scan, so none can stall another. The first pass starts after `ready`, never inside `start()`; each growth or gate pass of a diary queues another. A block read that fails is retried with the same backoff as the engagement scan (2 s, 10 s, 30 s, then every 60 s).
- **Bounded, never skipping.** Six caps bound memory, all constructor options of `Seeder` (not CLI flags): `topicMaxPosts` (default 200,000 indexed posts), `topicPerAuthorMax` (default 10,000 posts per diary, 5% of the post cap), `topicMaxTerms` (default 500,000 distinct tokens), `topicMaxPostings` (default 4,000,000 (token, post) slots), and two per-diary shares of the global budgets so one diary cannot fill them for everyone: `topicPerAuthorPostingsMax` (default 200,000 slots held per diary, `DEFAULT_PER_AUTHOR_POSTINGS_MAX`) and `topicPerAuthorTermsMax` (default 25,000 tokens CREATED per diary, `DEFAULT_PER_AUTHOR_TERMS_MAX`, counted when an accepted post introduces a token nobody had; tokens it created that other diaries also use stay when it leaves), each 5% of its global cap. When an add would exceed a cap it is refused (atomically: no partial write) and that diary's SEARCH cursor PAUSES at the refused block (`topicPaused`): nothing is skipped and the record stays pending. A search pause never touches the engagement index (`indexPaused`, its `complete` flag) and an engagement pause never touches the search index. Whenever room is freed (a dropped diary) every paused search cursor resumes once per tick; `add` is idempotent, so the paused block is simply read again. The search index is never rebuilt wholesale: a diary that is admitted again after a drop is scanned from block 1 like any new diary. A refusal by a per-diary share pauses only that diary's cursor; the others keep indexing. Memory: no stored string keeps a post's text alive (every token is canonicalized through the term table as a flat copy, so a V8 substring slice never pins the folded text). Measured on Node 24 (retained heap after a full GC): about 240 B per post for the core test's 50,000 posts of 4 words from 500 diaries (11.2 MB, ~59 B per (token, post) slot), and about 160-260 B per post for 4,000 posts of one word plus 986 non-Latin code points (1,000 code points each), against ~12,200 B before the canonicalization. With every default cap full (200,000 posts, 500,000 tokens, 4,000,000 slots) the security audit measured about 264 MB retained (about 320 B per token and 20 B per slot dominate); a retained-heap figure, not an RSS guarantee, to add to the engagement index's when sizing a seeder. A query picks the token list with the fewest entries not dated beyond the future limit, so hidden future posts cannot steer it onto a long list.
- **Honesty flags.** Every answer carries `indexing` (true until the first search pass over every diary served at start finished; that set is fixed before anything is served) and `complete` (false while `indexing`, while the seeder is closing, or while any served, gated diary's search cursor is paused or behind its contiguous length). Nothing is silent. A page can also be empty or short while carrying a `next` (below).
- **Moderation.** A diary leaves the search index SYNCHRONOUSLY inside `Seeder#_drop`, before the purge starts, on every runtime drop path (`blocked`, blocklist adoption included, `fork`, `quota`, a revoked issuer, a failed local gate): `removeDiary` runs in the same turn as the drop, with no timer. The blocklist still reaches an existing connection at most once per 10 s (`MIN_SNAPSHOT_INTERVAL_MS`, `packages/core/lib/blocklist-net.js`); that is a message interval, not a propagation deadline, and the removal does not wait for it. At read time every author is filtered again by `_indexVisible` (served, gated, not dropped, admitted, not blocked), and a post dated more than `MAX_FUTURE_MS` (86,400,000 ms, as the feed) after the seeder's clock is hidden, never removed, so it appears once the clock reaches it. As in account search ("Seeder name index"), a diary whose re-gate after a newly revoked issuer is still pending (`_onBlocklistAdopted` re-checks block 0's certificate asynchronously; `entry.searchHold`, `Seeder#_searchHeld`) is held out of results until that verdict clears it or the diary is dropped, and an answer that left a held match out says `complete: false`; a query the held diary does not match is unaffected.
- **Reads** (back the route of section 7): `indexTopic(path, cursor)` with `path` the `+`-joined tokens -> `{ items, next, authors, indexing, complete }` or `null` (malformed query or cursor, or a throw). A query walks only the SHORTEST posting list among its tokens, newest first, testing the other tokens against each entry, and stops after `SEARCH_PAGE` = 30 hits or `SEARCH_SCAN` = 2000 candidates examined; the cursor is found by binary search, so a page costs O(log n + 2000) whatever the corpus. Entries dated more than `MAX_FUTURE_MS` after the seeder's clock sort as newest; a second binary search starts the walk below them, so they are never examined and never consume the 2000-candidate budget (a query whose list holds only such entries answers `{ items: [], next: null }`). A short or empty page may therefore carry a `next`; ONLY `next === null` means the end. Order is `at` descending, then author ascending, then `seq` descending (`compareNewestFirst`); `authors` gives, for each listed author, the `profileSeq` / `usernameSeq` hints of the engagement index. No follow filtering and no `ageBand` filter.
- `stats()` adds `topicPosts` (posts held) and `topicSaturated` (true when a cap refused an add and diaries are paused).
- **Trust model.** Refs, hint seqs, completeness and the order are claims of the seeder. The member node fetches every ref with `diary.peek`, re-checks the words in the VERIFIED text itself (a ref whose text lacks them is dropped) and shows names only from verified peek fields. A lying index can hide or mislist posts, never forge text.
- **Privacy.** The seeder learns the words a device searches, together with the connection's address. The seeder logs nothing per request (section 7 "Seeder logs") and the public Caddy keeps no access log. BUT Caddy's error log is left on for every route (the only global `log` block in `deploy/seeder/Caddyfile` merely excludes the rate-limit logger), and when the proxy to the seeder fails (the seeder restarting or crashed, a deadline) it can record the request URI, which for this route contains the search words, together with the client address. Closing that gap needs a Caddy log filter, not yet done (docs/verification.md section 25 "Residual risks"). The words are not sent to the association or account service.
- **Not implemented.** (1) Age filtering: every v2 certificate is `unchecked` (`packages/core/lib/cert.js`) and the MVP is 18+, so the search cannot filter by age band. (2) Trending topics ("assuntos do momento"): any popularity order is a hidden weight.

### Seeder follow index (Contagens e listas de seguidores; `packages/core/lib/notices.js`, `packages/core/lib/seeder.js`)

By owner decision, follower counts and lists are PUBLIC: anyone on the association site or in the app tied to the installation sees, for any account, "N seguindo" and "N seguidores" and can open each list. Follow records were already public in every diary; this only makes them easy to read. The notices index (section 3 "Notices channel") already folds every diary's follow records, and the seeder serves it through `GET /index/follows/<author>` and `GET /index/follows/<author>/(seguindo|seguidores)[/<cursor>]` (section 7 "Seeder index route"): `Seeder#indexFollowCounts` and `Seeder#indexFollows`, reading `followCounts` and `followsPage` of `notices.js` from committed state only (a half-scanned diary never shows a follow its later unfollow would cancel).

- **Omit rule.** Only accounts the index may show count and are listed (admitted, served, gated, not blocked); a blocked member is in no list and no count, and an author held for a pending re-gate (`_searchHeld`) is the generic 404.
- **Caps.** At most 4096 follows per author (`MAX_FOLLOW_TARGETS_PER_AUTHOR`) and at most 64 non-member targets per author (`MAX_NONMEMBER_TARGETS_PER_AUTHOR`); further ones are dropped silently, and the author's `seguindo` list is marked incomplete (a target that joins later may be missing until that diary is read again). The caps only bound what the index keeps; they are not what makes a count lower. Counts and rows include only keys that pass `_indexVisible` (admitted, served, gated, not blocked members of this seeder), so an account outside the association never appears and the seeder's "seguindo" can be lower than the own "N seguindo" of the Diário, which counts every follow from the local node.
- **Incomplete rule.** `complete` is `false` while the seeder is still reading diaries (`indexing`, or any diary pending) and, for a `seguindo` list or count, when that diary hit a cap; for `seguidores`, while any diary is pending. The app then shows "A lista pode estar incompleta." under a list and "A contagem pode estar incompleta." under the counts of a profile. Known limit, by owner decision: a follower whose own diary hit a cap (`MAX_FOLLOW_TARGETS_PER_AUTHOR` or `NOTICES_MAX_EDGES` = 262144) can be missing from a `seguidores` list that says complete; the note only covers the scan in progress.
- **Order.** By the follow's `at`, clamped at 0, newest first, then key. `at` is author-supplied, so a follow dated in the future stays at the top of both lists.
- **Trust model.** The list only says where to look. The app re-verifies every row with `diary.peek` of the signed follow record (`type: 'follow'`, `on: true`, the same `at` and key) before it appears. A lying seeder can omit rows or point at an older authentic record (a follow that a later unfollow cancelled); it cannot forge a follow.
- **Privacy.** The seeder learns which profile's list was opened (the path carries the author key, like the other `/index` routes). The proof-gated notices channel is unchanged: Avisos stays private and shows no tally. Someone who polls a public list over time can infer, by difference, that a follower was blocked or held for a re-gate (the row leaves the list and the count, and a hold also turns `complete` false); the signed blocklist itself already reaches every member node, and the engagers and account search routes behave the same way.
- **Not hosted.** With manual configuration (`varanda-serve`) there is no seeder index, so no counts or lists beyond the node's own "N seguindo".

### Seeder testimonial index (Depoimentos; `packages/core/lib/engagement-index.js`, `packages/core/lib/seeder.js`)

A member's device cannot see who wrote it a depoimento without reading every diary, so the seeder points at the records (section 2, "Depoimentos"). Like the other indexes it only says WHERE TO LOOK: it holds no text and no sealed box, only keys and seqs, and the node reads and verifies every record itself (section 7 "Seeder index route", `GET /index/testimonials/<owner>`).

- **Inputs.** `INDEX_TYPES` includes `testimonial` and `testimonial-show`, so the index scan of section 5 "Seeder engagement index" hands both over, and they count in the same record budget and per-author cap. In memory only, rebuilt at start.
- **Lists, per owner O.** `pending`: per writer, the newest live `testimonial` with `to === O`, unless O ever wrote a `testimonial-show` of that block (live or tombstoned); a block dated more than 24 h ahead waits for the clock. `shown`: one row per writer, O's newest show decides (as `shownRows`), listed only when it is live, its writer block is live, indexed and passes `verifyShow` (hash and `to === O`), and `from` is not O; a show whose writer block the seeder does not hold is not listed. At most 64 each, newest first.
- **Withdrawal.** A tombstone of either record removes it: the writer's tombstone of its `testimonial` drops it from `pending` and drops the owner's show of it from `shown`; the owner's tombstone of its `testimonial-show` drops it from `shown`, and that writer block never returns to `pending` (the seeder remembers every writer block the owner ever showed, live or tombstoned); only a new `testimonial` block from that writer is pending again. Removing a writer from the profile tombstones every show of that writer, so an older show never comes back. Residual: a seeder that restarts without holding a tombstoned show's content can list that block as pending again; the app's per-device hide still applies.
- **Omit rule.** As the follow index: only owners and writers the index may show (`_indexVisible`: admitted, served, gated, not blocked) appear; anything else is the generic 404 or is left out.
- **Trust model.** The answer is a hint. A lying seeder can omit a pointer or point at a withdrawn or forged block; it cannot make a forged or revoked depoimento appear, because the node re-reads both blocks and applies `verifyShow`, and it cannot read a pending text, which only the recipient's box key opens.
- **Privacy.** The seeder learns which profile's depoimentos were opened (the path carries the owner key, like the other `/index` routes). That X wrote a sealed depoimento to Y is already public in X's diary; the index only makes it easy to find.
- **Not hosted.** With manual configuration (`varanda-serve`) there is no seeder index, so no pending list and no shown list beyond what the node reads itself.

### Seeder deletions (tombstones; `packages/core/lib/seeder.js` `_applyDelete`, `packages/core/lib/deletion.js`)

The seeder applies the `delete` record of section 2 to what it serves and indexes. Per diary it keeps the set of deleted seqs and the media ranges that effective tombstones listed, evaluated with the reader rule of section 2 (highest block first), so the result does not depend on the order in which blocks arrived.

- A deleted target leaves the engagement, topic and name indexes (`removeRecord`, `removePost`, `forget`; a deleted profile version falls back to the newest surviving profile the engagement index holds, if any; while that index is behind or paused the name stays out of the search until it catches up or is rebuilt). A like, repost, reply or quote that points at a deleted target is NOT indexed, and the counts omit it, including on a rebuild and after a paused index cursor resumes (a deleted seq the cursor never reached is skipped, not waited for).
- `Seeder#postBlock` (`/moderation-post`) answers `null` for a seq deleted by the SAME fold readers apply (`entry.tombstones`, highest seq first, a deleted tombstone has no effect), so the route is the generic 404 and the account service never receives a deleted post, while a post that readers show again (its tombstone deleted by a later one) stays readable by moderation. A post deleted after a report is not available for moderation either.
- `mediaRead` (`/media`) answers nothing for a range with a block that an effective tombstone listed and that no surviving post or profile of the same diary still shows, and re-checks that before every block it sends, so a range deleted meanwhile stops the answer. The seeder keeps per-block counters only for media cores the diary declared earlier in its log (ranges and descriptors in other cores are ignored), so a check costs the requested blocks and the state is bounded by the declared cores, never by the number of tombstones. For an honest diary (writers list only ranges no surviving record overlaps) this equals `deletedRanges` (`lib/deletion.js`). At most `MEDIA_TRACK_MAX` (2 x `MAX_MEDIA_CORES`) cores are counted per diary; a bound core that is not counted is refused, and when a scan ends with a bound core uncounted (an older device core declared again) the seeder recounts that diary's media once over its local blocks with the bound cores pinned (bounded per serve; refused while it runs). A diary that uses a media core before declaring it, or that tombstones a tombstone, is IRREGULAR: `/media` refuses every range of its cores until the next serve (honest writers never do either). `/media` and `/moderation-post` answer nothing until the diary's scan has reached its signed length, and `/moderation-post` never answers a seq the scan has not reached.
- Not applied by the seeder in this step: a tombstone that targets a `follow` or `media-core` record (the writers never emit one); a tombstone that targets a `username` record leaves the account search keeping the name; and a tombstone of a tombstone leaves the original target out of the seeder indexes (search, counts, member pages) while readers show it again; `/moderation-post` follows the readers, and `/media` is refused for the whole diary (irregular, above). Withdrawing a deleted like from the index does not fall back to an older surviving like of the same member.
- Unless `VARANDA_NO_CLEAR=1` (section 2 "Clearing deleted bytes"), the seeder discards the bytes of what the fold deletes. Before it serves or downloads anything, each diary clears the blocks its stored tombstones delete and the media ranges they name (`_prepareDiary`, then `_scheduleClear` / `_clearDiary` / `_clearMedia`, one clear at a time per diary, coalesced), reading only local blocks; the media counters decide per block of a declared media core, so a block a surviving post or profile still shows is kept. Diary and media downloads skip the deleted blocks (`downloadExcept`) and clear a block that arrives anyway. The seeder's scans, `blocks` and `bytes` statistics, admitted lengths and `have` use the present length, and a cleared block is a known hole for the index passes (they skip a deleted seq and stop at an unknown hole). `/media` ends a stream at a block cleared meanwhile, and the by-seq `diary.peek` of a cleared seq finds nothing (docs/rpc.md 5.27).

### Seeder startup (R5.6)

Lock before state (security fix cycle 2): `start()` takes the data-dir lock (opens the main store) BEFORE it reads admitted.json, so it never starts from a list a previous process was still writing; the main store still opens before the quarantine is wiped (R6.2), and the lock is still released last at shutdown. Step 1 (config, seeder key, seeder certificate) stays before the lock: a running seeder never writes those files and they are fully validated at start.

`start()` performs, in this order, and creates NO swarm and starts NO download before step 4 completes:

1. `Seeder.ensureConfig`; load `seeder-key.json` and `seeder-cert.json` and verify (errors of section 7, R5.2 f).
2. Load `admitted.json` into the `admitted` map (every listed key, including keys a previous run left mid-drop); `store = await openStore(dataDir)` FIRST (takes the RocksDB lock, fails fast when another process holds the data dir); only THEN `qstore = await openQuarantine(dataDir)` (R6.2).
3. For each admitted key: `core = await openReplica(store, key)`; if `core.fork !== 0` -> startup drop with reason `'fork'`; else if `core.contiguousLength === 0` -> startup drop with reason `'no-cert'`; else if `core.length > maxLogBlocks || core.byteLength > maxLogBytes` -> startup drop with reason `'quota'` (review item 5: the quota is part of the startup gate, so an over-quota stored log, e.g. after a restart with a lowered limit, is purged BEFORE step 4 and its topic is never joined nor served); else LOCAL gate -> pass: keep; fail: startup drop with that reason. Startup drop (R6.1 order) = log `{ event: 'rejected', key, reason }`, `await purgeReplica(store, key)` FIRST, then remove the key from `admitted`, then `await persist()`. If any purge throws, the key stays in the map and in the file and `start()` throws `Error('falha ao limpar diário rejeitado: ' + key8)` after closing the stores (fail closed).
3b. For each listed media core K (section 5 "Seeder media admission"), once the diaries are settled: its binding must be usable and its diary a step-3 survivor (else `unbound`); then `openMediaReplica`; `fork !== 0` -> `fork`; `contiguousLength === 0` -> `no-header`; over `MAX_MEDIA_BLOCKS` / `MAX_MEDIA_BYTES` -> `quota`; else the LOCAL media gate (`header-invalid` / `wrong-diary`). A pass beyond `maxMedia` survivors is purged with the event `{ event: 'warning', key, reason: 'media-full' }`; a failure logs `{ event: 'media-rejected', key, diary, reason }`. Both are purged FIRST, then removed from the `media` map, then `persist()`; a purge throw makes `start()` throw `Error('falha ao limpar mídia rejeitada: ' + key8)`.
4. For every surviving key: `watchFork`, live download, `watchContiguous`; every surviving media core is served the same way (step 5 joins its discovery key). A runtime `drop` that happens here (no swarm yet) skips the `swarm.leave` (`drop` is safe before the swarm exists).
5. `createSwarm({ bootstrap, keyPair, host })`; `done = store.findingPeers()`; join `topicFor(association)` + every surviving `discoveryKey` with `joinAndFlush` in try/finally `done()`; log `{ event: 'ready', topic, admitted }`.

The swarm uses the loaded `seeder-key.json` pair, so `socket.remotePublicKey` seen by members equals `cert.seeder`. Every seeder `hello` carries `seeder-cert.json` as `cert`.

### Seeder shutdown (`close()`), the data-dir lock released last

(1) stop the re-discovery, every replica watcher and every download range; (2) destroy the swarm; (3) close the quarantine store, wait for every in-flight admission step, `removeQuarantine(dataDir)`; (4) wait for every drop (purge) in progress; (5) close the main store, which releases the data-dir lock. A `seed` that takes the lock the instant it is released (and wipes/recreates `<dataDir>/quarantine` in its step 2) keeps its quarantine intact.

### Runtime purge failure (fail closed, R3 b / R4.7 / R5.5 e)

A purge throw at runtime (in `drop`, or propagated by `quarantineGet0` on the announce path) logs `{ event: 'fatal', key, reason: 'purge-failed' }` and closes the seeder. Corestore serves any stored core on request through its `ondiscoverykey`/`_attachMaybe` path, so a stored non-admitted core must never stay reachable; because a dropped key stays in `dropping` (and therefore in `admitted.json`) until its purge succeeds (R6.1), the next `start()` re-gates and re-purges it (step 3). Invariant (restated): every core in the main store is listed in `admitted.json` at every instant.

Moderation and issuer revocation (B2): the current blocklist snapshot is consulted before anything is admitted, queued or fetched, and re-checked after each await of an admission (section 3 "Enforcement").

## 6. Member sync (`packages/core/lib/sync.js` `runSync`) and the `--once` completion rule

Open, in this order, the member store (`store = await openStore(dataDir)` FIRST: it takes the RocksDB lock and fails fast when another process holds the data dir), `config.json` (trust root), the diary, only THEN the quarantine store (`qstore = await openQuarantine(dataDir)`, R6.2: a second `sync` on the same `--data` fails on the lock before touching `<dataDir>/quarantine`), and gate every follow (below) before any swarm. `cert` = block 0 (read raw and decoded with `decodeBlock`) when `certForKey(block0.cert, ownHex, association).ok`, else warn `sem certificado válido no bloco 0: o diário não será anunciado`. `runSync` takes `host` (R5.11).

Shutdown order (the `finally` of `runSync`, after the result is settled or on a throw), the data-dir lock released LAST: (1) stop every timer, the re-discovery, the diary and replica watchers, and destroy every download range; (2) destroy the swarm; (3) close the quarantine store, wait for every in-flight quarantine fetch, `removeQuarantine(dataDir)`; (4) wait for every pending purge and local re-gate; (5) close the main store, which releases the data-dir lock.
