A `sync` that takes the lock the instant it is released (and wipes/recreates `<dataDir>/quarantine` at its start) keeps its quarantine intact. `Seeder.close()` follows the same order (section 5).

### Member follow gate (R5.8)

(a) At `runSync` start, before any swarm and after the main store and then the quarantine store are open (R6.2), for each followed key K: `core = await openReplica(store, K)`;
- `core.fork !== 0` -> `purgeReplica` + skipped `'fork'`;
- `core.contiguousLength === 0` -> `await purgeReplica(store, K)` (an empty core must not exist in the main store) and K is PENDING (NOT skipped, nothing stored);
- otherwise LOCAL gate (section 5) -> pass: ACTIVE (live download `core.download({ start: 0, end: -1, linear: true })`, in block order, because `readAll` reads `0..contiguousLength-1` and a random-order range stores blocks nobody can read until the whole log is here; inventory entry with `contiguousLength`); fail: `purgeReplica` + skipped reason. A locally stored valid block 0 passes; it is never purged merely because it was read as raw bytes. The browser node's own-diary recovery range (`packages/web-member/lib/sync.js`) uses the same option, and seeder ranges (section 5, `packages/core/lib/seeder.js`) are unchanged.

(b) Pending keys are advertised with length 0 (R4.5). The remote gate for K starts ONLY when some connected peer's `remoteHave` reports K with length >= 1 (from hello, have or admitted) and no fetch for K is in flight: `sockets` = the `socket` of every connected peer whose latest advertisement of K is >= 1; `timeoutMs` = in `--once` mode `deadline - Date.now()` (deadline = start + `timeoutMs` of the run, see One deadline below; a fetch starts only while at least `GATE_MIN_TIMEOUT_MS = 250` ms remain, and never runs past the deadline), in long-running mode `GATE_TIMEOUT_MS = 15000`; `{ promise, addSocket } = startQuarantineFetch(qstore, K, sockets, timeoutMs)`, `r = await promise`. Late advertisers: while that fetch is in flight, every peer that then advertises K with length >= 1 (hello, have or admitted, after its own hello) is attached to the SAME in-flight fetch with `addSocket(peer.socket)`, so a working peer that arrives after a lying or empty advertiser can still supply block 0 before the deadline (the advertisement still counts as a new one for the retry rule below).

(c) Outcomes:
- `r.error === 'timeout'` -> K stays pending, `errorsSoft.push('bloco 0 indisponível: ' + key8)`; no message is sent and nothing is purged (nothing was stored). In `--once` mode a new fetch starts only when a new advertisement of K arrives and time remains; in long-running mode also every `GATE_RETRY_MS = 15000` while some connected peer still advertises K >= 1.
- A non-null verdict reason -> K FAILED: `skipped.push({ key: K, reason })`, `onEvent({ event: 'skipped', key: K, reason })`, broadcast `rejected { key: K, reason }` (R5.1 a). K is excluded from `hello.have`, from every goal and from `downloaded`.
- `null` -> K passes the REMOTE gate: `core = await openReplica(store, K)` (the main store now creates it; block 0 is re-downloaded from the connected advertiser(s)), `watchFork`, live download `core.download({ start: 0, end: -1, linear: true })` (in block order, because `readAll` reads `0..contiguousLength-1` and a random-order range stores blocks nobody can read until the whole log is here), `watchContiguous`. If the run started ending (deadline, abort, completion) or K left `pending` while that `openReplica` was in flight, the replica is closed and PURGED (review item 8: the main store already holds the core, and corestore serves stored cores); the purge is tracked like every other (the result waits for it), and a purge failure follows the purge-failure rule of (e) (`ok: false` with `falha ao limpar diário rejeitado: <key8>` while the result is not built yet; after it, a warning `aviso: falha ao limpar diário <key8>` through `log`, and the store is still closed only after the purge settled). Otherwise K is then in state `regate` (below) until block 0 arrives in the main store and passes the LOCAL gate; only then is it ACTIVE: `have` broadcast with `contiguousLength`, `onEvent({ event: 'downloaded', key: K, length })`, `downloaded[K] = core.contiguousLength`.

(d) `skipped` reasons are exactly `malformed | member-mismatch | untrusted-association | bad-issuer-signature | bad-signature | not-in-log | fork | quota` (never `timeout`).

(f) Log quota (S3): once a replica is open in the main store (`regate` or `active`), its signed `length` and `byteLength` advance on the upgrade proof (hypercore emits `'append'` before the blocks arrive); on every `'append'`, on every growth and once at open, `core.length > MAX_LOG_BLOCKS || core.byteLength > MAX_LOG_BYTES` fails the follow with reason `quota`: the download range is destroyed, `rejected { key, reason: 'quota' }` is broadcast, K is `skipped`. Chosen outcome: an ACTIVE replica keeps the blocks already verified (no purge; the next run re-gates it at startup, hits the quota again and skips it again, so the data dir never downloads past the limit), a replica still in `regate` holds nothing verified and is purged. `runSync` takes `maxLogBlocks` / `maxLogBytes` (tests only).

(e) A purge throw inside `quarantineGet0` or `purgeReplica` during sync is fatal for the run: `ok: false`, `errors: ['falha ao limpar diário rejeitado: ' + key8]`, then the usual cleanup.

### Follow entry states (as built)

- `pending`: nothing stored; advertised with length 0; the remote gate runs when a peer advertises K with length >= 1.
- `regate`: the remote gate passed and the replica is open in the main store, but block 0 has not yet arrived there and passed the LOCAL gate; still advertised with length 0, absent from `downloaded`, G1 unmet (`'<K>:0/<theirs>'`). On the first growth of `contiguousLength` (or at once if block 0 is already there) the local gate runs (`core.get(0, { wait: false, raw: true })` + the verdict). Pass -> `active`. Fail -> the advertiser served a different block 0 to the main store than to the quarantine: the run records the HARD error `'bloco 0 divergente: <key8> (<reason>)'` (a local-gate throw counts as `not-in-log`), K is skipped with that reason (`skipped`, `onEvent skipped`, broadcast `rejected { key: K, reason }`) and purged; a hard error makes the result `ok: false` with that string in `errors` (first) even when every goal holds, without ending a long-running run.
- `active`: local gate passed; live download `core.download({ start: 0, end: -1, linear: true })`, in block order, because `readAll` reads `0..contiguousLength-1` and a random-order range stores blocks nobody can read until the whole log is here; advertised with `contiguousLength`, listed in `downloaded`. The browser node orders this download newest-first for followed diaries (below, "Browser node download order").
- `failing`: gate failed or fork: its close + purge is in flight; excluded from goals and from `downloaded`, but `--once` completion (and the timeout result) waits until the purge settles, so the result reflects it.
- `failed`: skipped; excluded from every goal.

### One deadline (`--once`, review item 7)

`deadline = Date.now() + timeoutMs` is computed ONCE, when `runSync` is entered, before any startup I/O (store, diary, quarantine, the follow gate and its purges, the join). Both the completion timer (armed after the stores are open, with the REMAINING time `max(0, deadline - Date.now())`) and every gate fetch use that same deadline, so a slow startup never extends the run beyond `timeoutMs` (plus the time to settle in-flight purges and shut down).

### Startup interruption

In `--once` mode the deadline, and in both modes a `signal` abort, interrupt network startup: `joinAndFlush(swarm, topics)` is raced against the run's completion, so a stalled DHT join (unreachable bootstrap) never delays the result. When the deadline or the abort wins, the run finishes with the timeout result (`--once`: `['sem pares']` when nobody sent hello) or the abort result (long-running: `ok: true`), the swarm is destroyed in the `finally`, and the stores are closed as usual.

The `feed` command keeps its own gate (defense in depth).

### End-of-run purges (security fix cycle 2)

- The startup purge of a follow whose stored replica is empty or fails the startup gate is tracked like the runtime purges: an abort or deadline while it is pending waits for it, and a failure makes the result `ok: false` with `'falha ao limpar diário rejeitado: ' + key8`.
- When the run ends (success, timeout, abort or failure), every replica still in `regate` (opened in the main store after the quarantine gate, block 0 not yet through the local gate in this run) is closed and purged before the result is built; the purge is tracked, so the main store closes only after it settles. The key is reported as pending (unadvertised or unmet), not skipped; a purge failure makes the result `ok: false` (after the result exists, a failure in the `finally` path is only logged).

### Forks on followed keys (R5.9 d)

`onFork` for an ACTIVE K -> destroy its range, broadcast `rejected { key: K, reason: 'fork' }`, `purgeReplica`, remove K from the inventory and from `downloaded`, `skipped.push({ key: K, reason: 'fork' })` + `onEvent({ event: 'skipped', key: K, reason: 'fork' })`. At startup a forked replica is handled by (a).

### Connections and tracking

Join the topics of section 4. On each connection: replicate, `attachAdmission(role 'member', have = the member inventory of section 5: own diary length + active replicas' contiguousLengths + pending follows with length 0)`, then if `cert`: send `announce { key: own, cert }` once. Track per peer: `peerKey` (lowercase hex of `socket.remotePublicKey`, the Noise-authenticated static key), `remoteRole`, `remoteCert`, `verifiedSeeder`, `remoteCount`, `inventoryComplete`, `remoteHave` (latest-wins, section 5, R5.1 b), `admittedOwn`, `rejectedOwn` reason. Watch every core's `contiguousLength`: send `have` to all peers on growth and re-evaluate.

Verified seeders (R5.3 c): when a hello arrives, `peer.verifiedSeeder = peer.remoteRole === 'seeder' && seederCertForKey(peer.remoteCert, peer.peerKey, association).ok`, with `association` the member's fixed trust root. A peer claiming `'seeder'` that is not verified is treated exactly like a member for `peers`, G1 and G3, is excluded from `seeders`, its `admitted` for the own key is ignored and its `rejected` is a withdrawal only.

Withdrawals (R5.1 d): `admitted`/`rejected` for the own key count ONLY from verified seeders. A `rejected` for the own key from any other peer is only a withdrawal: `onEvent({ event: 'withdrawn', key, peerKey, reason })`, stdout line `withdrawn <key> <peerKey8> <reason>`, no effect on `ok` (its `remoteHave` entry is already deleted, R5.1 b).

Events: `{ event: 'peer', role, peerKey, verified }` (`verified` = `peer.verifiedSeeder`; `false` for members).

A peer that disconnects (`onclose`) is removed from `peers` and from every goal at once.

After flush + `done()`: grace = wait up to 5000 ms for `hello` from every currently connected peer; `peers` = connected peers that sent `hello`; `seeders` = number of connected verified seeders (R5.3 d). Goal evaluation waits for `inventoryComplete` of every peer that sent `hello` (R4.4).

### Peek link (Espiadinha, browser node only)

`peek.read` ([rpc.md](rpc.md), 5.22) computes discovery pages in the browser from the configured seeder's diary inventory, or, when the seeder offers it, from candidate pages of its discovery channel. `packages/core/lib/peek.js` defines the pure rule; `packages/web-member/lib/peek.js` provides the transport and memory. No persistent replica or storage core is opened and no key-value entry is written.

- **Link.** The first page opens a second, short-lived WebSocket connection to the configured seeder with an ephemeral Noise key. It sends `{ type: 'hello', v: 1, role: 'member', count: 0, have: [] }` (valid under section 5 "Bounded inventory"), no `announce`, and never attaches `varanda/blocklist/1`: the seeder's channel open is rejected as for a pre-B2 node (section 3). Before any core replication, the hello MUST have `role === 'seeder'` and `seederCertForKey(remoteCert, peerKey, association).ok`, using the Noise key as in "Connections and tracking". The admission open carries `CAP_NO_INVENTORY` (section 4) and the member attaches `varanda/discovery/1` (member role, "Discovery channel") on the muxer before the hello. When the verified seeder's open carries `CAP_DISCOVERY` and the discovery channel opens, the link is BOUNDED: it resolves without an inventory (the seeder's hello is lean) and candidates come in pages (below). Otherwise (a seeder from before discovery, or one run with discovery off, ignores the bit and rejects the channel) the link waits for inventory completion exactly as before, then checks `remoteHave.size < remoteCount`; completion by the 5 s inventory timeout leaves the page `incomplete`. A seeder that offers discovery but never opens the channel leaves the page `incomplete` with the (lean) inventory it sent. Socket, Noise, hello and inventory (or the discovery open) share a 15 s connection deadline; failure is `USAGE` `'sem conexão com o semeador'`.
- **Candidates and gate.** Candidates are distinct advertised diary keys with length >= 2, excluding the effective follow set and blocked keys. The viewer's own diary stays a candidate and is fetched like any other, so the keys opened on the anonymous link stay the complement of the follows and the blocklist and do not single the member out. It is paged exactly as if shown (same rounds, `needs`, windows and `next`) and its items (already in A Rua) are removed only from the page returned to its owner, and it counts in neither `diaries` nor `skipped`. In another member's Espiadinha it is a candidate like any other, so a stranger's repost of the viewer's post still shows. In the browser, the effective follows come from the own diary's `follow` records plus pending operations. Fetch order is random per snapshot so cutoffs do not systematically favour the same keys. On a bounded link the snapshot is built from candidate BATCHES instead of the whole inventory: the first page asks one discovery page of `PEEK_CANDIDATES` = 48 entries (the seeder's frozen view: eligible diaries, newest post first), filters it with the same rule (hex64, length hint >= 2, not followed, not blocked, the own diary kept) and shuffles it as batch 0; a later batch is asked only when the page needs it (see Pages), on the read's link with the view id of the first answer, and gets the next batch number. A key already in the snapshot is ignored, so a repeated key never makes a second row. The lengths in a discovery page are unsigned hints exactly like the inventory's: they only pick candidates. Each candidate first replicates ONLY block 0 into a memory Hypercore and passes the section 5 block-0 gate with the stored signed moderation snapshot's revoked issuers. Unavailable block 0 skips the diary and makes the page incomplete. Forks (`fork !== 0`) are refused, and `MAX_LOG_BLOCKS` / `MAX_LOG_BYTES` are enforced against the signed `length` / `byteLength` verified by Hypercore.
- **Window and history.** After the gate, fetch blocks 1..2 and the newest 8, then older windows 8 at a time as needed, up to 64 distinct blocks per diary including block 0. The window MUST use the length Hypercore verified with block 0, NEVER the unsigned advertised length (which only selects candidates). Requests within a window run highest seq first. Every later window uses a fresh memory core and accepts blocks only while its verified length AND `treeHash` match the first window's state. A different history, including growth since that state, stops extension of the diary for this snapshot (`changed`, page incomplete); the new window is discarded and previously accepted posts remain. A fresh first page is required to bind a new state. The 64-block cap can hide older posts behind non-post records and makes the page incomplete when fewer than 3 posts were found without a full scan.
- **Rule.** At most 3 rounds (`PEEK_MAX_ROUNDS`): round 0 takes each diary's highest-seq valid publication, round 1 its second, round 2 its third. A publication is a `post` (replies and quotes included, carrying their `replyTo` / `quote` ref into the item) or a `repost` still in effect (the last operation per target within the fetched tail, which is contiguous up to the top, so an undo is never missed; a repost record below the tail is never used); `like` records never show (the order uses no engagement). "mais recente" means highest seq (last written), not greatest `at`. Within a round, sort by `at` descending, then author key ascending; future posts are skipped as R5.10 (more than 24 h ahead). `at` remains author-supplied. The pt-BR `PEEK_RULE` is: "A Espiadinha mostra a publicação mais recente de cada diário que você não segue, da mais nova para a mais antiga; depois a segunda e, por fim, a terceira mais recente de cada um, do mesmo jeito: sem curtidas, sem sugestões e sem pesos escondidos." Equality is per certified diary, not per person.
- **Pages and moderation.** Default 26 items, maximum 26 (`PEEK_LIMIT_DEFAULT`, `PEEK_LIMIT_MAX`). `after` continues in `(round asc, at desc, author asc)` order; `next` is the last item's cursor exactly when the page is full, otherwise `null`; the end is `next === null`. Fullness is judged before the viewer's own items are removed, so for the viewer a non-last page may hold up to 3 fewer items than `limit` and `next` may point at a removed item's position. On a deadline or budget cut, still-pending diaries count as exhausted for that read only: the final page is `incomplete` and keeps a cursor if full. Posts that would have sorted before that cursor require refreshing the first page. On a bounded snapshot the order is `(round asc, batch asc, at desc, author asc)` (`buildPeekPage` with `batchOf`), so a later batch never reorders what an earlier page showed, and a round that runs out before the page is full first loads the next batch (it still owes items to that round) instead of opening the next round. When that batch cannot be loaded (the per-read page bound, a cut, a timeout or a stale answer) the page ends `incomplete` with a usable cursor: its last item's, else the `after` it was given, else a start cursor (round 0, `at` `9999-12-31T23:59:59.999Z`, the viewer's own key, seq 1) that sorts before every item of the snapshot; the next read continues the round from there. The own key is held at batch 0 for the snapshot's life so that cursor stays valid, and the batch map keeps every key that ever entered the snapshot. EVERY page re-applies current follows and the stored blocklist and re-verifies both certificates AND username records against current revoked issuers, including pages served entirely from retained records. Names are best effort within the fetched head and tail; `profile.read` does not gain a local replica from a peek. Rows already displayed are not retracted until another load, as with `feed.read`.
- **Budgets and lifetime.** One 25 s page deadline, 10 s per block request bounded by the remaining page time, at most 16 workers (`PEEK_PARALLEL`). Each worker fetches block 0 ALONE and first, then keeps up to 3 window blocks in flight (`PEEK_WINDOW_INFLIGHT`), consumed strictly highest seq first so every rule below applies exactly as in a serial read; a request for a block that a consumed tombstone deleted is cancelled (its core session is closed), and every exit path closes those sessions before the core. Every received raw block counts immediately against the 16 MiB page budget; reaching it closes the link. A further request starts only while the bytes reserved for the requests in flight (`MAX_BLOCK_BYTES` each) keep the page under that budget, so the overshoot stays at one block per worker as before. Residual: the reservation assumes blocks of at most `MAX_BLOCK_BYTES`; a seeder serving certified diaries with oversized signed blocks (up to the 1 MiB frame guard) can raise the data in flight to `PEEK_PARALLEL` x 3 frames before the first oversized block ends its diary. A block over `MAX_BLOCK_BYTES` (16 KiB) ends its diary as `oversized`, page incomplete. Admission messages remain bounded by `MAX_PAYLOAD = 16384` bytes; these are separate from replicated diary blocks. Memory cores use 64 KiB RAM pages and close after each window. Between pages only copied block-0, profile, username, post and repost records plus fetched seq sets survive; fetching stops at the 32 MiB retained-record budget, page incomplete. Candidate pages (bounded link) go one at a time, paced by the discovery channel at one per `DISCOVERY_PACE_MS` = 250 ms (the seeder's refill rate) and at most `MAX_PAGES_PER_READ` = 8 per read, the first included, each bounded by its 5 s request timeout and by the page deadline; the client never resends one. A timeout or a closed channel and a stale answer (the view expired) end the read `incomplete` and make the next batch start from a fresh first candidate page; the snapshot keeps its diaries and batches, and keys the fresh view repeats are ignored, so no row is shown twice or skipped. A timer drops an idle snapshot after 5 minutes from its creation, even without another request. Reload, abort and close discard it; the UI may keep already rendered rows until refreshed or reloaded.
- **Concurrency and capacity.** One peek at a time; a concurrent call is `USAGE` `'espiadinha já em andamento'`. Disconnect aborts it and frees the slot immediately. The read does not preempt sync; only the serialized own-key lookup may preempt once, while the key is not cached or an identity change is pending. A continuation needing more blocks opens a new short link; one fully served from memory opens none. Each page needing a link occupies one extra external socket against the seeder's default `maxExternal = 32` (section 4), while every hosted member already holds one for sync. A busy seeder can refuse the peek. The page never rides the running sync's link, even when one is live: that link announced the member's diary and follows, and browsing over it would let the seeder bind the browsing to the member, so the short link with an ephemeral key that announces nothing is kept whatever the sync state (privacy over capacity; `docs/rpc.md` 5.22).
- **Transport bounds and overload.** The client wraps its WebSocket with `guardedWsStream` (`packages/web-member/lib/ws-stream.js`: a `FrameGuard` with `handshakeFrames` 1, because an initiator receives one handshake frame, and it records the WebSocket close code). The peek link, and the notices link, bound every incoming frame at `PEEK_MAX_FRAME` = 1 MiB (measured against a real seeder with 64 diaries x 200 posts and a media core: the largest legitimate frame was 11,665 bytes; a 4096-key inventory stand-in: 9,031 bytes). A frame declaring more cuts the link at its 3-byte header: the peek page is incomplete with the warning `'aviso: quadro grande demais do semeador'`. The sync link and the seeder link use `MAX_EXTERNAL_FRAME` (8 MiB + 64 KiB) instead, because a legitimate replication frame may be that large; over it the link is cut and sync reconnects. A seeder at capacity closes the WebSocket with code 1013 (section 7 "Seeder WebSocket entry"); the peek and notices links turn it into `USAGE` `'Semeador ocupado; tente novamente em instantes.'` (a close without a frame, 1006, stays `'sem conexão com o semeador'`), and sync logs `'aviso: semeador ocupado; tente novamente em instantes'`. Residual: the browser has already allocated the whole WebSocket message before the guard sees it, so the guard bounds the secret-stream copy and cuts early but cannot stop that allocation; and on the sync link frames up to `MAX_EXTERNAL_FRAME` are still accepted.
- **Data flows.** A seeder from before discovery needs no change and gives the same pages as before: no new protocol message or server-side ranking. A seeder offering discovery orders its view by each diary's newest post time, which is the rule's own order, not a ranking; it learns which candidate pages the link asked, which is how far the member scrolled, not which diaries it follows (the filtering is local). (The topic search of section 5 "Seeder topic index" is not a peek: it uses the HTTP index route, in date order, with no ranking, and every ref it returns is still read through the peek.) It logs one `peer` event per link with the ephemeral Noise key (section 7 "Seeder logs"). Empty inventory and no announce do not prevent correlation: the same IP requests the complement of the follows already exposed by the sync inventory. The own diary is requested like any other, so the request pattern does not identify the member. The association and account service receive no peek request. No discovery page or new diary replica is persisted on a server or on the device; ordinary seeder logs still apply. Media cores NEVER travel on the admission channel: items copy the post's `media` descriptors, and the app fetches photos on demand through the same-origin `GET /media/<author>/<core>/<start>/<blocks>` route as A Rua, verifies SHA-256 and keeps blob URLs only in memory; the existing HTTP cache can retain image bytes across reloads (section 8 "Residual risks"). The seeder serves them only while the diary/core binding holds and the diary is not blocked.

### Notices link (browser node only)

`notices.list` ([rpc.md](rpc.md), 5.28) is answered in the browser by `packages/web-member/lib/notices.js`, on a second short-lived WebSocket to the configured seeder, shaped like the peek link above, one per scroll of chained pages (see Link lifetime). No persistent replica or storage core is opened and no key-value entry is written.

- **Link.** An ephemeral Noise key, the protomux, the admission channel opened with `CAP_NO_INVENTORY` (section 5 "Capabilities": a seeder offering `CAP_DISCOVERY` answers with a lean hello and sends no `have` message; an older one, or one started with `discovery: false`, sends its full inventory, which the link ignores) and the hello `{ type: 'hello', v: 1, role: 'member', count: 0, have: [] }`, and the THREE notices channels (`'varanda/notices/3'`, `'varanda/notices/2'` and `'varanda/notices/1'`) attached BEFORE the hello (so protomux pairs the seeder's open). The link is used only after the seeder's hello verified (`role === 'seeder'` and `seederCertForKey(remoteCert, peerKey, association).ok`, as the peek link); no core is replicated and no proof is signed before that. Failure to connect or verify is `USAGE` `'sem conexão com o semeador'`. The read tries v3, then v2, then v1. Each newer channel carries every older kind, so a v3 reader needs no v2 or v1 page. A rejected open settles `supported` false at once and the read falls through without waiting; only a channel the seeder leaves unanswered is waited for, up to the request timeout. When the seeder rejects v3, or leaves it unanswered and v2 answers, the read uses v2 (follows, replies, quotes and mentions; no likes or reposts) and `activity` stays `true`. When it takes neither v3 nor v2 the read uses v1 (follows only) and says `activity: false`. The channels not used are closed. `activity` is `false` only when the rows came over v1: every other result, including an empty or incomplete one, and a v3 or v2 read, says `true`. A seeder that opens none of the three gives `supported: false` (one warning, `'aviso: o semeador ainda não envia avisos'`); one that has not answered yet gives an empty `incomplete` read. Direct messages are not on this channel: they travel on `'varanda/dm/1'` (section 11), and a notices page never carries one.
- **Link lifetime.** A read that ends with `next !== null` on a live link it still owns (not lost, aborted, cut by its budget, with no page left unanswered and every row core closed) keeps that link idle for `NOTICES_LINGER_MS = 2000`. A read with `after` whose own key equals the one the link proved adopts it with its channel and its proof: no new socket, no new channel support or proof round trip. The own key is compared after it is resolved for the new read, so a proof is never reused for another identity. A first page (no `after`), another own key, the idle link's loss, `abort` (which the session calls on every lock, identity change, reset and disconnect), `close` or the 2 s timer close it; a read ending with `next: null` closes it at once. The deadline, the byte budget, the memory cores and the evidence of each read are its own: a chained read starts them afresh, and nothing is resent.
- **Proof and pages.** The node signs the proof with the in-memory identity key (a locked identity rejects the read), then asks pages of 64 (`NOTICES_LIMIT_MAX`) in the seeder's order, at most `NOTICES_MAX_SEEDER_PAGES = 8` per read.
- **Verification per row** (the seeder's list is only a set of candidates; the `kind` it sends is a hint that is never trusted). A key that is the own key, blocked, already followed (the stored follows, follows only) or a `(key, seq)` listed twice is dropped. Otherwise a memory Hypercore of the follower's diary fetches block 0 ALONE first and passes the block-0 gate (section 5) with the stored signed moderation snapshot's revoked issuers, no fork and the quota on the verified `length` / `byteLength`; then the NAMED block `seq` (it must carry exactly the claimed `at`, and be a `follow` of the own key with `on: true`, a `post`, or on a v3 read a `like` or `repost` with `on: true`, as the next bullets), then blocks 1..2 and the newest 8 (`PEEK_TAIL_BLOCKS`), from the length Hypercore verified (never a seeder-claimed one). A later signed `follow` of the own key with `on: false` in that tail drops the row. `diaryPeek` then yields the follower's profile name and username under the same rules as the Espiadinha; a row failing any step is dropped, and a block that does not arrive makes the read `incomplete`.
- **Kind, per row, from the signed block.** The named block `seq` is the own key's signed `follow` with `on: true` (kind `follow`, with the follow rules above), a signed `post` with exactly the claimed `at` (a post dated more than 24 h ahead, `MAX_FUTURE_MS`, is dropped), or on a v3 read a `like` or `repost` (next bullet). For a post the browser derives the kind itself: `reply` when its `replyTo` names one of the member's own post blocks (the key is the member's and the `seq` is a `post` of the own diary), else `quote` when its `quote` does, else `mention` when `mentionTokens` of the signed text includes one of the usernames the member's own diary verifiably held (`heldUsernames`, the window of `MAX_HELD_USERNAMES = 64`, filtered with the CURRENT revoked issuers on every read); a post earning none is dropped. A v1 read lists follows only.
- **Like and repost, on a v3 read only.** The named block `seq` is the engager's signed `like` or `repost` with `on: true`, exactly the claimed `at`, and `to.author` equal to the member. `to.seq` must be one of the member's own post blocks (a seq the own diary still holds; a deleted post is not among them). A later signed record of the same type and the same `to` in the tail drops the row: `on: false` (an unlike or an unrepost) or a newer `on: true` (that newer block is the row, when the seeder lists it). One row per engager, post and kind, the highest `seq`. The row's `post` is copied from the signed `to` (`<author>:<seq>`), never taken from the seeder and never the engager's block `key:seq`. A row dated more than 24 h ahead (`MAX_FUTURE_MS`) is dropped. The kind is the signed record's type, never the hint. A v2 or v1 read drops the block.
- **Own diary.** A v2 or v3 read reads the member's own diary once, inside the serial step of the session (`ownNoticeContext`, `packages/web-member/lib/member-ops.js`), for its post seqs and held usernames; the result is cached on the diary's key and its two lengths and re-filtered per read. A v1 read opens no storage core. The cursor of a v2 or v3 read carries `seq`; a two-key cursor from a v1 read is read as `seq` 1, the lowest of its `(at, key)` group.
- **Budgets.** One 25 s read deadline (`NOTICES_DEADLINE_MS`), 10 s per block request, 8 rows verified in parallel (`NOTICES_PARALLEL`), at most 16 blocks per row (`NOTICES_MAX_BLOCKS_PER_ROW`, block 0 and the follow included; block 0 and the named block are fetched alone and first, the rest with up to 4 in flight per row, `NOTICES_WINDOW_INFLIGHT`, consumed in order, with the same reservation, cancellation and residual as the peek link), a 16 MiB byte budget charged per received block (`NOTICES_BUDGET_BYTES`; reaching it closes the link) and a block over `MAX_BLOCK_BYTES` ends its row. A cut leaves the unfinished rows for a later read (no verdict), the read `incomplete`, and the cursor never skips past a row not decided.
- **Evidence cache.** The retained evidence of verified rows (block 0, profile, username, and the row's follow, post, like or repost record) is kept in browser memory for `NOTICES_CACHE_TTL_MS = 300000` (5 minutes), at most `NOTICES_CACHE_MAX = 512` rows. Every read re-runs the verdict from it with the CURRENT revoked issuers, blocklist, follows and clock, so a cached name or verdict is never served as is. Reset, lock and abort drop the slot; `close` clears the cache.
- **Concurrency and capacity.** One read at a time (`'avisos já em andamento'`), its own slot beside the peek's. Each read, or each scroll of chained reads within the link lifetime, occupies one extra external socket against the seeder's default `maxExternal = 32` (section 4), like a peek. The read does not preempt sync.
- **Seen token.** Each returned row also carries `seen`, a 32-hex BLAKE2b token keyed by the in-memory identity secret over the own key, the follower key and `seq` ([rpc.md](rpc.md), 5.28), unchanged by kind: stable per identity, unlinkable to the author without the secret, and the only thing the app stores as seen.
- **Data flows.** No new HTTP route. The proof reveals to the seeder only what it already knows: the diary key that the sync link advertises from the same IP. Nothing is stored on a server or on the device beyond the app's seen tokens (docs/app.md). The in-app list does not use push. Hosted Web Push for a follow, a reply, a like and a repost is a separate loopback relay ("Push relay", section 3), not this channel. A notice when a followed diary grows stays out of scope (the TODO in section 6).

### Goals (R4.6, R5.1, R5.3, R5.8)

`theirs(P, K)` = `P.remoteHave.get(K)` (absent = not advertised; the LATEST advertisement, never a max); `theirs(K)` = max of `theirs(P, K)` over connected, inventory-complete peers that currently hold K (used to start gates and to report `unadvertised`).

Unverified-peer budget (S2, security fix cycle 2 H1; `packages/core/lib/sync.js`, `UNVERIFIED_BUDGET_MS = 5000`, injectable as `unverifiedBudgetMs`): ONE budget per `--once` run for every peer that is not a verified seeder, started at the first hello of such a peer. There are no per-peer clocks: new identities, reconnects or progress never restart it.

- While it runs, an unverified peer counts as before: its claim above `core.length` raises the G1 target of an ACTIVE follow, its advertisement of our diary below `diary.length` keeps G3 unmet, and its incomplete inventory makes `--once` wait.
- Once it expires, for the rest of the run, unverified peers' claims (G1), interest (G3) and incomplete inventories no longer affect the goals. The G1 target of an active follow is then max(`core.length`, lengths advertised by verified seeders); `core.length` is the signed length, authenticated by the author's signature through replication. Each outstanding or later claim above `core.length` emits `claim-ignored { key, peerKey, length }` once per peer and key; each unverified peer below `diary.length` emits `peer-ignored { peerKey }` once.
- Verified seeders are never subject to the budget (a verified seeder that has not admitted the diary still counts for G3). PENDING and REGATE follows are unchanged: they stay bounded by the deadline, so a slow first-time follow is never declared done with nothing downloaded.
- Direct A<->B (no seeder): the signed `core.length` still drives B's G1, and A waits for B's full length only within the budget.
- Claim bookkeeping (H2): claims are kept only in `--once` mode while the budget runs, at most `MAX_CONNECTIONS` per follow; they are deleted when the claiming peer disconnects (unless the same remote key still has another live connection) or when `core.length` reaches them, and cleared when the budget expires. Long-running sync records none.
- `seederConfirmed` (SyncResult): true only when at least one verified seeder admitted our diary and advertises its full length (G2 met with a seeder). It can be false with `ok: true`, e.g. when the only peers are unverified and one self-reports our full length (a self-reported length is never proof of storage); the CLI then prints `sync done (sem confirmação de semeador)`.
- `admitted` / `rejected` from a seeder that is not verified change no goal (an unverified `rejected` for the own key is still a withdrawal, below).

- (G1) For every followed key K:
  - Gate failed -> excluded (listed in `skipped`).
  - Gate pending (R4.6, R5.8 d) -> unmet while some inventory-complete peer advertises K with length >= 1 (until the gate settles: pass -> `ours(K) >= theirs(K)`; fail -> skipped). At the `--once` deadline such a K appears in `errors` as `'tempo esgotado: <K>:0/<theirs>'` and is NOT listed in `skipped`. If no peer advertises K with length >= 1, K is listed in `unadvertised` and does not block.
  - Gate passed (ACTIVE) -> `ours(K)` = replica present length >= `theirs(K)` (0 when nobody advertised; such keys are listed in `result.unadvertised`).
- (G2) Own diary, only when the own genesis cert is valid and `seeders >= 1` (verified seeders only): met when at least one verified seeder sent `admitted` for the own key AND that seeder's `theirs(own) >= diary.length`.
  - A `rejected` with reason `'timeout'` from a verified seeder is NOT fatal: that seeder just does not count, and the event is listed in `errorsSoft` as `'rejeitado: timeout (<peerKey8>)'`.
  - Any other `rejected` reason for the own key from a verified seeder fails immediately: `ok` false, `rejected` = reason, `errors` `['rejeitado: ' + reason]`.
- (G3) Own diary to interested peers (R3 d, R4.6, R5.1 c), in `--once` mode: P is interested in the own key iff `P.remoteHave.has(own)` (any length, including 0) or P is a verified seeder that sent `admitted` for it; for every connected interested P require `theirs(P, own) >= diary.length` before resolving. A withdrawal or a disconnect removes P from G3 immediately; the author's `--once` therefore never waits on a follower whose gate failed. A peer that is not interested imposes nothing. With G3 (and R4.5 interest advertising) a direct two-member sync is deterministic: a first-time follower advertises the author's key with length 0 while its gate is pending, so the author waits until the follower advertises the full length.

Re-evaluate after every message, every growth event, every gate outcome, every disconnect, every `inventoryComplete` change and the grace timer.

Pending follows and fetch timeouts (security fix cycle 3):

- A PENDING follow K is unmet only while (a) a block-0 fetch for K is in flight, (b) a verified seeder advertises K at length >= 1, or (c) an unverified peer advertises K at length >= 1 while the unverified budget runs and no fetch that included that peer's socket has failed for K in this run. Once a fetch for K fails before the deadline, the unverified peers whose sockets were in it no longer hold K (K is reported in `unadvertised`), and in `--once` mode they are not asked again for K; a different advertiser still triggers a new fetch.
- In `--once` mode a block-0 fetch lasts `min(GATE_TIMEOUT_MS, time left before the deadline)`; a fetch cut by the deadline keeps K unmet (`tempo esgotado`). An honest first-time fetch slower than `GATE_TIMEOUT_MS` (15 s) is therefore reported in `unadvertised` and the run can end `ok` without that key.
- A pending key whose last fetch was cut by the deadline (and still had at least one advertiser socket open; when every advertiser left, the key is reported `unadvertised` instead) stays unmet whatever the unverified budget (`tempo esgotado`), and a follow still in `regate` when the run ends is unmet (`tempo esgotado`) and then purged.
- Failed advertisers are tracked only in `--once` mode and only while their connection is open (removed on close); a fetch includes at most `MAX_CONNECTIONS` open sockets (closed sockets free their slot at once and are no longer referenced by the fetch, so a later advertiser can still join; when an included socket closes, its slot goes to a connected advertiser of the key that was refused while the fetch was full).
- Quota at the member gate: the quarantine fetch's signed `length` / `byteLength` are checked against the log quota before the replica is opened in the main store (skipped with `quota`, nothing stored), the same order as the seeder's gate.
- `unadvertised` lists pending follows that no peer still holds (failed advertisers excluded) and active follows that nobody advertises or that are advertised only at length 0.
- Startup reads only block 0 of our diary (not the whole log) to find the genesis certificate.
- Re-discovery: a connection on a diary topic keeps rediscovery off only while its peer has not sent hello yet, is a verified seeder, or advertises the key at length >= 1; the rendezvous topic still counts any live connection.
- Residual: an attacker who reconnects with a fresh socket every gate timeout can keep condition (a) true until the deadline (`tempo esgotado`), bounded by the connection cap and the gate timeout.

### `--once` SyncResult

```json
{
  "ok": true,
  "peers": 1,
  "seeders": 1,
  "admitted": true,
  "rejected": null,
  "downloaded": { "<hex64>": 3 },
  "unadvertised": [],
  "skipped": [],
  "errors": [],
  "errorsSoft": []
}
```

Shape: `{ ok, peers, seeders, admitted: boolean, rejected: null | reason, downloaded: { hex64: present length }, unadvertised: [hex64], skipped: [{ key, reason }], errors: [string], errorsSoft: [string] }`. `seeders` = connected verified seeders; `admitted` = a verified seeder sent `admitted` for the own key (R5.3 d). `skipped` and `errorsSoft` are `[]` by default; the long-running mode reports them too (R3 d). `errorsSoft` entries are `'rejeitado: timeout (<peerKey8>)'` (verified seeder timeout) and `'bloco 0 indisponível: <key8>'` (follow-gate fetch timeout, R5.8 c). Group writer gate timeouts are not listed one per writer: they are aggregated into one line `'núcleos de grupo indisponíveis: <n> (<key8>, ...)'` (`<n>` counts the timeouts, at most 3 keys named), and `errorsSoft` holds at most 32 strings (31 other messages plus that line).

- `ok` true when `peers >= 1` and G1, G2 and G3 hold.
- If no peer has sent `hello` by the timeout -> `ok` false, `errors` `['sem pares']`.
- Timeout (default 30000 ms) -> `ok` false, `errors` `['tempo esgotado: ' + unmet goals as '<key>:<ours>/<theirs>' joined by ', ']`.
- Purge failure -> `ok` false, `errors` `['falha ao limpar diário rejeitado: ' + key8]` (R5.8 e).
- A failed local re-gate (follow entry states above) -> `ok` false, `errors` starting with `'bloco 0 divergente: ' + key8 + ' (' + reason + ')'`, K in `skipped` with that reason.
- Without `--once`: run until SIGINT/SIGTERM or signal abort, resolving `{ ok: true, ... }` (`ok: false` only after a purge failure or a hard error such as `bloco 0 divergente`).
- ALWAYS, before resolving: clear timers, destroy download ranges and the swarm, close the quarantine store and remove its directory (after every in-flight fetch settled), wait for every pending purge, and close the main store LAST (it releases the data-dir lock; the shutdown order above).
- TODO(phase1): push notifications when a followed log grows. That is a notice when a followed diary grows. The hosted Web Push relay (section 3, "Push relay") does not send it: it covers only a follow, a reply, a like and a repost aimed at the member.

### Browser node download order (`packages/web-member/lib/sync.js` `trackPresent`)

The browser node downloads a followed `active` or `regate` diary NEWEST-FIRST, so A Rua shows each followed account's latest posts before its history arrives. The core node (native app, `varanda-serve`, seeder) keeps the linear download above. Nothing advertised changes.

- Two strict phases, never both at once: Hypercore 10.38.2 picks among a core's open ranges at random, so a newest range open next to the history range would get about half of the requests.
- HEAD: blocks `0 .. HEAD_LOW - 1` (`HEAD_LOW` = 3: block 0 and where the first profile usually is; only those while block 0 is missing), then, once the signed length is known, the newest `NEWEST_BLOCKS` (32, one A Rua page) blocks below it, split around deleted seqs (all linear ranges).
- Tail fetch: `feed.read` shows a run only while it reaches the signed length (`docs/rpc.md` 5.13), so a window stored earlier would disappear behind a newer one. When the replica holds blocks above its present length and the top of that stored run, `T` (one past the highest block the initial scan found, then the end of each window completed in the run), is at most `TAIL_MAX` (256) blocks below the signed length, the window starts at `T` instead: the head asks for the whole new tail `[T, length)`, split around deleted seqs, and the run shown before reconnects to the new length once it arrives. A larger growth keeps the newest `NEWEST_BLOCKS` window. A growth during the same run (re-entering the head) follows the same rule from the window last completed. A length the seeder advertised that the core does not know yet only makes the head wait for the signed length; nothing is requested past the signed length.
- BODY: the linear download of the `active` bullet, from block 0, deleted seqs excluded.
- The body runs when the newest window already reaches the prefix (`target - NEWEST_BLOCKS <= max(present, HEAD_LOW)`, `target` = the larger of the signed length and the advertised one: a small diary downloads exactly as before), once every block of the window is stored or deleted, or when the window brought no block for `HEAD_STALL_MS` (3000 ms; the seeder may lack it). A target that grows past the one the body was chosen for, while the history is more than `NEWEST_BLOCKS` behind, runs the head again.
- The own diary keeps the linear order (goal G0, writes and the follow fold need the whole own diary).
- When the head finds every block of the window stored or deleted (the step that hands over to the body), the run emits the sync event `newest <key> <length>` once per key and signed length (`docs/rpc.md` §6), so the reader re-reads before any history arrives: the present length, and with it every `downloaded` line, does not move while only the window is stored. A diary still in `regate` holds the event until its block 0 passes the local gate.
- The quarantine (block 0 in memory first), the quota, fork handling and the present-length tracking are unchanged; the reported length stays the present length. What `feed.read` reads from a replica with a newest run is `docs/rpc.md` 5.13; `log.read` of a followed author reads the same blocks, paged by seq (5.12).

### Browser node on-demand reads (`diary.peek`, `packages/web-member/lib/sync.js`)

The browser node reads blocks of a diary it does not follow (a thread, a profile, a reposted or quoted post) without storing it. The contract is `docs/rpc.md` `diary.peek`; the rules here:

- Served by the RUNNING sync: the run publishes its peek function once its seeder link is verified and withdraws it when the link closes or the run ends. A peek never opens a storage core, never preempts or restarts a run, writes nothing and enters no index; nothing of the peeked diary is kept as a replica.
- The own diary and the followed diaries the run holds are read in place (Hypercore 10 refuses a second replication channel for a discovery key already on the stream). A followed key whose quarantine gate is in flight waits for that gate first.
- Any other key gets ONE in-memory core per key on the verified link, refcounted and shared by the quarantine fetch and every concurrent peek of that key, closed when the last holder releases it. It is gated like a quarantine fetch: block 0 a member certificate for the trusted association, issuer not revoked, the signed `length` / `byteLength` within `MAX_LOG_BLOCKS` / `MAX_LOG_BYTES`, `fork === 0`, not blocked.
- Only the requested blocks (plus the profile / username hint blocks) are asked for, each verified by Hypercore against the signed tree. A replica still catching up is read up to the length the verified seeder advertises; beyond it a seq is `missing`.
- Right before answering, the blocklist is checked again and, for a gated core, the fork and the block-0 verdict (revoked issuers included): a diary blocked, forked or revoked meanwhile is hidden. A hidden, ungated or unknown diary answers `'diário indisponível'`, never why; a run that cannot serve the peek any more answers `'semeador indisponível'`.
- Trust: the seeder decides which seqs are asked for and which `profileSeq` / `usernameSeq` count as the latest; the node verifies only that those blocks are the author's signed records (and the username record's issuer signatures). The core node (native app, `varanda-serve`, CLI) has no on-demand reads (section 9).

## 7. CLI (single bin: `node bin/varanda.js <command> [sub] [flags]`)

### Global flags

`--data <dir>` (`VARANDA_DATA`, default `./data`), `--association <hex64>` (`VARANDA_ASSOCIATION`), `--bootstrap <host:port>` (repeatable; `VARANDA_BOOTSTRAP` comma-separated), `--dht-host <ip>` (`VARANDA_DHT_HOST`; a non-empty string without whitespace, else UsageError `dht-host inválido`; passed as `host` to `runSync` and `Seeder`, R5.11), `--json`, `--help` / `-h`. Only `--flag value` syntax; booleans: `--json`, `--once`, `--help`, `-h`.

String command flags include `--seeder` (hex64 any case -> lowercase, else UsageError `chave inválida`), `--health-port <n>` (`VARANDA_HEALTH_PORT`; canonical decimal `0`-`65535`, `0` = the system picks a free port, else UsageError `health-port inválido`) and `--health-host <ip>` (`VARANDA_HEALTH_HOST`; a non-empty string without whitespace, else UsageError `health-host inválido`; default `127.0.0.1`, used only with `--health-port`). `--ws-port <n>` (`VARANDA_WS_PORT`; same rule as `--health-port`, else UsageError `ws-port inválido`) and `--ws-host <ip>` (`VARANDA_WS_HOST`; same rule as `--health-host`, else UsageError `ws-host inválido`; default `127.0.0.1`, used only with `--ws-port`). `--ws-origin <origin>` (repeatable; `VARANDA_WS_ORIGIN`, comma-separated) lists the browser origins allowed on the WebSocket entry (section 7 "Seeder WebSocket entry"). Only `seed` reads the two health flags and the three ws flags. As with `--dht-host`, a flag wins over its variable, and a variable that is set but invalid is a usage error for every command. `flags` keys include `seeder`, `dhtHost`, `healthPort`, `healthHost`, `wsPort` (`null` when absent), `wsHost` and `wsOrigins` (an array, or `null` when absent) (R5.12).

### Help (R5.12, exact)

`HELP` is exactly:

```

Comandos:
```
