### Exit codes and output

- Exit codes: 0 ok; 1 failure (`erro: <mensagem>` on stderr); 2 usage error: a flag parse error prints `uso: <mensagem>` followed by the full `HELP` text on stderr, a `UsageError` thrown by a command prints `uso: <mensagem>` alone, and an unknown command prints `erro: comando desconhecido: <comando>` (all exit 2; `apps/cli/bin/varanda.js`). No command, `help`, `--help` or `-h` prints `HELP` on stdout and exits 0.
- Human output and help in pt-BR; `--json` prints exactly one JSON object as the LAST stdout line (`sync`/`seed`: one JSON event per line, result last).
- Never print secret keys (`seed key` never prints the seeder's secret key).

### Terminal output safety (security fix cycle 2)

- Peer- or log-derived strings in human lines go through `safe()` (`packages/core/lib/safe.js`); every JSON line the CLI prints (`--json` results and events, `log` block lines) is produced by `jsonLine()`, which stays valid JSON and parses back to the same value.
- `feed` wraps by display columns: at `process.stdout.columns` on a TTY and at 80 otherwise (floor 20; `--json` is never wrapped). The width measure over-estimates: every code point >= U+1100 counts 2 columns except U+2000-U+206F (general punctuation) and U+20A0-U+20CF (currency), which count 1; everything below U+1100 counts 1 (residual: East Asian "ambiguous" characters count 1, so a terminal configured to draw them wide can under-measure). The header line wraps too; every row after an item's first is indented by two spaces, so no post or name can place a forged `[date] name (key8) #seq` header at column 0.
- The human feed header always keeps `(key8) #seq` and the reply marker on its first row: the author name is truncated by display width and marked with the ASCII `...` (never wider than the room left) so the whole header fits the width by any terminal's measure; when even the fixed parts exceed the width, row 1 is `[at] (key8) #seq` plus the reply marker and the name goes on its own indented row. A padded profile name therefore cannot forge a suffix and push the real one onto an indented row. Known limits (phase 0): below about 40 columns (about 54 with a reply) `(key8) #seq` or the reply marker itself wraps onto an indented second row.
- `feed` skips the own key and every `follows.json` entry that is not 64 hex characters (normalised to lowercase, deduped), warns once per bad entry on stderr (`aviso: entrada inválida em follows.json ignorada (...)`), and never purges the diary.
- `log --author <hex>` refuses a key with no local data (exit 1, `erro: sem dados locais para este diário`) and creates no core.
- `id create` publishes `config.json` durably: temp file opened `O_WRONLY | O_CREAT | O_EXCL | O_NOFOLLOW` (mode `0o600`), fsync, hard link into place, then fsync of the directory; the temp file is always unlinked.
- `seed` installs its SIGINT/SIGTERM handlers before startup; a signal during startup closes the seeder once `start()` settles.
- `sync` prints `sync done (sem confirmação de semeador)` when `ok` is true and `seederConfirmed` is false.

### Router shutdown hardening (R3 e)

After `run()` resolves or throws, `apps/cli/bin/varanda.js` sets `process.exitCode` and arms `setTimeout(() => process.exit(process.exitCode), 2000).unref()`, so a lingering socket or timer cannot keep `seed` or `sync` alive after their `close()` (the unref'd timer never delays a clean exit).

### Commands and `--json` results

- `assoc init` -> `{ publicKey }` (error if `association.json` exists).
- `assoc show` -> `{ publicKey }`.
- `assoc certify --member <hex64> [--age-band 18+] [--method stub] [--out <file>]` -> the Certificate object (with `--out`: written as pretty JSON + newline and the result is `{ out, cert }`); appends the `members.jsonl` line.
- `assoc certify-seeder --seeder <hex64> [--out <file>]` -> the SeederCertificate (with `--out`: written as pretty JSON + LF, result `{ out, cert }`, stdout `certificado do semeador gravado em <out>`); appends the `seeders.jsonl` line; USAGE `informe --seeder <hex>` when the flag is missing.
- `id create` (first run requires `--association`/`VARANDA_ASSOCIATION`; writes `config.json`; idempotent, same key on every run; a different association on a later run is an error) -> `{ publicKey, association }`.
- `id show` -> same (error `identidade não encontrada` when `config.json` is missing).
- `cert add --cert-file <file>` -> `{ seq: 0 }` (checks `cert.member === own key` and `certForKey` ok against the trust root; if block 0 already holds an identical cert -> ok without append; any other existing block 0 -> error `certificado já gravado`).
- `profile set --name <s> [--bio <s>]` -> `{ seq }`; `post --text <s>` -> `{ seq, ref }`; `reply --to <hex64:seq> --text <s>` -> `{ seq, ref }` (`seq` in `--to` must be >= 1); all three require block 0 (`grave o certificado de membro antes de publicar`).
- `follow --author <hex64>` -> `{ follows, changed }`; `unfollow --author <hex64>` -> `{ follows, changed }`; `follows` -> `{ follows }`. Once block 0 exists, `follow` / `unfollow` append a `follow` record (`on: true` / `false`) BEFORE writing `follows.json`, and only when the diary's follow set (`followSet`) disagrees, so a retry after a failed append writes the record exactly once and `changed` is `true` whenever either side changed; without block 0 only `follows.json` changes. An existing `follows.json` is never migrated into the diary (a key gets its record only when `follow` runs for it again), and the core node never folds another device's `follow` records into `follows.json`. Following yourself is an error. Following a new key when `follows.json` already holds `MAX_INVENTORY - 1` (4095) keys is an error: `erro: limite de diários seguidos atingido`, exit 1 (section 5 limits).
- `log [--author <hex64>]` -> `{ key, entries: [{ seq, record }] }` from local blocks only (no network; read through `readAll`, R4.1). Human output: one line per entry, formatted only from a validated copy of the record (peer strings through `safe()`): `#<seq> certificado`, `#<seq> perfil <name>`, `#<seq> publicação <text>` (line breaks as spaces, `[foto]` / `[N fotos]` appended for media), `#<seq> segue <key8>`, `#<seq> deixa de seguir <key8>`, `#<seq> mídia <key8>`, `#<seq> <type>` for any other valid type, and `#<seq> inválido (<reason>)`. With `--json`, one `jsonLine({ seq, record })` per entry and then the result, as before.
- `feed [--limit n]` -> `{ items: [FeedItem], skipped: [{ key, reason }], future: ['<hex64>:<seq>', ...] }`; own diary never gated; a followed replica is kept only if its block 0 is a valid member-cert for the trust root with `cert.member === key` and `core.fork === 0` (reasons: `no-cert` | `not-in-log` | `member-mismatch` | `untrusted-association` | `bad-signature` | `malformed` | `fork`); stderr `aviso: diário <key8> ignorado (<reason>)` (a forked replica: `aviso: diário <key8> ignorado (fork)`; it is read-only here and the next sync purges it, R5.9 e). Per future reference (R5.10), stderr `aviso: publicação <ref> ignorada (future)`. No network. In the human output a post with media shows `[foto]` (one) or `[N fotos]` on its own line after the text (alone for a photo-only post); the CLI never fetches media.
- `sync [--once] [--timeout-ms n]` -> stdout lines `sync ready`, `peer member <peerKey8>`, `peer seeder <peerKey8>` (verified) or `peer seeder <peerKey8> não verificado`, `admitted <key> <length>`, `rejected <key> <reason>`, `withdrawn <key> <peerKey8> <reason>`, `have <key> <length>`, `downloaded <key> <length>`, then `sync done` or `sync failed: <errors joined by ; >`; with `--json` one `{ event, ... }` object per line and the SyncResult last (including `{ event: 'peer', role, peerKey, verified }`, `{ event: 'withdrawn', key, peerKey, reason }` and `{ event: 'skipped', key, reason }` events). Exit 0 iff `ok`.
- `seed key` -> `{ publicKey }`, stdout `chave do semeador: <hex64>`; creates `seeder-key.json` when missing, otherwise idempotent (same key). Never prints the secret key.
- `seed cert add --cert-file <file>` -> `{ seeder }`, stdout `certificado do semeador gravado`; parses the file, requires `seeder-key.json` (error `chave do semeador não encontrada (use seed key)`), checks `seederCertForKey(cert, ownNoiseHex, trustRoot)` (error `certificado do semeador inválido (<reason>)`), writes `seeder-cert.json`; USAGE `informe --cert-file <arquivo>` when missing.
- `seed` (run) `[--health-port <n>] [--health-host <ip>] [--ws-port <n>] [--ws-host <ip>]` -> loads both files before any network activity: missing key -> `Error('chave do semeador não encontrada (use seed key)')`; missing cert -> `Error('certificado do semeador não encontrado (use seed cert add)')`; cert failing `seederCertForKey(cert, ownNoiseHex, trustRoot)` -> `Error('certificado do semeador inválido (<reason>)')` (all exit 1). With `--health-port`, the health endpoint (below) listens BEFORE `start()`, so a busy port fails with `erro: porta em uso` (exit 1) before the data dir or the network is touched (any other listen error: `erro: não foi possível abrir o endpoint de saúde (<code>)`). With `--ws-port`, the WebSocket entry (below) listens right after the health endpoint and also before `start()`: busy port `erro: porta em uso`, other errors `erro: não foi possível abrir o websocket (<code>)`. Stdout lines `health http://<host>:<port>` (only with `--health-port`, first), `ws ws://<host>:<port>/seeder` (only with `--ws-port`, after `health`), `seed ready`, `peer <role> <peerKey8>`, `admitted <key> <length>`, `rejected <key> <reason>`, `have <key> <length>`; `--json`, one per line: `{ event: 'health', host, port }` | `{ event: 'ws', host, port }` | `{ event: 'ready', topic, admitted }` | `{ event: 'peer', role, peerKey }` (unchanged: seeders do not verify members) | `{ event: 'admitted', key, length }` | `{ event: 'rejected', key, reason }` | `{ event: 'have', key, length }` | `{ event: 'fatal', key, reason: 'purge-failed' }` (section 5) | `{ event: 'media-admitted', key, diary, length }` (human `media admitted <key> <diary> <length>`) | `{ event: 'media-rejected', key, diary, reason }` (human `media rejected <key> <diary> <reason>`; reasons `timeout`, `no-peers`, `media-full`, `header-invalid`, `wrong-diary`, `quota`, `admission-failed`, `unbound`, `fork`, `no-header`) | `{ event: 'warning', key, reason: 'media-full' }` (section 5 "Seeder media admission"). Runs until SIGINT/SIGTERM, then closes the seeder (section 5 "Seeder shutdown") and then the health and WebSocket endpoints, and exits 0 (1 after a fatal purge failure).
- `seed status` -> `{ association, publicKey, certificate, admitted, blocklist, blocklistHeld, running }`, read without the data-dir lock and without opening a store, so it also answers while `seed` runs, and it writes nothing (`Seeder.status`). `association` = `config.json`'s (`Seeder.checkConfig`: a missing or non-seeder `config.json` -> `erro: diretório não é de um semeador`, a different `--association` -> `erro: associação diferente da configurada`, exit 1; config.json is never created); `publicKey` = the hex64 of `seeder-key.json` or `null` when the file is missing (an invalid file -> `erro: arquivo de chave inválido`); `certificate` = `'ok'`, `'missing'` (no `seeder-cert.json`), `'malformed'` (unreadable JSON) or the reason of `seederCertForKey(cert, publicKey, association)`; `admitted` = the number of hex64 keys in `admitted.json` (it includes keys a previous run left mid-drop); `blocklist` = `{ seq, date }` of the stored snapshot (`readBlocklist`, an absent or unreadable `blocklist.json` reads as none) or `null`; `blocklistHeld` = `true` when `blocklist` is `null` and a torn file is kept aside (`blocklist.json.ilegivel-<ms>`), the case in which a start holds new admissions again (`false` otherwise, and when the directory cannot be listed; it does not predict the first start after a torn file with no copy, or after a missing file with a copy that does not verify, because nothing is kept aside before that start); `running` = `lockHeld(dataDir)` (section 1 rule 9). Exit 0. Stdout, six lines: `associação: <hex64>`, `chave do semeador: <hex64>` or `chave do semeador: ausente (use seed key)`, `certificado: válido` | `certificado: ausente (use seed cert add)` | `certificado: inválido (<reason>)`, `diários admitidos: <n>`, `lista de bloqueio: nenhuma` | `lista de bloqueio: seq <n> de <data>`, `estado: em execução` | `estado: parado`.
- `seed list` -> `{ admitted: [{ key, length, byteLength, media: [{ key, length, byteLength }] }] }` in `admitted.json` order (each diary with the media cores bound to it; a listed media core whose diary is not listed is not shown, the next start purges it) (`Seeder.list`): `Seeder.checkConfig` as in `seed status`, then `openStore(dataDir)` (the data-dir lock: while `seed` runs -> `erro: diretório de dados em uso`, exit 1) BEFORE `admitted.json` is read; for each hex64 key, when the main store's storage holds its core (`store.storage.hasCore(discoveryKey)`), a read-only session reports `length = core.length` and `byteLength = core.byteLength` (the signed size, the one the quota checks); a listed key with no stored core is reported `0` / `0` and never opened, so listing creates no core. Stdout one line per entry, `<key> <length> blocos <byteLength> bytes`, followed by one indented line per bound media core, `  mídia <key> <length> blocos <byteLength> bytes`, or `nenhum diário admitido`.
- `seed drop <chave>` -> `{ key }`, stdout `diário removido do semeador: <key>` (`Seeder.drop`): `<chave>` is hex64 in any case (lowercased; else USAGE `chave inválida`; missing -> USAGE `informe a chave do diário`); `Seeder.checkConfig`; `openStore(dataDir)` (the lock, as in `seed list`); then, in the startup-drop order of R6.1, `purgeReplica(store, key)` FIRST when the storage holds the core, and only then the key leaves `admitted.json` (`writeJsonAtomic` of `{ v: 1, admitted, media }` with every other entry unchanged). A diary's bound media cores are purged first and leave `media` with it; `<chave>` may also be a media core key, which is purged and un-listed alone (the next scan of its diary binds it again). A key neither listed (as a diary or a media core) nor stored -> `erro: diário não está no semeador` (exit 1); a purge failure throws (exit 1) and leaves `admitted.json` untouched. A drop is not a block: the author's next `announce` runs the full gate again, and it is refused only when the key is in the blocklist snapshot (B2: `mod block`, section 3).
- `seed key`, `seed cert add` and `seed` share `Seeder.ensureConfig(dataDir, associationKey)`: an existing `config.json` must have `role === 'seeder'` (else `diretório não é de um semeador`) and must match `--association` when given (else `associação diferente da configurada`); when missing, `--association`/`VARANDA_ASSOCIATION` is required (USAGE `informe --association ou VARANDA_ASSOCIATION`) and `config.json = { "v": 1, "role": "seeder", "association" }` is written. So the first of these three commands fixes the seeder's trust root.
- Any other `seed` subcommand -> USAGE `uso: node bin/varanda.js seed [key|cert add|list|drop <chave>|status]`.

### Seeder health endpoint (`seed --health-port`, `apps/cli/lib/health.js`)

- Off unless `--health-port` (or `VARANDA_HEALTH_PORT`) is given. Plain HTTP on `--health-host` (default `127.0.0.1`: only this machine); nothing else listens. `0.0.0.0` or another address exposes it beyond the machine and is the operator's choice.
- `GET` or `HEAD` `/health` -> `application/json`, `{ status, uptimeSeconds, admitted, dropping, activeDay, activeWeek, blocks, bytes, connections, swarm, external, maxConnections, maxExternal, peers, firewalled, blocked, blocklistSeq, blocklistHeld, mediaCores, mediaBytes, indexRecords, indexSaturated, topicPosts, topicSaturated }` from `Seeder#stats()`: `status` `'starting'` until `start()` completes, `'ok'` while serving, `'closing'` from `close()` on; HTTP 200 when `status` is `'ok'`, else 503. `admitted` / `dropping` = sizes of the two maps (section 1); `activeDay` / `activeWeek` = the gated served diaries whose signed length grew inside the Brasília calendar-day windows (the next bullet); `blocks` / `bytes` = sums of `contiguousLength` / `byteLength` over the served replicas; `connections` = open swarm connections plus external sockets (`Seeder#acceptStream`, section 4; in-flight handshakes included), `swarm` and `external` = those two counts, `maxConnections` and `maxExternal` = their independent caps (section 4); `peers` = connections with an admission channel; `firewalled` = `swarm.dht.firewalled` (`null` before the swarm exists); `blocked` = size of the adopted blocked set and `blocklistSeq` = its `seq` (`0` without a snapshot; B2); `blocklistHeld` = whether new admissions are held because the list was lost (section 1; `true` until a verified snapshot is adopted); `mediaCores` = the number of bound media cores (persisted bindings) and `mediaBytes` = the sum of the served media cores' signed `byteLength`; `indexRecords` = the records held by the engagement index and `indexSaturated` = `true` when it reached its cap and diaries are paused (section 5 "Seeder engagement index"); `topicPosts` = the posts held by the topic index and `topicSaturated` = `true` when one of its caps refused an add and diaries are paused (section 5 "Seeder topic index"); `uptimeSeconds` since the endpoint started.
- `activeDay` and `activeWeek` (`packages/core/lib/activity.js`: `DAY_OFFSET_MS`, `dayStart`, `localDay`, `noteGrowth`, `countActive`). `countActive` counts a served entry only when `gated === true` and `activeAt` is finite. `noteGrowth` sets `activeAt` to the wall clock only when the signed length is a safe integer strictly beyond the length known at serve (`activityLength`, the `core.length` captured when the replica was served). An equal length (the synchronous first check of a serve, a restore, a re-gate or a rescan) or a value that is not a safe integer changes nothing, so a restart or a rescan never looks like activity. The windows are a Brasília calendar day (fixed UTC−3; no DST since 2019) on the wall clock: `DAY_OFFSET_MS` = `-3 * 3600000`, `dayStart(ms)` is 00:00 of the day containing `ms`, and `localDay(ms)` is that day as `YYYY-MM-DD`. `activeDay` counts `activeAt >= dayStart(now)`; `activeWeek` counts `activeAt >= dayStart(now) - (WEEK_DAYS - 1) * DAY_MS` with `WEEK_DAYS` = 7 and `DAY_MS` = 86400000 (the last 7 days, today included). An `activeAt` in the future (the wall clock stepped back after it was set) is `>=` both starts, so it counts in both until the next day and never hides activity. The state lives only on the in-memory served entry: it is never persisted, never logged and never returned per diary; the entry leaves `_served` as soon as the diary is dropped, and a restart zeroes both counts. A diary first admitted in this run counts once its signed length grows past the length known at serve. The two numbers never say which diary.
- `GET` or `HEAD` `/metrics` -> Prometheus text (`text/plain; version=0.0.4`), every value a gauge named `varanda_seeder_<name>`: `ready` (1 when `status` is `'ok'`), `admitted_diaries`, `dropping_diaries`, `active_diaries_day` and `active_diaries_week` (`varanda_seeder_active_diaries_day`, `varanda_seeder_active_diaries_week`; a missing or non-finite value reads 0), `blocks`, `bytes`, `connections`, `swarm`, `external`, `max_connections`, `max_external` (a missing or non-finite value reads 0), `peers`, `firewalled` (1 or 0), `uptime_seconds`, `blocked`, `blocklist_seq` and `blocklist_held` (B2: `varanda_seeder_blocked`, `varanda_seeder_blocklist_seq`, `varanda_seeder_blocklist_held`, 1 or 0), `media_cores` and `media_bytes` (`varanda_seeder_media_cores`, `varanda_seeder_media_bytes`), `index_records` and `index_saturated` (1 or 0; `varanda_seeder_index_records`, `varanda_seeder_index_saturated`), `topic_posts` and `topic_saturated` (1 or 0; `varanda_seeder_topic_posts`, `varanda_seeder_topic_saturated`; counts only).
- A query string is ignored; any other path -> 404; any other method -> 405 with `Allow: GET, HEAD`. Every response carries `Cache-Control: no-store` and `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` and never CORS headers. At most 16 sockets; headers within 4 s and the request within 5 s.
- It exposes counts and sizes only: never a key (diary, Noise or association), a certificate, an address, a block or any content. Nothing is logged per request.

### Seeder WebSocket entry (`seed --ws-port`, `apps/cli/lib/seed-ws.js`)

- Off unless `--ws-port` (or `VARANDA_WS_PORT`) is given: without it the module is not loaded and nothing listens. Plain `ws://` on `--ws-host` (default `127.0.0.1`).
- An HTTP server with `ws` (`noServer`, `maxPayload` = `MAX_EXTERNAL_FRAME` + 3-byte length prefix + secret-stream `ABYTES` + 1 KiB margin, about 8.06 MiB, so a WebSocket message never undercuts the seeder's frame guard: each secret-stream frame is one WebSocket message and a legitimate post-handshake frame may reach `MAX_EXTERNAL_FRAME`; the memory bound per connection is therefore about `MAX_EXTERNAL_FRAME`, the same as the guard's; no per-message deflate). Headers must complete within 4 s (checked every second); a refused upgrade is answered and then destroyed, and closing the listener destroys sockets still being upgraded. Only the path `/seeder` upgrades; any other upgrade path -> 404; a plain GET on `/seeder` -> 426; any other plain request -> 404 `não encontrado\n` (`text/plain; charset=utf-8`, `Cache-Control: no-store`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, never CORS headers, the URL is never echoed). An upgrade while `Seeder#stats().status` is not `'ok'` -> 503.
- Each accepted WebSocket becomes a Node duplex (`createWebSocketStream`: one binary frame per write) handed to `Seeder#acceptStream` (section 4 "External transports"); the socket is closed when that throws or returns `null`. The bytes on the socket are the Noise stream keyed with the seeder key: a TLS proxy may forward them but must not terminate Noise.
- Capacity refusal: `acceptStream(raw, { onRefuse })` hands a refusal for capacity (the external cap, `maxExternal`) to the caller without destroying the stream; the WebSocket entry then closes the socket with code 1013 and reason `'lotado'` and terminates it after `REFUSE_GRACE_MS` = 1000 ms if the client has not answered the close. The `'connection-limit'` log line is unchanged and the limits are unchanged; a caller without `onRefuse` keeps destroy-on-cap. Measured on loopback, a refused client sees the close 0-1 ms after the log line.
- Socket cap `socketCap`: `max(128, maxExternal + /media in flight + /index in flight + 64)`, or `VARANDA_MAX_SOCKETS` when set (used as given); headers within 4 s. Members stay unauthenticated by design (the announce, section 6), but WebSockets are not subject to CORS, so the entry guards against arbitrary web pages and DNS rebinding:
  - Host check: the `Host` header of every request and upgrade must name this listener, `<bound host>:<port>` (IPv6 bracketed; the port read from the listening socket, so port `0` works), and, when the bind is loopback, also `localhost:<port>`, `127.0.0.1:<port>` or `[::1]:<port>`; anything else -> 421 `host inválido` (an upgrade is answered and its socket destroyed).
  - Origin policy (upgrades): a request WITHOUT an `Origin` header (a non-browser client: the CLI, Node, native apps) is accepted. A request WITH one must be in the allow-list, else 403 `origem não permitida` (answered and destroyed); `null` and unparsable origins are refused. `startSeedWs({ origins })` takes exact origins (scheme, host and port); without it the default allows any `http:` origin whose hostname is `127.0.0.1`, `localhost` or `[::1]`, on any port, so the loopback spike page works and public pages are refused. `seed --ws-origin <origin>` (repeatable; or `VARANDA_WS_ORIGIN`, comma-separated; any flag wins over the variable) passes an explicit list, which REPLACES the loopback default: each value must be an exact `http:`/`https:` origin as a browser sends it (`new URL(v).origin === v`: no path, no trailing slash, no default port, lowercase host), else `uso: ws-origin inválido <value>`; a present source with no origin is `uso: ws-origin inválido (vazio)`. `flags.wsOrigins` is the array or `null`. The public deployment (`deploy/seeder/compose.web.yaml`) sets it to `https://<domain>` and puts a TLS proxy (Caddy, `deploy/seeder/Caddyfile`) in front that rewrites `Host` to `127.0.0.1:<port>` (`header_up Host {upstream_hostport}`) and forwards the Noise bytes untouched.
  - Order of the upgrade checks: Host (421), path (404), Origin (403), seeder ready (503).
  - The seeder's external cap (section 4 "External transports": `maxExternal`, default 32, independent of the swarm cap, `VARANDA_MAX_EXTERNAL`) bounds what WebSocket clients can hold, so a browser tab cannot starve swarm members.
  - Nothing is logged per connection and never an address.

### Seeder media route (`GET|HEAD /media/...`, `apps/cli/lib/seed-ws.js`)

The listener of `seed --ws-port` also serves media blocks over plain HTTP (the public Caddy proxies `/media/*` to it, `deploy/seeder/Caddyfile`):

- Path: exactly `/media/<D>/<K>/<start>/<blocks>`, `D` the diary key and `K` the media core key (lowercase hex64), `start` and `blocks` canonical decimals (no sign, no leading zero), `start >= 1`, `blocks` in `1..256` (`MEDIA_MAX_BLOCKS`), and no query string (a URL with one does not match).
- Order: the Host check of the WebSocket entry runs first (421 `host inválido`); a method other than `GET` or `HEAD` on `/media` or `/media/...` -> 405 `método não permitido` with `Allow: GET, HEAD`; then the path.
- 404 policy: a path that does not match exactly, and every request `Seeder#mediaRead` declines (no D <-> K binding, D blocked or not admitted, K not re-gated, a range outside K's length, a range an effective tombstone of D listed that no surviving post or profile of D still overlaps (section 5 "Seeder deletions"), a block not local, a seeder without `mediaRead`), is the same 404 `não encontrado` (`text/plain; charset=utf-8`, `Cache-Control: no-store`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`): the answer never says why.
- 200: `Content-Type: application/octet-stream`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable`, `Accept-Ranges: none` and the exact `Content-Length`; `HEAD` ends there. The body is streamed block by block with backpressure (a write that returns `false` waits for `'drain'`); more or fewer bytes than announced destroy the response; a client that goes away ends the stream.
- Caps (global in the seeder, which never sees a client address; the per-client limit is in Caddy, below): at most `MEDIA_MAX_IN_FLIGHT = 16` responses at once and a token bucket of `MEDIA_RATE = 64` requests per second (burst `MEDIA_BURST = 128`); beyond either, 503 `ocupado` with `Retry-After: 1`. A slot is released only once the response closed AND every seeder call it started has settled, so a stalled read keeps its slot until it settles.
- Deadline: a response still running after `MEDIA_DEADLINE_MS = 120000` is destroyed.
- Nothing is logged, and neither the path nor any key is ever echoed.
- Caddy: `handle /media/*` -> `127.0.0.1:{$VARANDA_WS_PORT}` with `header_up Host {upstream_hostport}` and the client-address headers (`X-Forwarded-For`, `X-Real-Ip`) removed; `/media/*` is excluded from `@mutavel` (no-cache) and from `@ausenteApp` (the app's no-store fallback), so the seeder's own cache header and 404 reach the client. The CSP is unchanged (`img-src` already allows `blob:`). The app's service worker never intercepts `/media/`.
- Per-client limit (Caddy, not the seeder): `handle /media/*` runs `rate_limit` (module `github.com/mholt/caddy-ratelimit`, built into the Caddy image by `deploy/seeder/Dockerfile.caddy`) with one zone keyed on `{remote_host}`: 120 requests per 10 s per client, then 429 with `Retry-After`, before the request reaches the seeder. The client address lives only in Caddy's memory for the window: the Caddyfile has no access log, the module's own `rate limit exceeded` log line (which carries `remote_ip`) is excluded by a global `log { exclude http.handlers.rate_limit }`, and `X-Forwarded-For` / `X-Real-Ip` are still removed upstream, so the seeder never learns it. 120 per 10 s leaves room for a screen of photos loading in a burst.

### Seeder moderation-post route (`GET /moderation-post/<D>/<seq>`, `apps/cli/lib/seed-ws.js`)

The listener of `seed --ws-port` answers this only when it is given `VARANDA_TRIAGE_TOKEN` (64 lowercase hex; the account service must hold the same value). Without the token the route does not exist: the path is the generic 404 and the `Authorization` header is not read. The public Caddy does not proxy it. Caddy forwards only `/seeder`, `/account/*`, `/media/*` and `/index/*` (`deploy/seeder/Caddyfile`); `apps/cli/test/caddyfile.test.js` checks that this path is absent.

- Path: regex `^/moderation-post/([0-9a-f]{64})/([1-9][0-9]{0,14})$` (`POST_RE`) on the raw URL. A query string does not match (404).
- Order: the Host check of the WebSocket entry first (421 `host inválido`); on this path, a method other than `GET` -> 405 `método não permitido` with `Allow: GET`; then the regex; then `Authorization: Bearer <token>`, both sides hashed with sha256 and compared in constant time (`crypto.timingSafeEqual`); then this route's own budget. The token check runs before the budget, so a request without the token cannot take a slot.
- 200: `Content-Type: application/json; charset=utf-8`, `Cache-Control: no-store`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, exact `Content-Length`. The body is exactly `{ v: 1, fork, length, block0, block }` (blocks in base64), at most 65536 bytes (`POST_MAX_BODY`). Raw blocks too large to fit are refused before they are encoded.
- 404 policy: a wrong or missing token, an unknown diary, a blocked diary, a dropped diary, a block that is not local, a fork other than 0, a seeder without `postBlock`, a `null` answer, a throw and a body over the cap are the same 404 `não encontrado` (`text/plain; charset=utf-8`, `Cache-Control: no-store`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`). The answer never says why.
- A post deleted by its author (an effective tombstone, section 2) is not local for this purpose: the route answers the same 404 and the account service gets nothing.
- Caps: at most `POST_MAX_IN_FLIGHT` = 2 responses at once and `POST_RATE` = 4 requests per second (burst `POST_BURST` = 8); beyond either, 503 `ocupado` with `Retry-After: 1`. A response still open after `POST_DEADLINE_MS` = 5000 (5 s) is destroyed.
- Nothing is logged: not the token, the path, a key or the body.
- `Seeder#postBlock` (`packages/core/lib/seeder.js`) reads only blocks already local (`{ wait: false }`). It never downloads, never admits a diary and never opens another Corestore. After each await it checks again that the diary is still admitted, served, gated, not dropped and not blocked, and that the moderation generation is unchanged. Any change answers `null` (the route's 404).
- Trust: the blocks are the bytes this seeder's own Hypercore verified when they arrived. The JSON on this local route is not a portable proof (no signature and no tree nodes travel with it), so authenticity rests on the local seeder plus the token. The account service re-checks the block-0 certificate, including issuers revoked by the list, that the requested block is a post, and that the text is well-formed UTF-16. That re-check rejects a malformed answer, a wrong type, a wrong fork, a bad certificate or a revoked issuer. It does not reject a malicious seeder.

### Seeder moderation-group-post route (`GET /moderation-group-post/<W>/<seq>`, `apps/cli/lib/seed-ws.js`)

Same listener, token, `Authorization: Bearer <token>` check, budget, limits and generic 404 as the moderation-post route above (path regex `GROUP_POST_RE` = `^/moderation-group-post/([0-9a-f]{64})/([1-9][0-9]{0,14})$`). It exists only with `VARANDA_TRIAGE_TOKEN` configured and the public Caddy never forwards it. 200: `{ group, diary, seq, texto, resposta }`, only when `Seeder#groupPostBlock(W, seq)` finds a valid `group-post` at that seq of a served, gated, unblocked writer core W (`diary` = the writer header's binding diary; for the root, its header diary; `resposta` = `{ writer, seq }` or `null`). `texto` is returned as signed: render it with `displayText`. Anything else is the same 404.

### Seeder index route (`GET|HEAD /index/...`, `apps/cli/lib/seed-ws.js`)

The same listener answers the engagement index of section 5 ("Seeder engagement index") over plain HTTP; the public Caddy proxies `/index/*` to it. The answers are refs, seq hints and counts only: a reader fetches every post, name and bio from the author's diary and verifies it (the browser node's `diary.peek`, `docs/rpc.md`).

- Routes, matched on the raw URL (so a query string never matches), lowercase hex only, canonical decimals:
  - `/index/post/<author>/<seq>[/<cursor>]`, regex `^\/index\/post\/([0-9a-f]{64})\/([1-9][0-9]{0,14})(?:\/([0-9]{1,16}\.[0-9a-f]{64}\.[1-9][0-9]{0,14}))?$` (`INDEX_POST_RE`). The cursor is `<atMs>.<authorHex>.<seq>`, the ordering key of the last reply of the previous page (`next`). -> `Seeder#indexPost`.
  - `/index/likes/<author>/<seq>[/<cursor>]` and `/index/reposts/<author>/<seq>[/<cursor>]`, regex `^\/index\/(likes|reposts)\/([0-9a-f]{64})\/([1-9][0-9]{0,14})(?:\/([0-9]{1,16}\.[0-9a-f]{64}\.[1-9][0-9]{0,14}))?$` (`INDEX_ENGAGERS_RE`). The cursor is `<atMs>.<authorHex>.<seq>`, the ordering key of the last edge examined (`next`). -> `Seeder#indexEngagers(kind, authorHex, seq, cursor)`.
  - `/index/counts/<ref>,<ref>,...` (1..`INDEX_MAX_REFS` = 31 refs `<hex64>:<seq>`, no trailing comma), regex `^\/index\/counts\/([0-9a-f]{64}:[1-9][0-9]{0,14}(?:,[0-9a-f]{64}:[1-9][0-9]{0,14}){0,30})$` (`INDEX_COUNTS_RE`). Duplicate refs collapse before the seeder is asked. -> `indexCounts`.
  - `/index/topic/<tokens>[/<cursor>]`, regex `^\/index\/topic\/([a-z0-9]{2,32}(?:\+[a-z0-9]{2,32}){0,7})(?:\/([0-9]{1,16}\.[0-9a-f]{64}\.[1-9][0-9]{0,14}))?$` (`INDEX_TOPIC_RE`) on the raw URL, with no percent-decoding: `<tokens>` are 1..8 tokens of 2..32 `[a-z0-9]` characters joined by `+` (already folded by the app; the seeder does not fold). The cursor is `<atMs>.<authorHex>.<seq>`, the ordering key of the last candidate examined (`next`). -> `Seeder#indexTopic`. The path carries the search words: the seeder never logs or echoes it, but Caddy's error log can record it with the client address when proxying fails (section 5 "Seeder topic index", Privacy).
  - `/index/follows/<author>`, regex `^\/index\/follows\/([0-9a-f]{64})$` (`INDEX_FOLLOW_COUNTS_RE`). -> `Seeder#indexFollowCounts`. Answer `{ seguindo, seguidores, indexing, complete }`.
  - `/index/follows/<author>/(seguindo|seguidores)[/<atMs>.<hex64>]`, regex `^\/index\/follows\/([0-9a-f]{64})\/(seguindo|seguidores)(?:\/([0-9]{1,16}\.[0-9a-f]{64}))?$` (`INDEX_FOLLOWS_RE`). The cursor is `<atMs>.<key>` (no seq: a follow pair is unique by key). -> `Seeder#indexFollows`. Answer `{ author, kind, items: [{ key, seq, at }], next: { atMs, key } | null, total, authors, indexing, complete }`: at most 20 items, ordered by the follow time clamped at 0 (descending), then key; `seguidores` rows point at the follower's follow block and `seguindo` rows at the author's own; `total` counts visible rows only. Same 404 policy as the other `/index` routes. Section 5 "Seeder follow index".
  - `/index/testimonials/<owner>`, regex `^\/index\/testimonials\/([0-9a-f]{64})$` (`INDEX_TESTIMONIALS_RE`). -> `Seeder#indexTestimonials`. Answer `{ shown: [{ seq, from, wseq }], pending: [{ from, seq }], indexing, complete }`: `shown` rows point at the owner's `testimonial-show` block (`seq`) and the writer's `testimonial` block (`from`, `wseq`), `pending` rows at a writer's `testimonial` block; at most 64 of each, newest first. Pointers only, never the text or the sealed box: the client reads every block with `diary.peek` and verifies it (section 2, "Depoimentos"; docs/rpc.md 5.100). Same Host check, 405, generic 404 policy and caps as the other `/index` routes. Section 5 "Seeder testimonial index".
  - `/index/member/<author>[/<before>]`, regex `^\/index\/member\/([0-9a-f]{64})(?:\/([1-9][0-9]{0,14}))?$` (`INDEX_MEMBER_RE`); `before` is an exclusive seq bound. -> `indexMember`.
  - `/index/page-end/<author>`, regex `^\/index\/page-end\/([0-9a-f]{64})$` (`INDEX_PAGE_END_RE`). -> `Seeder#indexPageEnd`. Answer `{ endSeq }`: the seq of the author's first `page-close` or `page-moved` that verified for its log (section 12, the lowest seq the name index noted), or `null`. A pointer only: `page.feed` fetches that block and verifies it itself.
  - `/index/search/<segment>`, regex `^/index/search/([a-z0-9]{1,20}(?:-[a-z0-9]{1,20}){0,3})$` on the raw URL (`INDEX_SEARCH_RE`); the segment is 1..4 folded words joined by `-` (section 5 "Seeder name index"). A segment under 2 characters is answered with the generic 404 by the seeder. -> `indexSearch`.
- Answers (200, `Content-Type: application/json; charset=utf-8`, `Cache-Control: no-store`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, exact `Content-Length`; `HEAD` sends the headers only), exactly these keys:
  - post: `{ ref, parent: ref | null, replies: [ref], next: cursor | null, quotes: [ref], likes, reposts, repliesTotal, quotesTotal, authors: { <hex64>: { profileSeq: int | null, usernameSeq: int | null } }, indexing, complete }`. `replies` = at most 50 visible replies strictly after the cursor, ascending by `(at, author, seq)`; `next` = the cursor of the last one when more visible replies follow, else `null`; `quotes` = the newest 50 visible quotes (always the first page); `likes` / `reposts` = distinct visible engagers currently on; `repliesTotal` / `quotesTotal` = all visible replies / quotes; `authors` = the hints of the target, the parent and every listed reply and quote author; `parent` = the post's own `replyTo` when its author is visible.
  - counts: `{ counts: { <ref>: { replies, likes, reposts, quotes } }, indexing, complete }`, one entry per ref whose post is indexed or that has engagement; unknown refs and refs of authors not visible are omitted.
- Deleted posts: every answer above omits a post its author deleted (an effective tombstone, section 2) and everything that points at it. A deleted reply or quote is not listed or counted, a deleted post has no `post` answer, and a like or repost of a deleted post is not counted. The `topic`, `member` and `search` answers drop a deleted post and a deleted profile version the same way. This is the seeder's own view: a modified seeder or app can keep serving it (section 8, "Residual risks").
  - topic: `{ items: [ref], next: cursor | null, authors: { <hex64>: { profileSeq: int | null, usernameSeq: int | null } }, indexing, complete }`. `items` = at most 30 (`SEARCH_PAGE`) refs of visible public posts containing every token, ordered by `at` descending, author ascending, `seq` descending; `next` = the cursor of the last candidate examined when older candidates remain in the shortest posting list (a page stops after 30 hits or `SEARCH_SCAN` = 2000 candidates), else `null`. A short or empty `items` may carry a `next`; only `null` ends the search. `authors` = the engagement-index hints of every listed author.
  - member: `{ length, profileSeq, usernameSeq, posts: [seq], postsTotal, [page, closed, moved], indexing, complete }`. `length` = the contiguous length the seeder holds; `posts` = at most 100 seqs below `before`, descending: post records (replies and quotes included) AND the seqs of the repost records currently on; `postsTotal` = the length of that whole timeline. A visible author with nothing indexed yet answers an empty page.
  - search: `{ members: [{ author, profileSeq, usernameSeq, [page, closed, moved] }], more, indexing, complete }`. `members` = at most 20 visible accounts in the order of section 5 "Seeder name index" (never both hints `null`); only a page row carries `page: true`, `closed` and `moved` (booleans), a person's row keeps its old shape; a closed or moved page is not listed by name; `more` = more matches exist than were listed. A valid query with no hit answers `members: []`.
  - engagers: `{ ref, kind, items: [ref], next: cursor | null, total, authors: { <hex64>: { profileSeq: int | null, usernameSeq: int | null } }, indexing, complete }`. `kind` is `likes` or `reposts`. `items` = at most 20 (`ENGAGER_PAGE`) refs `<engager>:<seq>` of each visible engager's current like or repost record on that post, one per engager, ordered by `at` descending (`Date.parse` of that record, a pre-1970 `at` clamped to 0), then author ascending, then `seq` descending, strictly after the cursor. A record dated more than 86,400,000 ms (`MAX_FUTURE_MS`) after the seeder's clock is hidden, not removed, and never examined, so it does not consume the scan. At most 200 edges (`PAGE_SCAN`) are examined per page, including edges that fail visibility; `next` = the cursor of the last listed engager when older edges remain, else `null`; it never carries an engager that failed visibility, so a page that stops on the scan budget after listing some engagers continues from the last listed one, and a page that examined 200 edges without listing anyone ends the list (`next: null`). `complete` is false whenever a held engager was skipped. `total` = the distinct engagers currently on, the same `likes` or `reposts` number as the post answer, not reduced by visibility or the future rule. `authors` = the hints of exactly the listed engagers. An indexed post with no engagement is a 200 with `items: []`. Visibility is `_indexVisible` (served, gated, not dropped, admitted, not blocked) for the target and for each engager, plus the pending re-gate hold (`_searchHeld`), as for topic and account search: a held engager is omitted when examined and that answer has `complete: false`; a held target is the generic 404. The worst-case body is about 4.5 KB; `INDEX_MAX_BODY` is unchanged.
- Order: the Host check of the WebSocket entry first (421 `host inválido`); a method other than `GET` or `HEAD` on `/index` or `/index/...` -> 405 `método não permitido` with `Allow: GET, HEAD`; then the regexes.
- Topic search 200 versus 404: a well-formed query always answers 200, an unknown word included (`items: []`, `next: null`); a malformed query or cursor (a route regex miss: uppercase, an accent, `%`-encoding, a token shorter than 2 or longer than 32, more than 8 tokens, a cursor that does not parse) is the generic 404. The words are never logged.
- 404 policy: a path that matches no route, a target post not indexed, an author that is not served, gated, admitted and unblocked, a target author held for a pending re-gate (`_searchHeld`), a seeder without the index methods, a `null` answer, a throw, and a body over `INDEX_MAX_BODY` = 65,536 bytes (never, by construction: the page sizes bound every list, and `apps/cli/test/seed-ws.test.js` proves the worst case) are all the same 404 `não encontrado`, which never says why.
- Caps (global in the seeder, which never sees a client address; the per-client limit is in Caddy, below): at most `INDEX_MAX_IN_FLIGHT = 16` answers at once and a token bucket of `INDEX_RATE = 32` requests per second (burst `INDEX_BURST = 64`); beyond either, 503 `ocupado` with `Retry-After: 1`. A slot is released only once the response closed AND the seeder call settled. A response still open after `INDEX_DEADLINE_MS = 30000` is destroyed. `/index/likes` and `/index/reposts` use this same budget. Token-verified reads: when the listener holds `VARANDA_TRIAGE_TOKEN`, a `/index/group/...` or `/index/groups/...` request carrying a valid `Authorization: Bearer <token>` (same constant-time check as the moderation routes) is charged to the moderation budget (the `/moderation-post` bucket and in-flight cap) instead of the public index bucket, so a public flood of `/index` cannot starve the account service's group checks. The answer body is the same; a missing or wrong token is just a public request charged to the public bucket, and every other `/index` route is always public.
- Search and the liker and reposter lists share these caps (16 in flight, 32 per second with burst 64, 30 s) and Caddy's 20 requests per 10 s per client. The 421 / 405 / generic-404 policy is the same, with no log and no echo of the query. The PWA service worker already bypasses `/index/`.
- Nothing is logged, and neither the path nor any key is echoed.
- Caddy: `handle /index/*` -> `127.0.0.1:{$VARANDA_WS_PORT}` with `header_up Host {upstream_hostport}` and `X-Forwarded-For` / `X-Real-Ip` removed; `/index/*` is excluded from `@mutavel` and `@ausenteApp`, so the seeder's `no-store` and 404 reach the client. The CSP is unchanged (same-origin `fetch`). The app's service worker never intercepts `/index/` (only that prefix: an app path such as `/indexes` keeps the shell).
- Per-client limit (Caddy, not the seeder): `handle /index/*` runs `rate_limit` with one zone keyed on `{remote_host}`: 20 requests per 10 s per client, then 429 with `Retry-After`. Tighter than `/media/*` because an answer costs the seeder more than a block read and a screen needs a handful of them (counts for up to 31 refs per request). Same privacy rules as `/media/*`: the address is only in Caddy's memory for the window, never logged and never forwarded.
- Trust: section 5 "Seeder engagement index". Counts, completeness and the freshness of the "latest" profile / username hint are the seeder's claims.
- Search residual risks: the seeder sees the folded query in the path (neither the seeder nor Caddy logs it); an anonymous client learns at most 20 keys per probe, keys already in the seeder's hello inventory; ordering, omissions and completeness are seeder claims, and the seeder may point at an older authentic profile / username record (a stale name); a member blocked while a search is on screen can stay in the rows already shown until the next search; there is no opt-out in this version.
- Engagers residual risks: a seeder can hide a liker or reposter, reorder the list or point at an older authentic on record; an engager can date `at` up to 24 h ahead of the seeder clock and be listed first (a record more than 86,400,000 ms ahead is hidden, not removed); the seeder learns which post's list was opened; Caddy's error log can record the URI when proxying to the seeder fails.

### Seeder logs

`seed` writes its events (above) only to stdout, one per line (`--json`: JSON Lines), and errors to stderr. No line carries a time stamp (the supervisor adds one), an IP address or port of a peer, a block's content, a certificate or a secret, and the health endpoint logs no request. Diary keys and peer keys (Noise public keys, `peerKey8` in human lines) do appear: they are pseudonymous identifiers. Rotation and retention belong to the supervisor (`deploy/seeder/`: Docker `json-file` with `max-size` / `max-file`, journald `MaxRetentionSec`); the values shipped there are provisional pending the legal opinion on Marco Civil Art. 15 access logs (issue #38). The README section "Política de registros" is the operator-facing policy.

### FeedItem

`FeedItem = { kind: 'post', author: hex64, name: string | null, username: string | null, seq, at, text, replyTo: null | { author, seq }, quote: null | { author, seq }, media: null | [MediaDescriptor] }` (`media`: a copy of the post's descriptors, `null` when it has none; `replyTo` and `quote` are never both non-null) or a repost item `{ kind: 'repost', author, name, username, seq, at, to: { author, seq }, text: null, replyTo: null, quote: null, media: null }`, one per target the author still reposts, with `seq` / `at` of the effective `on` record (`engagementState`, section 2); repost items follow the same order, `before` cursor, `limit` and future rule as posts (`username` comes from the author's newest own `username` record if it verifies, else `null`; section 3, "Username record"), ascending by `(at, author, seq)`; future-dated posts (R5.10) are excluded before `--limit` applies; `--limit` keeps the LAST n. `key8` = first 8 hex chars. In the CLI's human `feed` output a repost item is its header line alone, ending in `republicou <key8>#<seq>`, and a quote's header ends in `cita <key8>#<seq>`; `log` prints `#<seq> curtiu <key8>#<seq>` / `descurtiu`, `#<seq> republicou <key8>#<seq>` / `desfez republicação`, and a quote as `publicação <text> cita <key8>#<seq>`.

## 8. Module map and exports

- `packages/core/lib/peek.js` (pure, Espiadinha, section 6): `PEEK_HEAD_BLOCKS = 3`, `PEEK_TAIL_BLOCKS = 8`, `PEEK_MAX_BLOCKS_PER_DIARY = 64`, `PEEK_MAX_ROUNDS = 3`, `PEEK_PARALLEL = 16`, `PEEK_FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS = 10000`, `PEEK_PAGE_TIMEOUT_MS = 25000`, `PEEK_CONNECT_TIMEOUT_MS = 15000`, `PEEK_SNAPSHOT_TTL_MS = 300000`, `PEEK_PAGE_MAX_BYTES = 16777216`, `PEEK_RULE`; `peekCandidates({ inventory, follows, blocked })`, `peekWindow(length, fetched)`, `diaryPeek({ key, length, entries, fetched, association, revokedIssuers, now })`, `buildPeekPage({ diaries, limit, after, hidden })` (`hidden` null or a hex64 key, any case, `TypeError('hidden inválido')` otherwise; that diary is paged exactly as if shown, with the same rounds, `needs` and `next`, and its items are removed only from the returned page) -> `{ items, next, needs }`, `isPeekCursor(value)`, `retainRecords(entries)`. `diaryPeek` verifies the certificate and username, copies media and keeps at most 3 eligible posts in seq-descending order; `retainRecords` copies only valid block-0, profile, username, post and repost records.
- `packages/web-member/lib/peek.js`: `createPeeker({ storage, association, seederUrl, createSocket, ownKey, log, limits })` (`ownKey` a required getter returning the viewer's lowercase hex64 key or a Promise of it (the session supplies its cached own key); that key is fetched and paged like any other and its items are removed from the page; `TypeError('ownKey required')` when missing) -> `{ read({ limit, after }), abort(), close(), hasSnapshot() }` (`hasSnapshot` and `limits` are test hooks), `PEEK_SNAPSHOT_MAX_BYTES = 33554432`, `PEEK_CORE_PAGE_SIZE = 65536`. `read` returns the `peek.read` result of [rpc.md](rpc.md) 5.22; `abort` drops the snapshot and releases the current read slot immediately; `close` also refuses future reads.
- `packages/core/lib/notices.js` (section 3 "Notices channel", section 5 "Seeder follow index"): besides the proof-gated notices reader (`page`, `rowsFor`), `createNoticeIndex` returns the public readers `followsPage({ author, kind, after, limit, visible, now })` -> `{ items: [{ key, seq, at }], next: null | { atMs, key }, total, incomplete }` and `followCounts(author, visible)` -> `{ seguindo, seguidores, incomplete }`, from committed state only; exports `MAX_NONMEMBER_TARGETS_PER_AUTHOR = 64`.
- `packages/core/lib/cert.js`: `isHex(str, bytes)`, `normalizeHex(str)`, `certPayload(cert)` -> Buffer, `createCert({ member, ageBand = '18+', method = 'stub', date = new Date().toISOString() }, associationKeyPair)` -> Certificate, `validateCertShape(cert)` -> `null | 'malformed'`, `verifyCert(cert, associationHex)` -> `{ ok, reason }`, `certForKey(cert, keyHex, associationHex)` -> `{ ok, reason }`, `generateAssociationKeyPair()`, `refuseSymlinkDir(dir)` (throws `Error('diretório é um link simbólico')` when `dir` is a symlink; a missing dir is fine), `saveKeyPairFile(file, kp, { overwrite = false })` (writes through a unique temp name `.<base>.<12 random bytes hex>.tmp` opened with `'wx'` and mode `0o600`, fsynced; without `overwrite` the publish is a hard `link` to `file`, which fails with `EEXIST` when it exists, so an existing key is never clobbered; with `overwrite: true` a rename; the temp name is always unlinked and the directory fsynced; parent dir `0o700`, refused when a symlink), `loadKeyPairFile(file)` -> `{ publicKey, secretKey }` Buffers (throws `Error('arquivo de chave inválido')` on bad shape), `saveAssociationKeyPair(file, kp)` / `loadAssociationKeyPair(file)` (wrappers over the two above; the association loader keeps its message `chave da associação inválida`), `seederCertPayload(cert)` -> Buffer, `createSeederCert({ seeder, date }, associationKeyPair)` -> SeederCertificate, `validateSeederCertShape(cert)` -> `null | 'malformed'`, `verifySeederCert(cert, associationHex)` -> `{ ok, reason }`, `seederCertForKey(cert, noiseHex, associationHex)` -> `{ ok, reason }` (section 3), `USERNAME_RE`, `isUsername(s)`, `issuerCertPayload(cert)` -> Buffer, `createIssuerCert({ issuer, date }, associationKeyPair)` -> IssuerCertificate, `validateIssuerCertShape(cert)` -> `null | 'malformed'`, `verifyIssuerCert(cert, associationHex)` -> `{ ok, reason }`, `certPayloadV2(cert)` -> Buffer, `createIssuedCert({ member, date }, issuerKeyPair, issuerCert)` -> v2 Certificate, `usernameRecordPayload(record)` -> Buffer, `createUsernameRecord({ member, username, date }, issuerKeyPair, issuerCert)` -> username record, `validateUsernameRecordShape(record)` -> `null | 'malformed'`, `verifyUsernameRecord(record, memberHex, associationHex, { revokedIssuers })` -> `{ ok, reason }`, `moderatorCertPayload(cert)` -> Buffer, `createModeratorCert({ moderator, date }, associationKeyPair)` -> ModeratorCertificate, `validateModeratorCertShape(cert)` -> `null | 'malformed'`, `verifyModeratorCert(cert, associationHex)` -> `{ ok, reason }`, and the shape helpers `isLowerHex(str, bytes)`, `isCanonicalIso(s)`, `isPlainObject(o)`, `hasExactKeys(o, keys)` (section 3; `verifyCert` and `certForKey` also take `{ revokedIssuers }`). `validateCertShape`, `verifyCert`, `certForKey`, the four seeder-cert functions, the issuer-cert, moderator-cert and username-record validators and verifiers never throw (R4.3).
- `packages/core/lib/age-proof.js` (section 3 "Age attestations"; pure: `b4a`, `hypercore-crypto` and `./cert`, which also exports `checkIssuer`, `verifyIssued` and `validDelegation` for it): `AGE_VERIFIED_PREFIX`, `AGE_SINCE_PREFIX`, `MAX_AGE_PROOF_BYTES`, `ageVerifiedPayload(att)` / `ageSincePayload(att)` -> Buffer, `createAgeVerified({ member, date }, issuerKeyPair, issuerCert)`, `createAgeSince({ since, date }, issuerKeyPair, issuerCert)`, `validateAgeVerifiedShape(att)` / `validateAgeSinceShape(att)` -> `null | 'malformed'`, `verifyAgeVerified(att, memberHex, associationHex, { revokedIssuers })` and `verifyAgeSince(att, associationHex, { revokedIssuers })` -> `{ ok, reason }`. The validators and verifiers never throw.
- `packages/core/lib/phone.js` (section 3 "Reports and operator requests", the phone-verification request; pure: `b4a` and `hypercore-crypto` only): `PREFIX` = `'varanda-phone-v1'`, `ACTIONS` = `['send', 'check', 'status']` (frozen), `NONCE_RE` = `/^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{43,128}$/`, `BR_MOBILE_RE` = `/^\+55[1-9][1-9]9[0-9]{8}$/` (a Brazilian mobile in E.164; the number is never a signed field), `isBrMobile(value)`, `phonePayload({ action, nonce, member, association })` -> the UTF-8 bytes (TypeError on a bad shape), `signPhone(fields, keyPair)` -> lowercase hex128, `verifyPhone(fields, signatureHex)` -> `{ ok: true } | { ok: false, reason: 'malformed' | 'bad-signature' }`. `verifyPhone` never throws.

- `packages/core/lib/feed.js`: `MAX_FUTURE_MS = 86400000`, `buildFeed({ logs: [{ key, entries }], limit = Infinity, now = Date.now(), before = null })` -> `{ items: FeedItem[], future: ['<hex64>:<seq>', ...] }` (a valid post whose `Date.parse(at) - now > MAX_FUTURE_MS` is excluded from `items` and its `formatRef` reference is appended to `future`, ascending by author then seq; `before` = `{ at, author, seq }` keeps only the items strictly before that cursor in feed order `(Date.parse(at), author, seq)`, any other non-null value is `TypeError('before inválido')`, and `future` is never paged; `limit` then keeps the LAST `limit` items; `now` is injectable for tests; pure, inputs not mutated), `shortKey(hex)` -> first 8 chars, `formatFeedItem(item)` -> string.
- `packages/core/lib/store.js`: `openStore(dataDir, { lock = 'posix', recover = false, suspend } = {})` -> Corestore (takes the `<dataDir>/.varanda-lock` data-dir lock first, section 1 (rule 3: our own pid is live only with a token this process holds): `Error('diretório de dados em uso')` with `code 'LOCKED'` when another live process holds it, `LOCK_INVALID` / `STORE_INVALID` for a non-directory lock path or a symlinked `store/`; released once, by token, on a successful `store.close()` and on process exit; `lock: 'worklet'` and `recover` per section 1 "Lock modes", `Error('lock inválido')` for any other `lock`; `suspend` passed to Corestore only when not `undefined`), `lockHeld(dataDir)` -> boolean (section 1 rule 9), `storeExists(dataDir)`, `openDiary(store)` -> core, `openReplica(store, hex)` -> core, `openMediaReplica(store, hex)` -> core (the same read-only session without the JSON value encoding: media blocks are raw bytes; a key this store can sign for is refused with `Error('chave do próprio diário')`), `openQuarantine(dataDir, { suspend } = {})` -> Corestore (`fs.rmSync(path.join(dataDir, 'quarantine'), { recursive: true, force: true })`, then `new Corestore(path.join(dataDir, 'quarantine'))` (with `{ suspend }` when given) awaited ready), `removeQuarantine(dataDir)` (sync `rmSync`, called after `qstore.close()` on shutdown, best effort), `purgeReplica(store, hex)` (`const c = store.get({ key: b4a.from(hex, 'hex') }); await c.ready(); await c.purge()`: a fresh session, so it also works after hypercore closed the key's sessions on a conflict), `readAll(core)` -> `[{ seq, record }]` (R4.1: reads every block `0..present-1` (the present length of section 2 "Clearing deleted bytes"; `core.contiguousLength` before byte clearing) with `core.get(i, { wait: false, raw: true })` + `decodeBlock`; a `null` result becomes `{ seq: i, record: null }`; never throws `DECODING_ERROR`, never stops early), `appendRecord(core, record, limits?)` -> seq (throws `Error('limite do diário atingido')` when the encoded block exceeds `MAX_BLOCK_BYTES`, the diary already holds `MAX_LOG_BLOCKS` blocks, or `byteLength` + block would exceed `MAX_LOG_BYTES`; `limits` overrides them for tests), `readConfig(dataDir)` -> object | null, `writeConfig(dataDir, config)`, `readFollows(dataDir)` -> `string[]`, `writeFollows(dataDir, list)`, `readJson(file, fallback)`, `writeJsonAtomic(file, obj, mode)` (unique `.<base>.<pid>.<random>.tmp` in the same directory opened `O_WRONLY | O_CREAT | O_EXCL | O_NOFOLLOW` with `mode`, fsync, rename over `file`, then fsync of the parent directory (only EISDIR/EPERM/EINVAL ignored), temp unlinked on failure; section 1 "Files and replicas").
- `packages/core/lib/admission.js`: the `attachAdmission` implementation (and its message validation); `packages/core/lib/net.js` re-exports it, so callers keep requiring `attachAdmission` from `net.js`.
- `packages/core/lib/net.js`:
  - `topicFor(associationHex)` -> Buffer, `parseBootstrap(value)` -> `undefined | string[]`, `generateNoiseKeyPair()` -> `require('hyperdht').keyPair()`, `createSwarm({ bootstrap, keyPair, host } = {})` -> Hyperswarm (section 4, R5.11; `firewalled: false` only for a loopback `host`), `joinAndFlush(swarm, topics)` -> Promise, `startRediscovery(swarm, topics, { intervalMs = 1000, hasPeer = () => false, log })` -> `stop()` (section 4; `TypeError` unless every topic is a 32-byte buffer).
  - `attachAdmission(socket, { role, cert = null, have, onmessage, onclose, oninventory, log })` -> `Peer { peerKey, socket, role, remoteRole, remoteCert, remoteCount, remoteHave (Map, latest-wins), inventoryComplete, badMessages, opened (Promise<boolean>), hello (Promise<hello | null>), send(msg) -> boolean, close() }`: sends `hello` with `count`, at most 100 `have` entries and, when `role === 'seeder'`, `cert` (throws `TypeError('seeder role requires cert')` when `cert` is not a plain object), then the overflow as `have` messages (R4.4); `remoteCert` = the hello's `cert` or `null`; `socket` = the hyperswarm socket it was attached to; `remoteHave` follows R5.1 b (hello replaces, `have`/`admitted` set, `rejected` deletes before `onmessage`); a valid `hello` is dispatched to `onmessage` BEFORE its inventory completion check, so `oninventory` sees the caller's state updated from that hello (section 5 "Bounded inventory"); `opened`/`hello` settle `false`/`null` when the channel closes first (section 5) and never reject.
  - `validateMessage(msg)` -> boolean (rejects a `hello` with more than 100 `have` entries, `count < have.length`, `count > MAX_INVENTORY`, a seeder `hello` without a plain-object `cert` or a member `hello` with one, any `length > MAX_LOG_BLOCKS`, and a `rejected.reason` not matching `/^[a-z0-9-]{1,64}$/`).
  - Limits (S1, S6): `MAX_INVENTORY = 4096`, `MAX_BAD_MESSAGES = 16`, `HELLO_TIMEOUT_MS = 10000`, `MAX_CONNECTIONS = 64`, `MAX_FETCH_ATTACHMENTS = MAX_CONNECTIONS` (open sockets per quarantine fetch); `attachAdmission` also takes `helloTimeoutMs` and drops the peer (`{ event: 'peer-dropped', peerKey, reason }` through `log`, then `socket.destroy()`) for `inventory-limit`, `bad-messages`, `hello-timeout` and `duplicate-hello`; `refuseOverConnectionLimit(swarm, socket, { limit = MAX_CONNECTIONS, log })` -> boolean (section 5 "Per-connection limits").
  - `watchContiguous(core, onGrow)` -> `stop()`.
  - `watchFork(core, onFork)` -> `stop()`: listens to `'truncate'` and `'conflict'` on the session; calls `onFork('fork')` at most once; returns a remover (R5.9 b).
  - `startQuarantineFetch(qstore, keyHex, sockets, timeoutMs, opts?)` -> `{ promise, addSocket, attachedCount }`: `core = qstore.get({ key: b4a.from(keyHex, 'hex') })`; `promise` resolves `{ raw: Buffer | null, error: null | 'timeout', length, byteLength }` (`length`/`byteLength` = the quarantine core's signed length and byteLength after the fetch, from the upgrade proof that came with block 0; `0` on timeout; the seeder's quota gate, S3): `await core.ready()`; `core.replicate(s)` for every initial socket; `raw = await core.get(0, { raw: true, timeout: timeoutMs })` inside try/catch — any throw (REQUEST_TIMEOUT or other) gives `{ raw: null, error: 'timeout' }`; a `null` block (cannot happen with wait true, kept for safety) also gives `'timeout'`; FINALLY `await core.purge()` always runs (whatever the outcome) and a purge throw propagates to the caller, who treats it as a fatal purge failure (R5.5 e, R5.8 e). `addSocket(socket)` -> boolean attaches one more peer to the same quarantine core while the fetch is in flight (`core.replicate(socket)`, which hypercore defers until the core is open and ignores for a socket already attached); it returns `false` once the fetch has settled (the core is being purged) or for a socket already added. `sockets` must be non-empty (`TypeError` otherwise) hyperswarm sockets already replicated by the main store.
  - `async quarantineGet0(qstore, keyHex, sockets, timeoutMs)` -> the `promise` of `startQuarantineFetch` with the same arguments (no late sockets; used by the seeder, whose admission is attached only to the announcer).
- `packages/core/lib/seeder.js` (operator commands, issue #34): statics `Seeder.checkConfig(dataDir, associationKey)` -> `associationHex` (the read-only twin of `ensureConfig`: a missing `config.json` is `diretório não é de um semeador`, never written), `Seeder.status(dataDir, associationKey)` -> the `seed status` result, `async Seeder.list(dataDir, associationKey)` -> the `seed list` result and `async Seeder.drop(dataDir, associationKey, key)` -> `{ key }` (section 7); instance `stats()` -> `{ status, admitted, dropping, blocks, bytes, connections, peers, firewalled, blocked, blocklistSeq, blocklistHeld, mediaCores, mediaBytes }` (the health endpoint of section 7; counts only; `connections` includes external sockets), `mediaKeys()` -> the bound media core keys, `mediaRead(diaryHex, coreHex, start, blocks)` (section 5 "Seeder media admission"), constructor options `maxMedia`, `maxMediaBlocks`, `maxMediaBytes`; `Seeder.status` is unchanged by media, `acceptStream(rawStream, { handshakeTimeoutMs = 10000, onRefuse })` -> `NoiseSecretStream | null` (admits a member over a raw duplex that is not the swarm as a Noise responder keyed with `seeder-key.json`, behind the frame guard; section 4 "External transports"); exports `MAX_HANDSHAKE_FRAME`, `MAX_HANDSHAKE_BYTES` and `MAX_EXTERNAL_FRAME` (the frame guard's limits, for tests).
- `packages/core/lib/seeder.js`: `class Seeder` constructed as `new Seeder({ dataDir, associationKey = null, bootstrap, host, log = () => {}, purgeHook, gateHook, admitHook })` with `start()` -> `{ topic: hex, admitted: hex64[] }` (startup order of section 5, R5.6), `close()`, `admittedKeys()`; `log(eventObject)` receives exactly the `--json` events of section 7. `purgeHook(core)`, when given, replaces `core.purge()` inside `purgeReplica` calls made by the seeder (tests only); `gateHook(key)` runs after the quarantine fetch (R5.5 c, tests only); `admitHook(key, step)` with `step` `'persist'` | `'open'` is awaited right before that step of admission (d) and a throw simulates the step failing (tests only; exercises the `admission-failed` rollback). Statics: `Seeder.createKey(dataDir)` -> `{ publicKey }`, `Seeder.addCert(dataDir, certFile, associationHex)` -> `{ seeder }`, `Seeder.ensureConfig(dataDir, associationKey)` -> `associationHex`. Admission uses the per-key promise chain, the two in-memory maps `admitted` (keys being served) and `dropping` (keys being purged, R6.1) and ONE serialized file writer `persist()` that writes the union of both maps (R4.7, R5.7, R6.1); `admittedKeys()` returns only `admitted` keys; an announce for a key in `dropping` waits on that key's per-key chain; `start()` opens the main store before the quarantine store (R6.2); `close()` closes the quarantine store, waits for in-flight admission steps, removes the quarantine directory, waits for drops in progress and closes the main store LAST (the data-dir lock is released last, section 5 "Seeder shutdown"); constant `GATE_TIMEOUT_MS = 15000`; constructor options `maxLogBlocks`, `maxLogBytes`, `maxConnections` (tests only; defaults = the constants) and the quota gate/drop (`quota`) and S7 rollback purge of section 5.
- `packages/core/lib/sync.js`: `runSync({ dataDir, associationKey, bootstrap, host, once, timeoutMs, log, onEvent, signal, purgeHook, regateHook, joinHook, storeOptions })` -> SyncResult (includes `skipped` and `errorsSoft`); `storeOptions` is forwarded as is to `openStore` and `openQuarantine` (section 1 "Lock modes"; `undefined` = their defaults); opens the main store before the quarantine store (R6.2) and closes it LAST at shutdown (section 6 "Shutdown order": quarantine closed and removed, in-flight fetches and purges settled, then `store.close()` releases the data-dir lock); a peer advertising a pending key during its quarantine fetch joins that fetch through `addSocket` (R5.8 b); constants `GATE_TIMEOUT_MS = 15000`, `GATE_MIN_TIMEOUT_MS = 250`, `GATE_RETRY_MS = 15000` (R5.8). Test-only hooks: `purgeHook(key, store)` replaces `purgeReplica` for every purge of the run, `regateHook(key)` returns a verdict that overrides the local re-gate of a replica opened after a remote pass (`null`/`undefined` = run the real gate), `joinHook(swarm, topics)` replaces `joinAndFlush` (a never-settling join exercises the startup interruption of section 6); injectable `unverifiedBudgetMs` (constant `UNVERIFIED_BUDGET_MS = 5000`, exported), `maxLogBlocks`, `maxLogBytes`, `maxConnections`, test-only `maxFetchSockets` (per-fetch socket cap, defaults to `maxConnections`); test-only `inspectHook({ claimCount })`; SyncResult carries `seederConfirmed`; extra events `claim-ignored`, `peer-ignored`, `peer-dropped` (sections 5 and 6). `packages/core/lib/sync-format.js` exports `formatEvent(ev)` (the human line of section 7 `sync`, or `null`) and `key8(hex)`; `apps/cli/lib/commands/sync.js` re-exports `formatEvent` and `safe` from `packages/core/lib/safe.js` (S4).
- `packages/core/lib/config.js`: `validBootstrap(entry)` (the `--bootstrap` rule of section 4, used by `apps/cli/lib/cli.js` and the node session) and `isLoopbackBootstrap(entry)`.
- `packages/core/lib/session.js`: `createSession(...)`, `toRpcError(err, onInternalError)`, `checkConfig(...)` (section 9).
- `packages/core/lib/safe.js`: `safe(str)` (human lines: C0, DEL, C1, U+2028/9, U+202A-E, U+2066-9 escaped as `\u{hex}`), `jsonLine(value)` (`JSON.stringify` plus DEL, C1, U+2028/9 and bidi controls escaped as `\uXXXX`; parses back to the same value), `isUnsafe(cp)`, `cpWidth(cp)` / `displayWidth(str)` (over-estimating: >= U+1100 counts 2 except general punctuation and currency; below U+1100 counts 1). `apps/cli/lib/commands/member.js` exports `feedWidth(stream)`.
- `packages/core/lib/errors.js`: `class UsageError extends Error` (`code = 'USAGE'`), exported as `{ UsageError }`; `packages/core/lib/seeder.js` throws it and `apps/cli/lib/cli.js` re-exports the same class, so `instanceof` holds across the CLI and the core.
- `packages/core/lib/member.js`: the member operations over a data directory (`idCreate`, `idShow`, `certAdd`, `profileSet`, `postCreate`, `postReply`, `followAdd`, `followRemove`, `followList`, `logRead`, `feedRead`, plus `trustRoot`, `withIdentity`, `ensureMemberConfig`, `publishConfig`, `fsyncDir`, `deepEqual`), each taking `ctx = { dataDir, association, storeOptions }` and returning the object the CLI prints with `--json`. It never writes to stdout or stderr (`feedRead` reports the feed warnings through `onWarn`) and opens every store with `openStore(dataDir, storeOptions)`. `apps/cli/lib/commands/member.js` is the CLI wrapper: flags, usage strings, the certificate file and printing. `feedRead` also returns `incomplete` (docs/rpc.md 5.13); the CLI does not print it.
- `packages/core/lib/clock.js`: `now()`, monotonic milliseconds for the rate buckets (section "Runtimes").
- `packages/core/index.js`: `{ records, safe, feed, cert, store, net, sync, errors }`, each the module of the same name under `packages/core/lib/`; `seeder` and `assoc` are deep-require only (`@varanda/core/lib/seeder`, `@varanda/core/lib/assoc`). Inside the core, `#fs`, `#path` and `#process` resolve through the package `imports` of `packages/core/package.json` (conditions `bare` / `default`) to `packages/core/lib/node/fs.js`, `path.js` and `process.js` on Node, which re-export `node:fs`, `node:path` and `node:process`, and to `packages/core/lib/bare/fs.js`, `path.js` and `process.js` under Bare (see "Runtimes" below).
- `packages/core/lib/bare/fs-facade.js`: `createFs(backend)` -> fs object (pure: the backend is injected, so Node tests drive it with a fake backend; `STRING_FLAGS` exported). `packages/core/lib/bare/fs.js` = `createFs(require('bare-fs'))`; `packages/core/lib/bare/path.js` = `bare-path`; `packages/core/lib/bare/process.js` = `bare-process` (4.5.1 has `pid`, `kill`, `on`, `once`, `removeListener` and forwards Bare's `'exit'` event, so no shim). `packages/core/test/bare/smoke.js` is the script `packages/core/test/bare-load.test.js` runs under `node_modules/.bin/bare` (devDependency `bare-runtime`): it must print `BARE OK` and exit 0.

### Runtimes (Node and Bare)

On Node (`default` condition) the core uses `node:fs` directly: every statement of this document about `O_EXCL`, `O_NOFOLLOW`, `O_NONBLOCK`, `'wx'` and the fd forms of `readFileSync`/`writeFileSync` is the kernel's behaviour. Under Bare (`bare` condition; the iOS/Android app's worklet, react-native-bare-kit) `#fs` is the facade of `lib/bare/fs-facade.js` over bare-fs 4.8.4, whose `constants` lack `O_EXCL`, `O_NOFOLLOW` and `O_NONBLOCK` (so its `'wx'` is NOT exclusive) and whose `readFileSync`/`writeFileSync` take only a path. The facade:

- passes every backend function through; `constants` are the backend's plus `O_EXCL`, `O_NOFOLLOW`, `O_NONBLOCK` (the backend's own value when it defines one, else a synthetic bit above every real `O_*` bit, asserted at creation to collide with none);
- `openSync(path, flags = 'r', mode = 0o666)` parses the string flags itself (`r`, `r+`, `w`, `w+`, `a`, `a+`, `wx`, `wx+`, `xw`, `xw+`, `ax`, `ax+`, `xa`, `xa+`; anything else -> `TypeError` with `code 'ERR_INVALID_ARG_VALUE'` as on Node) and emulates the missing flags with ONE `lstat` of the path BEFORE the open: `O_CREAT|O_EXCL` and the path exists in any form (dangling symlink included) -> `EEXIST`; `O_NOFOLLOW` on a symlink -> `ELOOP`; `O_NONBLOCK` on a non-regular file (FIFO, socket, device; a directory opened read-only is allowed, as on Node) -> refused WITHOUT opening it, with `ENXIO` for a write open (what Node reports for a FIFO without reader: `lib/assoc.js` `appendPrivate` -> `registro de auditoria inválido`) and `ELOOP` for a read open (on Node the open succeeds and the caller's `fstat` check refuses it; `lib/cert.js` `readKeyFile` maps `ELOOP`/`EMLINK` to the same `arquivo de chave inválido`). Only real backend bits reach `backend.openSync` (synthetic bits stripped, never a string). Errors carry `code`, `syscall: 'open'` and `path` like the backend's;
- `readFileSync(pathOrFd, opts)`: a numeric fd is read from its current position until EOF (the `fstat` size is only a hint) and left open; a path is opened with `opts.flag || 'r'` through the facade and closed. `writeFileSync(pathOrFd, data, opts)`: a numeric fd gets every byte written at its current position (looping on partial writes) and is left open; a path is opened with `opts.flag || 'w'` and `opts.mode || 0o666` through the facade (so `{ flag: 'wx' }` is exclusive) and closed. Strings are UTF-8; results are Buffers unless an encoding is given.
- Residual (TOCTOU): lstat-then-open is a window in which a path replaced between the two calls is followed or clobbered. Accepted because the app's data dir is private to one worklet inside the app sandbox (no other writer races it); on Node the real kernel flags apply.
- Globals: the core relies on `structuredClone` only when `typeof structuredClone === 'function'` (bare-runtime 1.34.1 has it; `lib/records.js` falls back to a plain-data walker). The rate buckets (`lib/net.js` `tokenBucket`, `lib/seeder.js` `_takeAnnounceToken`) read the monotonic clock of `lib/clock.js`: `performance.now()` where the runtime defines `performance` (Node), else `process.hrtime.bigint()` in milliseconds through `#process` (bare-runtime 1.34.1 defines no `performance` global). No host shim is needed; the Bare smoke test runs without one.
- `apps/cli/lib/cli.js`: `parseArgs(argv, env, cwd)` -> flags (keys include `seeder` and `dhtHost`), `HELP` (pt-BR, section 7), `UsageError`. `apps/cli/bin/varanda.js`: router loading every `apps/cli/lib/commands/*.js`; each exports `{ commands: { <name>: { summary, run(flags) } } }`; applies the shutdown hardening of section 7 (R3 e).
- `apps/cli/lib/commands/assoc.js` (`assoc`, including `certify-seeder`), `apps/cli/lib/commands/member.js` (`id`, `cert`, `profile`, `post`, `reply`, `follow`, `unfollow`, `follows`, `log`, `feed`), `apps/cli/lib/commands/sync.js` (`sync`), `apps/cli/lib/commands/seed.js` (`seed`, `seed key`, `seed cert add`, `seed status`, `seed list`, `seed drop`).
- `apps/cli/lib/seed-ws.js`: `startSeedWs({ host = '127.0.0.1', port, seeder, origins, media })` (`origins`: optional array of exact browser origins; default http on a loopback hostname; `media`: test-only overrides `{ maxInFlight, rate, burst, deadlineMs }` of the media route, section 7 "Seeder media route"; also exports `MEDIA_MAX_BLOCKS`, `MEDIA_MAX_IN_FLIGHT`, `MEDIA_RATE`, `MEDIA_BURST`, `MEDIA_DEADLINE_MS`) -> Promise of `{ host, port, close() }` (listening; a listen error rejects with its `code`; `close()` is idempotent and terminates open sockets), `DEFAULT_HOST` (section 7 "Seeder WebSocket entry").
- `packages/core/lib/topic-index.js` (pure, section 5 "Seeder topic index"): `foldText`, `tokensOf`, `createTopicIndex({ maxPosts, perAuthorMax, maxTerms, maxPostings, perAuthorPostingsMax, perAuthorTermsMax })`, `compareNewestFirst`, `parseSearchCursor`, `SEARCH_PAGE = 30`, `SEARCH_SCAN = 2000`, `MIN_TOKEN`, `MAX_TOKEN`, `MAX_QUERY_TOKENS`, `MAX_FUTURE_MS`, `QUERY_RE`, `CURSOR_RE`, the `DEFAULT_MAX_*` caps and the per-diary shares `DEFAULT_PER_AUTHOR_POSTINGS_MAX`, `DEFAULT_PER_AUTHOR_TERMS_MAX`.
- `apps/cli/lib/health.js`: `startHealth({ host = '127.0.0.1', port, stats })` -> Promise of `{ host, port, close() }` (listening; a listen error rejects with its `code`; `close()` is idempotent and also closes open sockets), `metricsText(snapshot)` -> the `/metrics` body, `DEFAULT_HOST` (section 7 "Seeder health endpoint").
- `deploy/seeder/`: `Dockerfile` (build context = repository root, filtered by the root `.dockerignore`), `compose.yaml`, `varanda-seeder.service` (systemd), `seeder.env.example`, `journald-varanda.conf`; README "Operar o semeador".
- `scripts/run-tests.js`, `apps/cli/scripts/demo.js`, `apps/cli/scripts/local-dht.js`; `apps/cli/test/helpers/net.js` (`testnet(t)`, `tmpDir(t, prefix)`, `localSwarmOptions(bootstrap)` -> `{ bootstrap, host: '127.0.0.1' }`, `bootstrapFlags(list)` (appends `'--dht-host', '127.0.0.1'`), `until(fn, opts)`) and `apps/cli/test/helpers/ws-client.js` (`connect(server, origin)` and `openSession(server, origin)`: the scripted WebSocket client of the `varanda-serve` tests, with `send`/`response`/`call`, `event(name, match)` and `events(name)`); `apps/cli/test/*.test.js` with brittle: every test file creates the in-process testnet through the helper; every socket is opened against its bootstrap list and binds `127.0.0.1`; no test touches the public DHT.
- Scaffold rules (R5.13, R6.4, R6.5):
  - `scripts/run-tests.js`: a pool of `VARANDA_TEST_CONCURRENCY` files at a time (default `max(1, min(4, floor(cpus/2)))`, cpus from `os.availableParallelism()` or else `os.cpus().length`) with async `spawn(process.execPath, [file], { detached: true })` (the child becomes a process-group leader); stdio is `'inherit'` when the pool is 1, otherwise stdout and stderr are piped (`['ignore', 'pipe', 'pipe']`) and printed as one block per file after the child closes; a timer of `Number(process.env.VARANDA_TEST_TIMEOUT_MS) || 180000` ms calls `process.kill(-child.pid, 'SIGKILL')` (falling back to `child.kill('SIGKILL')` if that throws), prints `# tempo esgotado: <file>` and counts a failure; `exit` with a signal or non-zero status counts a failure; the runner's own SIGINT/SIGTERM kills every live group the same way, removes every live temp root and exits 130/143; `VARANDA_TEST_SHARD=I/N` selects round robin (0-based position `i`, in the sorted list or the command-line order, kept when `i % N == I - 1`); an empty slice prints `# nenhum arquivo neste fragmento` and exits 1, and an invalid slice exits 1; `VARANDA_TEST_SKIP_WORKSPACES` (comma-separated paths) prints `# workspace <caminho> pulado (coberto por outro job)` and exits 0 for a listed workspace; an empty value of `VARANDA_TEST_CONCURRENCY`, `VARANDA_TEST_SHARD`, `VARANDA_TEST_SKIP_WORKSPACES` or `VARANDA_TEST_TIMEOUT_MS` counts as unset; no `*.test.js` found (or `test/` missing) prints `# nenhum arquivo de teste` and exits 1.
  - Per-file temp root (R6.5): for each test file the runner creates `root = fs.mkdtempSync(path.join(os.tmpdir(), 'varanda-run-'))` and passes `VARANDA_TEST_TMPDIR=root` in the child's env; after the child exits for ANY reason (pass, failure, signal, timeout SIGKILL of the group) the runner removes `root` with `fs.rmSync(root, { recursive: true, force: true })`, so a SIGKILLed test (no `'exit'` handler runs) leaves nothing behind.
