  - `apps/cli/test/helpers/net.js`: `tmpDir(t, prefix)` creates its directories under `process.env.VARANDA_TEST_TMPDIR || os.tmpdir()` and records every directory in a module-level `Set`; a single `process.once('exit', ...)` handler synchronously `fs.rmSync`s every directory still in the set (brittle's `t.timeout` path runs no teardown at all); the teardown removes the entry it cleans. The exit handler and the teardowns cover a test run alone with `node apps/cli/test/<file>.test.js`; the runner's per-file root covers the SIGKILL case.
  - `until()` never leaks a rejection (R6.4): when the deadline wins the race, the still-pending predicate promise gets a no-op `.catch(() => {})` before `until` rejects, so a late rejection is not unhandled.
  - `apps/cli/test/smoke.test.js`: the DHT case asserts `dht.io.serverSocket.address()` has `host === '127.0.0.1'` and `port > 0`.
  - `apps/cli/scripts/demo.js` (`npm run demo`): non-interactive, an in-process `hyperdht/testnet(3)` bound to 127.0.0.1 (`VARANDA_BOOTSTRAP` + `VARANDA_DHT_HOST=127.0.0.1` in every child's env; the public DHT is never contacted), every command run through `apps/cli/bin/varanda.js` as a child process: two associations, identities A and B, member certificates as block 0, the seeder's key and certificate (`seed key`, `assoc certify-seeder`, `seed cert add`), profile and follow, the seeder started and awaited on `seed ready`, A publishes and `sync --once` (admitted), the seeder is restarted from disk (SIGTERM, exit 0, new process), B receives A's post through the seeder with A offline, B replies and A reads it, a certificate of another association and a seeder without certificate are refused, shutdown. Prints `DEMO OK` and exits 0; on the first failure prints `DEMO FALHOU: <passo>` plus the child's output and exits 1; a 150 s watchdog; `--keep` / `VARANDA_DEMO_KEEP=1` keeps the data directory. After `main()` settles (either way) it arms `setTimeout(() => process.exit(process.exitCode), 2000).unref()` so a referenced DHT timer can never keep the process alive.

### Residual risks (phase 0)

- Espiadinha: a seeder can bias discovery only by withholding inventory, blocks, length or a newer signed state, never by forging a post or choosing a post within the verified history. The tail comes from the signed length, and every later window must reproduce that length and tree hash. Audit: record the inventory and verified lengths/tree hashes, fetch the same diary histories, then re-run `diaryPeek` and `buildPeekPage` with the same follows, blocklist, revoked issuers, clock and cursor. Any node can recompute the page from the same inputs; reproducing an incomplete page also requires recording which blocks arrived before the cut. Dates remain author-supplied, and the 64-block cap can hide older posts. At most 16 blocks are in flight when a byte-budget cut closes the link; a hostile seeder can send that many oversized frames past the budget, bounded until receipt only by the transport frame limit. Discovery also consumes an extra external socket per fetching page, so the seeder's 32-slot external cap can refuse it while syncs remain connected.
- Fork window: with the default `allowFork: true` the rewrite is applied before we react; between the reorg and the purge (milliseconds, async) forked blocks are servable and the live range may fetch a few of them. `allowFork: false` would close the window but hides the rewrite entirely (no public signal), so detection was preferred.
- Re-gate window on the seeder: after admission the main store may hold a bogus block 0 (announcer serving a different history than it served to quarantine) until the local gate runs on the first growth event; third peers could read that single block in that window. Everything they read is still signed by the key holder and gated on their side.
- Stuck goals from lying advertisers (reduced by S2 and cycle 2 H1): peers that are not verified seeders, however many identities they use, can hold `--once` only for one `UNVERIFIED_BUDGET_MS` budget per run; a gate for a PENDING key still starts on any advertisement >= 1 and, when nobody serves block 0, the `--once` ends `tempo esgotado` (bounded by the deadline; no unsafe effect). Without a verified seeder, `ok: true` means only "every peer that showed interest within the budget said it has our diary": `seederConfirmed: false` says so.
- Sybil peers (S1): the connection cap counts connections, not identities, so a set of Noise keys can still occupy all `MAX_CONNECTIONS` slots of a seeder or a member (phase 1: per-address limits or certified-member priority).
- No seeder-wide byte budget (S3): the limits are per log, so a seeder may hold up to members x `MAX_LOG_BYTES`.
- Purging a core that a connected peer is replicating can race with that peer's messages inside hypercore (`flushHints` on a closed storage destroys the stream, observed with hypercore 11.39); the peer reconnects. The S7 rollback therefore purges only when the storage actually holds the core.
- Orphan cores: if a future bug writes a core into the main store without an `admitted.json` entry, corestore serves it on request and there is no public API to enumerate stored cores for a startup sweep. The write-ahead/purge-first ordering and the `dropping` map (a key stays persisted until its purge succeeds, R6.1) prevent this in every crash point and concurrent-persist interleaving analysed.
- `'conflict'` (equivocation without truncation) is handled like a truncation: hypercore closes the key's sessions and `purgeReplica` opens a fresh one to purge. It is exercised with two writable copies of one key (two corestores with the same primary key and name) holding different histories, the second one longer: `seeder.test.js` (the seeder logs `rejected` / `'fork'`, broadcasts it, purges once and un-lists the key) and `sync.test.js` (a followed key is skipped with `'fork'`, purged and withdrawn). Limit: hypercore only asks a longer peer for an upgrade, so two copies of the SAME length with different roots are not noticed by a node that holds one of them until a copy grows past the other. Until then the equivocation is not reported.
- Quarantine fetch waits the full `timeoutMs` when the offering peer disconnects mid-fetch and no other advertiser joins it (bounded; `--once` bounds it by the remaining time; a later advertiser joins the in-flight fetch through `addSocket`).
- Member quarantine attaches to every peer currently advertising the key >= 1, including those that advertise it while the fetch is in flight; those peers could pull block 0 back from the quarantine core, which they already claim to hold.
- Seeder admitted with 0 blocks (announcer left before the main store downloaded block 0) is dropped at the next restart (`'no-cert'`) and needs a fresh `announce`; admission is idempotent so this only costs one sync.
- Seeder certificates have no expiry or revocation (phase 2), and `announce` remains unauthenticated by design.
- `seed drop` is not a block: the author's next `announce` (any `sync` of theirs) admits the log again through the full gate. Keeping a log out is `mod block` (B2).
- B2 residual paths (the list is enforced by honest nodes, not by the wire): a node from before B2 does not enforce it; a modified app can still talk to a blocked author directly and can serve a blocked diary to other modified apps; the author's own official app keeps its diary in its store and the main Corestore serves any stored core to a peer that already knows its discovery key (section 4, "Quarantine"); the PWA enforces its stored snapshot offline and receives a newer one only through the seeder; a node is only as current as its last contact with a peer or the seeder; a torn or unparsable `blocklist.json` (or kv `'blocklist'`) reads as no list, so that node enforces nothing until a peer sends the snapshot again (only the core member warns when a stored snapshot fails verification); the web member's regate `'blocked'` branch is defensive (blocked keys are purged before they are regated); a peer that opens the blocklist channel after our open was rejected pairs again, but `supported` already resolved `false`. The list carries public keys only. `docs/verification.md` section 13 lists the tested and untested paths.
- The health endpoint has no `Host` check: a web page the operator opens could read it through DNS rebinding. It returns only aggregate counts (no key, address or content), so this was accepted rather than adding a host allowlist that would break scrapers on a private network.
- Containers that share one data dir across pid namespaces (two containers on one volume) defeat pid probing: a pid seen in another namespace means nothing here. Rule 3 keeps a restarted container from locking itself out; running two containers on the same data dir at once is unsupported (the RocksDB lock of `store/` remains the backstop). `docker compose run` against the seeder's volume is safe only with the service stopped. Observed with the service up (Docker 29): the `run` process (pid 1 in its own namespace) reads the running seeder's lock (`1 <token>`) as stale, takes it over, then fails on the RocksDB lock (`erro: File descriptor could not be locked`, exit 1, nothing purged or rewritten) and releases the lock it took; the running seeder keeps serving but has no `.varanda-lock` until it restarts, so `seed status` reports `running: false` meanwhile. Before rule 3 the same `run` failed with `LOCKED`, but a seeder killed with SIGKILL could never restart in its container.
- Tests read `dht.io.serverSocket.address()` and `dht.io.portRange` (internal but stable dht-rpc state) to prove loopback/ephemeral binding; `process.kill(-pid)` process groups are POSIX only.
- Quarantine directory garbage after a crash persists until the next start (disk only, never served: the main store never opens it).
- No check against known illegal media: `docs/design.md` (Moderação) promises a comparison of photos and videos with lists of hashes of known illegal material before showing or relaying; it is NOT implemented. Media is therefore images only (no video) until it is; a blocked diary's media cores are dropped with it.
- Old readers skip media posts entirely (section 2): a post with photos is invisible to a node from before this change, and a `follow` record is ignored there.
- Follows are public: a diary's `follow` records name every key the member follows.
- At most `MAX_MEDIA_CORES` = 4 media cores per diary, one per device: a 5th device's declaration makes the seeder drop the oldest core (`unbound`, 404 for its photos) until that device declares again with a new upload; if that device is gone, those photos are lost.
- A post can be visible before its media blocks reach the seeder (the write gate confirms the diary push; media blocks follow on the continuous sync); readers see the photo as unavailable until the author's device reconnects.
- One media core per device, no rotation: once it holds `MAX_MEDIA_BLOCKS` blocks or `MAX_MEDIA_BYTES` the browser node refuses uploads (`espaço de fotos deste aparelho esgotado`). The app uploads only when the person presses Publicar; bytes uploaded for a post that then fails stay in the core, public and unreferenced.
- Media responses are cacheable for a year (`public, max-age=31536000, immutable`): a later block of the author does not purge browsers' caches (readers' feeds hide a blocked author's posts). `mediaRead` reads each block twice (once to sum the exact `Content-Length`, once to stream).
- Push confirmation of a media core (browser node) counts a block as held when it is below what the seeder advertised OR was served to the seeder on this link; "served" is not proof of storage (`docs/rpc.md` section 11).
- The media route's seeder caps are global (16 in flight, 64 requests/s); the per-client limit (120 per 10 s) is in Caddy. Clients behind one shared address (CGNAT, an office) share one window, and many distinct addresses can still drain the global caps; it reads only what the seeder already serves over the swarm.
- Engagement index: in memory and capped (`indexMaxRecords`); at the cap cursors pause and answers say `complete: false` until a drop frees room, so a seeder at scale can stay incomplete. Counts, completeness and the freshness of the latest profile / username hint are trusted to the seeder; content never is. The index route has only global caps (16 in flight, 32 requests/s, burst 64), like the media route.
- Old readers skip `like`, `repost` and quote posts entirely (section 2); likes and reposts are public, like follows.
- Profile avatars (section 2): (a) a tab still running a bundle from before the avatar shows no name for members with a photo in search, member pages and threads, because its node rejects the 5-key block the upgraded seeder names as `profileSeq` (`packages/web-member/lib/sync.js`), until it reloads; (b) an older writer (a stale tab, an old CLI) that saves a profile writes a plain record and so removes the photo; (c) the Espiadinha reads the avatar from the peek window (`packages/core/lib/peek.js`), so a newer profile outside that window is not shown there; a `notices.list` row carries the same window-bound avatar, and the Avisos screen completes a missing photo with one `diary.peek` of the profile block the seeder index names (`apps/app/src/screens/Avisos.js`), so that photo is only as current as the index hint; (d) a removed avatar is withdrawn by a tombstone (below), which does not discard its bytes in this step, and the immutable `/media` cache (`apps/cli/lib/seed-ws.js`) can keep copies; (e) there is no matching against hash lists of known illegal content; moderation is an account report decided by a person (account reports always go to a human, `apps/cli/lib/account/ai/triage.js`) plus the blocklist (`mediaRead` refuses a blocked diary's media, `packages/core/lib/seeder.js`).
- Deletion (section 2 "Deletion" and "Clearing deleted bytes", section 5 "Seeder deletions"; `post.delete` and `profile.delete`, docs/rpc.md 5.33 and 5.34). A tombstone makes the official, up-to-date readers and seeders stop showing a post or profile version and, unless `VARANDA_NO_CLEAR=1`, discard its bytes (the block and the media ranges only it used). It is not forensic erasure and does not claim to be: (a) a fresh replica may hold a deleted block for the time between the arrival of the block and the arrival of its tombstone (it is cleared as soon as the tombstone is known), and a third peer that still holds a deleted block can hand it to a replica that asks before it knows the tombstone (the hypercore late-write window); (b) copies already obtained cannot be recalled: the immutable `/media` cache (`Cache-Control: immutable`) in browsers, proxies and the Caddy in front of the seeder, the app's image cache until it is revoked, screenshots, and what the account service quoted in a report; (c) old or modified apps, CLIs and seeders do not obey a tombstone and may keep or serve the bytes (section 2 "Deletion and old readers"); (d) browser replicas outside the registry: the browser enumerates its stored replicas through the `replicas` index, so one written by a bundle from before that index is not enumerated and is neither cleared nor purged; (e) readers from before byte clearing read up to `contiguousLength` and see an author's diary cut at the first cleared block, so official readers are upgraded before clearing is enabled (rollout order: seeders, web bundle, app, CLI; `VARANDA_NO_CLEAR` until then); (f) an author who tombstones a tombstone undoes their own deletion; if the revived target's bytes were already cleared it stays absent, and a fresh replica that receives the later tombstone before the earlier ones can stall that author's diary at the first hole (self-only: honest writers never do it, and nobody else can write into the diary); the seeder's moderation route follows the readers, its indexes do not (section 5 "Seeder deletions"); (g) a crafted half-deleted compatibility pair is no longer recognised after its bytes are cleared, and the two tombstones of a pair carry the same `at`; (h) the present-length walk reads the blocks above a hole no local tombstone targets, which costs reads on a very sparse replica; (i) a tombstone is public: anyone holding the diary sees that something was deleted, which seq and when; (j) while a diary is behind its signed length, `/media` and `/moderation-post` answer nothing for it until its scan catches up (fail closed); (k) the seeder keeps a small in-memory entry per applied tombstone in its indexes (bounded by the diary limits, outside the index quotas), and a post its author deletes before the account service reads the report answers 404 to moderation (the report keeps the reporter's quote); (l) a full diary at `MAX_LOG_BLOCKS` cannot append a tombstone; (m) the browser calls the hypercore 10 internal `peer._maybeWant()` in `seederHoldsMedia` (`packages/web-member/lib/sync.js`) to learn which media blocks the seeder holds on a new link after it cleared some; the call is guarded (`typeof` and `try/catch`, falling back to the earlier behaviour) and pinned to hypercore 10.38.2, and a browser peer may advertise a contiguous range across a cleared hole, whose requests then time out; (n) unfollowed replicas are purged at sync startup (`sweepUnfollowed`), and the kill switch turns that off too; (o) hypercore 10.38.2 replicating a diary with cleared blocks to another hypercore 10.38.2 can stall the event loop (measured in-process with plain, linear and gap ranges); browser nodes only replicate with hypercore 11 seeders over WebSocket, where this does not happen, so it must be re-checked before any browser-to-browser replication is added; (p) every present-length walk is bounded by `MAX_LOG_BLOCKS`, so a signed length past the limit (blocks withheld) costs at most that many cheap `has()` checks and allocates nothing for the withheld tail; (q) a replica opens at most 1024 download ranges per diary (`MAX_RANGES`, core and browser): past that many gaps between deleted seqs the rest is one live range, so a deleted block in it can still be fetched from a stale peer and is cleared again on arrival.
- Rename (somos -> varanda): the signature domains, the admission channel and the rendezvous topic changed, so certificates, keys and data dirs made before the rename do not verify or interoperate; start a fresh setup (assoc init, id create, seed key).

## 9. Node session (`packages/core/lib/session.js`)

The one serialized session over a member data dir that implements every method of `docs/rpc.md` section 5 (the contract is normative there). The in-app Bare worklet (framed IPC) and `varanda-serve` (loopback WebSocket) both pass `session.handlers` to `createServerDispatcher` and `dispatcher.emit` to `session.attach`.

- `peek.read` -> `USAGE` `'Espiadinha disponível só no navegador'`; the Espiadinha runs only in the browser node (section 6 "Peek link", [rpc.md](rpc.md) 5.22).
- `createSession({ dataDir, association, bootstrap = null, dhtHost = null, allowPublicDht = false, storeOptions, onInternalError })` -> `{ handlers, attach(emit), detach(), suspend(), resume(), close(), state }`. Configuration is checked at creation, before anything is written or the network is touched: `association` is 64 hex (lowercased); a data dir whose `config.json` is not a member's (a seeder dir, unreadable JSON) or that holds `association.json` -> `UsageError('diretório não é de um membro')`; `bootstrap` is `null` or an array of entries passing `validBootstrap`; without `allowPublicDht: true` an absent or empty bootstrap is `UsageError('bootstrap inválido (vazio)')` (the public DHT is never a silent fallback), and `allowPublicDht: true` with a bootstrap is a `UsageError`; `allowPublicDht: true` with a loopback `dhtHost` (`isLoopbackAddress` in `lib/config.js`: `localhost`, `127.0.0.0/8`, `::1`) is `UsageError('dht-host de loopback e DHT pública são exclusivos')`, since that DHT never reaches a public peer; when every bootstrap host is loopback (`localhost`, `127.0.0.0/8`) and no `dhtHost` is given, `dhtHost` is `127.0.0.1` (section 4: a loopback host disables firewall probing).
- Serialization: handlers run one at a time in arrival order. The data-dir lock (section 1) is taken by every member operation and held by a running sync, so before any operation that opens the store the running `runSync` is aborted through its signal and awaited (store closed); a continuous run the user did not stop is restarted afterwards with a new `runId`, only once no other operation is queued (several queued requests preempt the run once, and the last of them restarts it). Events: `sync.started` per run, `sync.stopped` with `'preempted'` for a paused run (`docs/rpc.md` section 6.1). `runSync` always gets a `signal` (so it installs no process signal handlers) and the session's `storeOptions`.
- `storeOptions.recover` (worklet lock takeover, section 1 "Lock modes") is spent by the session's first store open: every later open, of a member operation or of a sync, uses `recover: false`, so a stray second worklet's live lock is never taken over.
- Error state: a `LOCKED` store open after creation (another process took the dir, or a sync restart found it held) makes that request and every later one answer `LOCKED` `'diretório de dados em uso'`; no sync restarts.
- Errors reach the wire only through `toRpcError`: `UsageError` and the user-caused member messages -> `USAGE`; `identidade não encontrada` and `sem dados locais para este diário` -> `NOT_FOUND`; codes `LOCKED` and `LOCK_INVALID` -> `LOCKED`; everything else (`STORE_INVALID` included, whose message carries a path) -> `INTERNAL` `'erro interno'`, the original error going to `onInternalError(err)` for the host's own log. A thrown `runSync` becomes a `warning` `'erro: ' + <that mapped message>` then `sync.stopped` `'error'`.
- Human lines: `sync.event` lines are `formatEvent` (section 7), a `skipped` event is the warning `aviso: diário <key8> ignorado (<reason>)`, `runSync` log lines are warnings through `safe()`; all clipped to `MAX_LINE`.
- Host controls: `detach()` (connection closed) clears the emitter, stops a running sync (`'stopped'`, not delivered) and bumps a connection epoch, so a `sync.start` of the closed connection still awaiting the identity starts no run (it answers `USAGE` `'conexão encerrada'`, undelivered) and nothing restarts a run until a new `sync.start`; a `resume()` that arrives while `suspend()` is still stopping the run takes effect once the stop completed; `suspend()` waits for the current operation, pauses the sync (`'preempted'`) and queues new requests until `resume()`, which restarts a paused continuous run; `close()` refuses later requests with `LOCKED` `'sessão encerrada'`, stops the sync and awaits the queue (idempotent). `state` is `'open'`, `'suspended'`, `'error'` or `'closed'`.
- Media: `post.create` / `post.reply` / `post.quote` with `media` -> `USAGE` `'mídia disponível só na versão web'`, before anything is opened (media cores are written only by the browser node). `profile.set` with a non-null `avatar` -> the same refusal, before anything is opened; with `avatar` absent while the newest own profile has one -> `USAGE` `'perfil com foto: mude o nome e a bio na versão web ou tire a foto'`; with `avatar: null`, or absent with no avatar in effect, one plain profile record (`docs/rpc.md` 5.6). `diary.peek` -> `USAGE` `'leitura sob demanda só na versão web'`, before anything is opened; `like.set`, `repost.set`, `post.quote` and `engagement.read` work as in the browser node, without a write gate. `follow.add` / `follow.remove` append `follow` records as `follow` / `unfollow` do (section 7); the core node emits no `follows.changed`.
- The own key is cached once known and `memberCert` once true (block 0 is append-only), so `node.info` and `sync.status` do not preempt a running sync.
- `profile.read` (`docs/rpc.md` 5.21) reads the own diary and the stored replicas of the given authors under one store open, with the admission rules of `feed.read` (`skipReason`: blocklist, fork, block-0 certificate, revoked issuer), and creates no core. Records, signing payloads and P2P messages are unchanged by it, and by the `before` cursors of `feed.read` and `log.read`.

## 10. Groups and communities (`packages/core/lib/group.js`, `groups-net.js`, `group-index.js`)

A group is a conversation with several authors. Nothing about it lives in a shared log: every member device writes its own single-writer Hypercore, a reader folds all of them into one view, and the diaries of section 2 stay untouched except for three records, `group-join`, `group-cut` and `group-confirm`. This section is normative for communities, which contain groups ("Communities contain groups" below), for public groups and for private groups ("Private groups" below), whose posts, hides, reactions and meta travel sealed end to end. This is protocol v2: every group has exactly one parent community, fixed in its header, and every core is written by a member device bound to a diary. Cores with `v: 1` headers are `legacy`: the fold ignores them, seeders refuse them as `header-invalid` and nodes purge them at startup ("Member sync").

### Cores and headers

Headers are plain objects with exactly the keys below. Keys called `hex64` are lowercase hexadecimal strings of 64 characters; `at` is canonical ISO as in section 2. Both header validators reject JSON encodings larger than `MAX_BLOCK_BYTES` (`too-large`). They return `null` on success or a reason; the builders throw `TypeError` for invalid input.

- **Root core.** G is the key of a single-writer root core, also the creator's first writer (`W = G`). Only the creator writes it. Block 0 is exactly `{ "type": "group-header", "v": 2, "kind": "group" | "community", "diary": "<hex64>", "community": "<hex64>" | null, "member": "<hex64>" | null, "visibility": "public" | "private", "join": "open" | "approval", "name": <name> | null, "box": null | "<hex64>", "at": <ISO> }`. `diary` is the creator D0. For `kind: 'group'`, `community` is the immutable parent C and `member` is the key of the creator device's community writer in C (it may equal C itself: the creator device's writer of C is C's root). For `kind: 'community'`, `community`, `member` and `box` are null and `visibility` is `public`. Public headers require `box: null`; `name` may be null. Private headers require `join: 'approval'`, `name: null` and a hex64 `box`: the X25519 public key of the creator's device, to which epoch keys for the root writer are sealed ("Private groups"). Other `kind`, `visibility` or `join` values are invalid.
- **Header check order.** The header validator returns the first hit of: not a plain object, `malformed`; JSON larger than `MAX_BLOCK_BYTES`, `too-large`; `type: 'group-header'` with `v: 1`, `legacy` (tested before the exact keys, because a v1 header has other keys); keys other than exactly the eleven above, another `type` or a `v` other than 2, `malformed`; `kind` not `group` or `community`, `diary` not hex64 or `join` not `open` or `approval`, `malformed`; `kind: 'group'` with `community` or `member` not hex64 (`member` may equal `community`), `malformed`; `kind: 'community'` with a non-null `community` or `member`, a `visibility` other than `public` or a non-null `box`, `malformed`; the visibility rules (a public header needs `box: null`; a private one needs `join: 'approval'`, `name: null` and a strong hex64 `box`), `malformed`; a `name` outside 1..`MAX_GROUP_NAME` code points, `name-length`, or with forbidden characters, `control-chars`; an `at` that is not canonical ISO, `date-invalid`. The builder is `groupHeader({ kind = 'group', diary, community = null, member = null, visibility = 'public', join = 'open', name = null, box = null, at })`.
- **Writer cores.** Every other participating device writes W with block 0 exactly `{ "type": "group-writer", "v": 2, "group": "<hex64>", "diary": "<hex64>", "member": null | "<hex64>", "box": null | "<hex64>", "rejoin": null | ref, "at": <ISO> }`. `group` is G (or C for a community writer) and `diary` is D. `box` is null or a hex64 X25519 public key (the device's recipient key, "Private groups"): the shape validator accepts both, and the fold and member sync refuse (`header-invalid`) a writer whose `box` does not match the root's visibility, a hex64 `box` in a public group or a null `box` in a private group. The shape validator accepts `rejoin: null` or a record reference as defined below (`seq >= 1`); the fold checks its target under "Rejoining". The writer validator returns, in order: `malformed` (not a plain object), `too-large`, `legacy` (`v: 1`), `malformed` (exact keys, `type`, `v: 2`, hex64 `group` and `diary`, `member` null or hex64, `box` null or a strong hex64, `rejoin` null or a ref), `date-invalid`. The builder is `writerHeader({ group, diary, member = null, box = null, rejoin = null, at })`.
- **`member`.** In the header of a group root it is a hint only (the root is gated at diary level, "The community gate"). In the header of a writer of a group it is required: the key of the device's community writer in the group's community, checked by the fold (`header-invalid` when null or not a counted writer of the same diary in C). In the header of a community writer it is null, else the fold rejects the writer (`header-invalid`). A `rejoin` in a community writer names a `group-leave` of the same diary inside C (C's root or another counted community writer), exactly as for groups.
- **No signature per record.** A record is authenticated by its writer core's Hypercore signature and attributed to D by the binding. The core key is not D's signer key (section 1), so records have no signer field.
- **Binding is two-way.** In `buildGroup` (and, for a community writer, in `buildCommunity`), W is bound to D when at least one valid `on: true` join for `(G, W)` ever existed in D's diary and W's header names G and D (the root header names D0). Either side alone binds nothing. A later diary `on: false` only updates seeder indexes: it never withdraws the writer from this fold and never purges its core at seeders or nodes. All bound devices act as the same diary for membership and moderation. The v1 limit is that D cannot cut off a lost device's writer through its diary.

### Records (writer cores; blocks 1..)

Every record is a plain object with exactly the listed keys and canonical ISO `at`. `validateGroupRecord(record, seq, { root, community, private })` (`community: true` means that the core is a community core: the root C when `root` is true, else a community writer; `private` and `community` are never both true) requires a nonnegative safe-integer `seq`, validates the appropriate header at 0, and returns `null` or a reason. Invalid and unknown records are skipped without renumbering. All integers below are safe integers. `ref` is exactly `{ "writer": "<hex64>", "seq": <integer >= 1> }`. A non-null `name` is a string of 1..`MAX_GROUP_NAME` code points; names and text obey section 2's forbidden-character rules (LF allowed only in text).

- **`seen`.** Every writer record below (post, hide, admit, ban, leave, invites, create, and the root-only meta and admin) carries `seen` (reactions too): `null` or a `ref` with seq >= 0 (seq 0 names a rejoining writer's block 0). It is an ordering reference only and grants nothing; validation checks its shape, not its target (`seen-invalid`). Nodes always write `seen` = the `last` of their own fold (below), reactions included; `last` never names a reaction, so no record ever depends on one.
- `{ "type": "group-post", "text": <string 1..1000 code points>, "replyTo": null | ref, "seen": null | ref, "at": <ISO> }`. `replyTo` names the record answered; `seen` is defined above. Validation checks reference shape, not whether the target exists or is a post. A post with media, a link card, or a forwarded post (`fwd` 1), uses the one other key set, v2: `{ "type": "group-post", "text": <string 0..1000 code points>, "replyTo": null | ref, "media": null | [Item], "card": null | LinkCard, "fwd": 0 | 1, "seen": null | ref, "at": <ISO> }` (`GROUP_POST_V2_KEYS`, "Group post media" below). `media` is `null` or 1 to `MAX_GROUP_MEDIA` = 4 items (`packages/core/lib/group-media.js`; the public post cap does not apply to groups), else `media-invalid`; `null` is legal only when `card` is not `null` or `fwd` is 1, and a v2 post with `media` `null`, `card` `null` and `fwd` 0 is `media-invalid` (it must be the v1 record). `card` is `null` or a LinkCard (section 2, "Link cards") and is `null` whenever `media` is a list; anything else is `card-invalid`. The card's `image` is `null`, a MediaDescriptor with `mime` `'image/jpeg'` and `bytes` at most 32768 in a plain post of a public group, or a private item (below) with `plainMime` `'image/jpeg'` and `bytes` at most 32768 (one block) inside a private group's sealed inner, never mixed (`validGroupCard(card, sealed)`). `fwd` is the integer 0 or 1, else `malformed` (a boolean is not 1). The text may be empty only when `media` is a list. A post with no media and `fwd` 0 is always the v1 record above, byte for byte (`groupPost` writes v1 when `media` and `card` are `null` or absent and `fwd` is 0 or absent; `fwd` defaults to 0; `groupPost({ ..., card })` copies exactly the card keys and the image keys of its kind), so every post has one encoding. Any other key set, the v1 keys plus `media` alone included, is `malformed`.
- `{ "type": "group-hide", "target": ref, "on": <boolean>, "seen": null | ref, "at": <ISO> }`: sets or clears a hide layer as defined below.
- `{ "type": "group-admit", "member": "<hex64>", "on": <boolean>, "cuts": [Cut], "seen": null | ref, "at": <ISO> }`: sets or clears admission for diary `member`.
- `{ "type": "group-ban", "member": "<hex64>", "on": <boolean>, "cuts": [Cut], "lifts": null | ref, "seen": null | ref, "at": <ISO> }`: sets or clears a ban for diary `member`. `on: true` requires `lifts: null`; `on: false` requires a `lifts` ref, whose target is checked by the fold.
- `{ "type": "group-leave", "seen": null | ref, "cuts": [Cut], "at": <ISO> }`: ends the writer diary's membership. `cuts` has at most `MAX_GROUP_WRITERS_PER_DIARY` entries and matters to the fold only for the root (see "Authority and layers"). A node lists every counted writer of the leaving diary (retired writers and the creator's root included) at its local length (the format is unchanged); the fold reads the cuts only for the root, and every other record of the diary is withdrawn whatever they say.
- `{ "type": "group-invites", "on": <boolean>, "seen": null | ref, "at": <ISO> }` (builder `groupInvites`), in the root or any writer: whether the members of G may invite others into G (`memberInvite`, "Members may invite" below). Only the group's creator or a group admin sets it; anyone else's record is valid and folded as a node, with no effect. It is never sealed, also in a private group ("Private groups"). Readers from before this type skip it as an unknown type, like any invalid record.
- `{ "type": "group-react", "target": ref, "emoji": <string>, "seen": null | ref, "at": <ISO> }` (builder `groupReact`), in the root or any group writer: the writer diary's reaction to the `group-post` `target`. `emoji` is the empty string (take the reaction back) or exactly one emoji by `isReaction` (`@varanda/rpc`, `lib/reaction.js`): 1..8 code points and at most 40 UTF-8 bytes, checked with explicit code-point ranges (never `\p{...}`): code points of a frozen pictographic table, a superset of Unicode 17's Extended_Pictographic made only of symbols (no letter but U+2139 INFORMATION SOURCE, an emoji Unicode classes as one; no number, separator, control, format or private-use code point), so every replica agrees whatever its Unicode version and the table never changes without a protocol change, each optionally followed by a skin tone (U+1F3FB..U+1F3FF) and then U+FE0F, joined by U+200D; or a pair of regional indicators; or a keycap (`0-9`, `#` or `*`, optional U+FE0F, U+20E3). No letters, whitespace or controls; two emojis, a lone joiner, modifier or regional indicator are invalid (`emoji-invalid`). A `target` that is not a ref is `malformed`; validation does not check that it names a post. In a community core it returns `group-only`; in a private group it travels sealed only ("Private groups"). Fold: "Reactions" below.
- Root only: `{ "type": "group-meta", "name": <name> | null, "join": "open" | "approval", "seen": null | ref, "at": <ISO> }` and `{ "type": "group-admin", "member": "<hex64>", "on": <boolean>, "cuts": [Cut], "seen": null | ref, "at": <ISO> }`. These types elsewhere return `root-only`. In public groups the last valid meta by root seq sets name and join. In a private group the meta travels sealed and sets only the name ("Private groups"); join stays `approval`. Visibility is immutable.
- `Cut` is exactly `{ "writer": "<hex64>", "seq": <integer >= 0> }`. `cuts` is an array of at most `MAX_GROUP_CUTS` entries, with each writer at most once and no holes. Its `seq` is an **observed length**: the actor saw blocks `0..seq-1`. The event affects records at `seq >= cut` in that writer; an unlisted writer has an implicit cut of 0, except for an `on: true` `group-admin`, which gives nothing to an unlisted writer (below). A `group-leave` reads its cuts only for the root (below). Its ordering reference is the last observed block `{ writer, seq: cut - 1 }`, with no reference for cut 0.
- `group-key` and `group-sealed` exist only in private groups ("Private groups"), where plaintext posts, hides, meta and reactions are skipped; in a community core they return `group-only`, as do `group-post` and `group-react`. `group-create` exists only in community cores (below); in a group core it returns `community-only`. The shapes above are those of **group cores** (a group root and its writers). `group-rejoin` is only an internal fold node, never a valid stored record. The v1 link records between groups and communities no longer exist: a stored record of an unknown type is skipped.

**Community-core records.** A community core is a community root C or a community writer (CW). It accepts exactly the types below, each with exactly the listed keys and canonical `at`:

- `{ "type": "group-create", "group": "<hex64>", "seen": null | ref, "at": <ISO> }`: C's catalogue entry for the group `group`, in C's root only: only the community creator creates groups, so in a CW it returns `root-only`, and in a group core `community-only`. `seen` is a ref with seq >= 0, else `seen-invalid`.
- `{ "type": "group-admit", "group": null | "<hex64>", "member": "<hex64>", "on": <boolean>, "cuts": [Cut], "seen": null | ref, "at": <ISO> }` and `{ "type": "group-ban", "group": null | "<hex64>", "member": "<hex64>", "on": <boolean>, "cuts": [Cut], "lifts": null | ref, "seen": null | ref, "at": <ISO> }`, in C's root or in a CW. `group: null` acts on the community itself (the member's status in C; `cuts` list the member's CWs); `group: G` is an act of community authority inside the group G (`cuts` list the member's writers in G; `lifts` names a ban of G's fold, which may sit in a G writer or in a CW). `cuts` has at most `MAX_GROUP_CUTS` entries (`cuts-invalid`). A ban with `on: true` needs `lifts: null` and one with `on: false` needs a `lifts` ref with seq >= 1 (`lifts-invalid`).
- `{ "type": "group-hide", "group": "<hex64>", "target": ref, "on": <boolean>, "seen": null | ref, "at": <ISO> }`, in C's root or in a CW. `group` is never null (a community has no posts) and `target` is a post of G.
- `{ "type": "group-invites", "group": null | "<hex64>", "on": <boolean>, "seen": null | ref, "at": <ISO> }` (builder `communityInvites`), in C's root or in a CW: `group: null` sets whether the members of C may invite (C's `memberInvite`), `group` = G sets it for the group G (a community-authority node in G, "Communities contain groups"). The group-core shape (without `group`) in a community core, and this shape in a group core, return `malformed`.
- `group-leave` (the shape above, `cuts` of at most `MAX_GROUP_WRITERS_PER_DIARY` entries, `cuts-invalid`), in C's root or in a CW: ends the diary's membership in C.
- `group-meta` and `group-admin` (the shapes above), in C's root only (`root-only` in a CW). A community `group-admin` has `cuts` over the member's CWs, at most `MAX_GROUP_CUTS`.
- **CW demotion.** `{ "type": "group-admin", "group": null | "<hex64>", "member": "<hex64>", "on": false, "cuts": [Cut], "seen": null | ref, "at": <ISO> }`, in a CW only (builder `communityDemote`): a demotion of a C admin (`group: null`) or of an admin of a group of C (`group` = G). It is how a creator whose root lost its authority still removes admins. `on: true` in a CW, and the root-shaped `group-admin` (no `group` key) in a CW, return `root-only`. A CW demotion never grants anything.
- **CW meta and CW drop (community repair).** `{ "type": "group-meta", "group": null, "name": <name>, "join": "open" | "approval", "seen": null | ref, "at": <ISO> }` (builder `communityMetaCw`) and `{ "type": "group-drop", "group": "<hex64>", "on": <boolean>, "seen": null | ref, "at": <ISO> }` (builder `communityDrop`), in a CW only. `group-drop` is a community-core type (a drop without `on` is `malformed`; `on: false` puts the group back): on C's root it is `malformed` and in a group core `community-only`. How they count is in "Community repair after the creator's return".

A CW record (admit, ban, hide or invites) whose `group` names a key outside C's catalogue is a valid record and a node in C's causal order, with no effect anywhere. Community admins moderate (ban, admit, hide) but never create groups. Nodes write `seen` = the `last` of the fold the record acts on: C's for `group: null`, `group-leave` and `group-create`; G's for `group: G`. Builders (`lib/group.js`): `groupCreateRecord({ group, seen, at })`, `communityAdmit`, `communityBan`, `communityHide`, `communityInvites({ group, on, seen, at })`; `groupLeave` and `groupAdmin` (built with `community: true`) serve both core kinds. Ordering edges: `group-create` has `seen` only; a community-core admit, ban or hide has the edges of its group form (`seen`, each cut's last observed block, `lifts`, `target`).

### Limits

`MAX_GROUP_WRITERS_PER_DIARY` = 8: after rejoin validation, `buildGroup` counts per diary at most 8 bound writers, those whose `on: true` binding join is EARLIEST in that diary's log (ties by key); a bound root is one of its creator's 8. `MAX_GROUP_WRITERS` = 512: of the writers that remain, the fold keeps the root, then the other writers in this order of tiers: those of the creator's diary (root bound only), those of the diaries that are admins in the final root state (group admins, and the creator and admins of the group's community), all others, and last those of banned diaries (ranked by the final ban layer); inside a tier by key. A rejoining writer whose named leave fell outside the cap is dropped as well, because the fold would otherwise never see that leave. `incomplete` is true when the 512 cap or that rejoin rule drops a writer; the per-diary cap alone does not set it. `MAX_GROUPS_PER_DIARY` = 64 is enforced per diary by the seeder, which keeps the 64 EARLIEST live joins by diary seq (the root counts toward the 512 writer cores of a group at the seeder). `MAX_GROUPS_PER_COMMUNITY` = 32 counts the `group-create` records of a community in its causal order ("Communities contain groups"). `MAX_GROUP_CUTS` = 16 caps the cuts of admits, bans and admins, and also of a community `group-admin` (over the member's CWs, of which a diary binds at most 8): there is no separate community cut cap. A community core set (the root C and its CWs) uses the same per-diary and total caps and the same tiers as a group. `MAX_GROUP_NAME` = 80. Blocks use `MAX_BLOCK_BYTES` = 16384; each writer core uses section 2's `MAX_LOG_BLOCKS` = 100000 and `MAX_LOG_BYTES` = 64 MiB. Text length is defined in the post shape above.

### Folding: `buildGroup`

`buildGroup({ root: { key, entries }, writers: [{ key, entries, diary }], joins: Map<diary, [{ writer, on, seq }]>, community, keys, marks, confirms, now })` is a pure fold (`marks`, optional, is `Map<diary, [{ writer, cut, seq } | { gap: true }]>` from `groupCutHistory`, "Creator marks on group roots"; `confirms`, optional, is `Map<diary, [{ writer, seq, digest, dseq }]>` from `groupConfirmHistory` (`dseq` the confirmation's own seq in the diary), "Confirmed leaves"; `buildCommunity` takes `confirms` too); `community` (`null`, or the parent's input `{ root: { key, entries }, writers, joins }`, exactly `buildCommunity`'s input) is defined under "Communities contain groups", and `keys` (optional, `{ writers, unseal, onKey? }`: the reader's means to open epoch keys, and the hook that hands a node the accepted keys) and the private-only results `sealed`, `epoch`, `rekeyPending`, `entitled` and `removals` under "Private groups". It never throws and gives the same result for any delivery order of the same blocks. The first valid copy of a seq is retained. It returns `kind`, `name`, `join`, `visibility`, `creator`, `admins: Set<diary>` (the community's creator and admins included, below), `community` (the parent's fold result when C authorizes G, else null), `orphan` (true when C folds but does not authorize G, below), `members: Map<diary, 'member' | 'requested' | 'banned' | 'left'>`, `posts: [{ writer, diary, seq, at, text, replyTo, hidden }]` in causal order, `requests` (requested diaries), `future` (skipped post refs), `last` (the newest node in causal order, a rejoining writer's block 0 included, never a reaction, as `{ writer, seq }`, or null), `bans: Map<diary, ref>` (the latest valid ban of every diary banned now: the `lifts` an unban must name), `leaves: Map<diary, ref>` (the causal latest leave of every diary that has left now: the `rejoin` a new writer must name), `lastLeave: Map<diary, ref>` (the causally latest leave of every diary that ever left, kept after a rejoin, as in a community), `liveWriters: Set<key>` (the counted writers that are live now: not retired, not banned, and, under the gate, with a live CW in C; the root only when `rootLive`), `authorityWriters: Set<key>` (the live writers that hold moderation power now, for nodes to pick a writer that can sign an admin record), `writers: Set<key>` (the writer cores the fold counted; the root only when bound), `memberInvite` (whether members may invite, "Members may invite") and `incomplete`. A missing named writer sets `incomplete: true` without blocking other writers. An invalid or absent root header yields the empty incomplete result, and so does a root whose header kind is `community` (a community is folded by `buildCommunity`, never by `buildGroup`). An unbound root (no `on: true` join for `writer = G` in the creator's diary) sets incomplete, carries no authority and contributes no posts: only its header is read, and the header's diary is an ordinary diary (not listed in `admins` or `members` unless it has counted writers of its own); `creator` in the result still echoes the header. A node whose own writer is outside `writers` (beyond the cap) refuses to write (`limite do grupo atingido`) and never reports `member` for it.

**Authority and layers.**

- The creator is fixed, and the creator's bound writers are always authorized, but only when the root is bound: creator powers (authorization as creator, shielding, automatic admission, the entry in `admins` and `members`, the `creator` role) exist only when the header diary's own `on: true` `group-join` names writer = G. Only the creator appoints or demotes admins, through `group-admin` in the root, with cuts (in a group of a community, C's creator may also demote through a CW demotion, "Communities contain groups"); an admin record naming the creator is ignored. Known limitation of the creator root exception: a creator may list a root cut beyond the root's current length on a leave from another writer and later append root records below it with a backdated `at`; they keep counting (indistinguishable from honest root records not yet seen, and the creator gains nothing it would not regain by rejoining). Another known limitation: the account service's linked-account shortcut lets a group's creator link the root key as an "account" (the link proof is only a signature by that key), so a report naming that root as target skips the seeder confirmation; the impact is limited to the creator's own group and accountable through the account link. The Google sub is no longer issued for a new member: the member Google sign-in is retired (an old build gets 410 `versao-antiga`), an earlier member's Google sub and e-mail stay, and a new link's sub is `telefone:` plus the member key.
- For each diary, the admin layer is built from all valid root admin records in root seq order. The admit and ban layers contain only authorized events in causal order. For a record `(writer, seq)`, each layer uses the last event whose cut applies, or false if none. Thus demotion cuts remove authority from affected records regardless of their timestamps, preserving earlier actions below the cut.
- Another diary's administrative action is authorized only if its admin layer is on at that writer/seq, its ban layer is off there, and the diary has not left at that causal point. These checks govern admits, bans and hides of other authors' posts. A timestamp alone grants no authority.
- **Admits list their writers.** A positive `group-admit` covers only the writers its `cuts` list (unlisted writers keep their previous state); a writer created after an admit stays not admitted until a later admit lists it. An admit withdrawal (`on: false`) still covers unlisted writers from seq 0. In approval groups `requests` also lists an admitted member with a counted writer the current admission does not cover, and admins may admit again (the new cuts name every held counted writer).
- **Admin grants list their writers.** An `on: true` `group-admin` gives authority ONLY to the writers listed in its `cuts`, from the listed seq on; an unlisted writer gets nothing from that grant, so an admin's new device needs a new grant. An `on: false` `group-admin` keeps the restrictive rule (an unlisted writer is cut at 0), and `group-admit` and `group-ban` keep unlisted = 0. A node writing a promotion lists every counted writer of the promoted diary at its local length (never seq 0, so records written before the promotion gain nothing) and refuses with `limite do grupo atingido` when the diary has more counted writers than `MAX_GROUP_CUTS`.
- **Cut lengths.** A node writing an `on: true` admit, an `on: true` admin grant or an unban (`group-ban` with `on: false`) cuts each writer at max(contiguous length, signed length), so records the member signed during the ban that the admin has not downloaded stay banned; `on: false` admits, bans and leaves keep the contiguous length. A pending leave (group root not stored yet) is refused with `aguarde a sincronização do grupo para sair` when the own diary's join history binds any writer of the group other than this device's (the fold is unknown, and the leave would withdraw those writers). A loaded leave never waits for other devices: anyone leaves at once, and the records of the diary's other devices are withdrawn (below). Its only refusal is `saia pelo aparelho que criou o grupo`: the own diary is the creator's, the root is bound and the leave's cuts do not list the root (holding the root core suffices, its key is not needed; in a loaded group this practically never triggers). Leaving a root that does not fold appends a `group-leave` on the own writer and an `on: false` `group-join`, then drops the `groups.json` entry. An `on: true` admit is refused with `aguarde a sincronização do grupo para admitir` when the member's diary is not held or binds a counted writer that is not held, because an admit missing an unheld writer would leave that writer hidden (the browser compares against the held writer cores, like the core node); `on: false` admits and bans are unchanged. Member nodes accept a group root only when its kind is `group`, public or private (any other root is `header-invalid`); a `groups.json` entry (core) or kv `groupWriters` entry (browser) whose stored root does not fold is treated as left: a leave drops it (the core node never drops the entry of a group it created) and the room logic can give its slot up. The browser follows the same rules.
- **A leave withdraws records.** A `group-leave` L of diary D withdraws, until a later rejoin node of D that follows L causally, wherever `at` puts the record in causal order: every record of D on L's own writer at `seq >=` L's seq (L itself excepted), and every record of D on every OTHER writer of D from seq 0, whatever L's cuts say (honest posts and moderation from the leaver's other devices included). A later leave also withdraws the records of earlier generations on the diary's other writers (leave, rejoin on a new writer, leave again: the first period's posts on the old writer are withdrawn too). Exception: when D is the creator and the root is bound, the ROOT keeps the cut rule: root records below L's listed cut for the root survive, and an unlisted root is withdrawn from seq 0 (from L at the latest when L is in the root). Leaves and rejoin nodes themselves are never withdrawn. A withdrawn admit, ban, hide, meta or admin carries no authority, and a withdrawn post is hidden. This is by design: cuts cannot tell records signed before the leave from records signed after it on another device. Exemption: a leave never withdraws an author's `group-hide` of their own post (the author layer), so a member who left can still hide what they wrote; the admin layer of a withdrawn record carries no authority.
- **Shielding reads the FINAL root admin state**, never the causal position (clock skew must not decide whether two admins can ban each other). A ban of the creator or of a diary that is an admin in the final state is ignored, and so is a withdrawal of its admission (`group-admit` with `on: false`); a demoted admin loses the shield. A positive admit (`on: true`) is never shielded, so a promotion keeps the admissions that covered the earlier posts. This is distinct from the cut-based authority check on an actor's record above.
- A ban never voids authority granted by the root: only a demotion's cuts do (by design; a ban of a final admin is ignored, so no ban layer covers an admin).
- An unban counts only if `lifts` names the latest valid ban of that diary emitted before it. An unban covers only the writers its `cuts` list: an unlisted writer stays banned, including one created after the unban (a new device, a rejoin's new writer), until a later unban lists it; a ban still covers unlisted writers from seq 0. The `bans` result keeps the latest ban while any counted writer of that diary stays banned, and an unban may cite the same ban again. A void, unknown or superseded ban cannot be lifted. Admit and ban remain separate layers: an admit does not cancel a ban, an unban does not grant admission, and a ban dominates (a banned diary's posts stay hidden whatever admissions it holds).
- Hides have independent author and admin layers. An action from any bound writer of the target's diary sets the author layer, even if that diary has left or is banned; otherwise an authorized action sets the admin layer. Each layer takes its last such action in causal order. Either layer being on hides the post; clearing one does not clear the other. Hidden posts remain in the fold and in their cores.
- **Reactions.** A reaction's place never moves another record: every other record orders and folds exactly as a reader from before `group-react` computes from the same blocks. So reactions are never nodes of the causal order. In a public group they are set aside when a core is read, as an old reader drops them: the previous retained record of a writer skips them, and a `seen`, `replyTo` or `target` that names one resolves to the nearest lower retained record of that writer. In the opened pass of a private group a reaction's envelope stays the sealed node it is for an old reader (writer and `kid` edges only), and the opened reaction rides beside it without adding its inner `seen` or `target` edges; it does not count in `sealed`. Reactions are then folded in a separate step that never feeds back into that order. Each is judged at an anchor taken from the reaction-free order: in a private group its own envelope (the withdrawal, membership generation and admin standing the envelope had in the plaintext pass), in a public group the node before it on its own core (or the start of the order when there is none), as if it were a post right there; its own edges place nothing. A reaction counts only where a post at that anchor and at its own writer and seq would (not withdrawn by a leave, not under a ban layer at its writer and seq, admitted or admin under approval; creator records bypass these as for posts), from a diary whose final status is `member` (in a private group, its status from the plaintext pass), not more than `MAX_FUTURE_MS` ahead of `now`, on a target that is a folded `group-post` that is not hidden; reactions by non-members, by banned or departed diaries, and to hidden, future or unknown targets (a reaction, a hide, a missing block) are ignored. One reaction per member diary per target, and a later one replaces an earlier one, chosen without any causal position: on one writer core the higher seq wins whatever its `at`; across the diary's cores, the winner of each core is compared by `at` (later wins), then writer key, then seq (greater wins). An empty `emoji` as the winner clears the diary's reaction. Per target at most `GROUP_REACTIONS_MAX` = 12 distinct emojis count: the winners are taken in (`at`, writer, seq) order and a reaction whose emoji would be the 13th distinct one is ignored, the same on every replica. Each post with at least one counted reaction gains `reactions: [{ emoji, count, by }]` (`by` the diary keys in the order of their winning reactions by `at`, writer, seq), sorted by `count` descending then `emoji` (UTF-16 code unit order); a post with none has no `reactions` key. The fold is linear in the number of reactions, plus one sort of the winners. `group.read` items carry only the compact summary `[{ emoji, count, mine }]` (at most 12), which with a maximal private card per item brings `GROUP_LIMIT_MAX` and `GROUP_LIMIT_DEFAULT` to 26 (26 items = 261,882 bytes, 27 = 271,939; rpc `test/sizes.test.js`); the who-reacted answer lists at most `GROUP_REACTORS_MAX` = 324 diary keys per emoji (12 emojis of 324 keys = 261,574 bytes, 325 = 262,378).
- **Reactions and old clients.** A reader from before `group-react` has no such type in `RECORD_KEYS`: in a public group it marks the record `malformed`, `readLog` omits it without renumbering, later records still read, and a `seen` that names a reaction resolves to the nearest lower retained seq of that writer, so posts and moderation keep their places. In a private group an old reader opens the envelope, finds an inner type it does not accept, and counts it as one more undecryptable record (`sealed`, shown as "N mensagens cifradas"); the envelope stays a sealed node for it, exactly as for a new reader. Since a new reader orders every other record as if reactions were absent (public) or unopened (private), old and new readers of the same blocks agree on everything but the reactions themselves, `sealed` (a new reader does not count the reactions it opens) and, in a private group, a `last` that would name a reaction envelope. Rollout order: seeders, then the web bundle, then the app.
- **Members may invite (`memberInvite`).** `memberInvite` is the `on` of the last `group-invites` record in causal order that is authorized as an administrative action (the same check as an admit: the creator, or a diary whose admin layer is on and ban layer off at that writer and seq, not withdrawn by a leave, and in a group with a parent community not void by the community gate and with `rootLive` for the creator; a root record at or above the creator marks' cut counts for nothing). In a group with a parent community, C's `group-invites` records naming G take part in the same order as community-authority nodes ("Community-authority actions inside G"). With no such record it is `false`, as in the empty result, an orphan with no counted record, and a pending group. A demotion's cuts and a leave end an admin's later records exactly as for its other admin acts. The boolean names no person and is visible to whoever replicates the cores; it only lets members invite (an invite notifies, it admits nobody), and changes no authority.
- Inside a group with a parent community, the community gate and community authority (admins, bans, hides of C's creator and admins) apply on top of these rules ("Communities contain groups").

**Membership and rejoining.**

- In open groups, a bound diary is a member unless banned or left. Approval groups require an admit or admin status; otherwise the diary is `requested`. Non-creator status checks, in order, are the last ban toggle, leave state, and the last admission toggle since the latest leave (or the last root admin toggle). The creator is `member` or `left`. Status uses these last toggles; post visibility uses the layers at each post's writer/seq and the final join policy.
- Only `group-leave` inside a writer ends membership; a diary-level `on: false` join never does. A leave in any writer of D ends every device's writer of D (nodes stop writing from them), and a repeated leave writes nothing. A banned diary can neither join nor leave through a node, and a node refuses a join while its own diary is blocked. Posts emitted while the diary is left are hidden; earlier posts retain their ordinary visibility, apart from the records a leave withdraws ("Authority and layers"). Every leave advances that diary's membership generation for admission checks; the generation an admit carries is defined next.
- **Rejoining.** A new bound writer's `rejoin` MUST name a valid `group-leave` of the same diary in the root or another accepted writer; nodes name the causal latest leave (`leaves`). This is checked to a fixed point. A wrong diary/type, a missing record within the known target range, or a dependency on writers that never become accepted (including cycles) rejects the writer. An unknown target writer or a target beyond its known records leaves the writer out and sets `incomplete` instead. Once accepted, its block 0 clears the diary's leave state in causal order: a fresh membership after the referenced leave. Earlier admissions do not carry over, so an ordinary member needs a new admit in approval groups; creator/admin privileges still apply. A ban stays in force. A header with `rejoin: null` does not clear leave state.
- **Admit generation.** An admit's generation is the number of the member diary's leaves it OBSERVED: the leaves its cuts cover (a cut on the leave's writer above the leave's seq) plus the leave named by a rejoining writer of that diary that the admit cuts past block 0 (`observedLeaves` in `group.js`), and only leaves that also precede the admit in causal order count. Leaves that merely sort before the admit in causal order do not count, because `at` is the author's claim and would let a backdated leave carry an admission into a rejoin. A post of generation g is admitted by the last admit in causal order with generation >= g whose cut applies; status uses the last such admit for the current generation. Consequence: an admission withdrawal (`on: false`) whose cuts did not observe a leave applies only to the earlier generation (bans are unaffected). A missed leave only asks for a new admit.
- For each post, the fold remembers whether the diary had left and its leave generation at emission. Admission events of a lower generation than the post's cannot authorize it. Creator posts bypass ban/admission checks; other posts are hidden by the applicable ban or, under approval, by missing admission and admin authority, in addition to leave and hide states.

**Order.** Display order is causal, deterministic and never stalls.

1. Every valid record at seq >= 1 is a node, except a `group-react`: reactions are never nodes ("Reactions"). A rejoining writer also has a block-0 node referencing `rejoin`. A node becomes ready only after all its resolved predecessors have been emitted: the previous retained record of the same writer, `replyTo`, `seen`, a hide's `target`, each cut's last observed block (`cut - 1`, none for cut 0; the cuts of admits, bans and admins are edges, a leave's cuts only on the root), `lifts`, and for the block-0 node the `rejoin` leave. Thus an unban follows the ban it lifts whenever that dependency resolves, subject to cycle recovery below. The nodes of the community virtual logs ("Community-authority actions inside G") are nodes too.
2. References to a missing writer, beyond its last known valid record, or with no retained node at or before the referenced seq are dangling and ignored for ordering; the fold never waits for missing blocks. For a gap within the known range, `group.js` resolves to the greatest retained seq at or below the reference. References to the same writer at the node's own seq or later are ignored.
3. Kahn's algorithm emits the smallest `(at, writer, seq)` among ready nodes: compare `Date.parse(at)`, then writer key, then seq. If none is ready, break a forged cycle by releasing the smallest **writer head** by that key (each writer's first un-emitted node), preserving each writer's seq order.
4. After folding, posts more than `MAX_FUTURE_MS` = 86400000 (24 h) ahead of `now` are excluded from `posts` and listed in `future`, sorted by writer then seq. They return when the clock catches up. This skip applies to posts only; their causal dependencies and moderation records still participate in the fold.

Late blocks can change the causal order of posts already displayed. The RPC surface and pagination are defined in [rpc.md](rpc.md), sections 5.36-5.49. In particular, `group.requests` is paged with `offset` and `limit`, like `group.members` (section 5.38).

- **Group cores and deletion.** Group roots, community roots, CWs and group writers are read without the diary deletion fold: deletion is a diary feature, and groups hide posts with `group-hide`. A `delete` block on a group core removes nothing, and the read of a group core ends at the first block not held. Sync never clears group-core blocks. Diaries keep their deletion behaviour (section 2).

### Communities contain groups

A community C is the parent of groups. C is the key of a single-writer root core whose block 0 is a `group-header` with `kind: 'community'` (`community`, `member` and `box` null, `visibility` `public`, "Cores and headers"; `diary` is the community creator). Unlike a group root, C has writer cores: each device of a member writes a **community writer** (CW), a `group-writer` core with `group` = C and `member: null`, bound to its diary exactly like any writer (the join `{ group: C, writer: CW, on: true }` and a `rejoin` that names a `group-leave` of the same diary inside C). A group G has exactly one parent, the `community` of its header, immutable. C lists its groups in a catalogue made of `group-create` records ("Community-core records"); the first group of a community, created with it, is called Geral. Readers decide the kind from the root's own header.

- **Bound root.** As for a group root, the header's `diary` is only a label until that diary binds the root with a join `{ group: C, writer: C, on: true }`. An unbound community root keeps only its header: it has no creator, no admins, no members and no catalogue, gives no power in any group, and `buildCommunity` returns `incomplete: true`.
- **`buildCommunity`.** `buildCommunity({ root: { key: C, entries }, writers: [{ key, entries, diary }], joins: Map<diary, [{ writer, on, seq }]>, now })` is the input of `buildGroup` without `community` and `keys`. It runs the group fold on C's logs in community mode: the same binding (`readJoins`), per-diary cap, 512 cap with the same tiers, rejoin check, causal order, leave withdrawal, root admin layer (its cuts are over CW keys), admit and ban layers, shield and results `members`, `requests`, `bans`, `leaves`, `last` and `writers`. The differences are:
  - Only records with `group: null` act in C. Records with `group !== null` (admit, ban, hide) are nodes in C's causal order and change nothing in C; a `group-hide` never sets a hide in C.
  - **Members may invite in C.** C's `memberInvite` is the `on` of the last `group-invites` record with `group: null` in C's causal order that counts as an administrative node of C: on C's root, the creator's while the root has its powers (root seq below `rootCut`); on a CW, the creator's diary or a diary whose C admin layer is on and C ban layer off at that CW and seq, the CW counted, not retired (no authority after leaving, past records included) and the record not withdrawn by a leave (`authorizedC` in `lib/group.js`, the gate of a `group: null` admit). A returning creator's record on a creator CW also counts when it passes the repair gate ("Community repair after the creator's return"), as for a CW meta. A member's record never counts. Absent, it is `false` (also for an unbound or empty C). A `group-invites` naming a group changes nothing in C.
- A community has no posts: `posts: []`, `future: []`, `sealed: 0`, `epoch: null`, `rekeyPending: false`, `entitled: []`, `removals: []`. `join` and `name` come from the header and the last not-withdrawn `group-meta` by root seq, overridden by the latest counted CW meta ("Community repair after the creator's return").
  - **Catalogue.** `created: Map<G, { writer, seq, diary }>` and `general`. Only C's root counts: the fold reads the `group-create` records of C's root in root seq order, skipping a create at a root seq at or above the creator's create cut (`createCut`, "The creator's root after a leave" below) and a create that names C itself. The first valid create is Geral (`general`). Each group is listed once, at its first create, and the 32 cap (`MAX_GROUPS_PER_COMMUNITY`) counts distinct groups in that order: a create beyond it puts the group in `overflow` (an **orphan**, below). A group attaches only when its header diary is C's creator. `groups` is the keys listed, sorted. There is no catalogue entry from an admin or from a CW (`group-create` is `root-only`), and so no per-diary creation state.
  - **CW demotions in C.** A `group: null` CW demotion counts only once a confirmed creator leave bounds C's root (written on it or listing it; the earliest bounding set), or a counted first-set creator leave written on C's root itself bounds it ("The creator's root after a leave"); the community result exposes this as `cwDemote`. An unconfirmed leave on another device never enables it. It counts only when C's creator diary wrote it on a counted CW whose record is not withdrawn, and the CW was not retired, or was retired only as "Community repair after the creator's return" allows. It is an `on: false` event in the member's admin layer that cuts every writer (as a root demotion: unlisted writers at 0) and a removal. After it the diary loses the C admin title and the shield. A `group: G` CW demotion changes nothing in C: it acts in G (below).
  - **Community repair after the creator's return.** When the root's powers ended (`cwDemote` is true), C's creator diary repairs C through a counted CW whose record is not withdrawn. A repair (CW demotion with `group: null`, CW meta, CW drop) counts only when written on a creator CW whose generation is greater than S, the lowest leave set that ended C's root powers by either route (a counted set-0 leave on C's root makes S 0 even when a confirmed bounding leave of a later set exists; a later confirmed leave never raises it): CWs of generation S or lower never repair, including repairs written before the leave. Repairs on those later CWs survive retirement: a repair on creator CW w at seq s keeps counting after w retires, unless a diary-confirmed counted leave of the creator voids it with a bound b(w) <= s, where b(w) is that leave's own seq when it is written on w, the cut it lists for w when w's generation is not later than the leave's set, and 0 for any creator CW of generation not later than the leave's set that the leave neither is on nor lists, except a CW that has its own confirmed counted leave, which only that leave bounds, at its own seq. An unconfirmed leave voids no repair, and retirement by generation alone voids none. The gate that opens repairs is unchanged, and "No authority after leaving" is unchanged for every other record type. A CW `group-meta` with `group: null` sets `name` and `join`, overriding the root's metas; the latest such CW meta in causal order wins. A CW `group-drop` with `on: true` removes `group` from the catalogue (the group then resolves as an orphan) and one with `on: false` puts it back; per group the latest counted CW drop wins. `on: true` for Geral is ignored. An undo lists a group again only if its `group-create` still counts. A drop frees no catalogue slot and never creates a group or grants anything. The community result's `dropped` lists the groups currently dropped (sorted hex keys; only groups whose create counts; never Geral). Neither record is copied into a group's community logs. So a returning creator can still fix the community's name and entry and take a group out of the list, while naming admins and creating groups stay ended.
  - **Leave sets and stale leaves.** Before the fold, C's leaves are taken in causal order and grouped per diary into leave sets: the leaves between two rejoins of that diary form one set, numbered 0, 1, ... in the order the sets first appear. A device's **generation** comes from its own header and its diary: k + 1 when the header's `rejoin` names a counted leave of set k (unknown, so never stale, while that leave is not counted yet) AND the diary's earliest `on: true` `group-join` binding that CW names the same `rejoin` (writer and seq); otherwise 0 (a header `rejoin` without a matching join, a join without `rejoin`, or a match only in a later join). A generation-0 CW of a diary that has any leave set is retired (no authority, and its repairs do not count). Only the generation and the closed sets need the diary's signature this way; the header `rejoin` still orders block 0 and must name a valid leave of the same diary. G writers keep the header rule. A leave counts unless its writer already wrote a counted leave, or the writer's generation is below the set the leave would join. A sibling's cut never makes a leave stale: otherwise a stolen device could list its owner's other devices and void the leave a later rejoin names. A stale leave is dropped from the logs: it opens no set, retires nothing and is no removal, so a lost or stolen old device can never end its owner's current generation. A counted leave retires its own writer and the devices its cuts list of the same diary whose generation is the set's or an earlier one, never a later device; every CW whose generation is below the diary's number of sets is retired too. Retirement is monotonic.
  - **Confirmed leaves.** A counted leave on CW w of diary d is **confirmed** only by an exact matching `group-confirm` of d's own diary: `group` C, `writer` w, `seq` the leave's seq and `digest` the `leaveDigest` of that leave (section 2). The diary is signed by the identity, so a device key alone never confirms a leave, and a confirmation names one block, so it never confirms another leave on the same CW. A diary `group-join` with `on: false` confirms nothing: it still unbinds the writer and clears indexes. Honest nodes write the `group-confirm` for exactly the leave they appended (rpc.md, `community.leave`). Only confirmed leaves move another device's history boundary, the root's bounds and the catalogue limit, and the leave a new device names on a rejoin.
  - **The creator's root after a leave.** A leave of C's creator ends the authority of C's root (owner decision): root records at root seq >= `rootCut` carry no authority in C or in its groups, and group creates at root seq >= `createCut` do not count. A leave touches the root when it is written on the root (bound: its own seq, whatever cut it lists, so the retired root can void only what it appends itself) or lists the root in its cuts (bound: the listed cut). Only diary-confirmed creator leaves that touch the root count for the confirmed bound. `rootCut` = `createCut` comes from the EARLIEST leave set that holds such a leave: the lowest bound within that set. A later set never lowers an existing bound and bounds the root only when no earlier set did. A confirmed leave that touches neither gives no bound. Until such a confirmation arrives, only a counted first-set leave written on C's root bounds it, at its own seq; otherwise there is no bound. A leave on another device, whatever it lists and whatever a rejoin names, never bounds the root or the catalogue until the diary confirms it, so a creator who returns after a forged, unconfirmed leave keeps its powers. Leave withdrawal on C's root follows the same chain rule: the root is one append-only chain, so only a leave written on it withdraws its records (those at a seq at or above that leave's), and a leave on another device never withdraws root records. Honest nodes never write a creator leave whose cuts miss the root (rpc.md, `community.leave`). Consequence: after an honest leave (confirmed, or written on the root), a returning creator can no longer name admins or create groups through the root, while one that returns after a forged, unconfirmed leave on another device keeps those powers; it still moderates C (admit, ban, hide) through its new CW, as any member with C authority, and can repair the community (name, entry, catalogue removals, demotions: "Community repair after the creator's return", "CW demotions in C").
  - The result is `{ kind: 'community', name, join, creator, admins: Set<diary>, members, requests, bans, leaves, lastLeave, leaveOf, leaveSetOf, confirmedLeaveOf, cwDemote, rootAuthority, creatorConfirmSeq, dropped, admittedCws, liveWriters, authorityWriters, last, writers, groups, general, created, memberInvite, incomplete }`. `lastLeave` maps every diary that ever left to its causally latest counted leave (kept after a rejoin), `leaveOf` maps each core to the counted leave written on it, `leaveSetOf` maps each such core to that leave's set number, `confirmedLeaveOf` maps each diary to the first confirmed leave of its latest leave set, `cwDemote` tells whether a creator CW demotion with `group: null` can count ("CW demotions in C"), and `rootAuthority` is true exactly when the creator is bound and C's root has no bound (`rootCut` is Infinity: no confirmed first-set creator leave touches the root and no leave is written on the root; "The creator's root after a leave"); the root's held length and next seq play no part, and it is `false` for an empty or unbound community. `creatorConfirmSeq` is the lowest diary seq of the `group-confirm` records that confirm the bounding leaves of that earliest set, or Infinity when there is none. A creator leave on another device that was never confirmed does not end `rootAuthority`, even though it takes the root out of `authorityWriters` (that set follows liveness by generation, not root authority).
