Nodes decide the creating device's root powers by `rootAuthority`, the fold's own criterion (rpc.md 5.36, 5.60, 5.69); nodes use them to name a rejoin (rpc.md, `community.join`). `admins` is the creator (when the root is bound) plus every diary whose root admin layer is on now and whose status in `members` is `member`: a left or banned admin is not listed (its shield in C follows the group rule). An unbound root, or an empty community, has `creator: null`, empty sets, `groups: []`, `general: null` and `incomplete: true`, keeping `name` and `join` from the header.
  - It is pure, never throws and is order independent.

```
authorizedC(n):                         // n = node (cw, seq) of diary d in C's order
  if withdrawnC(n): return false        // C's leave withdrawal
  if cw == C and seq >= rootCut: return false   // the creator's root after a leave
  if cw != C and retired(cw): return false      // no authority after leaving, past included
  if d == ownerC: return true           // C root bound, or a live CW of the creator
  return layerAt(rootAdminC[d], cw, seq) and not layerAt(bansC[d], cw, seq)
```

`buildCommunity` also keeps, for `buildGroup` and never in its result, `authorizedC`, `withdrawnC`, the set of retired CWs (the computation of "Entitled writers" applied to C), the leaves of each diary, each CW's `rejoin`, its diary, the set of counted CWs (C included when bound), the held length of every C core, the root admin and ban layers per diary, and whether an `on: true` grant ever counted for a diary.

- **`buildGroup` input and parent resolution.** `community` is `null` or exactly `buildCommunity`'s input; `buildGroup` folds it once with the community fold. The parent is resolved in this order:

| Case | `community` result | `orphan` | `incomplete` | Gate |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `input.community` null, or its root has no block 0, or its key differs from `header.community` | `null` | `false` | `true` | off (every writer counts, as for any group) |
| C given but forked copies, invalid header, kind not `community`, or unbound | `null` | `false` | `true` | off |
| C folds but is `incomplete` and `created.get(G)` is missing (the part of C's root holding G's `group-create` may not have arrived) | `null` | `false` | `true` | off |
| C folds and G's header diary is not C's creator, or C is complete and `created.get(G)` is missing (`G` in `overflow` is simply not created) | `null` | `true` | unchanged | off |
| C folds and authorizes G | C's result | `false` | also when C is `incomplete` | on |

Readers always read, so an orphan or a group with an unresolved parent stays readable; nodes decide writes: they refuse every write in an orphan and wait for the parent when it is missing (rpc.md).

- **The community gate** (only when `community` is not null). For every candidate writer w of G with header `h` and diary d = `h.diary`, `cw = h.member`:

```
if w == G:                                  // the root: diary-level gate
  rootLive = rootBound and C.members.get(d) == 'member'
else:
  if cw == null: reject w                   // header-invalid
  if not C.counted.has(cw) or C.cwDiary.get(cw) != d: reject w   // header-invalid; when C is incomplete and cw is simply absent, drop w and set incomplete
  live(w) = C.members.get(d) == 'member' and not C.retiredC.has(cw)
```

  `live` and `bannedC(d) = C.members.get(d) == 'banned'` read the final state of C, never a position, and act in G as follows.
  - **Status.** `bannedC(d)` makes d `banned` in G whatever G says. Otherwise, if d has no counted writer w with `live(w)` (the root counts for the creator with `rootLive`), d is `left` in G; otherwise the ordinary rule applies. A diary that is `left` by the gate is never listed in `requests`: it must enter C first.
  - **Only admitted community writers count.** C's fold returns `admittedCws`: every counted CW of an open community, the creator's CWs, and in an approval community the CWs that d's CURRENT admission covers (the latest applied `group: null` admit layer of d's current generation (a retired CW keeps the generation it was admitted in, so the leave rule holds for it), or the strict root grant layer, on for that CW at its end). A record on a non-root G writer whose `member` CW is not in `admittedCws` is withdrawn (posts hidden, no authority, never live): a request never admitted cannot post or moderate in C's groups, an admission withdrawal hides that diary's records (earlier ones included) until it is admitted again, and a later admission makes them count. A leave withdraws no admission, so the leave rule below holds, and a ban hides everything anyway. `live(w)` also requires w's CW in `admittedCws`.
  - **Gated root.** When G's creator is not a member of C and has no leave at all in C (it never joined, or its CW was never admitted), every record on G's root is withdrawn from seq 0, as if the root had no records. A creator that has a leave in C follows the leave anchor under "Community leave keeps earlier messages"; once it is a member again its root records count as before.
  - **Community ban hides.** When `bannedC(d)`, every record of d in G is withdrawn from seq 0: posts are hidden and no authority remains (an author's `group-hide` of their own post excepted, as for groups). An unban in C restores them (final state). G's own ban layer is untouched. The ban also acts per CW: a CW that C's ban layer still covers now (an unban that did not list it) is out of C's live and authority writers (`liveWriters`, `authorityWriters`), and in every group of C its G writers are not live and all their records are withdrawn from seq 0 (final state), until an unban lists it. So a partial unban never revives a device it did not name. `community.leave` still works from such a CW (rpc.md), so a member is never locked in C.
  - **Community leave keeps earlier messages.** Every counted leave of d in C (a `group-leave` in one of d's CWs, or in C's root for C's creator) is an internal node `community-leave` in G's causal order (a virtual log, below). A CW's generation is fixed by its header and its diary's first binding join ("Leave sets and stale leaves": k + 1 only when both name the same leave of set k, else 0). A counted CW is RETIRED, monotonically, as "Leave sets and stale leaves" says (its generation is below the diary's number of leave sets in C, or a counted leave is written on it, or a counted leave of the same diary lists it and the CW's generation is not later than that leave's set); nothing appended to it later revives it. C's fold records, for each retired CW, its withdrawal boundary (`retiredBy`): the CW's own counted leave; otherwise only a confirmed leave of its generation's set (in a closed set, the leave the rejoin names if it is confirmed, else the first confirmed leave of the set in causal order; in an open set, the first confirmed leave). With none, a community leave withdraws none of that CW's records in G (its authority stays void), so an unconfirmed leave from a stolen sibling never hides another device's history. A record on a G writer w whose header `member` is cw is withdrawn when cw is retired and the record sorts after the `community-leave` node of cw's boundary (`retiredBy`); a record on a writer whose CW is not retired is never withdrawn by a community leave, so the order between a leave and a later rejoin (whose clocks may disagree) never matters. A retired CW with no boundary (no own leave and no confirmed leave of its set) has none of its G writers' records withdrawn by a community leave; its authority is void all the same, and a CW C never admitted is withdrawn by the admission rule above. For G's root (the diary-level gate): when G's creator is not a member of C now, its root records that sort after the `community-leave` node of the first leave of its latest leave set in C that is confirmed or written on C's root (a later or far-future leave never moves the anchor) are withdrawn, and with no such leave none are; once it is a member again, its root records count as before. Rejoining C needs a new CW and never resurrects old group writers: a G writer must name the live CW in its own header.
  - **No authority after leaving.** Records on a retired CW and on the G writers bound to it carry no authority, past included: a diary that left C keeps no moderation power in any group of C, whatever the position of its records. G's root carries none while G's creator is not a member of C. Posts keep the leave rule above (earlier messages stay visible); only authority is withdrawn whole. The same holds in C: a retired CW gives no authority, and C's root gives none at or above `rootCut` ("The creator's root after a leave"), in C and through C in its groups (`authorizedC`). A private group's key block that relies on C (`auth`) must cite its writer's own CW: reading keeps the historical citation, while sending needs the cited CW to hold C authority now (in C's `authorityWriters`).
  - **Creator marks on group roots.** In a group fold only the `group-cut` marks of G's creator diary whose `writer` is G's root count. G-root records at root seq >= the lowest such `cut` carry no authority, whether or not the creator is a member of C now: root grants and demotions, `group-meta`, root admit, ban and hide, the send validity of the root's key blocks (reading history stays, as for any key block), and the root's entry in `authorityWriters`. Posts are unaffected. When the creator's diary as read skips a seq (`{ gap: true }`) and the creator ever left C, a mark may be missing, so the G root carries no authority until the history is complete (the browser node reads a prefix plus a newest tail; the core node reads contiguously). The creator's diary writes the marks when it leaves C (rpc.md, `community.leave`), so a returning creator's G-root powers do not come back; it still acts in G as C's creator through its new CW. A marked G root is never entitled to private-group keys; the returning creator receives keys on its new G writer. `buildGroup` returns `rootCut`: the lowest mark cut on G's root; 0 while the gap rule above voids the root; 0 also when C's creator has a confirmed leave bounding C's root and the creator's diary holds no valid `group-cut` mark for G's root; else `null`. In that last case of 0, G's root carries no authority at any seq (grants and demotions, meta, admit, ban and hide, the send validity of root key blocks, private-key entitlement, the root's entry in `authorityWriters`): the confirmed leave already ended the creator's root powers in C, and only a mark can say how far G's root went. With a valid mark the lowest cut applies; with no confirmed leave bounding C's root, an unmarked root is unaffected. Only the confirmed leave triggers this rule. Nodes never use a marked G root as the device's own writer again (not live, not used for leaving): a returning creator joins G through a new G writer bound to its live CW. The creator's leave writes the marks before the diary `group-confirm` that confirms the C leave; if a mark fails, the confirmation is not written and the leave stays unconfirmed until a later `community.leave` writes the missing marks and the confirmation (rpc.md). Marks count only up to the creator's confirmation: once the creator's diary holds a `group-confirm` that confirms a bounding creator leave of C (the earliest bounding set, "The creator's root after a leave"), only marks at a diary seq below that confirmation's seq count (C's fold keeps that seq as `creatorConfirmSeq`). Later marks are ignored: they never give authority back to a root left unmarked (`rootCut` 0) and never move a cut. Honest nodes write the marks before the confirmation (rpc.md 5.64). With no confirmed creator leave, marks and the gap rule work as above.
  - **CW demotions in G.** A `group: G` CW demotion is a community-authority node in G's virtual log. It counts only when the creator's diary holds at least one valid `group-cut` mark for G's root (a diary gap alone does not enable it), and only when C's creator diary wrote it, C's repair gate is open (a confirmed leave bounding C's root, or an on-root bound), the CW's generation is greater than S and C's authority holds at that CW position (`authorizedC`), a retired creator CW counting as "Community repair after the creator's return" allows. It is an `on: false` event in G's admin layer for the member, cutting every writer, and a removal ("Removals"). It never grants. After it the diary loses the G admin title and the shield.
  - **Authority.** An administrative node of d in G needs `!bannedC(d)` and not to be withdrawn by a community leave. The creator powers of G (`ownerAt`) additionally require `rootLive`.
  - **Admin title per writer.** A C admin counts (the `admins` result, the shield, the entitlement of rule 2) only with a counted, non-retired CW that is admitted and that the root grant covers now: an admin that left C and rejoined needs a new grant. In a gated G the same holds for a G admin: the G grant must cover a bound writer of d (below).
  - **Shield and `admins`.** Under the gate, `adminNow(d)` (a G admin now) requires a bound G writer of d (counted, or dropped by a writer cap) whose CW is live and admitted in C, with the root grant layer on now for that writer (a grant gives nothing to writers it does not list) (in a private group, only grants that saw every leave): a G admin that left C loses the shield, the title and the admission exemptions, also after rejoining C on a writer the grant never listed, until it is granted again. `shielded(d) = (d == owner and rootLive) or adminNow(d) or C.admins.has(d)`. The `admins` result is the owner (when `rootLive`), every diary with `adminNow(d)` whose status in C is `member`, and `C.admins`.

- **Community-authority actions inside G.** For each counted CW of C (diary d) the fold adds a **virtual log** to G's logs when it has anything to contribute: the admit, ban, hide and `group-invites` records of that CW with `group === G`, plus the internal `community-leave` nodes. C's root counts as the CW of C's creator. The records are real nodes in G's causal order, with their own `seen`, cuts, `lifts` and `target` edges resolved against G's writers and the other virtual logs; the internal nodes have as edge only the previous node of the same virtual log (their `seen` points into C and dangles in G). Virtual logs never enter `writers`, the per-diary cap, the 512 cap, the rejoin check or the counted set, and `last` may name a G-targeted CW record, never an internal node. A CW record `group-admit`, `group-ban` or `group-hide` with `group: G` follows the authority switch of the same records in a group writer, with this authority check:

```
authorizedG(n):                          // any moderation node n in G
  d = n.diary
  if bannedC(d) or withdrawnByCommunityLeave(n) or withdrawn(n): return false   // withdrawn = G's leave withdrawal
  if n.writer is a CW (virtual log): return C.authorizedC(n.writer, n.seq)       // creator of C or admin layer on, C ban off
  if d == owner: return rootLive and ownerAt(n)                                 // creator of G
  return adminAt(d, n.writer, n.seq, n.pos) and not layerAt(bans[d], n.writer, n.seq)   // G's grants and ban layer
```

  A community admin needs no admit in G while it is an admin of C now (its posts in an approval G read C's current admins: after a demotion they need a group admit, earlier ones included) and cannot be banned in G (the shield), and a leave of d in G changes nothing in its community authority. A `group-admin` can never come from a CW (`root-only`). The shield applies to the target as in a group, and a positive admit by a CW of the admitted diary itself is ignored. An admit by a CW counts its generation with the same `observedLeaves` over G's cuts.

- **Orphans and the catalogue cap.** A group is attached to its community only by C's catalogue. A root nobody created in C's root, a group whose header diary is not C's creator, and a group beyond the 32nd distinct `group-create` in C's root order are orphans: they stay readable and nobody can write in them. There is no race for the last slot: only C's creator writes creates, and C's root order is total.
- **Missing and forked roots.** An entry without a block 0 (no entries, or no header) counts as not given. Several copies of one root are merged by seq, so their input order never matters; every copy with a block 0 takes part whatever its header kind, even when that block is not a valid header (the header is validated only after the copies are merged), and copies that disagree on the header (a `group` header and a `community` header for the same key included) or on the block held at any seq (compared before validation, so a valid block against an invalid one at the same seq is a fork too) are a fork: that community is ignored (parent resolution above: `community` null, `incomplete`). Copies of one writer core follow the same rule: every copy with a block 0 takes part whatever group or diary its header names, the merged header must name G (or C) and a diary that bound the writer, and a forked writer is left out.
- **Out of scope** in this version: nesting communities, transferring a community or a group to another creator, private communities (a community is always public: its name, members and group list are public; a private group of a community is allowed, "Private groups"), moving a group to another community, and a community-wide feed. Community powers never reach root-only group records.

### Private groups

A private group (`visibility: 'private'` in the root header, kind `group`) is a group whose posts, hides, reactions and meta travel sealed: only the devices that hold the group's current key can read them. Membership, moderation and the key distribution itself stay plaintext and keep every rule above; sealing changes what a reader can see, never who has authority. Visibility is fixed at creation. A private header always has `join: 'approval'`, `name: null` and a hex64 `box`, and every writer header in it a hex64 `box` ("Cores and headers"). Crypto primitives are libsodium's, through `sodium-universal` (`packages/core/lib/group-crypto.js`).

**Box keys.** Each device has ONE X25519 key pair (crypto_box) for every private group it takes part in; its public key is the `box` of every writer header that device writes in a private group, and the `box` of the root header for the creator's device. The secret never leaves the device, never travels in a record and is never printed. A `box` that is a low-order X25519 point (libsodium's blocklist of seven points, compared with the top bit of the last byte cleared; `isWeakBoxKey` in `packages/core/lib/group.js`) makes the header invalid, the root's (`malformed`) as a writer's (`malformed`, so `header-invalid` in the fold and member sync): sealing to such a point fails in sodium-native and yields a constant shared secret elsewhere, and nodes also skip such a recipient defensively. The core node keeps it in `group-box.json` and the browser derives it from the passkey PRF ("Data-dir files"). Because epoch keys are sealed to this key, a device that loses it loses every private group it was in; nodes never replace it silently.

**Epochs.** An epoch key is 32 random bytes (a crypto_secretbox key). An epoch is identified by its `kid`: the `ref` `{ writer, seq }` of the FIRST `group-key` block of that epoch, never by a counter (two admins may open epochs at the same time, below). Readers compare kids by writer then seq.

**`group-key`.** A first block is `{ "type": "group-key", "kid": null, "basis": [ref], "keys": [{ "writer": "<hex64>", "sealed": "<base64>" }], "auth": null | { "writer": "<hex64>", "seq": <integer >= 1> }, "commit": "<hex64>", "at": <ISO> }` and a continuation `{ "type": "group-key", "kid": ref, "basis": [ref], "keys": [...], "auth": null | { "writer": "<hex64>", "seq": <integer >= 1> }, "at": <ISO> }` (no `commit`: a continuation carrying one, or a first block without one, is malformed), in any writer core of a private group (the root included).

- `kid: null` marks the first block of a new epoch, whose kid is then the block's own `{ writer, seq }`. **Key commitment.** Its `commit` is the lowercase hex of `keyCommit(key, G, kid)` (`packages/core/lib/group-crypto.js`): `crypto_generichash(32, utf8('varanda/group-key-commit/v1') || G || kidWriter || kidSeq || epochKey)`, unkeyed BLAKE2b with a 32-byte output, over the 27-byte domain string, G and the kid's writer key (32 raw bytes each), the kid's seq as an 8-byte big-endian unsigned integer and the 32-byte key. It binds the epoch to ONE key, so no continuation (from a departed or current admin alike) can hand a recipient another key for the same kid. With 48 entries, 16 basis refs and the commit a first block is about 12.2 KB. A **continuation** block carries the kid of an existing epoch and hands that same key to more recipients: more than 48 recipients at a rotation, a newly admitted member's writers (history, below), or a new device. A continuation may sit in another writer than the first block. A continuation whose kid does not name a counted first block (a `group-key` with `kid: null`) is ignored.
- `keys` has 1..`MAX_KEY_RECIPIENTS` = 48 entries, each `writer` at most once. `sealed` is canonical base64 (standard alphabet, padded) of `crypto_box_seal(epochKey || tag, box)`, where `box` is the recipient writer's header `box` (the root header's `box` for `writer = G`), `epochKey` is the 32-byte key and `tag` binds it to this group and epoch (**context binding**): `tag = crypto_generichash(16, utf8('varanda/group-key/v1') || G || kidWriter || kidSeq)`, unkeyed BLAKE2b with a 16-byte output, over the 20-byte domain string, G (32 raw bytes), the kid's writer key (32 raw bytes) and the kid's seq as an 8-byte big-endian unsigned integer. For a first block (`kid: null`) the kid is the block's own `{ writer, seq }`: the distributor knows its writer and the seq it appends at. The plaintext is 48 bytes, so `sealed` decodes to exactly 96 bytes (48 + `crypto_box_SEALBYTES` 48) and is 128 base64 characters. On open the fold (not the caller's `unseal`, "Reading") recomputes `tag` from the block's own G and kid, compares it in constant time and discards the entry (as if it did not open) when the tag differs, so a sealed blob copied from another group or another epoch never yields a key. Size: an entry is about 220 bytes of JSON, so 48 entries are about 10.6 KB and 16 basis refs about 1.5 KB, under `MAX_BLOCK_BYTES` = 16384 with room for the other fields.
- `basis` has 0..`MAX_KEY_BASIS` = 16 refs, each at most once: the removal records this epoch responds to ("Eligibility"). The fold reads the basis of first blocks only; nodes write `basis: []` on continuations and on the first epoch.
- No `seen`: its ordering edges are the previous record of its writer, every `basis` ref and, on a continuation, `kid`.

**`group-sealed`.** `{ "type": "group-sealed", "kid": ref, "nonce": "<hex48>", "box": "<base64>", "at": <ISO> }`. `nonce` is 24 random bytes in lowercase hex, fresh for every envelope (never derived, never reused). `box` is canonical base64 of `crypto_secretbox_easy(inner, nonce, epochKey)`, where `inner` is the UTF-8 compact JSON (`JSON.stringify`) of the inner record, at most `MAX_SEALED_INNER_BYTES` = 12000 bytes including the binding fields below; the whole block still obeys `MAX_BLOCK_BYTES`. The inner record has exactly the keys of a `group-post` (`type, text, replyTo, seen, at`, or the v2 set `type, text, replyTo, media, card, fwd, seen, at`, whose items are then private: "Group post media"), a `group-hide` (`type, target, on, seen, at`), a `group-react` (`type, target, emoji, seen, at`) or, in the root only, a `group-meta` (`type, name, join, seen, at`), plus three **binding fields**: `"writer": "<hex64>"` (the writer core the envelope is appended to), `"group": "<hex64>"` (G) and `"kid": ref` (equal to the envelope's `kid`). After opening, the fold requires `inner.writer` = the envelope's writer, `inner.group` = G, `inner.kid` = the envelope's kid (same writer and seq) and `inner.at` = the envelope's `at`, then validates the record without the three binding fields against the shape above; so an envelope copied into another writer, another group or under another kid never reads as a record there (secretbox has no associated data). A nested `group-sealed` or `group-key`, any other type, a missing or extra key, a binding mismatch, bad JSON or a failed decryption leave the envelope unread: it counts in `sealed` and never fails the fold.

**Validation.** `validateGroupRecord(record, seq, { root, community, private })` checks the shapes above. With `private: true`, a plaintext `group-post`, `group-hide`, `group-react` or `group-meta` returns `sealed-only` (skipped); with `private` false, `group-key` and `group-sealed` return `private-only`. A community core (root or CW) rejects both new types (`group-only`). The inner record of an envelope, once its binding fields are checked and removed, is validated with `private` false, `sealed: true` and the envelope's `root`. `sealed` only changes what a v2 `group-post`'s media items must be: private items with it, public items without it ("Group post media").

**Distributors.** A `group-key` block counts for reading when its writer was authorized to distribute at that writer/seq: a bound writer of the creator (root bound), or a writer that passes the ordinary authority check for administrative actions there (group admin layer, ban layer off), or, through community authority, a writer whose block carries a valid `auth` (below). Leave withdrawal does NOT apply to reading, neither to the `group-key` block nor to the grant that gave its writer authority (the distributor check reads the root's `group-admin` records and C's admin layer without leave withdrawal), so an admin who later leaves or is demoted, or a creator whose leave withdraws the grant, never takes back the epochs already handed out and history stays readable. For reading, a demotion cuts only the writers it lists, at their listed cut (a writer it omits keeps the read validity of its blocks, though it is cut at 0 for everything else), and a root demotion never reaches a block that sorts before it in causal order, whatever its cuts; grants still give nothing to unlisted writers and bans still apply by their cuts. So no demotion, honest or not, makes history unreadable. Sending keeps the strict layer: a first block counts for Eligibility, coverage and the key-holding and power records of "Removals" only when its writer passes the ordinary check with demotions cutting every writer, so no member ever sends under the epoch of a writer that lost power. That check also applies leave withdrawal to the grants: a grant that a creator's leave withdrew keeps the blocks it authorized readable but gives no sending power. A block valid for sending must also come from its diary's current generation: when a leave of that diary sorts before the block in causal order, its authority must come from a grant that saw every leave of that diary sorting before the block ("Entitled writers"; for the creator, a writer whose rejoin names the latest such leave). A block that sorts before every leave of its diary keeps its authority, so the epochs an admin opened before leaving stay valid history, and a block that covers a leave has that leave as an edge, so it needs a grant written after the leave. The writer that left, any other writer of that diary from before the leave (whatever grant or community power still lists it) and a creator's root after the creator left keep reading power only, so a block they write after a rejoin, wherever its `at` sorts it, never becomes an epoch anyone sends under, never counts as a recipient or authority record and is never reported by `onKey`. A rejoined distributor regains sending power on its new writer once a grant that saw its leave lists that writer. Other blocks are ignored. By owner decision, the creator of C and the admins of C (`C.admins`) distribute and receive keys in every private group of C through community authority, like group admins, while they hold that power; losing it is an admin change (below). For sending, an epoch must also pass "Eligibility".

**Community distributors (`auth`).** A `group-key` block K in a G writer w of diary d, when d is not G's creator and no G grant covers (w, seq), counts through the community only when `K.auth` is not null and names a position `{ writer: cw, seq: s }`, where `s` (an integer, at least 1) is the length of the distributor's own CW when it wrote the block (the next position in that CW, like a cut), such that, with C's fold, `cwOk` holds (`auth` must cite the writer's own CW): `C.cwDiary.get(cw) == d` (C's root for C's creator) and `s` is at most the held length of that core (the length it cites exists here). Reading (`dist`) needs `cwOk` and, for C's creator, the root bound, or, for anyone else, some counted `on: true` root grant of C that lists cw with a cut at or below s (demotions, C bans and leave withdrawal are ignored for reading, as history must never become unreadable: a grant and a later demotion can both cut at the same length when the admin wrote nothing in its CW in between). The community gate never withdraws a key block's read validity either. Sending (`send`) needs the strict layer: d's grant in C's current admin layer on at (cw, s) and C's ban layer off there, `C.admins.has(d)`, `live(w)`, and the cited CW holding C authority now (in C's `authorityWriters`: a CW retired, banned per CW or demoted never sends, even when its diary is still a C admin through another device). `auth` is ignored (as null) on a block whose writer holds G authority, so G authority wins; a block with `auth: null` from a diary without G authority is not counted. `auth` is a validity citation, not an ordering edge: an edge into another core's chain would let a CW position pin a block's place in G's order. Nodes write `auth = { writer: ownCW, seq: ownCW.length }` when they distribute through community authority, and `null` otherwise. Citing the length matters: a grant's cut is the CW's observed length L, so an admin promoted at L that wrote nothing in its CW since cites L, which the grant covers; a demotion's cut M turns the strict layer off from M on, so a demoted admin's blocks are never sent under. A demoted admin can still write blocks that read as valid by citing an old position; nobody sends under them, and only its own posts could use them, which a community ban or a hide covers. Everything else (kid, commit, tag, basis, rekey-pending, `onKey` only for full epochs) is unchanged.

**Reading.** `buildGroup` takes an optional `keys`: `null` or `{ writers: Set<hex64>, unseal(sealed), onKey? }`. `writers` are the reader's own writer keys in this group (current and retired writers, and G when the reader's device created the group: they all share the device's box key). `unseal(sealed)` takes one `sealed` base64 string and returns its raw 48-byte plaintext `epochKey || tag` (`crypto_box_seal_open` with the device key pair) or null; it never checks the tag. The fold requires exactly 48 bytes, checks the tag against the block's G and kid ("`group-key`"), copies the 32-byte key and zero-fills both the 48 bytes and the buffer `unseal` returned. The fold calls `unseal` only on entries naming one of `writers` inside counted `group-key` blocks; an epoch whose first block is valid only for reading (or withdrawn) is opened only when some counted, not withdrawn envelope names its kid, since nothing else could be read under it; an opened key counts only when `keyCommit(key, G, kid)` equals the epoch's first-block `commit` (compared in constant time); an entry that fails is ignored and later entries and blocks are still tried, so for each kid the fold keeps the committed key from the first block in causal order that opens to it. It never puts a key in its result. The optional `onKey(kid, key, recipients)` is called once per epoch the fold accepted and opened whose first block is valid for sending and not withdrawn by a leave (after the tag and commitment checks, in causal order of the first block; an epoch valid only for reading, such as a departed or demoted distributor's, stays readable inside the fold but is never reported, so it is never handed on), with a `{ writer, seq }` copy of the kid, a COPY of the 32-byte key that the caller owns and must zero-fill, and `recipients`, the sorted writer keys listed by every block of that epoch valid for sending and not withdrawn by a leave; an error it throws is ignored and changes nothing. A node gets the keys it may hand on (history, rotation, sealing) only from this hook, never by recomputing distributors itself. An opened envelope becomes its inner record at the envelope's `{ writer, seq }`, with the inner record's ordering edges plus the envelope's `kid` edge (so it never sorts before its epoch's first block), and with the authority, leave withdrawal and membership generation (the leaves of its diary before it) the envelope had in the plaintext pass below; an envelope that stays sealed is a node with only its writer edge and its `kid` edge, and is never shown. An opened `group-react` is the exception: its envelope stays that sealed node and the reaction is folded beside the order ("Reactions"), without counting in `sealed`. The fold runs twice. The plaintext pass keeps every envelope sealed and alone decides membership and moderation (`members`, `requests`, `admins`, `bans`, `leaves`, `community`, `writers`) and the epoch machinery (Distributors, Removals, Entitled writers, Eligibility); the opened pass, under that same authority, only places content (posts, hides, reactions, the name) and orders it, so decrypted `seen`, `replyTo` and `target` edges never change who is a member or who may act. The fold is pure and order independent for a given `keys`; two readers with different keys may see different posts and order, never different membership. The name of a private group is the `name` of the last opened, valid, not withdrawn `group-meta` in the root by root seq; a sealed meta whose `join` is not `approval` is skipped, and `join` is always `approval`. Without a key the name is null.

**Fold result.** For a private group `buildGroup` also returns `sealed` (counted `group-sealed` envelopes that stayed unread), `epoch` (the kid of the newest epoch eligible for the reader to send under, or null), `rekeyPending` (true when the reader holds the key of at least one counted epoch and none is eligible) and `entitled` (the entitled writers of "Entitled writers", sorted hex: exactly the recipients a rotation seals to, from the plaintext pass) and `removals` (sorted `{ writer, seq }` refs: the latest counted removal per writer core of G plus the counted removals from C's cores of "Removals", never an ignored, void or unauthorized record; a distributor builds a rotation's `basis` only from `removals`, never by scanning records itself). A group with no parent community, or an orphan, returns `entitled: []` and `epoch: null`, and `rekeyPending: true` when the reader holds a key. A public group returns `sealed: 0`, `epoch: null`, `rekeyPending: false`, `entitled: []` and `removals: []`. RPC exposure: rpc.md (`group.read`, 5.61 `group.rekey`).

**Removals.** A removal is a counted record that takes someone's reading rights away: an applied `group-ban` with `on: true` (not void, not shielded), an applied `group-admit` with `on: false`, a `group-leave` that ends a generation able to hold keys (below), a counted `group-admin` with `on: false` in G's root, and the following records of C's cores (C's root or a CW), each a ref `{ writer: <core>, seq }`:

- a `group-ban { group: null, on: true }` applied in C (authorized in C, not shielded in C) counts when the banned member `everHeld` keys in G;
- a `group-admit { group: null, on: false }` applied in C counts when the member `everHeld` keys;
- a community leave set of d (its leaves between two rejoins, in C's order) counts under the same generation rule as G's own leaves: only when some leave of the set is not covered by C's ban layer at its writer and seq, and d held C power at a leave of the set or, for d's first set, held keys or power in G (`everHeldG`), or, for a later set, was handed power or keys in G after the previous counted set; a banned diary, or one that rejoined without readmission, never adds a requirement by appending leaves. The requirement is covered by a basis ref at or after ANY leave of the set on that leave's CW (one anchor per leave), so a backdated leave on a fresh CW never moves it;
- a root `group-admin { on: false }` of d counted in C counts when d held key power in G only through C (G's own grants do not hold it);
- a `group-admit` or `group-ban` with `group: G` in a CW is a record of G's virtual log and counts through the ordinary rules above: the latest per core (the CW key is the core).

`everHeld(d)`: d is G's creator, a recipient of a counted epoch, has a G root grant on, has a G admit on, or `C.everOn` records an `on: true` grant for d, or d is C's creator. Withdrawn, void or ignored records are not removals. A ban, admission withdrawal or root demotion counts only when its member could hold keys: the creator, a diary with an applied `on: true` admit or root grant, a creator or admin of C, or a diary with a writer listed in a counted `group-key` block. **Leaves.** The leaves of one diary between two of its rejoins (in causal order) form ONE requirement, met when F covers any of them; it counts only when the generation they end could hold keys: the diary is the creator, or some leave of the set is not banned at its own writer and seq by the ban layer's cuts (a leave on a writer past a standing ban's cut, or a writer the ban did not list, is banned whatever its `seen` or `at`) and the diary has admin or community authority there or now, or is admitted now for the generation of the set's first leave. So a banned, departed or pending diary (a rejoin without a new admit) never creates or advances a requirement with more leaves, repeated or backdated on another writer, the leaves of a diary never admitted cost nothing, and a genuine leave of an entitled member, admin or creator still does. This holds for a diary's first counted set; a later set counts only when the diary was handed power or keys after the last counted set's first leave and before this set's last leave (in causal order: an applied `on: true` admit or root grant for it, or a counted `group-key` block, valid for sending and not withdrawn by a leave, listing one of its writers; a diary's own blocks never count for itself). A standing grant, an old admit, or a generation count shifted by a rejoin that names an older leave and is backdated never makes a new requirement from a fresh rejoining writer, while a re-admitted member's genuine leave still does. `removals` carries the first leave of each counted set in causal order. **Coverage.** Of the removals in C's cores only the latest counted one per C core matters (single-writer chains): a distributor that saw it saw every earlier record of that core, so a counted first block listing it (or a descendant of one) covers them all, and a basis ref covers it when `b.writer` is the core and `b.seq` is at or past the removal and at or before the length held for that core. `removals` (result) carries at most one ref per C core with a removal, sorted together with the refs of G's own cores (by writer, then seq). A community demotion whose subject still holds key-holding power in G itself (G's creator, or a diary whose root grant of G still counts) takes nothing away and is not a requirement; a C demotion counts only for a diary that held key power in G through C (`C.everOn`). A C ban or a community leave of such a diary still counts, because the gate takes its keys away. Of the other removals in G's cores only the latest per writer core matters: covering it (an ancestor of F, and a basis ref is an ordering edge) covers the earlier ones on that writer through the writer chain. The fold checks coverage with one forward walk per remaining removal and keeps no per-removal state, so memory stays linear in the blocks. Leaving C ends a diary's participation in all its groups: it is a removal through the `group-leave` row above and leaves the group `rekey-pending` until a distributor rotates.

**Entitled writers.** Keys go to WRITERS, never to a diary as a whole. In a reader's fold, a writer w of diary d is **entitled** when w is counted (in `writers`), d has not left, w belongs to d's current generation (not **retired**: no leave was written on w, no leave withdraws w's newest record, w has written something since d's latest leave, for instance a rejoining writer's block 0, and a w with no record at all, of a diary that has left before, is listed by an admit of d's current generation), the ban layer of d is off for w at its current end, in a group with a parent community the writer must also be **live** ("The community gate": C folds and authorizes G, `C.members.get(d)` is `member` and w's CW is not retired; the creator's root needs `rootLive`), and one of these holds at w's current end:

1. d is the creator, the root is bound, and w is the root or a bound writer of the creator;
2. w holds admin authority there: a group `group-admin` grant lists w and no later demotion cuts it, or d is in `C.admins` (the creator of C or an admin of C) and w is live, by the owner decision under Distributors;
3. w is admitted there: an applied `on: true` `group-admit` of d's current generation lists w in its cuts and no later applied withdrawal covers w (the admit layer is on for w). A private group is always `approval`, so the open-group rule (every bound writer of a member) never applies.

**Leaving ends the nomination and the admission** (owner decision). Once d has a leave, a root grant or an admit for d counts for rules 2 and 3 only when its cuts prove it saw EVERY leave of d: for each leave L, it lists L's writer with a cut above L's seq, or lists a writer of d whose block 0 `rejoin` names L with a cut of 1 or more. The per-writer layers of those events then apply as usual. Cuts are written by the creator or the admin, so no `at`, `seen`, join order or extra writer the leaver chooses makes an event from before a leave count after it, and a leave written after an honest re-admission or re-grant ends it again. Rule 1 (the creator) after a leave holds only on a writer whose rejoin names the creator's causally latest leave. A positive admit written by a writer of the diary it admits never counts (nobody admits itself), and no event written by its subject's own diary proves it saw a leave. In a private group the same rule decides moderation: an admit, ban, unban, hide or admission withdrawal by a diary with leaves sorting before it counts only through a grant that saw every such leave, and the creator after a leave acts only from a writer whose rejoin names its latest such leave. Diaries without a leave are unchanged. Authority is judged on w itself: a retired writer never stays entitled through an earlier grant or creator binding, so after a leave and a rejoin only the writers the new grant, admit or creator writer covers receive keys. A writer that is only pending (its diary is `member` but no admit or grant lists that writer, so the diary is also in `requests`), banned, withdrawn by a leave, or not counted is never entitled, even when its diary's status is `member`. Diary status alone never makes a recipient.

**Eligibility.** A sender seals under the NEWEST eligible epoch in causal order of its first block. An epoch E with first block F is eligible for a reader when all hold:

1. F counts for reading (Distributors), is not withdrawn by a leave, and F's diary is the creator (root bound) or a diary in the final `admins` (community admins included) that has not left.
2. The reader holds E's key.
3. Every requirement the reader's fold counts ("Removals": the latest counted removal per writer core of G, each leave set, the latest counted removal per C core) is **covered** by F: it is an ancestor of F, or F or an ancestor of F is a counted first block (valid for sending and not withdrawn) whose own `basis` names it (a leave of the set, or a ref at or past that latest removal on its writer core or C core; such a ref must point at a block that exists, at or before the last block held for that core, and must not be a ref into the lister's own writer at or past the lister's own seq) and that is not written by the requirement's subject diary (a diary's own epoch never covers its own leave, ban, admission withdrawal or demotion). Where that lister sorts does not matter: naming the requirement proves its distributor saw it, so no forward `seen`, forward cut, forward `kid` or cycle the subject builds can hide it from the rotation that names it. Ancestry follows plaintext ordering edges only (writer chains, the edges of plaintext records, `basis` and `kid`), never the edges inside an envelope, so every reader of the same blocks agrees, and only the edges the final causal order honors: a forged cycle that the order breaks never makes a block a descendant of what it sorts before.
4. Every recipient writer of E (in F and in every continuation valid for sending and not withdrawn by a leave; a block valid only for reading decides which entries a reader tries to open, never the eligibility of an epoch) is entitled now ("Entitled writers"), or is an EARLIER writer of the reader's own device (in `keys.writers`, the same box key, and no longer counted or retired: a leave was written on it or withdraws its newest record). The reader's current writer is never exempt: a pending current device is `rekey-pending` until an admit lists it. This catches a loss of power that has no record (C dropped, forked or blocked, so that `community` is null, or a diary no longer live in C) and a recipient no basis names.

With no eligible epoch the reader is `rekey-pending`: nodes refuse to post, hide or rename in that group (rpc.md) until a distributor rotates. A rotation is never automatic beyond the cases below.

**Rotation.** A rotation is a new epoch: a fresh random key, a first block with `kid: null` and a `basis` listing the removals the distributor observed that its own epochs do not cover yet (oldest first), sealed to exactly the writers entitled in the distributor's fold (the creator through the root box and its other bound writers; never a pending writer of an admitted diary, whose new device waits for an admit that lists it), then continuation blocks on the same writer for recipients beyond 48. With more than 16 uncovered removals the node writes several epochs in a row on its writer, each covering up to 16; only the last is needed (the earlier ones are its ancestors). A node that may distribute rotates right after it writes a ban, an admission withdrawal, or a demotion, and on `group.rekey` (rpc.md 5.61); a node that writes `community.ban`, `community.admit` with `on: false` or `community.admin` with `on: false` also rotates, after the write in C and best effort, every held private group of C in which it distributes; a member's leave (leaving C included), or a removal written by a device that cannot distribute, leaves the group `rekey-pending` until a distributor rotates. Two distributors that rotate from the same state open two distinct epochs with distinct kids; both are readable by their recipients, and each sender picks the newest eligible one in its own causal order. Creating a private group writes, on the root, the header, then a first `group-key` sealing epoch 1 to the root box, then a sealed `group-meta` with the name.

**History on admit.** By owner default a newly admitted member reads the group's history. After it writes an `on: true` admit, an `on: true` grant (group or community admin), an unban, and on `group.rekey`, a node that may distribute in G seals every epoch key it holds (from `onKey`, "Reading") to every ENTITLED writer (the fold's `entitled`) that the epoch's `recipients` do not list yet, in continuation blocks right after that record. This also recovers an earlier delivery that failed, and an unbanned member is re-admitted and reads history like an admitted one. No pending, retired or otherwise not entitled writer receives anything. An entitled device that holds no key yet gets the current key from the next rotation as well. The browser node also runs this hand-off by itself, with no rotation, when its own diary or the group's cores grow (a creator's or admin's second device is entitled by rule 1 or 2 above; [rpc.md](rpc.md) 5.61), in a bounded number of passes each time its own diary reaches a new length, the browser unlocks during a sync, or a sync stores a group writer core here for the first time (once per writer key, so the hand-off runs after the new device's writer arrived, whichever diary it belongs to); a requested writer still receives nothing.

**What stays plaintext.** Block 0 headers (root: kind, creator diary, parent community, `member`, visibility, join, creator `box`, `at`; writers: group, diary, `member`, `box`, `rejoin`, `at`); every diary `group-join`; `group-admit`, `group-ban`, `group-leave`, `group-admin`, `group-invites` (a reader without the epoch key still gets `memberInvite`; a `group-invites` inside an envelope never opens, since only posts, hides, reactions and metas do), and every record of the community cores (`group-create` included); every `group-key` field (the recipient writer keys, the basis, the kid, `auth`, `at`); the `kid`, `nonce` and `at` of every envelope; block sizes, counts, writer lengths and the timing of every block. Sealed: the text, `replyTo` and `seen` of posts, the target of hides, the `target`, `emoji` and `seen` of reactions, the name in meta. A reaction's author (the writer's diary, from its plaintext header) and its time (the envelope's `at`) stay plaintext, as for every envelope. There is no padding, so an envelope's size tells the length of its text within a few bytes.

**Seeders, communities and reports.** Seeders admit, serve and replicate private roots and writers like public ones; the headers are plaintext and the blocks are served as they are. `/index/group-search` never lists a private group; `/index/group/<G>` answers `visibility: 'private'` with the writers and `name: null`; `/index/community/<C>` lists a private group of C as a row with `visibility: 'private'`, `name: null` and `writerCount: null`. No route ever returns a sealed block's text, and `/moderation-post` never resolves a group ref. **Sealed reports.** The token-gated `GET /moderation-group-post/<W>/<seq>` answers a `group-sealed` block with HTTP 200 `{ "sealed": true, "writer": "<W>", "diary": "<D>", "group": "<G>", "seq": <integer >= 1> }` and no other field (no text, kid, nonce or box), only when all of these hold at the seeder: W is a served writer core of G whose admitted entry names diary D (bound by D's join, `admitted.json` `groups`), the block at `seq` is held in W and is a valid `group-sealed` record, and the verified root header of G (served or kept in `groupHeaders`) says `visibility: 'private'`. Everything else keeps the route's existing answers (a plaintext post as before, anything else the generic 404). For a report whose group is private, the account service replaces the plaintext read of "Blocklist and moderation" with this structural check: the route answers `sealed: true` with the report's writer, diary, group and seq, and the `/index/group/<G>` check passes as for public groups (writer row with the reported diary, the seq rule, a verified root header, here with `visibility: 'private'`, so `root` is true). Then BOTH decisions are allowed for that report: block the diary or block the group root key. The report carries the reporter's own quote, which the panel shows labelled `cifrada: citação do denunciante` (never "Texto conferido no semeador:"), and it stays human only: it never reaches AI triage. Any failure refuses both blocks with `grupo-nao-confirmado`, and discarding always works. Reports on public groups keep the rules of "Blocklist and moderation" exactly. Every private group belongs to a community ("Communities contain groups", with the owner decision under Distributors); the community itself is always public.

**Residual risks (private groups).**

- No ratchet: within an epoch there is neither forward nor post-compromise secrecy. Whoever holds an epoch key reads every record of that epoch, past and future, wherever the ciphertext is copied.
- A device's box secret opens every epoch ever sealed to it. Stolen from a core data dir (`group-box.json` is plaintext at rest, mode 0600) or from an unlocked browser with a persistent passkey session, it reads every private group of that device until a rotation excludes the device, and all history regardless.
- A removed member keeps what it already read and the old epoch keys it holds: it can still read any old-epoch ciphertext it downloads later. Rotation protects only what is sealed afterwards, and only once a distributor rotates (`rekey-pending` blocks posting until then; with no distributor online the group stalls).
- Admins, the creator and empowered community admins are trusted with the content: they can seal keys to anyone, and a distributor can hand a recipient a wrong key, which makes those records read as sealed for that recipient (no key commitment).
- History on admit: a newly admitted member reads everything the admitting node holds.
- Context binding stops transplants (a key blob or an envelope copied to another group, epoch or writer reads as sealed), but a member can still copy its OWN older envelope again within the same writer and epoch; it reads as a second, identical record.
- Metadata stays visible (above): membership, who writes when, sizes, the moderation history and the recipient sets of each epoch. One box key per device also links that device's writers across private groups, as the public joins already do.
- A seeder or peer can withhold `group-key` blocks, which leaves readers with sealed counts; it cannot read or forge them.
- Readers built before this revision refuse private roots and show nothing of them.

### Group post media (`packages/core/lib/group-media.js`)

A group post carries photos with the v2 key set of `group-post` ("Records" above). The same key set marks a forwarded text post, whose `media` is `null` and whose `fwd` is 1. The bytes live in the media cores of the writer's diary (section 2, "Media cores"), exactly like a diary post's photos; group media never uses the blob channel of section 13. Retention therefore follows the writer core and its diary's media core declarations, never the blob pool's sweep. Each item sits at its own place: no two items of one post share a `core` and `start` (`media-invalid`).

- **Public item** (a public group): a MediaDescriptor (section 2, `validDescriptor`), still images only (`MEDIA_MIMES`), or an AudioDescriptor (section 2, `validAudioDescriptor`), a voice note.
- **Private item** (a private group, only inside a `group-sealed` inner record): exactly the keys `core`, `mime`, `start`, `blocks`, `bytes`, `sha256`, `key`, `nonce`, `plainMime`, `plainSha256`, `w`, `h`, `ms` (`PRIVATE_ITEM_KEYS`, `validPrivateItem`). `core`, `start` and `blocks` follow the MediaDescriptor rules; `mime` is `SEALED_MEDIA_MIME` = `'application/octet-stream'`; `bytes` is exactly `blocks * MEDIA_BLOCK_BYTES`; `sha256` is the SHA-256 of the stored ciphertext; `key` is the item's own secretbox key (lowercase hex64) and `nonce` its own nonce (lowercase hex48), both fresh random values per item, never an epoch key and never reused; `plainMime` is one of `MEDIA_MIMES` or `AUDIO_MIME` (`'audio/mp4'`); `plainSha256` is the SHA-256 of the plaintext bytes; for an image `w` and `h` are the image size (integers `1..MAX_MEDIA_DIMENSION`) and `ms` is `0`; for a voice note `w` and `h` are `0`, `ms` is `1..MAX_AUDIO_MS` and `blocks` is at most 49 (a `MAX_AUDIO_BYTES` plaintext with its pad and MAC). The item has no `width` or `height`: a ciphertext has no image size.
- **Voice notes are alone.** A post whose media holds a voice note (public `mime` or private `plainMime` `'audio/mp4'`) has exactly one item: two voice notes, or a voice note next to images, are `media-invalid`.
- **A plain `group-post` refuses a private item and a sealed inner refuses a public one** (`media-invalid`; items are never mixed). In a private group a plaintext post of either version is `sealed-only` as before, so private media is only ever read out of an envelope, and an envelope whose inner post carries a public item stays unread (it counts in `sealed`).
- **Sealing** (`sealMedia(plain, { plainMime, w, h, ms })` -> `{ cipher, item }`, the caller adds `core` and `start`). A voice note must be at most `MAX_AUDIO_BYTES` and pass `audioBounds` (section 2), else `sealMedia` throws. The image bytes (1 to `MAX_SEALED_MEDIA_PLAIN_BYTES` = `MAX_MEDIA_ITEM_BLOCKS * MEDIA_BLOCK_BYTES - 17`) are followed by one `0x80` byte and zeros up to `blocks * MEDIA_BLOCK_BYTES - 16`, with `blocks` the fewest that hold the image, the mark and the 16-byte MAC; then `cipher = crypto_secretbox_easy(padded, nonce, key)`. Every block of a private item is therefore full: the short last block of a plain item, which gives its exact size away, never exists, and a seeder learns only the number of blocks. The key buffer and the padded plaintext are zeroed after use (best effort, as for epoch keys).
- **Opening** (`openMedia(cipher, item)` -> image bytes or `null`, never throws) requires a valid private item, `cipher` of exactly `bytes` bytes hashing to `sha256`, a successful secretbox open under the item's key and nonce, a pad that is exactly `0x80` then zeros after at least one image byte and no wider than the last block, and image bytes hashing to `plainSha256`; for a voice note the bytes must also be at most `MAX_AUDIO_BYTES` and pass `audioBounds`, so a container with a video track relabelled as `'audio/mp4'` does not open. Its key buffer and padded plaintext are zeroed; the returned bytes are the caller's.
- **Budget.** `MAX_SEALED_INNER_BYTES` stays 12000: the worst inner record (1000 lone surrogates, each serialized as six bytes, a `replyTo` and a `seen` at the largest safe seq, four private items at their longest and the binding fields) is 8542 bytes (a post's single voice note is smaller than four items; `packages/core/test/group-media.test.js` seals the largest voice post), and its `group-sealed` block 11642 bytes, under `MAX_BLOCK_BYTES` = 16384 (`packages/core/test/group-crypto.test.js`). A card never goes with media; the worst card inner (the same text, references and binding fields, `fwd` 1, a 300-character URL, 80 and 160 four-byte code points and a private thumbnail at the largest place and sides) is 8378 bytes, and the worst public card post 7870 bytes (`packages/core/test/group.test.js`).
- **Old readers.** To apps, CLIs and seeders from before group media, the v2 key set is unknown: the post is `malformed` and skipped whole (no text-only fallback), in a public group as plaintext and in a private group after opening the envelope, which then counts in `sealed`. The fold never rejects a writer for it. `buildGroup` returns `media` (copied items) on a post only when the post has media, and `card` (a copy) only when the post has a card, so a v1 post's fold entry is unchanged.

### Data-dir files

- Member data dir: `groups.json` = `{ "v": 1, "groups": [{ "group": "<hex64>", "writer": "<hex64>", "nonce": "<hex>" }] }` (plus `kind: 'community'` on every entry whose `group` is a community: the root this device created (`writer === group`) or the CW of a joined community (`writer` is the CW key); absent means a group; the core node skips an entry with another kind, the browser rejects it; the entry of a community this device created is never evicted, the entry of a joined community can be given up like a group's once left), mode `0o600`, written through `writeJsonAtomic` (section 1 "Files and replicas"): the groups this device writes to and the corestore name `group:<nonce>` of its writer core. `group` equals `writer` for a group this device created; the root is then a writer too. An absent or odd-shaped file reads as no groups. At most `MAX_GROUPS_PER_DIARY` entries. A full list gives up its oldest entry of a group this device only joined and no longer writes to (its writer holds a leave, or the fold says the diary left); the entry of a created group is NEVER evicted, because its nonce is the only way back to the root. With no entry to give up, a new group or join is refused (`limite de grupos atingido`).
- Core node (member data dir): `data/community-create.json` = `{ "v": 1, "community": "<hex64>", "communityNonce": "<hex>", "group": "<hex64>", "groupNonce": "<hex>", "name": <name>, "join": "open" | "approval", "at": <ISO> }`, mode `0o600`, written through `writeJsonAtomic` by `community.create` after the two cores are opened (their keys are then known, nothing is appended yet) and deleted after the last step; the browser keeps the same shape in kv `communityCreate`. The steps are: the community root header, its `groups.json` entry and the diary `group-join` for C; the Geral root header (`kind: 'group'`, `community: C`, `member: C`, `visibility: 'public'`, `join: 'open'`, `name: 'Geral'`), its entry and join; a `group-create { group: Geral, seen: null }` in C's root (root seq 1); then the record is deleted. A later `community.create` call on the device while the record exists repeats the steps, each skipped when already done, through the normal mutating path (the browser's write gate syncs the diary first), and answers the recorded keys instead of creating another community. `community.info` never writes: while the record exists it answers the community as pending. Nothing retries without a call. If the first step never ran, a later `community.create` with other parameters discards the record and starts over.
- Core node (member data dir): `data/community-leave.json` = `{ "v": 2, "notes": [{ "community": "<hex64>", "writer": "<hex64>", "seq": <integer >= 1>, "digest": "<hex64>", "roots": [{ "group": "<hex64>", "cut": <integer >= 0> }] }] }`, one note per community, mode `0o600`, written through `writeJsonAtomic` and removed when the list is empty; the browser keeps the same shape in kv `communityLeave` (v 2), cleared with the rest of the group state on identity wipe. For C's creator, `community.leave` writes the note BEFORE appending the C leave: `writer` its CW, `seq` the CW length then, `digest` the `leaveDigest` of the leave it is about to append, and `roots` exactly the group roots the leave marks, each at its held length (read after the creator's own group leaves): the roots of C's catalogue the creator's diary bound that are held, unforked and non-empty (rpc.md 5.64, including `force`). After the append the block at `seq` must be that leave, else the note is kept and the call fails. The note is cleared after the `group-confirm` and the off join. A later `community.leave`, and `community.info`'s `leavePending`, act only on the leave the note names (at `note.seq` on an unforked own CW, with `note.digest`): a leave on the own CW that does not match the note is never confirmed by this device (rpc.md 5.59, 5.64).
- Core node (member data dir): `data/group-seen.json` = `{ "v": 1, "marks": [{ "group": "<hex64>", "at": <ISO>, "writer": "<hex64>", "seq": <integer >= 1> }] }`, mode `0o600`, written through `writeJsonAtomic` by `group.seen` (rpc.md 5.82) only when a mark moves forward: this device's seen mark per group, the position of the post marked: the screen's cut-off when the call carried one, else the newest readable post; always a post the node listed at the time. Newest last, at most 512 marks: a group marked again moves to the end and the least recently marked is dropped first, then counts as never seen. It holds only keys, an ISO time and a seq, never post text. A missing or malformed file reads as no marks, and an invalid mark is dropped on read. `community.info` reads it for `unread` (rpc.md 5.59) and never writes it. Local only: it is never replicated, sent to a peer or the seeder, or synced to other devices, with one exception: the browser node shares these marks with the other devices of the same passkey through the encrypted seen blob (section 14, which the service stores). No peer or seeder sees them. The browser keeps the same shape in kv `groupSeen`, cleared with the other group kv on identity reset.
- Seeder data dir: `admitted.json` gains `"groups": { "<hex64>": { "diary": "<hex64>", "group": "<hex64>", "seq": <integer >= 1>, "by"?: "<hex64>", "bound"?: true, "issuer"?: "<hex64>" } }` next to `admitted` and `media`. For a writer W the entry names its diary D, its group G and the seq of D's join block; for a root the entry is `{ diary: D0, group: G, seq, by }` with `group` equal to the key and `by` the diary whose live join caused the admission (never taken from the header; read only when hex64, so an older file without it charges the root to nobody; `issuer` is read only with `bound: true`). The file also gains `groupHeaders` (section 1). A missing `groups` reads as `{}`. The same write-ahead rule as media applies: listed before the core is opened, purged before it leaves the file, a purge failure fatal.
- Browser node: kv `groupWriters` `{ "v": 1, "entries": [{ "group", "key", "nonce", "rootNonce" }] }` (plus `kind: 'community'` on every entry whose `group` is a community: the root this device created (`writer === group`) or the CW of a joined community (`writer` is the CW key); absent means a group; the core node skips an entry with another kind, the browser rejects it; the entry of a community this device created is never evicted, the entry of a joined community can be given up like a group's once left) (one entry per group or community, at most `MAX_GROUPS_PER_DIARY`; the entry of a joined community has `rootNonce: null`; `rootNonce` is the root's nonce for a group this device created, kept apart from the current writer's so that a creator who left and rejoined can still write root-only records, else null; created groups are never evicted), kv `groupReplicas` `{ "v": 1, "keys": [...] }` (foreign group cores and former own writers) and kv `groupDiaries` `{ "v": 1, "keys": [...] }` (binding diaries of accepted foreign writers, never in `follows`) and kv `groupCores` `{ "v": 1, "keys": [...] }` (a write-ahead list of every group core key, written before a core is created and cleared on purge); no secret is ever stored in a persisted header. **Identity reset** deletes every core listed in `groupCores`, every group core named in `groupWriters` (roots with a `rootNonce` included), every core in `groupReplicas` and every replica in `groupDiaries` that is not in `follows`, then clears the lists (cores created by older builds of this branch are not in `groupCores`), so the next person on a shared device inherits no group data. Kv `groupCerts` `{ "v": 1, "entries": [["<diary hex64>", "<block 0 hex>"]] }` holds the binding certificate (block 0) of the diary behind each accepted group core, newest last, at most `MAX_GROUP_REPLICAS`, so a revocation can be judged after a restart and after the binding diary itself is gone; it is deleted with the lists above on an identity reset.
- Core node (member data dir): `group-certs.json` = `{ "v": 1, "certs": [["<diary hex64>", "<block 0 hex>"], ...] }`, mode `0o600`: the binding certificate (block 0) of the diary behind each foreign writer or root activated here, newest last, at most (groups listed x `MAX_GROUP_WRITERS`) entries (the oldest dropped), written when a foreign writer or root activates and pruned at start of entries no stored core names. An unreadable or malformed file reads as none. A revoked issuer takes a stored writer or root even when its binding diary's block 0 is not stored, by the recorded certificate (the browser keeps the same in kv `groupCerts`).
- Core node (member data dir): `group-box.json` = `{ "v": 1, "publicKey": "<hex64>", "secretKey": "<hex64>" }`, mode `0o600`: the device's X25519 box key pair for private groups ("Private groups"), created once with an exclusive create (never overwritten) by the first group operation that needs it and read only by group operations; `publicKey` must equal the key derived from `secretKey`. A malformed or mismatched file is refused (private group operations fail) and never replaced, because a new key would lose every epoch sealed to the old one. It is never printed or logged.
- Browser node: kv `groupBoxDevice` `{ "v": 1, "nonce": "<hex64>" }` holds only a random 32-byte nonce; the box key pair is derived per session as `seed = HKDF-SHA-256(ikm = prf, salt = utf8('varanda/member-group-box/hkdf/v1'), info = utf8('varanda/member-group-box/x25519/v1') || nonce, 32)` and `crypto_box_seed_keypair(seed)`. Neither the secret nor any epoch key is ever stored in the browser: epoch keys are reopened from the `group-key` records in each session, kept in memory and zero-filled on lock, sign-out and close.
- Core node (member data dir): `bound-roots.json` = `{ "v": 1, "roots": ["<hex64>"] }`, mode `0o600`, at most 256 roots (the newest kept): the roots this node saw bound by their creator's diary, by the gate that proved the creator's join or by its own fold. It is used when the creator's diary replica is gone: only a recorded root is purged for a blocked or forked creator; a root never seen bound is kept. An absent, unreadable or malformed file reads as no record.

### Seeder group admission (`packages/core/lib/seeder.js`, `group-index.js`)

Mirrors "Seeder media admission" (section 5) with the same invariants, plus the root. Nothing travels on `varanda/admission/1`: no `announce`, no `have`, no new message type for group keys.

- **Scan.** The diary scan folds `group-join` records into the diary's declared set (`groupDecls`, the newest join per writer) with the media scan's rules (raw reads from block 1, `decodeBlock` + `validRecordCopy`, never trusting a peer), and keeps the newest `on: true` join per writer as the binding (`groupBound`, which survives a later `on: false`). A creator's `on: true` join for its root key G (writer = group = G) binds G for good: a later `on: true` join naming G as a writer of another group does not unbind it, matching the fold, which counts any historical `on: true` join of the pair. The scan also records each writer's earliest `on: true` join seq per group (`groupFirst`). A live join is kept only if its writer is among the diary's `MAX_GROUP_WRITERS_PER_DIARY` earliest-bound writers for that group (a root joined by its own key takes one slot), and the per-diary cap then keeps the 64 EARLIEST live joins by diary join seq, the same way on every scan. A writer turned off by a later join keeps its slot, so leaving or withdrawing frees nothing. Joins past the per-diary cap are never admitted and never indexed.
- **Writer W for D in G.** Admitted when D is served, gated and not blocked, D's live join names W, G is not blocked, the blocklist is not held (`_blocklistHeld`: reason `blocklist-pending`) and a group slot is free (bound + reserved < `maxGroupCores`, default `maxServed`, and fewer than 512 cores of G, else `group-rejected` `group-full`). Admission also counts the writer cores bound or reserved for D: `group-full` at 8 in one group or at `MAX_GROUP_CORES_PER_DIARY` (64 x 8 = 512) in total, left ones included. A root is charged to its binding's `by`, not to the header diary (any header can name any diary): at most `MAX_GROUPS_PER_DIARY` roots and `MAX_GROUP_CORES_PER_DIARY` cores in total (writers and roots, in-flight included) per diary, else `group-full`. The charge stays after the join is withdrawn and is restored at startup from `admitted.json`. Retry waits (`GROUP_BACKOFF_MS`): a `timeout`, `no-peers` or `group-full` refusal is remembered per (D, key) with waits of 2 s, 10 s, 30 s, 60 s, 5 min, then 15 min; the wait lasts until the core's header verifies in the main store (not until admission), so a core whose block 0 is then withheld keeps growing its wait across repeated admissions (the wait is kept while the core is served awaiting its header, and cleared when the header verifies or the core is dropped definitively before it), which lets its group stop waiting for it after the retry; a success clears it. A raw connection retries only `no-peers`; each `timeout` wait arms its own retry timer, so the join is retried when the wait ends without depending on a peer's `hello` (when the wait ends while the timed-out core is still being purged, the retry runs once the purge settles); blocklist adoption reconciles every diary (bounded by the caps below). After a fetch, a `quota` verdict is remembered per (D, key, join seq): the same join is never fetched again (a quota change needs a new join). A root refused because its creator (the header's diary) is blocked is remembered in a bounded map and refused without a fetch while the creator stays blocked; adopting a new blocklist clears the map, so lifting the block lets the same join admit the root. A late (pre-fetch) `group-full` backs off like `timeout` (a refused writer may wait up to the backoff after space frees). Admissions in flight are capped at `GROUP_INFLIGHT_MAX` = 32 overall (option `maxGroupInflight`) and `GROUP_INFLIGHT_PER_DIARY` = 2; a diary over the cap waits in `_groupDeferred`, a FIFO of diaries: a freed slot serves the head diary, which is queued again at the tail if it still waits. `/index/group`, `/index/groups/<D>` and `/index/group-search` answer `complete: false` while a join waits for an admission slot, while a join holds a first `timeout` backoff (not yet retried) or a `group-full` backoff caused by the seeder's own capacity (`maxGroupCores`, `maxGroupWriters`): the seeder must not claim a final page while it refuses a writer for capacity. A join whose retry past the first wait also timed out is still retried but stops counting (pages, search, a diary's group list), until an admission succeeds, even under a new join seq. Per-diary budget refusals (`MAX_GROUP_CORES_PER_DIARY`, `MAX_GROUP_WRITERS_PER_DIARY`, `MAX_GROUPS_PER_DIARY`) never hold pages back. A refusal is logged once per change of (key, reason, join seq), not per attempt. A wait left by a first `timeout` is cleared when the join ends in a final refusal (header or size verdict, no longer wanted), so the group's answers become complete again. Admission order: (1) still wanted; (2) a quarantine fetch of block 0 attached to the sockets of ALL connected peers, `GATE_TIMEOUT_MS` (no peer -> `no-peers`, timeout -> `timeout`); (3) verdict: block 0 a valid `group-writer` (`header-invalid`) naming G and D (`wrong-group`, `wrong-diary`) and the fetched length / bytes within the per-core limits (`quota`); (4) `admitted.json.groups[W] = { diary, group, seq }` persisted BEFORE the open, with media's rollback (`admission-failed`, a stored core purged first, a purge failure fatal); (5) after the open: still wanted, `fork === 0`, within quota; (6) served: live download, fork watcher, quota on every append, `swarm.join(discoveryKey)`, log `{ event: 'group-admitted', key, diary, group, length }`. An admitted group core whose block 0 never reaches the main store is dropped and purged after the gate timeout (15 s, constructor option `groupHeaderTimeoutMs`) with `group-rejected` reason `timeout`, and its join backs off; after a timeout on the retry it no longer holds the group's pages or global search incomplete.
- **Root G.** Admitted by the same steps as a writer (including quarantine, persistence before the open and the held blocklist) when its block 0 is a valid `group-header` and some served, gated, unblocked diary has a live join for G (the creator's join with `writer == group` is the normal path). **Root binding.** The header's diary counts as the root's creator (and as support) only when that diary is served, not dropped, not blocked and has its own `on: true` `group-join` for writer = G. Otherwise the root is supported only by real member joins, and `/index/group/<G>` answers `creator: null` (also during the startup window before the creator's scan). **Support:** a served root stays while at least one served, unblocked diary keeps it: the creator if it bound the root, a diary with a bound writer in it, or a live join. Withdrawing every join does not drop a root that still has a bound writer. At startup a root does not survive just because its creator's diary is kept: decisions that find a root unsupported are deferred until the startup scans of the served diaries end, and it is judged after them; an unsupported root is then dropped with reason `unbound`. **Blocked or revoked creator:** a served root is purged for a blocked diary, or one whose certificate issuer is revoked, only when that diary BOUND the root (`bound: true`: its header diary has its own `on: true` join for writer = G). Reason `blocked` (also during the startup window), the root stays refused at admission and at startup while its creator stays gone, even while members support the group. A root whose header merely names a blocked diary that never bound it has no creator and stays while members support it. A creator that is both blocked and from a revoked issuer has that issuer recorded too (`revokedCreators`, and `goneRoots` for its bound roots), and a stored issuer is never replaced by null: lifting only the block keeps its bound roots refused, also after a restart, while the issuer stays revoked. A creator dropped or refused for a revoked issuer is remembered with that issuer (`revokedCreators`, a moderation outcome, also across restarts) and judged against the CURRENT revoked-issuer set: adopting a list that lifts the revocation lifts it. An announce of a revoked-issuer certificate is not remembered at all, not even in memory: only a diary's own block-0 verdict (refused at startup, or dropped as revoked-issuer while served) feeds `revokedCreators`, a `goneRoots` issuer and the refusal of a root at admission for a revoked creator, so a root whose creator was never served on this seeder is not refused for a revoked issuer (the root only names that diary). A repeated revoked announce of the same key from one peer counts as a bad announce; the first does not. A purged root is remembered with its creator and, for a revocation, its issuer (`goneRoots`) and refused again while that creator stays gone; lifting the block or revocation lets the same join admit it, and a root admitted again after that keeps `bound`. A diary admitted with a verifying certificate clears its stale revoked entries (`revokedCreators` and the `goneRoots` entries naming it with an issuer). A root whose header names a currently blocked diary, or a diary whose known issuer is revoked, is refused at admission even without a purge record (a root admitted before the block stays until its binding says otherwise). Roots bound before this version get `bound` only after their creator's next scan. A root its creator bound stays listed on `/index/group/<G>` (and in the writer pages) under that creator diary while members keep it served after the creator is dropped for a reason other than moderation (quota, fork, seeder full), also after a restart; `header.diary` stays `null` then, so no creator authority is shown. Members' own writers stay under the rules above. **Issuer kept with the binding.** A bound root's binding keeps the issuer of its creator's block-0 certificate (`issuer`, taken at the creator's scan).
