When that issuer is revoked under the current list and the creator is not served, the bound root is purged with reason `revoked-issuer` and remembered like any revoked-issuer drop (`revokedCreators`, `goneRoots`), also at startup and also when the creator left first for another reason, such as its quota, so a member that keeps the root served cannot outlast the revocation. **Header fields kept.** While any core of group G is served or being admitted, the seeder keeps the verified header fields of its root (`kind`, `visibility`, `join`, `community`, `member`, and `name` for a public root) in `groupHeaders`, also after the root was purged or refused or its block 0 is withheld, and drops them once no core of G is left or G is blocked; past the bound the record is not kept. A persisted record without `community` and `member` is a protocol v1 leftover and is dropped at start.
- **Protocol v2 admission and scan.** A header whose validator returns `legacy` is `header-invalid` (no new wire reason), and the startup re-gate purges every stored v1 root and writer with that reason. A group root must carry a hex64 `community`; the seeder never checks a writer's `member` or any authorization (the fold does). CWs are admitted like any writer and take part in the writer pages. For a served community root the index folds `group-meta`, `group-admin` and `group-create` from the root only (creates are indexed from C's root alone; a CW is not scanned for them, `group-create` being `root-only` there). A group root is not scanned.
  **Denial bounds.** `revokedCreators` and `goneRoots` never evict an entry still in force: when a map reaches 4096 entries the lifted ones go first, and `admitted.json` may hold up to 8192 entries in each. While 4096 or more denials are in force in either map, or after a denial found no room (past 8192), the seeder refuses every group root with the group-rejected reason `denials-full` and logs `{ event: 'warning', reason: 'denials-full' }`, so a purged root cannot return for lack of memory.
- **Drops** (purge before un-listing, runtime order of media (e), each logs `{ event: 'group-rejected', key, diary, group, reason }`): the diary is dropped or blocked (D's writers go with it, with `blocked` or the diary's own reason; the root stays while supported), the root key is in the blocklist snapshot (`group-blocked`, for the root and every writer of that group; the blocklist apply loop also walks the group map, because it otherwise walks only `_served`), a fork (`fork`), the quota (`quota`), an unsupported root (`unbound`), a root whose creator (the header diary, when it bound the root) is blocked or has a revoked issuer (`blocked`) or a failed header re-check (`header-invalid`). NEVER on a `group-leave` and never on a join turned off.
- **Retention.** Index withdrawal and membership end are distinct, as defined above. Admitted writer and root cores stay served and listed across either event (the index only marks the writer `left`), preserving the moderation records of departed members.
- **Startup** re-gates listed group cores like listed media cores (a core whose binding is gone, or whose root is blocked, is purged first), applying the same per-diary counts to persisted bindings; for bindings written before the cap that count follows iteration order, not earliest join. `seed list` prints the group cores indented under their diary (`grupo <key> (grupo <group8>) <length> blocos <bytes> bytes`), `seed drop` purges them as auxiliaries, and `stats` adds `groupCores`.
- **Local re-gate.** Block 0 in the main store must be byte-identical to the quarantined block and a valid header naming the same keys, else `fork`. A diary (or group core) admitted whose block 0 is still missing in the main store spends the diary's group stall budget (`GROUP_SCAN_STALL_MS`, above) and is dropped with reason `timeout` after the gate timeout (option `regateTimeoutMs`); the key returns only through a new announce and the full quarantine gate.
- **Purges and concurrent replication.** While the seeder purges a stored key (diary, media or group core), a peer asking for that key over replication is not attached (no core is opened for it, and the connection stays up), and a purge first waits for the peer opens of that key already in flight. This relies on a replication hook of the exactly pinned corestore (`_attachMaybe`): `Seeder` start refuses to run when the installed corestore lacks it, and a test guards the pin.

### Groups channel (`'varanda/groups/1'`, `packages/core/lib/groups-net.js`)

Hands a member the writer set of a group so it can open and verify the writers itself. Writer sets are public (joins are public), so there is no proof. It travels on the SAME protomux as replication (`socket.noiseStream.userData`), attached right after the notices channel and before the admission `hello`. Messages are UTF-8 JSON (compact-encoding `c.buffer`), exact keys, lowercase hex, 1..`MAX_GROUPS_MESSAGE = 16384` bytes (checked on the raw buffer before `JSON.parse`). All numeric fields below are safe integers; messages and items are plain objects.

- `writers` request, member to seeder: exactly `{ "type": "writers", "group": <64 hex>, "after": null | <64 hex>, "limit": <integer 1..64> }`. `after` is the exclusive cursor (the last writer key of the previous page).
- `writers` answer, seeder to member: exactly `{ "type": "writers", "group": <64 hex>, "items": [{ "key": <64 hex>, "diary": <64 hex>, "length": <integer >= 0>, "seq": <integer >= 1> }], "next": null | <64 hex>, "incomplete": <boolean> }`, 0..64 items, each with exactly the four shown keys. `seq` is the seq of the join block in `diary`. The channel reply carries no `signed`; only the `/index/group` rows and the in-process writer pages do. `next` is the continuation cursor, or null. The index supplies ascending key order; channel validation does not check item ordering, uniqueness, cursor consistency or whether the reply exceeds the requested limit. The member treats every item as a hint and re-verifies it (below); `incomplete` reports an unfinished scan or a failed page lookup.
- **Bad messages.** An empty or oversized frame, a JSON or shape failure, a second request while the seeder is answering, an unsolicited answer or one for another group, and any message of the other role. More than `MAX_BAD_GROUPS = 5` bad messages destroys the socket (peer-dropped reason `'groups'`). Message rate: the blocklist channel's token bucket (`MESSAGE_BURST = 512`, `MESSAGE_RATE = 2` per second), charged for every message before parsing; exhausted tokens immediately drop the peer (reason `'message-rate'`). **Member pacing** keeps a member under that refill: the core sync paces writer-page requests with a token bucket of 1 request per second per connection (burst `MESSAGE_BURST / 8`), and re-pages a group in a long-running run after 60 s or sooner when the seeder sent a `have` or a known writer of the group grew; the browser spaces requests 1 s apart per link, across every group and re-page. Group-heavy syncs are slower for it (about 1 s per page request).
- **Member handle.** `requestWriters({ group, after, limit })`, one request in flight, `GROUPS_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS = 10000` (10 s) from the call; timeout rejects the request and closes the channel terminally, leaving the socket connected. A late reply cannot satisfy another request on that handle. Rejection of the channel open by an older peer resolves `supported: false` without destroying the socket. A failed or invalid seeder page lookup replies with empty items, `next: null`, `incomplete: true`. The admission bytes are unchanged.
- **Community pages.** The shapes above are unchanged in v2. A page for a community lists its root and its CWs (every item under its diary at the binding join seq), like the page of a group; a CW is a writer of C.

### Index routes (`apps/cli/lib/seed-ws.js`, `GET|HEAD /index/...`)

Added to section 7 "Seeder index route", under the same caps, Host check, 405 and generic 404 policy, no logging and no echo of paths or keys. Answers are hints; a reader verifies them from the cores.

- `/index/group/<G>[/<after>]`, regex `^\/index\/group\/([0-9a-f]{64})(?:\/([0-9a-f]{64}))?$` (`INDEX_GROUP_RE`) -> `{ kind, visibility, join, name, creator, community, attached, rootServed, writers: [{ key, diary, length, signed, seq, left }], next, indexing, complete }`, at most 64 writers per page, ascending by key. The header fields are `null` until the seeder verified the root's header; `rootServed` is true only while the root core itself is served. While any writer of G is served, the verified header fields (`groupHeaders`) keep being answered after the root was purged, refused or withheld, with `rootServed` false and `creator` null; a page with a served writer and no root header at all is never complete. An older seeder sends no `rootServed`. `creator` is `null` unless the header's diary bound the root ("Root binding" above); `name` is `null` for a private group; `length` is the core's contiguous length (the downloaded prefix); `signed` is the writer core's signed length, never below `length` (an older seeder sends no `signed`), and a gap between the two never makes a page incomplete; `left` is true when the diary withdrew the join (the writer stays served). `community` is the header's parent (hex64; `null` until the header is verified, and for a community root) and `attached` is true when the index has the parent as a community root served here and C's catalogue hints attach G (below); both are hints; `attached` is false while G's root itself is not served here (a purged root keeps `community` from `groupHeaders` but not the creator diary the catalogue check needs). For a community root `writers` lists its CWs.
- `/index/groups/<D>`, regex `^\/index\/groups\/([0-9a-f]{64})$` (`INDEX_GROUPS_RE`) -> `{ groups: [{ group, writer, seq, left }], indexing, complete }`: ONE row per group of the diary, live ones first (then by group key), at most 64 (`MAX_GROUPS_PER_DIARY`); the row names the diary's newest live writer, and `left` is true only when every writer of D in that group has left.
- `/index/group-search/<segment>`, the segment as in `INDEX_SEARCH_RE` -> `{ groups: [{ group, name, writerCount }], more, indexing, complete }`, at most 20 PUBLIC groups whose name contains the words (`search-text` tokens); `name` is the ROOT HEADER's name only and `writerCount` counts the diaries with a writer that has not left. A later `group-meta` rename is not reflected (known limitation). Private groups never appear. A query under 2 characters is the generic 404.
- **Catalogue hints.** For a community root the index keeps `admins` (the root's `group-admin`, the last record of a diary wins) and `created(G)`, the `group-create` records of C's root, at most 32 distinct groups, the first ones it holds. `attached(C, G)` holds when G's verified header names C and G's header diary is C's header diary (the creator) and has a create for G. `members` of a community is the number of diaries with a served CW in C that have not left, plus the bound creator (the root). Hints only: a node verifies with `buildCommunity` and `buildGroup`.
- `/index/community/<C>`, regex `^\/index\/community\/([0-9a-f]{64})$` (`INDEX_COMMUNITY_RE`) -> `{ name, join, creator, members, groups, next, indexing, complete }`. `name` is the public community's last `group-meta` name, else its header name (`null` after a `group-meta` that clears it); `join` is the last `group-meta`'s `join`, else the header's (`open`, `approval` or `null`); `creator` is a hex64 or `null` (null until the creator diary bound the root); `members` is an integer >= 0; `groups` lists at most 32 attached groups served here and not blocked, in key order, each row exactly `{ group, visibility, join, name, writerCount }` (`name` and `writerCount` are `null` for a private group); `next` is `null` or the last key of the list. A blocked root, a root that is not a community and a malformed path are the generic 404. While the root is being admitted the answer is an empty list with `complete: false`. An unbound community root lists no groups and is never `complete`. `complete` also waits while any group root named by a `group-create` of C (attached or not) and held here is in admission or has an unverified header, and while a served CW of C is in admission; a failed read retries with backoff (2, 10, 30, 60 s) and keeps the answers incomplete. Hints only.
- `/index/community/<C>/groups/<cursor>`, regex `^\/index\/community\/([0-9a-f]{64})\/groups\/([0-9a-f]{64})$` (`INDEX_COMMUNITY_GROUPS_RE`) -> `{ groups, next, indexing, complete }`: the rows of the same shape with a key greater than the cursor, in key order.
- `/index/group-search` lists group roots only, never community roots.
- 404 policy: a blocked group root, a diary that is not served or is blocked (`/index/groups`), and every malformed path are the same 404 `não encontrado`; an unknown group answers 200 with no writers.
- **Completeness.** `indexing` is true while the seeder is not ready. `complete` is false while the seeder is not ready or is closing, while a served diary's join scan is behind (the scan stops at the contiguous length and the signed length advances before the blocks arrive, so it compares both; a scan caught up with its local prefix while the signed length is ahead (a gap) spends a cumulative stall budget of the diary: `GROUP_SCAN_STALL_MS` = 10 s, option `groupScanStallMs`, of time caught up inside gaps within `GROUP_STALL_WINDOW_MS` = 10 min, monotonic clock; the budget is kept per diary key across a drop and a re-serve within that window, at most 2 x `maxServed` budgets are kept, and a served diary's budget is never evicted; progress inside a gap and gaps closing do not refill it, so a diary that trickles blocks or reopens gaps stops making OTHER groups' `/index/group` pages and group search answers incomplete once the budget is spent, until 10 min after it ran out, while that diary's own `/index/groups/<D>` list stays incomplete while behind), while a group core is in admission (a pending admission), and while a served group core awaits its local header check (answers hide it until then). `/index/groups/<D>` is also incomplete while one of D's live joins names a pending core; the search is complete only when no group in the seeder is unsettled.

### Member sync (core `runSync`, browser `sync.js`)

- **Own writers.** Opened by nonce (`group:<nonce>` in the core node; storage core `group/<hex>` in the browser) and pushed to the seeders. In the browser they are replicated only on the verified link and are never listed in `hello` or `have`. A push is confirmed by the seeder HOLDING the core, not by the blocks uploaded (the seeder may take them on its quarantine channel and store none): in the browser only with the replication peer's evidence (its contiguous length at no less than the local length) plus an agreeing page. **Known limitation (accepted):** a browser that shares a group writer key with another instance of the same profile (a second tab, or a device restored from older storage) trusts the length its one seeder reports. A malicious seeder that hides blocks of that writer it holds can make the device append at a stale length and fork its own writer or root. No proof can tell a seeder that hides blocks from one that never got them, and the browser has no other peer. An honest seeder that is merely behind is covered by `group-behind`. The browser's own-core write gate uses the larger of the page length and what the seeder's replication peer holds, never reports an own group core in sync (no group write is allowed) until the seeder's replication peer on that link has synced, even when the page lists the core, takes the seeder's length as the larger of the page and the peer, and refuses to append while the peer is ahead (`group-behind { key, length, theirs }`, surfaced by the session), so a device restored behind the seeder cannot fork its own writer. Browser revocation: the binding certificate (block 0) of each accepted core is kept in kv `groupCerts`, so a revocation is judged after a restart and after the binding diary is gone; active cores are re-checked by the revocation sweep, and a binding diary's block 0 is judged when it arrives. The join history used for the per-diary cap is cached per run. The session's write gate needs the reported synced length to be at least the core's current local length. Browser kv `boundRoots` = `{ v: 1, keys }` (at most `MAX_GROUP_REPLICAS`) is written when a gate proves a root's creator join, read at start, removed when the core is purged and cleared on reset, so a root bound in an earlier run goes with its blocked or revoked creator even if the join block was never stored; the revocation sweep judges a binding replica without block 0 by the certificate kept at the gate (kv `groupCerts`).
- **Binding diary comparison (core node).** The binding diary stored in the main store is compared with the blocks the gate read (block 0 and the join block). A mismatch is a hard error `bloco divergente: <key8> (diário de grupo)`, and the diary and the writers that depend on it are purged. A stored writer whose binding diary diverges while its gate runs is purged as `not-in-log`, and nothing is charged to the seeder. A stored forked diary is swept at startup, and a forked creator's bound root is purged (a root counts as bound when the stored creator replica proves it OR `bound-roots.json` names it, section "Data-dir files"). A binding diary that diverged is refused for the rest of the run: later writers of that diary are refused as not-in-log. In a continuous run, a binding diary first read through the quarantine re-attaches its main-store replica once the quarantine read ends, so a later writer of that diary is gated in the same run. On a cold start the blocked-root sweep runs before blocked diaries are purged. A writer that fails before activation leaves the admission table, and a stored core always reaches its stored-header check.
- **Foreign writers.** The member joins the discovery keys of the roots and known writers, asks a verified seeder for the writer pages (groups channel), and gates each unknown writer before opening a replica: (1) quarantine fetch of block 0 (a valid writer header, or root header, naming G and a diary D), (2) D's block 0 and the join block (`on: true`, naming this writer) through quarantine on the same sockets, (3) a valid certificate (`certForKey`, revoked issuers included) and neither D nor G blocked, (4) the signed length within the quota. Only then the core is opened in the store with a fork watcher and the quota. Failures emit `{ event: 'group-skipped', key, reason }`. A page marked `incomplete` is gated but never completes the pass nor confirms an own core. **Binding diaries** of accepted foreign writers are stored in full: the core node keeps them in its main store, the browser as replicas listed in kv `groupDiaries`, never in `follows`; they feed the join evidence and the author names. Scale cost: a core node stores every binding diary entirely. A group operation reads only that group's cores (block 0 first, a full read only for writers of that group) and only the diaries that bind them (own diary, creator, the authors of its writers).
- **Bounds on hints.** At most `MAX_GROUP_WRITERS_PER_DIARY` accepted cores per (group, diary), and a pass keeps at most `MAX_GROUP_WRITERS` items; in the browser the per-diary cap keeps the earliest proven joins: only live accepted cores count, and when (group, diary) is full a listing is gated as a contender (no slot, at most 2) only if a held writer has a higher proven join seq; after its gate proves a lower seq, the held writer with the highest proven seq fails `quota`. The per-diary cap reserves a slot synchronously during the gate. At run start, stored cores beyond `MAX_GROUP_WRITERS_PER_DIARY` per (group, diary) stay at rest (not opened), the earliest proven joins live, a bound root always live; before an eviction, each writer's join seq is refined to its earliest `on: true` join from an open binding diary. Writers stored by an earlier run prove their join seq at run start from their stored binding diary (the earliest `on: true` join naming them; each run start reads every stored binding diary of stored writers once); a writer whose binding diary is not stored stays unproven and is never evicted, and roots are never evicted. A browser listed key with no core is paged as a probe and unlisted after one complete page pass with no root for it; a core discarded during its local re-check is unlisted from kv `groupReplicas`; a writer evicted by the per-diary cap while still in its local re-check (not yet listed) is purged, a listed writer evicted is kept. In the core node the per-diary cap is applied at gate time to every entry, against the diary its own block 0 names and the join seq the binding diary proved (never a listed diary or seq): when (group, diary) already holds `MAX_GROUP_WRITERS_PER_DIARY` verified slots (active, or past the gate and opening), the entry gets the slot only if its proven seq is lower than the highest active one, whose writer then fails as `quota` (remembered as proven later while that diary stays full; a writer stored in this run is purged, while one stored before the run is never the victim, "Round 10, core node" below); otherwise the entry itself is refused as `quota` and remembered the same way. The group root is one of its creator's writers and is never evicted. A foreign writer stopped for quota with its blocks kept holds its (group, diary) slot for the rest of the run (except a stored writer refused for the cap, "Round 10, core node" below), and at run start the stored writer cores of listed groups count toward their (group, diary) cap. A listing is gated at most `MAX_GROUP_WRITERS_PER_DIARY` + `MAX_CAP_CONTENDERS` (2) at a time per hinted (group, diary), and the admission table is bounded; an item that finds either bound full only waits (the group is rescanned when a slot frees) and nothing is decided about it. An advertised length above the per-core log quota never changes a held writer: the item is ignored, reported once per unheld key as `quota`, and charged as a bad hint to that seeder without remembering the triple, so an honest identical listing is still gated (core node); the browser fails only an unheld entry, checks a held core's quota on its own length, and sets no once-run goal after an over-quota listing of a held core. Nothing is fetched in either case. Writer targets (the lengths used for `--once` goals) are recorded only for writers held in the bounded admission table, clamped to the log quota, capped in total, and deleted with the entry or on purge.
- **Bad hints and bounds on memory (core node).** Four rules. (1) What keeps a writer out for longer than one gate (refused, a per-diary cap slot, an eviction, remembered as proven later or cap-refused) comes only from data this node verified: the writer's own block 0 (its group and diary) and the binding diary's join block, never from a page item's diary or seq alone. A never-admitted, unstored writer is refused for the run (dropped from the admission table and remembered in a bounded set) only on such a verdict: a blocked writer key or group, an invalid header, a header naming another group, a fork or over-quota of the writer core itself; `header-invalid` and `wrong-group` are charged as a bad hint to the listing seeder. (2) A verdict that depends only on the page item (diary, seq, length: a blocked, forked or revoked hinted diary, the hinted diary's quota, `wrong-diary`, `unbound`, not in log) discards only that hint: it emits `group-skipped`, removes the entry without purging, remembers the hint per (group, key, diary, seq) so the same listing from any seeder is not gated again (a different listing is), charges the listing seeder once per hint, and rescans the group skipping that seeder for that key, so other seeders' listings of the writer (and of the other writers of that diary) are gated now. The group is part of the hint, so a key disproved under one group never judges an honest listing under another. The writer's header is checked against the hinted diary before anything about that diary is believed, and a join seq at or past the diary's known length is a disproof (`unbound`), not a wait. (3) Every run-wide or per-seeder memory (disproved hints, refused writers, proven-later and cap-refused marks, timeouts, charges) is bounded: in long-running mode it also expires after `pageRefreshMs`, and when full the oldest entry is evicted, so remembering never stops. A seeder charged `MAX_BAD_HINTS` = 32 bad hints or `MAX_TIMEOUT_HINTS` = 16 timeouts of unstored writers (each hint counts once) is cut off: closed for good for the rest of a `--once` run, suspended for `pageRefreshMs` in long-running mode and then paged again with a clean slate. In `--once` the gate timeout of an unaccepted writer always drops the entry, and `MAX_GATE_TIMEOUTS` = 2 timeouts per (seeder, key) refuse that key from that seeder for the run (one seeder's listings never drop a writer another seeder lists); in long-running mode a timeout of an unstored writer is charged to the listing seeder and the writer is rescanned from the other seeders. A timeout is transient, never a disproof: the quarantine path waits for a join block past the length learned with block 0 until the gate deadline, and a group gate naming the device's own diary likewise waits for block 0 and the join block until the gate deadline (a missing block is a `timeout`, never a disproved hint). An item refused because the admission table or the gates in flight are full schedules a rescan of its group when a slot frees. A writer another seeder lists correctly stays a goal until gated, and an accepted writer evicted by a proven-earlier contender is still condemned if its binding diary later forks. A writer core already stored binds by the diary named in its own validated block 0, not by the hint. (4) A core already stored that is listed under a group it does not write in leaves the admission table (its blocks stay), so its honest listing under its own group proceeds. In the browser at most `MAX_GATE_HINTS` = 4 distinct listings re-gate a key per run, a key that failed `header-invalid` is never gated again, each seeder is charged once per distinct bad hint (at `MAX_BAD_HINTS` its writer pages are no longer requested for the run), and a gate failure caused only by the page item (`wrong-group`, `wrong-diary`, `unbound`, `header-invalid`, `diary-unavailable`) on a core never stored is remembered per (group, diary, join seq). A core stored or listed before the current browser run is never purged by a cap eviction, an abandoned gate or a regate discarded at the end of a run; nothing never accepted is purged. Round 7, core node: block 0 in the main store is compared with the gated block 0 before any quota decision (a mismatch purges); writers evicted by the per-diary cap are remembered for the whole run, and a diary fork also purges stored cores whose block 0 names that diary; each writer's DHT discovery session is released when it stops; an accepted writer that later fails leaves the admission table (remembered so it is not gated again); a suspended seeder is not paged for the rest of a pass; the blocked-root sweep purges a stored root only when its header diary bound it. Round 7, browser: the binding certificate is checked again against the current blocklist right before storing a core and before activating it, revocation sweeps include cores still being gated, a stored root is purged for a blocked or revoked header diary only when that diary bound it, and keys un-listed during a run no longer count as stored before the run.
- **Round 10, core node.** (1) A revoked issuer takes a stored writer or root even when its binding diary's block 0 is not stored, by the certificate recorded in `group-certs.json` (section "Data-dir files"). (2) At start, while the snapshot revokes any issuer, a stored foreign writer with no binding evidence (no stored block 0 and no record) is purged (`group-skipped` reason `not-in-log`); roots are not covered by this check. (3) A diverged binding diary also purges the stored writers it binds that left the admission table, and a root it bound (`not-in-log`). (4) The per-diary cap uses each writer's earliest proven `on: true` join when the binding diary is held locally (as the fold and the browser do), otherwise the gated seq. (5) The per-diary cap converges on the fold's earliest proven joins across runs: a writer stored in an earlier run (active or only held on disk) gives its slot to a newcomer with an earlier proven join, and its blocks are purged (`group-skipped` reason `quota`); a newcomer whose join is not earlier is still refused (`quota`); a holder whose join this node has not proved never gives way. The browser also evicts a stored victim but keeps its data (`evictionPurges`), where the core purges it. A stored writer's gate counts the other keys held on disk; a stored writer refused for the cap keeps its blocks but holds no slot for the run; excess left by an earlier run is not purged, and it never grows. (6) A listing refused for this node's own verdict (its blocklist, a fork or divergence it saw in this run) is not charged to the seeder and is not gated again while the verdict holds.
- **Round 13, core node.** (1) A new writer key of a group is refused (`group-skipped` reason `quota`) while the group's foreign writer cores in the admission table plus those stored on disk, including writers kept after a quota stop, reach `MAX_GROUP_WRITERS` (`runSync` option `maxGroupWriters`; the group root is no writer slot and is never refused by this cap); a stored writer keeps its place, and a refused key is gated again once a place frees. (2) Block-0 divergences of group writers become one hard-error line `bloco 0 divergente em escritores de grupo: <n> (<up to 3 key8>)` (the result is still not ok), and a run keeps at most 32 hard-error strings.
- **Membership and purges.** A fetched historical join block proves binding only. Current membership comes from the writer fold above. A stored writer core is purged only for an ACCEPTED writer (one that passed the gate): when D is blocked or its issuer is revoked, on a fork, or when its local block 0 differs from the gated one. A bad hint from a seeder never purges an unaccepted core. A blocked group root fails and purges every foreign core of that group with reason `blocked`, in the core node and in the browser alike; the member's own cores are never touched, and what is stored can still be read locally until it is purged.
- **Binding diary release (core node).** A group writer's binding diary is downloaded and kept in the main store only while something needs it: a follow holds it, a writer in the admission table names it, or a stored foreign writer or root names it (the census is rebuilt from disk at start). When a writer is purged, retires or is evicted and nothing else needs its diary, the node stops downloading the diary and purges it from the main store. A writer stopped for quota keeps its diary (and its blocks). At run start a binding diary recorded in `group-certs.json` that no stored core names and that no follow holds is purged. At core node start, a member of any group also purges an unfollowed, unlisted diary unless a stored group writer or root names it or `group-certs.json` records it; that cleanup always rebuilds the census first, also when `groups.json` and `group-certs.json` are empty, and never purges a group core this node can write. A `group-join` block that its author deleted (section 2 "Clearing deleted bytes") withdraws the writer it bound: the core node drops the writer from the run (`group-skipped` reason `withdrawn`) without purging its blocks, and the group goal stops waiting for it. At browser node start, a stored replica that a stored group core binds (or that the run holds as a binding diary) stays indexed and is never purged by the unfollowed-replica cleanup.
- **Completion goals** (`--once` and the browser's once runs). Each group goal runs once per run and is met when (a) every writer page of every joined group was fetched (not `incomplete`) or the channel timed out, (b) every admitted foreign writer, and the diary that binds it up to its join, reached the length its seeder advertised, exactly like followed replicas (on the core node a core admitted through quarantine counts only after the block 0 its main store received was compared with the gated one), and (c) own writers were pushed to the length the seeder advertises for them (browser: confirmed by `group-pushed { key, length }`); all bounded by the run's deadline. Against a seeder without `varanda/groups/1` (groups unsupported) the browser's own group pushes are not once-run goals, so a once run finishes. The goal of a binding diary is met at its present length (section 2 "Clearing deleted bytes"), so a deleted post no longer keeps a once run open, and join blocks after a cleared hole are read. Group writer and root cores keep their own tracking, since no delete records exist for them. Goals G1 to G3 of section 6 are unchanged. The browser's write gate for a group write confirms the own writer's length, not only the diary. For each community the goals are C's root, every page of its CWs, and the binding diaries, as for groups.
- **Parent paging.** For every local group root held the node reads its header's `community`, and for every local community its key. For each community C it pages C on the groups channel (the root and its CWs), gates each CW like a group writer (a valid header with `group` = C and `member: null`, then the binding diary's block 0 and join block), stores the CWs and their binding diaries, and pages only the roots of C's catalogue (the `/index/community/<C>` rows are a hint, the fold verifies) so that `community.info` can name them. Each root is judged by its own block 0 under a role fixed for the run: a key joined as a group must be a `group` root, a key reached as a parent must be a `community` root, and a `v: 1` header is `header-invalid`; the `group-skipped` reason `not-community` (a parent that is not a community root; the node's own verdict, the seeder is not charged) is kept. A run holds at most groups x 512 plus communities x 512 cores. A node folding a group passes `buildGroup` the parent's input (C's root, its CWs and their joins). The core sync emits `community-parent { key, group }` (`key` the community root C, `group` the group G) the first time a held group root names a community this node does not already hold, and `group-parent { key: C, group: G }` once per local group whose held root names C, held or not (the session uses it to fan `groups.changed` out from C's growth to its groups; the CLI prints neither).
- **Diary-bound roots (browser).** Besides kv `groupWriters` and kv `groupReplicas`, the browser sync pages every root its own diary binds now as a replica: the newest `group-join` per writer still `on: true`, at most `MAX_GROUP_WRITERS_PER_DIARY` earliest writers per group, the first 64 groups by live join seq (`diaryGroupKeys`, the seeder's live-join rule). It reads them at run start from the stored diary and again whenever the own diary is caught up with the seeder, so a second device of the same passkey (same diary, fresh storage) rebuilds its communities and groups from the diary. A once run does not end while that read of the own length is pending: the roots it adopts are paged before the run's goals are met. Such a root's writers are gated like any replica's, a catalogue child the diary joined is not paged root only (one already paged root only in the run is paged again in full), and a community among them is paged in full with its catalogue. Its own header decides its kind: another root's header naming it as its community never makes it a parent key, so it is never refused as `not-community`. A root no join binds now (every device left) is not adopted, a blocked key never is, and a key with no root at the seeder is dropped by the probe rule and not asked for again in that run. Nothing is appended, no writer is created and nothing is listed in kv for an adopted key until its block 0 is stored (the gate then lists it in kv `groupReplicas`). The core node is unchanged: its diary is per device (section 1), so another device never shares it.
- **Automatic device enrolment (browser).** After a sync, a device of the same passkey enrols by itself in each diary-bound root it holds and has no kv `groupWriters` entry for, while the fold says the diary is a `member` there and the diary binds fewer than `MAX_GROUP_WRITERS_PER_DIARY` - 2 (6) writers in it: the last two slots, which the seeder never releases, are spent only by an explicit join. It writes what `community.join` or `group.join` writes (a new writer naming the diary's latest leave, and the diary's public `group-join`), communities first, then a group only when this device's community writer is admitted in C (an open community or its creator). Geral is planned like any group of C with its own checks: a diary that left Geral is not put back, and Geral's last two slots stay reserved too. A kv writer the diary never bound (an interrupted join) is bound again, reusing that writer only when this device derives its key from the entry's nonce; otherwise the root is skipped (`foreign`), and an explicit join writes a new writer instead. Only a writer this device derived is ever reused by a join. A root that is left, banned, requested, blocked, unheld, an orphan, not joined by the own diary (an opened link) or not foldable yet (no pending join) is skipped, and nothing is admitted or granted automatically. Rules in [rpc.md](rpc.md) 5.63. The core node is unchanged (its diary is per device, section 1).
- **Legacy sweep.** At the start of a run, before any fold, the node purges every `v: 1` group core. Core node: for each `groups.json` entry it opens the core by nonce and runs the block 0 validator; a `legacy` verdict purges the core and drops the entry (the one case in which a created entry is not kept), and every other stored core whose block 0 is a v1 `group-header` or `group-writer` is purged too. Browser: the same over kv `groupWriters`, `groupReplicas` and `groupCores`. Each purge emits `group-skipped { key, reason: 'legacy' }` (in the sync's JSON events like every other `group-skipped`; the session turns it into a warning). Diary `group-join` records of old roots stay (a diary is append-only); seeders refuse those roots as `header-invalid`.
- **Events.** Core sync: `group-downloaded { key, group, length }`, printed as `grupo <key8> <length>`, and `group-skipped { key, reason }`. Browser sync: `groups-changed { group }` (coalesced per group over 50 ms of growth, also when no diary grew), `group-skipped { key, group, reason }`, `group-pushed { key, length }`, `group-writer { group, writer }` (a writer core, never a root, stored for the first time in the run; it arms the session's device upkeep). Both sessions emit the RPC event `groups.changed { group }` ([rpc.md](rpc.md), section 6), the core session also from sync growth (`group-downloaded`).

### Blocklist and moderation

A blocked D (or one whose issuer is revoked) removes every writer of D at readers and seeders, and the own diary is never purged. A blocked root key removes the whole group: readers purge every foreign core of it (reason `blocked`), and a seeder also drops a served root whose creator (a header diary that bound it) is blocked or has a revoked issuer ("Seeder group admission"). The account service intake refuses a `varanda-report-v2` whose group equals the target, or whose writer equals the target (`pedido-invalido`); a writer equal to the group key is accepted (the creator writes through the root core). A report whose group key is the moderator's own diary is listed for that moderator and can be discarded; only a block of kind group on it is refused (HTTP 404 `nao-encontrado`). A block decision on a group report, of either kind (account or group), also requires (except for a sealed message of a private group, confirmed structurally as in "Private groups", "Sealed reports") the account service to read the reported message through the token-gated `GET /moderation-group-post/<W>/<seq>` (section 7, "Seeder moderation-group-post route") and to match its group and diary with the report (`texto` is returned as signed and shown with `displayText`; the panel shows it as "Texto conferido no semeador:" and keeps both blocks disabled otherwise). The account service therefore needs the same `VARANDA_TRIAGE_TOKEN` as the seeder for any group-report block, even with AI triage off: without it both blocks are refused. A malformed `VARANDA_TRIAGE_TOKEN` is a startup error even with AI off. A group index still being built (`indexing` or not `complete`) is a transient answer, never a cached not-found. Besides that, a block decision is made only after the account service confirms, on the seeder's public `GET /index/group/<G>` route (paged with the `/<after>` cursor, at most 8 pages, one 5 s deadline, never retried), that the reported writer is listed there with the reported diary and holds the reported seq (writer row length greater than seq; block 0 is the header), else the definite reason `sem-mensagem` only when seq is at or past the row's `signed` length (a row without `signed`, from an older seeder, keeps the `length` rule; a malformed `signed` is `recusado`). When seq falls in a hole (`length` <= seq < `signed`) the check still reads the message through `/moderation-group-post` and, if it is not handed out, answers transiently (not cached). A writer too short for the reported seq on a partial index (`indexing` or not `complete`) is a transient answer (refused, never cached), not a definite one; those index reads carry `Authorization: Bearer <VARANDA_TRIAGE_TOKEN>` when it is configured, so the seeder charges them to its moderation budget (section 7); any failure, including an unreachable or unconfigured seeder, refuses the decision with HTTP 409 `grupo-nao-confirmado` and nothing is decided. Discarding always works. Every block (kind account) of a report WITHOUT a group likewise needs the local seeder to confirm a served diary (`GET <seeder>/index/groups/<D>` answers 200), whether or not the target has a linked account (a group's creator could link the root key as an "account"); otherwise HTTP 409 `alvo-nao-confirmado`, also when the seeder is unreachable or not configured (fail closed: only discarding works). The panel looks the key up for every report without a group. Its lookup is `GET /account/mod/api/reports/<id>/alvo` (`{ confirmed }` or `{ confirmed: false, reason }`), in the same 30 per minute window as `/grupo`. The panel's confirmation lookup (`GET /account/mod/api/reports/<id>/grupo`) has its own per-session budget of 30 per minute, separate from the general session window; `group-check.js` caches definite answers (`ok`, `ausente`, `nao-membro`, `sem-mensagem`) for 60 s, shares concurrent checks of the same (group, writer, diary, seq) and runs at most 2 seeder reads at once (32 waiting). Blocked and pending keys keep their `grupo` label from a scan over all stored reports. A group message is reported with the reporter's signed statement `varanda-report-v2`, which names the writer and the group (`docs/rpc.md`, `report.sign`); the quote is the reporter's own text and proves nothing about the group (section 3 "Reports"). Group reports never enter AI triage and `/moderation-post` never resolves a group ref. The check result and the `/grupo` answer carry `root`, true when the index carries a verified root header (`kind`; the root may be served or, with `rootServed` false, purged or withheld while writers of G stay served). A block of kind account is decided when the seeder confirms the writer row (writer to diary) and hands out the message, even with `root` false; a block of kind group also needs `root` true (HTTP 409 `grupo-nao-confirmado` otherwise), and the panel then hides 'Bloquear grupo' and explains that only the account can be blocked. The remedy is blocking D (all its writers) or, with `root` true, blocking the group root key.

### Residual risks (groups)

- Membership is public: every `group-join` is readable by anyone holding the diary, like follows. The posts of a public group are public text at members and seeders.
- `at` is claimed by the author and breaks ties among causally ready records; it does not establish admin rights or replace cuts.
- The creator's device is the only holder of the root core. If it is lost, nobody can appoint admins or rename the group: v1 has no transfer of the creator role. Existing admins keep acting.
- A hidden record, a ban and a leave never delete anything: the blocks stay in the writer cores and at seeders, to be read by anyone who has them.
- Old readers skip `group-join` (section 2); a group is invisible to them.
- The seeder is trusted only for hints (writer sets, lengths, completeness); every block is verified from the cores.
- Private groups add their own risks ("Private groups", Residual risks).
- Departed-device backdating: a community leave keeps the member's earlier messages in every group, by the leave's position in the group's causal order. A departed device can still append backdated POSTS on its old G writer below that position and they read as earlier posts; a community ban or a hide covers them. The alternative of hiding everything from seq 0 contradicts the owner's rule that earlier messages stay. Backdated AUTHORITY is closed by owner decision (2026-11-01): NO AUTHORITY AFTER LEAVING. A G writer whose CW C retired carries no authority at all (moderation, admits, grants, hides, sending keys), for its earlier records too, and so does G's root while G's creator is not a member of C; a departed admin's bans, admissions and nominations stop counting, and C's admins re-issue what they need. Any bound a departing device writes about its own past could be filled in later by that device (forward references, a second leave), so no such bound is trusted. Posts keep the leave rule above (earlier messages stay). `community.leave` still writes a `group-leave` on the device's live writer in every held group of C, which cuts that device's later records by its own seq. The root's records are also withdrawn while the creator is not a member of C and has no counted leave there (an admission withdrawal of a group creator hides its root posts too). Consequences: when a group's creator leaves C, the group's admin nominations and renames from its root stop counting, and a private group waits for another distributor (a community admin) to rotate. `community.leave` writes that `group-leave` in every held group of C where the device has a live writer, in the same call, so an honest leaver keeps its earlier moderation.
- The root gate is at diary level: the root of a group is live while its creator's diary is a member of C. A creator who leaves C and rejoins regains the charter of its groups.
- Community ban and unban are final-state rules: unbanning restores the posts written during the ban.
- The catalogue cap is 32 distinct groups in C's root order, and only C's creator creates groups, so there is no race; a group beyond the cap is an orphan and nobody can write in it.
- A person who leaves C from one device loses its moderation power in every group of C, past records included.
- Community metadata is public: name, members, admins and the group list are readable by anyone holding the cores. There is no private community in this version, because joins, bans and recipient sets stay public by construction; approval is not confidentiality.
- Old clients see nothing of v2: they refuse the v2 headers and skip `group-join` records for them. Data in v1 cores is purged at the next start of an updated node ("Member sync", Legacy sweep).
- `fwd` is asserted by the sender alone; there is no original-author field, so "Encaminhada" proves nothing about where the content came from, and a sender can mark an original message as forwarded or forward without marking.

## 11. Mensagens diretas (`packages/core/lib/dm.js`, `dm-net.js`, `dm-pool.js`, `dm-stamp-net.js`, `dm-postage.js`, `dm-fold.js`)

Conversas: private 1:1 text, and photos (inner v2, below), between two diaries. The text is end-to-end encrypted, and the pairing (who writes to whom) is hidden from the public, from the seeder's persisted files and from its specified logs.

### Threat model

- Content is end to end: only the holder of the recipient's box secret key (and the sender, through its self copy) reads a message.
- Neither the public nor a seeder (its persisted files, its specified log events) can pair a sender diary with a recipient diary. `crypto_box_seal` uses an ephemeral sender key and does not name the recipient, so a seeder holding every `dm-key` still cannot test which drop opens under which key without the secret keys.
- There is ONE global pool per seeder: no per-recipient address and no bucket. Any address that is a function of a published key (a hash of the box key, of the diary key, a truncated tag) is joinable by a seeder that stores every diary, so every member downloads the whole pool and tries every drop.
- Why not a private group (section 10, "Private groups"): a group publishes its membership (`group-join` is public, section 2) and its recipient sets, so a 1:1 group would name both parties. Private groups protect content, not the social graph; direct messages must protect both.

### Key: the `dm-key` record and its derivation

- Record (section 2): `{ "type": "dm-key", "box": "<hex64>", "gen": <integer >= 1>, "at": <ISO> }`, published once per IDENTITY. Every device of an identity derives the same pair, so a second device publishes nothing new.
- Derivation (browser identity, `packages/web-member/lib/passkey.js`): `seed = HKDF-SHA-256(ikm = prf, salt = utf8(DM_KEY_HKDF_SALT), info = utf8(DM_KEY_HKDF_INFO) || uint32be(gen), 32)`, then `crypto_box_seed_keypair(seed)`, with `DM_KEY_HKDF_SALT = 'varanda/member-dm-box/hkdf/v1'` and `DM_KEY_HKDF_INFO = 'varanda/member-dm-box/x25519/v1'`. No device nonce: one box key per identity. Rotation appends a `dm-key` with `gen + 1`. `gen` is at most `MAX_DM_KEY_GEN = 4294967295` (2^32 - 1, the uint32be range), and a larger `gen` is `malformed`: otherwise a record no device can derive would outrank every real key forever.
- Readers take the valid `dm-key` with the highest `gen`, ties by highest seq. A `dm-key` is a key advertisement only: never a message, an accept, a block or a peer key. It produces no push candidate and no notice row.
- Refresh: a reader that does not hold the diary scans it newest first in bounded windows (`packages/web-member/lib/session.js`). So that scan always finds the key, the owner's node re-appends the same `{ box, gen }` when its newest valid `dm-key` is not within the newest `DM_KEY_REFRESH_BLOCKS = 256` blocks (checked in `dm.enable`, once after unlock, and once after the next catch-up of a continuous sync when one of those checks ran on a diary that had not caught up). A replica that holds part of a diary and no `dm-key` in what it read falls back to that scan; it never answers "no key".

### Inner plaintext (exactly `DM_INNER_BYTES = 5120` bytes)

`uint16be jsonLen || JSON bytes || 64-byte Ed25519 signature || zero padding`. The JSON is `JSON.stringify` of an object with exactly these keys in this order: `v` (1), `kind` (`'msg' | 'ctl'`), `id` (hex32, 16 random bytes), `from` (hex64 diary key), `to` (hex64 diary key), `replyTo` (`null` | hex32), `text` (string), `op` (`null | 'accepted' | 'declined' | 'blocked' | 'unblocked' | 'deleted'`), `peer` (`null` | hex64), `at` (canonical ISO), `cert` (`null` | the sender's membership certificate object, section 3, v1 or v2, with `cert.member === from`). The signature is by `from` over `utf8(DM_SIG_PREFIX) || the exact JSON bytes`, `DM_SIG_PREFIX = 'varanda-dm-v1'` followed by LF; the raw text is never LF-joined into the payload.

- `kind: 'msg'`: `op` and `peer` are `null`; `text` is 1..`DM_MAX_TEXT = 1000` code points (the post limit), well formed (no lone surrogate) and without control characters except LF (the post rule); `from !== to`.
- `kind: 'ctl'`: `text` is `''`, `replyTo` is `null`, `op` and `peer` are set and `from === to`. A ctl envelope is sealed only to the own box: it syncs the folder state between the devices of one identity and never reaches the peer.
- Sender binding: a `msg` must carry `cert`, the sender's association-signed membership certificate. Without it one member could write from any number of throwaway Ed25519 keys and escape blocks, reports and the association blocklist. A `ctl` (own key only) may leave `cert` `null`.
- `buildInner(fields, signKeyPair)` (`fields.cert` optional; a `msg` built without one never opens) throws `TypeError('mensagem longa demais')` when `jsonLen + 66 > DM_INNER_BYTES` and `TypeError('mensagem inválida')` on any shape failure, including a `cert` that is not a certificate shape or names another member. 5120 was sized for the worst case: 1000 code points of 4-byte characters, the JSON with two hex64 keys, the largest certificate (a v2 one, about 700 bytes; about 5035 bytes in all), the 64-byte signature and the 2-byte length.
- `openInner(buf, ownKey, { association, revokedIssuers? })` -> body or `null`, never throws. A `msg` is refused when `cert` is missing, when `certForKey(cert, from, association, { revokedIssuers })` fails (another association, a bad signature, a revoked issuer) or when `cert.member !== from`; no association given means no `msg` opens. Also checked: the length, the zero padding, the exact key order (a reordered or re-escaped JSON is refused even when signed), the per-kind rules and the signature with `from` as the Ed25519 public key (browser diaries are raw Ed25519 keys, section 3; a core-node manifest key never verifies, which is intended). A `msg` with `to === ownKey` returns `mine: false`; a `msg` with `from === ownKey` is the sender's self copy and returns `mine: true` (the signature by `from` proves it); a `ctl` requires `from === to === ownKey`; anything else is `null`. The plaintext buffer is zeroed after reading.

### Inner v2: media, link cards and forwarded text

Same frame, same `DM_INNER_BYTES`, `DM_DROP_BYTES`, `DM_VERSION` (1) and magic `VDM1`; the pool record size does not change. Only the JSON inside differs.

- v2 is ONE exact key list, in this order: `v` (2), `kind`, `id`, `from`, `to`, `replyTo`, `text`, `media`, `card`, `fwd`, `op`, `peer`, `at`, `cert` (`INNER_KEYS_V2`). `media`, `card` and `fwd` are always present. The signature is by `from` over `utf8(DM_SIG_PREFIX_V2) || the exact JSON bytes`, `DM_SIG_PREFIX_V2 = 'varanda-dm-v2'` followed by LF, so a v1 signature never verifies a v2 body and the reverse.
- A text-only message with `fwd` 0, and every `ctl`, stay v1 (`INNER_KEYS`, `v` 1), so readers from before v2 still open them. A text-only message with `fwd` 1, or with a link card, is v2 and carries `media: null`. `buildInner` writes v2 only when the body needs it (`media` not `null`, `card` not `null` or `fwd` not 0; `dmInnerVersion` of `@varanda/rpc`), and `openInner` refuses a v2 body that does not need it (`media` `null` with `card` `null` and `fwd` 0), so each message has one encoding. A v2 body is always `kind: 'msg'`.
- `media`: an array of 1 to `DM_MEDIA_MAX = 4` items, distinct `id`s. Each item has exactly the keys `id`, `key`, `nonce`, `chunks`, `bytes`, `sha256`, `mime`, `w`, `h`, `ms`, written in that order (`DM_MEDIA_KEYS`). The first six are the blob descriptor of section 13 and pass `validateBlobDescriptor` (hex64 `id`, `key` and `sha256` of the CIPHERTEXT, hex48 `nonce`, `chunks` 1..257 and exactly `ceil(bytes / 32768)`). `mime` is one of `image/jpeg`, `image/png`, `image/webp`, `image/gif` (`DM_MEDIA_MIMES`), with `w` and `h` integers 1..16384 and `ms` 0, or `DM_AUDIO_MIME` = `audio/mp4` (a voice note), with `w` and `h` 0, `ms` an integer `1..DM_AUDIO_MAX_MS` = 120000 and `bytes` at most `DM_AUDIO_MAX_BYTES` = 1572864 + 16 (the plaintext cap of section 2 plus the blob MAC). A voice note is the only item of its message: two voice notes, or one next to images, refuse the inner. No other audio mime and no video mime is accepted. The opened blob of a voice note must pass `audioBounds` (section 2), with the item's `ms` within `AUDIO_MS_TOLERANCE` of the container's, before it is shown or played. Any other key, value or order refuses the whole inner.
- `card` is `null` or a LinkCard (section 2, "Link cards"), written in `CARD_KEYS` order with its `image` in `DM_MEDIA_KEYS` order (`DM_CARD_KEYS` in `dm-budget.js`; a reordered card is refused even when signed). Its `image` is `null` or a media item as above with `mime` `'image/jpeg'`, `ms` 0 and `bytes` at most 32768 (the sealed blob, MAC included; `validDmCard`). A message with media carries `card: null`; a card goes with text only, and its text follows the text-only rules. `fwd` is independent: a forwarded card is `fwd` 1. Any other `card` value refuses the inner. `fwd` is the integer 0 or 1; any other value, including a boolean, refuses the inner.
- `text` may be `''` only in v2 when `media` is a list (a message with media and no caption). A text-only forward keeps the v1 text rules (1 to 1000 code points, well formed, no control characters except LF). A v1 `msg` with empty text stays refused.
- The canonical JSON, the key lists and the text budget live in `@varanda/rpc` (`packages/rpc/lib/dm-budget.js`: `canonicalDmInnerJson`, `DM_INNER_KEYS`, `DM_INNER_KEYS_V2`, `DM_MEDIA_KEYS`, `dmTextBudget`, `dmTextBytes`), which core signs with, so a UI that cannot load core measures the very bytes the node signs. `dmTextBudget(fields)` (also `textBudget` in `packages/core/lib/dm.js`) measures the complete canonical JSON with the given `cert`, `media`, `card` and `replyTo` (fixed-length stand-ins for `id`, `from`, `to` and `at` when absent) and returns how many text bytes, counted as `dmTextBytes` (UTF-8 inside the JSON string, escapes included), still fit under `DM_INNER_BYTES - 66`. `buildInner` stays the authority and still throws `TypeError('mensagem longa demais')`. Measured with the largest v2 certificate, a `replyTo` and the largest items (257 chunks, a ten-character `mime`, 16384 x 16384): the v1 worst case of 1000 four-byte characters is 5035 bytes; four items with empty text are 2497 bytes; the text budget is 3697 bytes with one item and 2623 with four, so 1000 four-byte characters with one item do not fit. With the largest voice note (49 chunks, `ms` 120000) the budget is 3702 bytes: a voice note with empty text fits, a voice note with 1000 four-byte characters does not. The card counts in the budget: with the largest v2 certificate, a `replyTo`, `fwd` 1 and the worst card (a 300-character URL, 80 and 160 four-byte code points, a thumbnail item at 16384 x 16384) the inner without text is 2738 bytes and the text budget 2382 bytes (`packages/core/test/dm.test.js`).
- `openInner` accepts v1 with `INNER_KEYS`, v2 with `INNER_KEYS_V2`, v3 with `INNER_KEYS_V3` ("Inner v3" below) or v4 with `INNER_KEYS_V4` ("Inner v4" below), each through the exact key check and the canonical order of its own list (items included: an item with reordered keys, or a number spelled another way, is refused even when signed). A v2 `msg` has the same certificate, recipient and self copy rules as v1. A v2 or v3 body carries `media`, `card` and `fwd`; a v1 body carries none of them, and a v4 body carries the reaction keys of "Inner v4" instead (`kind` `'react'`, `target`, `emoji`, `rev`, no `text`, `media`, `card` or `fwd`).
- Old readers: to apps, CLIs and nodes from before v2, a v2 JSON has an unknown key set, so `openInner` returns `null` and the drop is skipped like any drop that does not open. There is no text fallback: the caption is lost to that reader with the photo, the same precedent as a post with media (section 2, "Old readers"). A forwarded text message is v2 too, so those readers skip it whole.

### Inner v3: a Página's digest-signed inner

Same size as v1 and v2 (`DM_INNER_BYTES`); the frame carries an expiry after the signature. Only Páginas (section 12, "Conversas como página") write v3, and a page writes nothing else: every `msg` and every `ctl` with `from` = the page key is v3, a text-only one included. A person's node never writes v3 (`buildInner` never does; `dmInnerVersion` never answers 3).

- Keys: exactly the v2 list (`INNER_KEYS_V3 = INNER_KEYS_V2`, `DM_INNER_KEYS_V3` in `dm-budget.js`), `v` 3, written by the same canonical JSON. `canonicalDmInnerJson` takes the v2 list when `v` is 2 or 3; `dmTextBudget({ v: 3, ... })` measures a v3 body.
- Frame: `uint16be jsonLen || json || signature (64) || uint64be expiresAt (8) || zero padding`, `DM_INNER_BYTES` in all, so `jsonLen + 2 + 64 + 8 <= DM_INNER_BYTES` (`innerDigest` throws `mensagem longa demais` otherwise; `dmTextBudget({ v: 3 })` counts the 8 bytes, `DM_INNER_V3_EXPIRY_BYTES`). `expiresAt` is ms since the epoch, a safe integer > 0.
- Signature: Ed25519 by `from` over `utf8(DM_V3_PREFIX) || sha256(json) || uint64be(expiresAt)`, `DM_V3_PREFIX = 'varanda-dm-v3'` followed by LF, `json` the exact JSON bytes. A device that holds no page key builds the body and the holder of the key signs 54 bytes only: it never sees the message. A v1 or v2 signature never verifies a v3 body and the reverse, and an `expiresAt` in the frame other than the signed one fails the signature.
- Expiry: the holder of the key picks `expiresAt` itself, never the caller: the account service signs with `expiresAt = now + DM_V3_TTL_MS` (`DM_V3_TTL_MS = 600000`, 10 minutes; `dm-sign`, docs/rpc.md 5.84), and the device sends it unchanged in `dm.pageSend` (docs/rpc.md 5.114).
- Receiver stale rule: every receiver of a v3 drop (a member's personal pull of a message from a page, and an admin device's pull of the page box) drops it at filing time when `pageInnerStale(body, filedAt)`: `filedAt > expiresAt + DM_V3_STALE_MS`, `DM_V3_STALE_MS = DM_RETENTION_MS + 86400000` (8 days), `filedAt` the receiver's clock. Why: `dm-sign` signs any digest an admin sends, so an admin could stockpile page signatures and send them as the page after its removal. A drop that was put while its signature was fresh lives at most `DM_RETENTION_MS` (7 days) in the pool, so it is filed before `expiresAt + DM_RETENTION_MS`; the extra day is clock slack between the service and the receiver. A drop filed later was put after its signature expired: a replay of a stockpiled signature. The rule bounds that window, it does not close it (section 12, "Riscos residuais (Conversas como página)").
- `msg`: every combination is valid. Text only (`media` `null`, `card` `null`, `fwd` 0, text by the v1 rules), and every v2 combination with the v2 rules for `media`, `card`, `fwd` and empty text with media. The v2 rule that refuses a body that does not need v2 does not apply: v3 is the page's only encoding. Certificate, recipient and self copy rules are those of v1 (a `msg` carries the page's certificate).
- `ctl`: `media` `null`, `card` `null`, `fwd` 0, `text` `''`, `replyTo` `null`, `op` and `peer` set, `from === to`. It carries a page folder op between the admin devices (section 12).
- API (`packages/core/lib/dm.js`): `innerDigest(fields)` -> `{ json, digest, body }` (fields as `buildInner`, with `from` hex64 and no key; `v` is always 3; throws `mensagem longa demais` when `json + 74 > DM_INNER_BYTES` and `mensagem inválida` on a shape failure); `signDigest(digestHex, secretKey, expiresAt)` -> hex128, the only bytes a key holder signs through this API (it adds the prefix and the expiry itself; `TypeError` on a bad digest, key or expiry); `assembleInner(json, sigHex, expiresAt)` -> the `DM_INNER_BYTES` frame; `pageInnerStale(body, filedAtMs)` -> boolean (always `false` for v1 and v2). `openInner` reads `expiresAt` from the 8 bytes after the signature (refused unless a safe integer > 0, and refused when any padding byte after it is not zero), checks the signature over the bytes above, applies every other check unchanged and returns the body with `expiresAt` (a v1 or v2 body has no such property). Constants `DM_V3_TTL_MS` and `DM_V3_STALE_MS` are exported.
- Old readers: a reader from before v3 sees an unknown version, `openInner` returns `null` and the drop is skipped like any drop that does not open.

### Inner v4: a reaction

Same frame as v1 and v2 (`DM_INNER_BYTES`, no expiry). Either person of a conversation reacts to any message of it, their own or the other's, with one emoji, or removes the reaction.

- Keys: exactly `v` (4), `kind` (`'react'`), `id` (hex32, the reaction's own id), `from`, `to` (hex64, `from !== to`), `target`, `emoji`, `rev`, `at` (canonical ISO), `cert`, in this order (`INNER_KEYS_V4`; `DM_INNER_KEYS_V4` in `dm-budget.js`, re-exported by `@varanda/rpc`). `target` is exactly `{ from, id }` in that order (`DM_TARGET_KEYS`): the sender (hex64) and id (hex32) of the message reacted to, since ids are unique per sender; `target.from` is `from` or `to`. `emoji` is one emoji by `isReaction` of `@varanda/rpc` (section 10, the `group-react` rule) or `''`, which removes. `rev` is an integer `1..DM_REACT_REV_MAX` (1000000). Any other key, value or order refuses the inner.
- Signature: Ed25519 by `from` over `utf8(DM_SIG_PREFIX_V4) || the exact JSON bytes`, `DM_SIG_PREFIX_V4 = 'varanda-dm-v4'` followed by LF. The domain is its own, so a reaction signature never verifies as a v1, v2 or v3 message and no message signature verifies as a reaction.
- Sender binding as a `msg`: a reaction must carry `cert`, the sender's membership certificate of the association, with `cert.member === from`. `openInner` returns it with `mine: false` when `to` is the own key and `mine: true` for the self copy; anything else is `null`. `buildInner({ kind: 'react', to, target, emoji, rev, id?, at?, cert? }, signKeyPair)` writes v4.
- Size: with the largest certificate, the longest emoji `isReaction` accepts (30 UTF-8 bytes) and `rev` 1000000 the frame takes 1140 of the 5120 bytes (`packages/rpc/test/dm-budget.test.js`).
- Fold: `dm-fold.js` "Local semantics" below.
- Old readers: a reader from before v4 checks `v` 1, 2 or 3 and its `validBody` rejects any other `v` in its final `else`, so `openInner` returns `null` and the drop is skipped like any drop that does not open: nothing is shown, nothing is counted as unread or in a cap. A v4 body relabelled 1, 2 or 3 fails those key sets and the signature.

### Seal and drop frame

- `sealInner(inner, recipientBoxPkHex)` = `crypto_box_seal`, exactly `DM_SEALED_BYTES = DM_INNER_BYTES + 48 = 5168` bytes (`DM_SEAL_BYTES = 48` of overhead). A weak box key (`isWeakBoxKey`) is refused. `openSealed(sealed, boxKeyPair)` -> inner or `null`, zeroing on failure. Sizes are fixed, so the sealed size says nothing about the text.
- Drop frame, exactly `DM_DROP_BYTES = 333 + DM_SEALED_BYTES = 5501` bytes: offset 0 magic ASCII `DM_MAGIC = 'VDM1'` (4), 4 version `DM_VERSION = 1` (1), 5 `dropId` 32 random bytes, 37 `kid` 8 bytes, 45 `rsaSig` 256 bytes big-endian, 301 `token` 32 bytes, 333 `sealed` 5168 bytes. No diary key, no box key, no timestamp, no length field, no bucket. `encodeDrop` / `decodeDrop` (copies, `null` on a wrong size, magic or version).
- `dropId` is not the inner `id`: every copy of a message (the recipient copy, the self copy) has its own `dropId` and its own stamp.

### Stamps (`packages/core/lib/dm-postage.js`)

A drop carries a stamp: a Chaum blind RSA signature (RSA-2048, `e = 65537`) over a random 32-byte token. The seeder signs blinded values, so it cannot link a stamp it issued to the drop that spends it.

- Public key `{ v: 1, kid: <hex16>, n: <decimal string>, e: <decimal string> }`; `kid` = the first 8 bytes of BLAKE2b-256(utf8(canonical decimal `n`)).
- `representative(token)` = MGF1-SHA-256(utf8(`DM_STAMP_FDH_DOMAIN`) || token) of the modulus length (256 bytes) as an integer, `DM_STAMP_FDH_DOMAIN = 'varanda/dm-stamp-fdh/v1'`. If it is `>= n` the token is unusable and the client draws another (`isUsableToken`). The representative covers the token only, so stamps can be issued ahead of time and spent later.
- `blind(rep, n, e)` -> `{ blinded, r }` with `r` in `1..n-1` and `gcd(r, n) = 1`; `sign(blinded, n, d)` returns `null` unless `1 <= blinded < n` and `gcd(blinded, n) = 1`; `unblind(sig, r, n)`; `verify(token, sig, pub)` holds when `sig^e mod n` equals the representative. Wire integers are canonical base64 of a fixed 256-byte big-endian value.
- Wallet: the client keeps `DM_STAMP_WALLET = 20` unspent stamps (sealed at rest, "Local storage" below), topped up during sync when below `DM_STAMP_WALLET_LOW = 10`. The seeder issues at most `DM_STAMPS_PER_DAY = 200` stamps per diary per Brasília day. A key announced on the stamp link becomes the wallet's current key only once a stamp issued under it verifies; a top-up that yields no stamp leaves the wallet as it was, so an announced key alone never makes the client drop unspent stamps.
- Issuance happens on a SEPARATE short-lived link: a fresh ephemeral Noise key, a hello with an empty inventory and a diary proof (like the notices v2 link of `packages/web-member/lib/notices.js`), never on the sync socket and never on the anonymous dm link.
- BigInt modexp is not constant-time: a timing leak on `d` lets someone forge stamps (spam), never decrypt a drop.
- Postage key rotation (`apps/cli/lib/dm-postage-file.js`): the seeder keeps `<seeder dir>/dm-postage.json` = `{ "v": 2, "keys": [{ "kid", "n", "e", "d", "createdAt" }] }` (mode 0600, decimal strings, newest first, at most 2 keys: the current one and the previous one). A key older than 7 days is replaced by a new RSA-2048 key, and a key older than 14 days is dropped. Any other version or shape fails closed (`dm-postage inválido`) and is never overwritten (there is no migration: a key never gets a later `createdAt` than it had). The file is created only when a peer first opens the stamp channel or puts a drop, never at start.
- Stamps are issued under the current key only (the `key` answer is its public key, never `d`); a drop is accepted when its `kid` is the current or the previous key, never an older one. A stamp channel keeps the key it opened with: once that key is no longer current it signs nothing (`ruim`, no allowance spent), and the seeder's 60 s key refresh closes it; the member reopens it on the same socket and gets the current key.
- Invariant: the key acceptance window (14 days after a key is created) is at most the pool's spent-token retention (`tokenRetentionMs`, 14 days after the drop arrived, on its own clock, `dm-pool.js`). A token is spent no earlier than its key was created, so its hash is kept at least until its key stops being accepted, and a spent token can never be spent again.

### Anonymous channel `'varanda/dm/1'` (`packages/core/lib/dm-net.js`)

`DM_PROTOCOL = 'varanda/dm/1'`, on a protomux channel with no challenge and no proof: no diary key ever travels. UTF-8 JSON with exact keys, at most `DM_MAX_MESSAGE = 65536` bytes per message; a frame over the limit, a JSON or shape failure, an unsolicited reply or a second request in flight is a bad message, and more than 5 destroy the socket.

- member to seeder: `{ "type": "put", "drop": <canonical base64 of exactly DM_DROP_BYTES> }` and `{ "type": "page", "after": <safe integer >= 0, exclusive seq cursor>, "limit": <1..DM_PAGE_MAX> }`.
- seeder to member: `{ "type": "ack", "ok": <boolean>, "reason": null | "cheio" | "invalido" | "ritmo" }` and `{ "type": "items", "items": [{ "seq": <integer>, "drop": <base64> }], "next": <integer> | null, "oldest": <integer> }`, ordered by seq ascending.
- `DM_PAGE_MAX = 8`: a page of 8 base64 drops (7336 characters each) inside JSON stays under `DM_MAX_MESSAGE` (about 59 KB); `lib/dm.js` refuses to load if it did not. A pull reads at most `DM_PULL_BUDGET_BYTES = 8388608` bytes per run. Per channel: 8 puts per 60 s; pages 20 per second, burst 40; one request in flight per direction.

### Stamp channel `'varanda/dm-stamp/1'` (`packages/core/lib/dm-stamp-net.js`)

`DM_STAMP_PROTOCOL = 'varanda/dm-stamp/1'`, attached by the seeder on every peer socket and opened by the member only on the separate stamp link above. A proof comes first, as in notices v2: the payload `'varanda-dm-stamp-v1'` + LF + nonce hex + LF + the member's own Noise public key hex + LF + the diary key hex, signed by the diary key and checked against this connection's remote Noise key (a notices v2 proof never verifies: different prefix). Then:

- `{ "type": "key" }` -> `{ "type": "key", "v": 1, "kid", "n", "e" }`.
- `{ "type": "issue", "blinded": <canonical base64, 256 bytes> }` -> `{ "type": "stamp", "kid", "sig": <base64> }`, or `{ "type": "stamp", "error": "teto" }` past the daily budget, or `{ "type": "stamp", "error": "ruim" }` when `sign` refuses. At most 10 issues per 10 s per channel.
- Before computing the blind signature, the seeder checks, without changing anything, that the proved diary could get a stamp now: not blocked, admitted or served, under its daily allowance and within the row cap. If not, it answers `teto` without signing. After 3 `teto` answers in a row on one channel, the seeder drops the peer.
- The seeder stores nothing about the blinded input and logs only `{ event: 'dm-stamp', reason: 'ok' | 'teto' | 'ruim' }`, with no key.
- Blob stamp kind (media chunks, section 13). The same channel also issues a second kind of stamp, only when the seeder has a blob key: `{ "type": "key", "kind": "blob" }` -> `{ "type": "key", "kind": "blob", "v": 1, "kid", "n", "e" }` and `{ "type": "issue", "blinded": <canonical base64, 256 bytes>, "kind": "blob" }` -> `{ "type": "stamp", "kid", "sig" }` or `{ "type": "stamp", "error": "teto" | "ruim" }`. The DM messages above are unchanged and the kind `'dm'` never travels (a message with `kind` other than `'blob'` is a bad message). A seeder without a blob key (and any seeder from before this kind) treats the blob messages as bad.
- The blob kind is separate from the DM kind in every part: its own RSA-2048 key and `kid` (a blob key whose `kid` is a DM key is refused), its own rotation file `<seeder dir>/blob-postage.json` (same shape, 7-day rotation, 14-day acceptance and 2 keys as `dm-postage.json`; errors read `blob-postage inválido`), its own FDH domain `BLOB_STAMP_FDH_DOMAIN = 'varanda/blob-stamp-fdh/v1'` (the DM domain is `varanda/dm-stamp-fdh/v1`), and its own budget `BLOB_STAMPS_PER_DAY = 1024` per diary per Brasília day, kept in `<seeder dir>/blob-stamps.json` (the `dm-stamps.json` v2 shape and rules: admitted or served and not blocked, 3 recent days, row cap, `teto` without signing when over). A blob issue never spends the DM allowance and a DM issue never spends the blob one. Issues of the blob kind have their own bucket per channel (`BLOB_ISSUE_BURST = 32`, `BLOB_ISSUE_RATE = 4` per second); the DM bucket stays at 10 per 10 s. The 3 `teto` answers in a row that drop the peer count both kinds together.
- DM verification only uses DM keys and the DM domain, and blob verification only blob keys and the blob domain: a stamp of one kind never pays for the other (the seeder refuses a `kid` that belongs to the other kind's keys). The seeder logs a blob issue as `{ event: 'blob-stamp', reason }`, never a key.

### Seeder storage

- The pool lives under `<seeder dir>/dm-pool/` (`log.bin` of `uint64be receivedAt || uint32be seq || drop`, `head.json`, and `seq.json` = `{ "v": 1, "nextSeq" }` so a seq is never handed out twice), mode 0600.
- A sweep removes expired drops from the pool at once. `log.bin` is rewritten only when expired records are more than one eighth of it or at least 512 records; until then they stay in the file, are never served, and are skipped when the pool loads. Their stamp tokens stay spent. A `receivedAt` more than one day ahead of the seeder's clock is read as the time of the load.
- The seeder reads its postage keys only when a direct message peer needs them, on the first stamp channel or put. The periodic refresh runs only after that first load, so a seeder no member has used creates no key file. A drop is stored when its frame decodes and its stamp verifies; a repeated identical drop is an idempotent success; a spent token (by BLAKE2b-256 of the token) on different bytes is refused.
- Retention `DM_RETENTION_MS = 7 * 86400000` by the seeder clock; at most `DM_POOL_MAX = 20000` drops (a full pool answers `'cheio'`). There is no deletion on request: a drop leaves only by the retention sweep, which runs every 60 s.
- Stamps are issued only to a diary the seeder admits or serves and that is not blocked; any other diary is answered `teto` and adds no row. The daily stamp budget is `<seeder dir>/dm-stamps.json` = `{ "v": 2, "days": { "<diary hex>": { "<YYYY-MM-DD, Brasília>": <count> } } }`, mode 0600: per diary a count for each of its 3 most recent days, charged and checked for the current day, so a clock stepped forward and back finds the earlier day still charged and a jump never touches other diaries' counts. Each write drops days older than 7 days before the current one (the file is no activity log; a clock jump past that window can restore an allowance). At most min(100000, 2 x maxServed) rows: past that no new diary gets a row nor a stamp until old rows are dropped. A `v` 1 file (`{ "<diary>": { day, count } }`) is read as one day per diary; an unreadable file counts as empty.
- The seeder logs `{ event: 'dm', reason: 'guardada' | 'cheio' | 'invalido' | 'postage' }` per put (a put over the rate is answered `'ritmo'` by the channel itself) and `{ event: 'dm-stamp', reason }`: counts only, never a key, an address or a bucket.
- A failed put or page is logged with a fixed reason, never with the error text.
- A failure of the seeder itself (its postage key file, its disk) is never `invalido`: the put throws, the channel closes without an ack and the member keeps the put as uncertain. Only a drop that is itself bad (frame, stamp, spent token) is `invalido`.
- `seed --no-dm` attaches neither channel: a member then sees both as unsupported. It also turns off the blob stamp kind, the blob channel and the `/blob` route (section 13).
- The seeder sees an ephemeral Noise key, an IP address, the time, a fixed-size frame and, on the stamp link, that some diary bought stamps today. It does not see the sender, the recipient, the text, nor which stamp belongs to whom.

### Multi-device

A sent message is two drops: one sealed to the recipient's box, one self copy sealed to the own box (each with its own `dropId` and stamp), so every device of the sender sees its own sent messages. Folder ops (`accepted`, `declined`, `blocked`, `unblocked`, `deleted`) travel as `ctl` envelopes sealed to the own box only, never to the peer. A ctl is filed locally at once and joins kv `dmCtlQueue` (ciphertext only, at most 32, the oldest dropped with a warning), which is put oldest first. A queued item is a sealed envelope until a stamp exists; the first time it is put it is stamped and its exact encoded drop (dropId and stamp inside) replaces it in the queue BEFORE the put. Any later put of it (after no ack, `cheio`, `ritmo`, no link) repeats those bytes, which the pool answers as a repeated success, and spends no second stamp: the node never re-sends a state change as a new request. The queue is put only after unlock and on the next user-initiated `dm.set` (before that op's own ctl, so order is kept), never by a top-up or a timer. A ctl the seeder refuses (`invalido`) is dropped with a warning. A failed top-up is retried after 60 s; a successful one waits 10 minutes.
