### Local semantics (`packages/core/lib/dm-fold.js`)

The only copy of the folder rules; the node calls it and the app renders the RPC results.

- Every message and op given to the fold carries `filedAt`: the LOCAL time in ms at which this device stored it, assigned by the node and monotonic (an own sent message: the send time; a ctl op from an own device: the time this device filed it, or the op's local creation time). A row without a valid `filedAt` is ignored. The sender chooses `at`, so no cutoff reads it: every cutoff below compares `filedAt`, and ordering uses the effective time `min(at, filedAt)`, so a future `at` (say `9999-12-31`) cannot escape a cutoff nor pin a thread first. Every message and thread row the fold returns carries `at` = that effective time as canonical ISO and, on messages, `sentAt` = the sender's original `at` (information only). Ordering, the caps and the `dm.list` cursor all use the effective `at`.
- The peer of a message is `to` when `from` is the own key, else `from`; a message where neither is the own key is ignored.
- Within one conversation an `id` belongs to one row. An own message always keeps its `id`: it is signed by the own key, so a peer copy with that `id` can only be the peer reusing an id it received, and on another own device that copy may even be filed before the self copy arrives. Between other copies the first filed wins (lowest `filedAt`, then input order). A peer can therefore neither shadow nor backdate an own message; ids are unique per thread (the same `id` in two different conversations is two messages).
- Newest op per class and peer wins, by `filedAt`: folder (`accepted` | `declined`), block (`blocked` | `unblocked`), delete (`deleted`); at equal `filedAt` the restrictive op wins.
- While the newest block op is `blocked`, the peer's thread is hidden and nothing in it is unread; after `unblocked`, messages filed at or before the last `blocked` stay hidden. `canSend` is false for a blocked peer and for the own key.
- A `deleted` op hides the messages (own ones included) filed at or before it.
- A thread is in the inbox when the peer is followed, or the newest folder op is `accepted`, or it holds an own sent message; otherwise it is a request. A `declined` op hides the PEER's request messages filed at or before it, never own sent messages (only `deleted` removes those); a message filed later opens a new request. Unfollow never demotes an accepted thread; a later follow moves an open request to the inbox.
- Threads sort by the newest kept effective time descending, then peer ascending. Requests are capped at `DM_REQUESTS_MAX = 100` (newest), messages per peer at `DM_MESSAGES_PER_PEER = 500` (newest).
- `dm.thread` pages by `before`, a message id, unique within the thread by the rule above, so the cursor names one row; an id no longer kept pages from the newest.
- Message rows carry `media`: the verified body's item list, or `null` for a v1 body. `previewText(row)` is what a conversation list shows: the text, or when it is empty and `media` is set the fixed label `'GIF'` (every item `image/gif`) or `'Foto'` (any other mix), never a peer-chosen string in place of the photo.
- Reactions (inner v4) are a separate fold input, each with its own `filedAt` under the same cutoffs as messages (a reaction filed at or before a `blocked` or `deleted`, or the peer's filed at or before a `declined` in a request, is ignored; a row without a valid `filedAt` is ignored). The reactor is the signed `from`. Per (target, reactor) the op with the highest `rev` wins, ties by the higher `id`; `''` as the winner removes. Never the sender's `at` nor the local filing time (one `id` signed twice with one `rev` falls to the higher emoji, so input order never decides). A reaction applies only when its target is a kept row of the same conversation: a target that is unknown, behind a cutoff, past the `DM_MESSAGES_PER_PEER` cap or itself a reaction is ignored. The fold runs over everything stored, so a reaction filed before its target counts once the target is present. Reactions never change `unread`, the preview, the thread order, a cutoff, the cap nor the newest message. A message row carries `reactions: [{ emoji, mine }]`, the other person's first, only when at least one person reacts (at most two entries).
- `unread` is a boolean (a received message not yet seen). Seen marks are keyed by sender and id, `seenKey(from, id)` = `from + ':' + id`, so a peer reusing an id already seen elsewhere still shows as unread and the tab shows a dot, never a count. A reply targets only a message of the same peer's thread, named by its `id`, which the thread rule above makes unique (if rows sharing an `id` ever reach `replyTarget`, the smaller sender key wins, the same on both devices). Nothing produces a read, delivered or typing state, nor any ack the peer can see.

### Pull checks

On pull the browser node opens a `msg` only when its `cert` verifies for the configured association with the stored blocklist snapshot's revoked issuers, and drops one whose `from` is on that snapshot's blocked set (`packages/web-member/lib/dm.js`). When sending, the node includes its own certificate from block 0 of its diary (read once per unlock); without one it refuses with `grave o certificado de membro antes de publicar`.

### Local storage

Messages are ciphertext at rest: a local-only core whose blocks are `crypto_secretbox` under `HKDF-SHA-256(ikm = prf, salt = utf8(DM_LOCAL_HKDF_SALT), info = utf8(DM_LOCAL_HKDF_INFO))`, `DM_LOCAL_HKDF_SALT = 'varanda/member-dm-local/hkdf/v1'`, `DM_LOCAL_HKDF_INFO = 'varanda/member-dm-local/secretbox/v1'`. Before sealing, each row gets a `pad` field of spaces so its JSON is a multiple of 1024 bytes (`packages/web-member/lib/dm-store.js`), stripped on open: a block's length shows neither the text length within a class nor a message from a ctl (both fall in the first class when short).

List summary: so the first `dm.list` page answers before every row is opened, the browser node keeps kv `dmSummary` (a Página's store `<prefix>dmSummary`) = `{ v: 1, nonce, box }`, the `crypto_secretbox` under `BLAKE2b-256('varanda/member-dm-local/summary-key/v1\n' || local key)` of `{ v: 1, core, length, follows, seen, inbox, requests }`: `core` the hex key of the local core, `length` its length, `follows` and `seen` the SHA-256 hex of the kv follows and seen texts the fold read, `inbox` and `requests` the first-page `dm.list` results of each folder (30 conversations at most). It is written about 1 s after a fold whose inputs changed, only while that unlock and those rows are current. An unlock reads it right after the key is installed, while the rows load: a first page (no `before`) answers from it only when it opens under this key, names this core, `length` equals the core's current length and both digests equal the current kv values; anything else (another key or identity, another core or length, a changed follows list or seen set, a tampered box) is ignored and `dm.list` waits for the rows, off the request chain. Every other request waits for the rows. While locked nothing new is readable: the entry is ciphertext under a key derived from the passkey PRF, and a lock drops the opened copy. A lock, Sair, a reset and an unlock under another key delete it, as does forgetting a Página for its own.

Stamps and queued ctl drops are bearer values: whoever holds an unspent stamp, or a stamped drop not yet put, can spend it. So the browser node keeps both sealed under the same local key (`crypto_secretbox`, `{ nonce, box }`), readable only while unlocked: kv `dmStamps` = `{ v: 2, items: [{ kid, nonce, box }], kids }`, each box holding `{ token, sig }`, and kv `dmCtlQueue` = `{ v: 2, items: [{ nonce, box }] }`, each box holding the base64 envelope or drop. A locked device reads no stamp. Plaintext entries of the earlier `v: 1` form are dropped on read (a top-up refills the wallet). Stamps are never bound to the diary: that would undo the blind signature's unlinkability.

Media of a message (inner v2): the row keeps the media items (blob descriptors with `key`), sealed like the rest of the row. The plaintext of an opened item is kept in a separate sealed cache on the browser node: `crypto_secretbox` under a key derived from the local key, entries under opaque keyed-hash names, an index written before the items (an unfinished entry never counts as cached and is always wiped), a total budget of 256 MiB of plaintext (`CACHE_BYTES_MAX`) with the oldest entries evicted first (a message larger than the budget is returned but not cached), wiped on lock, Sair, reset and on an unlock under another key. Opening is lazy: a `dm.thread` read starts it in the background, at most 2 opens at once (`OPEN_MAX`, queued in order; a new page or a lock drops the ones not started), never for a conversation in the `'request'` folder: there the bytes are fetched only on an explicit read of that message (`media.readDm`, a tap) or once the conversation is accepted, so a stranger's message cannot make the device download anything by itself. Downloads go only to the seeder origin and are verified (frame check, sha256 of the ciphertext, secretbox, image bounds) before anything is cached; a blob that is gone (404) or fails verification with nothing cached reads as `{ unavailable: true }` while the text still returns; a transient error leaves it retryable.

### Reports

Only the chosen message leaves the device: the report carries that message's plaintext and the sender's diary key as `target`, so moderators can block the account. The report statement is `'varanda-dm-report-v1'`, or `'varanda-dm-report-v2'` with an anexo (section 3, "Reports and operator requests"). It never carries the sender's signature, so the quote cannot be proven to a third party and the moderator sees it as `não conferida`.

- HTTPS only: `POST /account/report/start` for a nonce, then `POST /account/report` with `{ kind: 'dm', member, nonce, target, reason, text?, quote, signature }`.
- Refusals: `nao-vinculado` (the member has no linked account), `expirado` (a forged, expired, foreign or spent nonce), `pedido-invalido` (`target === member`, any of `seq`, `writer`, `group` or `peer` present, a bad reason, a too long `quote`, a too long `text`, an `anexo` on `/account/report`; an empty `quote` without an anexo fails the statement as `assinatura-invalida`), `assinatura-invalida` (the statement does not verify) and `limite` (the report budget). An open report with the same reporter, target and quote is answered as a duplicate.
- Intake never asks the seeder: the message is on no diary, so there is nothing to fetch, and the AI triage only marks the report for a human.
- **Anexo.** A photo, GIF or other image of the message may go with the report: the reporter's own decrypted copy, sent as `POST /account/report/anexo` with the same body plus `anexo` (standard base64 of the bytes). The statement is then `'varanda-dm-report-v2'` (section 3), whose last line is the SHA-256 of those bytes, and `quote` may be `''`. The same route takes a group message report (`writer`, `group`, statement `'varanda-report-v3'`); `/account/report` refuses an `anexo` and keeps its 8192-byte body cap. Only the anexo route reads a larger body: at most 1 MiB of image (`ANEXO_MAX_BYTES`), so a body past `ANEXO_BODY_MAX` (the base64 of 1 MiB plus 8192 bytes) answers HTTP 413 unread, and at most 4 such bodies are read at once (`limite` beyond). The request must declare its `Content-Length` (a chunked body answers HTTP 411 `pedido-invalido`), and a body that sends nothing for 5 s, or is still arriving 2 s plus its declared length at 1 Mbit/s after the read began, is cut without an answer. A genuine unspent nonce is spent on this route as soon as it is checked, before the image and the statement, so a request that then fails (`assinatura-invalida`, `pedido-invalido`) needs a fresh nonce from `report/start`. The bytes must be a JPEG, WebP or GIF that passes `mediaBounds` (`@varanda/rpc`); the format comes from the bytes, never from the request, and PNG or anything else is `pedido-invalido`. The account service stores them as `<dataDir>/report-media/<report id>` (mode 0600, directory 0700), written before the row, with at most 2000 stored anexos (`limite` beyond); the row records only `{ sha256, mime, size }`. The panel lists such a report as `com mídia` and shows the image (from `GET /account/mod/api/reports/<id>/anexo`, session only, read from local disk and re-hashed, CSP `default-src 'none'; sandbox`) labelled as sent by the reporter and `não conferida`, like the quote: nothing proves the sender sent it. The moderator path never fetches a blob, the seeder or any third party, and no log carries the bytes, a blob id or the digest. A report with an anexo is always decided by a person: a private message is marked for a human as above (the AI never reads the bytes), and a group message never reaches the AI triage. An open report with the same reporter, target, quote and anexo digest is a duplicate.

### Push wake

When a drop is stored the seeder may send `{ v: 1, kind: 'dm-wake' }`: no actor, no recipient, a body that names nobody (`Pode haver uma mensagem nova na Varanda`), coalesced to one per 15 minutes and fanned out to every subscription, since the seeder cannot know the recipient. A device suppresses its own wake locally (the sender's device, and an open focused app window), and no server learns the sender.

### Riscos residuais (DM)

- IP and timing correlation at the seeder between stamp issuance, puts and pages: mitigated by the wallet (stamps bought ahead, in batches), not removed.
- A `diary.peek` of the recipient's `dm-key` is the same exposure as opening a profile (section 6, "Browser node on-demand reads").
- The full-pool download is what hides the recipient, and it grows with the network (mobile data and battery cost).
- A device offline for more than 7 days misses the drops swept meanwhile.
- No forward secrecy: one long-lived box key per identity, rotated only by `gen`.
- A stolen passkey reads every drop still in retention.
- The inner signature is non-repudiable between the two parties: the recipient holds a message signed by the sender.
- The JS RSA code is not constant-time: forged stamps mean spam only.
- A flooder with stamps can fill the pool to `DM_POOL_MAX` (`'cheio'` for everyone until the sweep).
- The operator who changes the seeder binary always wins (logs, timing, refusal of service).
- The native app has no system push (`apps/app/src/pushClient.native.js`): it learns of messages only while open.
- The postage key is not checked across members: a seeder running modified code could hand each member its own postage key and so tag drops by sender (`kid` travels in clear in every drop). Members cannot yet cross-check the key they were given.
- Own devices can race `ctl` ops: each device orders them by its own filing time (last filed wins), so two devices can briefly disagree.
- `fwd` is asserted by the sender alone; there is no original-author field, so "Encaminhada" proves nothing about where the content came from, and a sender can mark an original message as forwarded or forward without marking.

## 12. Páginas (`packages/core/lib/page.js`, `page-proof.js`, `page-lifecycle.js`)

A página is a business or project identity with an @nome, run by an owner and up to 16 admins. To every seeder, follower, feed and old reader it is an ordinary diary. The record shapes and signed bytes are in section 2 ("Página records"); this section says what they mean and who may write them.

### Identity and custody

- A page is a v2-certificate compat diary (section 3, "Membership certificate v2") whose key equals its signer key: the hosted account service generates one Ed25519 pair per page and the block-0 `member-cert` is `createIssuedCert({ member: pageKey })`. A v2 certificate asserts no age: `ageBand` is `'unchecked'` and `method` is the fixed value `'google'` for every v2 certificate. On a page it records only that the hosted service issued the certificate, not a Google subject (section 3 explains the fixed value). The member Google sign-in is retired: an old build gets 410 `versao-antiga` (section 1).
- Only the hosted account service appends to a page diary. Admins hold no page key: every act is an HTTPS request carrying a statement signed by the actor's OWN diary key (`page-proof.js`), and the service checks it against its ACL before it appends. The page secret key stays in the service (encrypted at rest, never logged or returned) and is never in the Hypercore storage directory.
- Seq 1 is the page's `username` record, seq 2 the genesis `page-roster`. Later blocks are the page's `profile` (with an avatar in a declared media core), `post`, `like`, `repost`, `follow`, `delete`, `dm-key` when Conversas for pages exist, more rosters and the other `page-*` records. Old readers skip every `page-*` type and see a person.
- Pending keys: `POST /account/pages/start` returns the new page key for `create` and K2 for a move. Both are derived from the start nonce alone (`page-keys.js`, `pending`: HMAC-SHA256 under a secret derived from `VARANDA_PAGE_KEYS`), so the same nonce gives the same key again, after a restart too, and nothing is stored until the create or move commits. A resend of a create or move with the same nonce reaches the same key; there is no table of pending keys and no `limite` from one. The route nonces are bound to the member, the action and an expiry, so only a minted nonce reaches a page key.
- Limits: at most 16 admins per page, 8 pages per owner, 256 pages per host (one admission socket, below `MAX_INVENTORY`), invites and offers valid for 7 days.
- The service pauses a page whose core length moves outside its own lock (an unexpected length means a leaked key appended). A paused page takes no new block from the service and `mod block` is the operator switch.
- Creating the first page is a one-way migration of the account directory (the journal gains the page operations). Older builds refuse the migrated journal and the operator cannot go back by running them.

### The roster

- The `page-roster` is the full state: `gen`, `owner`, `admins` and `prev`. It is signed by the owner and countersigned by the issuer, and it carries `association` because the delegation shape requires it.
- Refresh: the service re-appends the newest roster unchanged when it is not within the newest `PAGE_ROSTER_REFRESH_BLOCKS = 256` blocks, so a reader that does not hold the diary always finds it in a bounded scan (the `dm-key` rule of section 11).
- Bounded verification (`pageState`, a reader that scans the newest windows): among shape-valid rosters naming the log key, take the highest `gen` (ties: highest seq) that verifies. The reader that does not hold the diary scans 8 windows of 36 blocks, 288 >= 256.
- Full verification (a reader that holds the diary) also walks the chain by `prev` and `gen`. The genesis (`gen` 1, `prev` null) names the owner and may carry admins only when the diary's `page-origin` at seq 3 verifies (a key move, below); otherwise a genesis with admins is invalid. A removal is a subset by the same owner. An add is valid only when it immediately follows that member's `page-accept` with the same `gen` and `rosterSignature`. A transfer changes `owner`, puts the previous owner in `admins`, and needs an unexpired `page-offer` to the new owner at `gen - 1`.

### Admins and transfer

- The service ACL (a sidecar journal) is the enforcement point; rosters are its public, verifiable mirror, appended in the same step so the order is total, under one lock per page.
- Invite: the owner's node signs a statement to the service, a private record that is NOT in any diary. It names the member, the next `gen` and the `rosterSignature` of the resulting roster. The service checks that the signer is the ACL owner, the member is linked, accepted the current terms, is not blocked and not a page key, and that admins plus pending invites stay within 16. It keeps the invite for 7 days.
- Accept: the invitee signs `page-accept`. The service checks that the invite is pending and its `gen` is still current plus 1 (else `expirado`), then appends the accept and the owner-signed roster, countersigns it and updates the ACL.
- Remove: the owner signs a roster without the member (`gen + 1`). From the ACL commit the removed member's requests answer `sem-permissao`; the only window is a request already past the ACL check inside the same lock.
- Transfer: the owner signs `page-offer` (`to` and `exp` of at most 7 days; both `null` cancels). The recipient signs the transfer roster (owner `to`, admins = previous admins minus `to` plus the previous owner, `gen + 1`). The service checks that the offer is the newest unexpired one, the recipient is linked and owns fewer than 8 pages, then swaps the owner. The previous owner stays an admin until removed.
- Idempotency: a request with the same member and nonce that was already applied answers the stored seq; a nonce is spent once.

### Link to the seeder

The service opens one loopback Noise WebSocket to the seeder that runs on the same host and announces each page with `announce { key, cert }` (section 5, "Seeder admission algorithm"), so the seeder admits and serves the page like any diary. Media cores are never announced (section 5, "Seeder media admission"). The link carries the page cores only and never a secret key.

### Seguir como página

A follow is a `follow` record in the PAGE diary, appended by the service on a signed action from the owner or an admin, never in the admin's own diary. The service refuses the page's own key, a blocked key and a follow set at 4095 (`MAX_INVENTORY - 1`). Another page is a legal target. `page.feed` ("A Rua da página") is a 64-key peek: the app picks the 64 newest follows and the browser node reads them over a dedicated short-lived seeder link (the Espiadinha machinery of section 6, `PEEK_HEAD_BLOCKS` and `PEEK_TAIL_BLOCKS` per diary, block-0 gate, blocklist and revoked issuers) and answers `feed.read` rows with `incomplete` when a diary was not served or the budget cut. It is not a full sync, and the core session does not answer it.

Terminal seq. The seeder name index keeps, per page, the seq of the first `page-close` or `page-moved` that verifies for the page's log (countersigned, revoked issuers applied; the lowest seq noted wins, as in `pageLifecycle`; an unverified record sets nothing), and answers it on `GET /index/page-end/<author>` (section 7). For each diary that may be a page (block 1 or 2 a `page-roster`, or block 2 not read, also when it is unread only because a tombstone names it: a leaked page key may delete the roster and the close together), `page.feed` asks that route on the seeder's host (one GET of at most 1024 bytes, charged to the byte budget, under the fetch timeout) and ALWAYS fetches the pointed block on its own, even outside the peek window and even when a tombstone names it. The pointed block joins the entries ONLY when it verifies as this diary's terminal record (`verifyClose` or `verifyMoved` with the association and the revoked issuers, its `page` equal to the diary key); anything else, such as a person's deleted post a hostile seeder points at, is dropped and never shown. The cut is then applied by `pageLifecycle`. The seeder's word is a pointer only: a seq outside the log is ignored; a pointed block that does not arrive before the deadline, or that is not a verified close or move at or below that seq, makes the answer `incomplete: true` and drops that diary's rows above the head (fail closed); no answer at all changes nothing (the seeder can withhold, as for any block). The check is "a verified cut at or below the pointer", not "the first cut": a log with two countersigned terminal records is an issuer failure, outside this guarantee. A diary read as personal (block 2 read as another record) is never asked about. One whose block 2 is an unheld deletion target is asked, but its other deletion targets are never fetched; a hostile seeder pointing it at a deleted post can at most hide that diary's tail (the pointer does not verify, so the diary is incomplete with head-only rows), which it could already do by withholding, and never makes the deleted post show. The remaining targets of a page diary in the window are asked at once under one shared 1 s probe timer.

### Fotos

Photos in page posts and the page avatar are uploaded raw to the service (`application/octet-stream`, the signed `media` action carrying an `opHash` over `{ sha256, mime, width, height, bytes }`). The limits are a person's: 1 to 10 items in a post, reply or quote, 1 to 4 (`feira.MAX_LISTING_MEDIA`) in a listing, one photo for the avatar, `MEDIA_MIMES`, at most `MAX_MEDIA_ITEM_BLOCKS * MEDIA_BLOCK_BYTES` (8 MiB). The service re-hashes the bytes; jpeg and webp must match `stillImageSize`; png and gif pass the magic bytes and `validDescriptor`, the depth `verifyMedia` uses with no decoder (width and height of png and gif are not re-decoded). The service appends the blocks to the page's declared media core and keeps an item table, and a post, reply or quote `media` must equal rows of that table (`midia-invalida` otherwise). A core node uploads the same way (a core node writes no media core, section 2).

### Encerrar

Only the owner closes a page, in one lock, in this order: the page leaves every group and community (`group-join` with `on: false`, before anything terminal; if one leave fails the close answers `indisponivel`, nothing terminal is written and the lifecycle use is refunded), then a follow off for every follow, then a `delete` tombstone (with media ranges) for every post, like, repost and profile version still shown, then the countersigned `page-close` (`gen` plus 1). Right after the `page-close` is appended, in the same step and BEFORE the push to the seeder is awaited, the service clears the deleted blocks and media ranges (never block 0 or a tombstone). Then the service KEEPS SERVING the closed page read-only, so a reader that syncs later still gets the `page-close`: it wipes the page's keys (signing, writers, group box, DM box) only after the close has reached the seeder (`pushed`), and that wipe is re-armed after a restart. The page's sealed Conversas inbox is removed (`pagesDm.forget`). A paused core is closed in the ACL and the directory without a new block (a close without a block, `pushed: false`); `mod block` stays the operator switch. A page that is `movida` or `encerrada` answers every later action with that token (HTTP 409). Readers fold `closed: true` and ignore blocks after the close. Public afterwards: block 0, the username record, rosters, tombstones and `page-close`. The @nome is burned (it never returns to the pool) and the name index forgets it. Old readers skip `delete` and `page-close` and keep showing posts, and copies already taken remain (section 2, "Deletion and old readers"). Admins fail at the ACL commit.

### Mudar de chave

The owner moves a page from K1 to a new key K2 pre-generated by the service. `page.sign` with action `move` returns three owner-signed records: `page-moved` (for K1), `page-origin` and the genesis roster (for K2).

- K2 gets, in one lock: block 0 (a v2 cert), seq 1 the username record with the SAME @nome, seq 2 the genesis roster (`gen` 1, the current owner and admins, no re-accept) and seq 3 `page-origin` (`from` K1, `fromSeq`, K1's final `gen`). `fromSeq` is the last K1 seq the owner saw when signing (the signed `status` length minus 1). The group leaves and `page-moved` land after it, so it is at most the seq of the final `page-moved`. A non-empty genesis is valid only with a verifying `page-origin` at seq 3.
- Before `page-moved`, if K1 is not paused, the service makes K1 leave every group and community (`group-join` with `on: false`); a leave that fails answers `indisponivel`, writes nothing terminal and refunds the lifecycle use. K2 re-joins the communities; approval communities go back to requested. For a paused K1 this is an accepted limit that `mod block` covers.
- On K1 the service appends `page-moved`. The LOWEST-seq verified `page-moved` is final: readers ignore later K1 blocks and the feed shows no K1 item above its seq. A reader checks both directions: `page-moved.to === K2` and `page-origin.from === K1`, with `page-origin.fromSeq` at most the seq of that `page-moved`.
- K2 is the key `start` derived from the move nonce (see "Identity and custody"); a resend with the same nonce reaches the same K2, and a `to` that is not that key answers `expirado`. K2 is announced to the seeder only after `page-moved` is on K1, or after the paused-K1 fallback below. A move that fails after the writer step finishes on a retry with the same nonce, and K1 is paused in the ACL meanwhile. At most 2 closes or moves per owner per 24 h (a window in memory that resets on restart); a retry is not charged twice and a definite writer refusal refunds the charge.
- If K1 is paused, the service signs a detached `page-moved` and serves it at `GET /account/pages/successor/<K1>` (64 hex digits, no session, `Cache-Control: no-store`; answers `{ moved }` or 404 `nao-encontrado`). A client applies it only together with a verified `page-origin`. The route answers `limite` over 120 requests per minute per host (the GET is anonymous), and the service caches a verified answer per K1 in memory (at most 1000 K1 keys).
- Follower diaries are not rewritten. A follower's node, after it verified both `page-moved` and `page-origin` itself (never from a seeder hint), appends ONE `follow` of K2, once, by itself (Terms 15). Both nodes do it: the core node in `packages/core/lib/sync.js` and the browser node in `followMovedPage`. The switch `VARANDA_NO_PAGE_MIGRATE=1` exists in the CORE node only; the browser node takes `noPageMigrate` only through test options. The app's "Seguir a chave nova" is the manual way. Chains stop at 4 moves.
- Page Conversas do not follow a key move: K2 gets a new box. Old readers cannot see a move and keep showing K1; a leaked K1 can only fork or append to K1 (it cannot forge issuer countersignatures, K2, a rename or a roster), and `mod block` on K1 stops it for old readers.

### Nó core: delegado

A core diary key is a manifest hash, not an Ed25519 member key, so a core node cannot sign a page statement as itself. `page.register` generates an Ed25519 delegate pair, stores it in `private/page-actor.json` (mode 0600) and appends `page-delegate { type, actor, at }` to the manifest diary; a second call appends nothing. The delegate signs the page statements and the page stays service-held (no self custody). The service accepts a member absent from its link table only when it replicated that diary from the loopback seeder, the certificate verifies under revoked issuers, the newest `page-delegate` names the actor, neither key is blocked, and the actor accepted the current terms (the terms are accepted by the actor, not the diary). The replication check (the certificate under the revoked issuers and the newest `page-delegate` naming the actor) runs on every request and is cached for 60 s per diary (`pages-delegate.js`); a diary the service has no row for is probed in at most 1 of its 4 verifier slots and a miss is cached for 60 s. The service appends a `page-link` (issuer-signed) so readers show the diary's profile for the actor, only when the delegate creates a page or moves one (K2 gets one); an accept appends none. A delegate has no @nome of its own. The `terms` action carries the delegate's acceptance: `page.sign` answers `{ signature, termsSignature }` and the request body carries `termsSignature`, checked with `verifyTerms` (section 3). The `terms` action is rate limited before the nonce is claimed and before any seeder read: 5 per 10 minutes per actor, 20 per minute for the host, and one in flight per actor (`limite`). A delegate with no link row is refused `nao-vinculado`; for its own requests a row accepted under older Terms is refused `termos-pendentes`, but as the RECIPIENT of an invite or an offer a delegate whose row holds older Terms is refused `nao-vinculado`. A linked member is refused `terms` and can never register a delegate for its own linked diary (a phone link, or an earlier Google link that stays). The member Google sign-in is retired: an old build gets 410 `versao-antiga`.

### Avisos de uma página

A page reads its notices like a person (section 3, "Notices channel v2"): the page key proves the v2 payload with a short-lived loopback seeder link. The service re-verifies every row (the block-0 gate, the named block is the author's signed follow of the page, or a post whose kind is DERIVED from the post itself, never taken from the seeder) and answers owners and admins in the `notices.list` shape. The action is `notices { after?, limit? }`, a signed read for the owner or an admin, at most 8 per minute per page (a window in memory that resets on restart; `limite`; one load of the app follows `next` up to 4 times). On a moved page notices and every `dm-*` action answer `movida` (409), on a closed one `encerrada`. No Web Push goes to a page.

### Comunidades como página

A page joins communities and groups through writer cores of its own. The service holds only the random Ed25519 writer pairs for those cores. Caps are `MAX_GROUP_WRITERS_PER_DIARY` writers per diary per group, 64 groups per diary and 16 writers per page. The page has its own link and replica store, and it is never an admin: it does not create, admit, ban, appoint, hide or rekey (`sem-permissao` is the only answer to those). Pages in communities are on whenever pages are on; there is no separate switch.

For a private group, the page group box is the `groupBox` derived from the page-secrets bundle held by the owner and admin devices ("Conversas como página"). The service holds no group-box secret. An admin device opens records with `group.read`, seals a post with `group.seal`, and sends its sealed record in the signed `group-seal` action. The service appends it only after checking the page member has a live counted writer equal to `writer`, that writer's header box equals the page `groupBox`, `kid` is exactly the epoch the members' fold makes eligible for that writer now ("Eligibility": decided from the plaintext kids and recipient lists, with a random stand-in key in place of each entry sealed to the writer, never from a real key), the fold is complete, and the writer core length equals `seq`. An envelope sealed under an epoch that a later removal superseded is refused, so a removed member's key never reads a post the page sends after the removal. It sees the header, membership and timing, but not sealed post text.

Changing the page `groupBox` requires a rebox. `group-rebox` files a step per group, then leaves the old writer (`group-leave`, then its diary join off), binds a new writer whose header has `box` equal to the new group box and `rejoin` naming that exact leave (the writer header field; `group-rejoin` is only an internal fold node), and joins the diary. A failure is completed, sub-step by sub-step in that order, by the next `group-rebox` or by the service, which retries every page with rebox-pending at its start and on a tick, one page at a time under the page lock. A group that no longer folds (orphaned, blocked, unreadable) and a membership whose writer is no longer live but still boxed to an old key are left (diary join off, writer secret dropped), never holding the others. A partial fold still gets the leave at once; only the new writer waits for a complete fold. `group-leave` is never held by a pending rebox. Until group admins admit the new writer and a new epoch covers the leave, the page is rekey-pending and cannot post. At the writer cap, the page only leaves that group. The service runs the same work after a `dm-box` or `remove` changes `groupBox`, persisting rebox-pending first; while it is pending every page action in a private group answers `aguardando-chave`. After all private groups are handled, it deletes the legacy service-held group-box secret; every service start also deletes it from every page, since nothing reads it any more. `dm-box-read` and `status` expose `reboxPending` and `reboxNeeded` (private memberships still boxed to another key). Epochs before this rotation were known to the service.

The signed actions are `community-join` and `community-leave { community }`, `group-join { group, community? }`, `group-leave { group }`, `group-post { group, text, replyTo? }` and `group-read { group, before?, limit? }` (a signed read, `before` = `{ at, writer, seq }`, `limit` 1 to 32, default 32, answering like `group.read` plus `role` and `state`). Joins and leaves answer `{ state, role }`, with `state` `'requested'`, `'member'` or `'left'` (`community-join` also answers `group`, its Geral group or null); `group-post` answers `{ seq }`. The owner-or-admin check runs under the page lock before any block, so a removed admin is `sem-permissao` and a closed or moved page `encerrada` or `movida`; `fora-da-comunidade` and `aguardando-chave` refuse a group action the page cannot take yet. The signed `status` carries `communities: [{ community, role }]`, from the page's writer rows. After a key move K2 re-joins, in the background, the communities K1 left (approval communities come back as requested; a failure only logs); groups are not re-joined. A `group-post` resent after a service restart may be posted twice: its nonce record lives in memory only. A leaked writer backdates until its leave, the departed-device limit of section 10.

The account service can append as the page's writer. It can forge a sealed block only without a valid epoch key, and readers drop that block. It also sees page membership and the timing of page group activity.

### Anúncios como página (Feira)

A page sells in the Feira like a person does (section 15): the account service appends the same four record types to the page diary under the service's own key, after it checks a statement signed by the acting admin's own diary key. The signed actions are `listing`, `listing-edit`, `listing-status` and `listing-consent` (`ACTIONS` of `packages/core/lib/page-proof.js`; `docs/rpc.md` 5.84), each owner or admin only, with the role read again after the checks that read the diary.

- `listing { kind, title, text, price, unit, cat, place, media? }` appends a `listing` root and answers `{ seq, length, pushed }`. The fields are validated as in section 2; at most 30 live listings per page diary (`limite`), counted from the diary itself.
- `listing-edit { to, expectedRevision, title, text, price, unit, cat, place, media }` appends a `listing-edit` with the full content and, in the same append, the tombstone of the edit it replaces. `to` names a live listing of this page (`<page>:<seq>`), else `pedido-invalido`; `expectedRevision` must be the seq of the live edit, or 0 when there is none, else `conflito` (HTTP 409) and nothing is written. The answer is `{ seq, length, pushed }` with `seq` the seq of the EDIT block (the next `expectedRevision`), not of the tombstone after it, also on a replay of the same nonce.
- `listing-status { to, status }` appends a `listing-status`. A `procura` is never `reservado` (`pedido-invalido`).
- `listing-consent { to, rater, offer }` appends nothing and answers `{ consent }`: the page key signs the deal bytes (`dealPayload`, section 15) of an offer a member sent the page in Conversas. The service cannot read the page's Conversas, so it does not look for the offer: the admin's signed statement `{ to, rater, offer }` is the whole claim, checked against the ACL (owner or admin, again after the diary read and after signing) and a live listing of this page (`to`, else `pedido-invalido`). The admin's browser node checks the offer itself before it asks (`listing.consent`). Only the deal bytes are ever signed with this key.
- `delete` also takes a `listing` or `listing-edit` of the page: a listing goes with its live edits in one append, as in the nodes. Closing a page deletes its listings with its posts (the lists of "Encerrar" now include `listing` and `listing-edit`), and a closed or moved page leaves the Feira (section 15).
- Refusal tokens here are the service's, not pt-BR messages: `conflito`, `pedido-invalido`, `limite`, `sem-permissao`, `midia-invalida`.

### Conversas como página

A page has Conversas (section 11). Its box secrets live on the devices of its owner and admins, not at the account service: the service stores them only sealed, holds no box secret and reads no page message.

- **Page secrets.** The owner's device generates a seeds bundle (`packages/core/lib/page-secrets.js`, `newPageSecrets`): `dmSeed` and `groupSeed`, 32 random bytes each. The page's dm box pair and its group box pair (private groups, "Comunidades como página") derive from them with `crypto_box_seed_keypair` (`pairsOf`); a weak public key (`isWeakBoxKey`) refuses the bundle (`chave fraca`).
- **Envelope.** The bundle travels to the owner and to every accepted admin sealed to that member's identity-wide dm-key box (section 11): `sealSecrets(secrets, { page, gen, member, box })` = canonical base64 of `crypto_box_seal` of exactly `PAGE_SECRETS_BYTES = 165` bytes, `PAGE_SECRETS_SEALED_BYTES = 213` sealed: `u8 v` (`PAGE_SECRETS_V = 1`), page key (32), `gen` (uint32be), member key (32), `dmSeed` (32), `groupSeed` (32), tag (32) = `crypto_generichash(32, utf8('varanda/page-secrets/v1') || page || uint32be(gen) || member)`. `openSecrets(b64, boxPair, { page, gen, member })` returns the seeds or `null` (length, version, page, gen, member and tag compared in constant time, plaintext wiped), so the service cannot hand back an envelope of another page, member or rotation.
- **Share signature.** The sharer (the owner, or an admin passing the seeds on) signs each envelope with the identity key: `shareBytes({ page, gen, member, sealed })` = `utf8('varanda-page-share-v1' LF) || sha256(JSON of { gen, member, page, sealed } in that key order)`; `signShare` / `verifyShare`. A device takes seeds only from a share signed by someone who is owner or admin of the page, never on the service's word.
- **Storage.** The service stores each member's envelope and its signature opaque and returns them to that member's devices. It cannot open them.
- **Generations.** The page's `dm-key` `gen` advances on every rotation of the seeds. New pages start at `gen` 1 on device-held seeds. A legacy page's `gen` 1 box was generated and held by the service (below); its first device-held generation is `gen` 2.
- **Signing.** The service still holds the page Ed25519 key. It signs only digests (`dm-sign`): the admin's device builds the v3 inner (section 11, "Inner v3") with `innerDigest`, the service answers `signDigest` over the 32-byte digest and an expiry it picks (`{ signature, expiresAt }`, section 11 "Inner v3"), the device assembles, seals to the recipient's box and to the page's own box, and posts the drop. The recipient sees the page, not the admin who wrote. Every page message is v3, a text-only one included, with media, cards and forwards as in v2.
- **Pool reader.** The admin devices pull the pool and try each drop against the page's dm box, as they do for their own box. Folder ops of the page (accepted, declined, blocked, unblocked, deleted) travel as page-signed v3 `ctl` inners sealed to the page box, so every admin device folds the same folder. Seen marks stay per admin device.
- **Removal.** Removing an admin cuts `dm-sign` and the share at the ACL commit, and on a page whose box is device-held the removal carries the rotation (`dmBox`, required, docs/rpc.md 5.84): a new `gen` is published and sealed to the remaining roster in the same lock, so the removed admin holds only superseded seeds. Drops a sender sealed to the old box before seeing the new `dm-key` stay readable to the removed admin. What the admin already read or sent stays read or sent.
- **Shares.** `dm-box-share` never overwrites a share of the current `gen` unless the owner sends it: an admin only adds a share for a roster member who has none for the current `gen`, and only the owner writes the owner's own share (`conflito` otherwise, nothing written). So an admin cannot replace another holder's envelope and keep the keys from reaching it.
- **Move.** Conversas do not follow a key move (see "Mudar de chave"): the new key gets new seeds and an empty inbox.
- **Antes (gen 1).** Before page secrets, the service generated the page box pair, kept it with the page secret key, pulled the pool itself, stored per-page rows sealed under a key from `VARANDA_PAGE_KEYS`, and built and signed text-only v1 inners in `dm-send`. Migration happens at the owner's first `dm-box`, which publishes `gen` 2 from new seeds: before anything is deleted the service seals one legacy bundle (the `gen` 1 box secret, every old message row and every old folder, block and delete op, in filing order, each with its position `n` in one filing sequence shared by rows and ops, so a `deleted` op hides only what was filed before it, no cap) to the owner's own dm-key box in chunks of at most 40000 bytes (`bundleId || uint16be index || uint16be count || bytes`, each `crypto_box_seal`ed), records it with the rotation, and only then deletes the old rows and the secret (docs/rpc.md 5.84). The owner's device fetches the chunks (`dm-legacy-read`), imports them (`dm.pageLegacyChunk`, docs/rpc.md 5.114: the old rows and ops filed locally, the `gen` 1 box adopted so old threads still open there, the old blocks kept for the owner to apply again as the page) and confirms (`dm-legacy-done`), and only then does the service drop the sealed chunks. The bundle is sealed to that owner alone, to the own box the owner's device sent after checking it against its published `dm-key`: after a transfer the new owner cannot read it, and later rotations or removes never drop it; a move carries it to K2, still sealed to the same member; a close does drop it.

### Riscos residuais (Conversas como página)

- A removed admin can still send messages as the page that it had signed through `dm-sign` before the removal, for at most `DM_V3_STALE_MS` (8 days) after its last `dm-sign`: the signature is valid and receivers file the drop until `expiresAt + DM_V3_STALE_MS` (section 11, "Inner v3"). Each such message is one it prepared (a digest of a fixed body), and the `dm-sign` rate limit bounds how many.
- The account service holds the page Ed25519 key and can sign any v3 digest with any expiry: it can write as the page. It still cannot read page messages (it holds no box secret).
- Drops a sender sealed to the old box before seeing the new `dm-key` stay readable to a removed admin ("Removal" above).

### Service answers and limits

- Tokens: `nao-vinculado`, `sem-permissao`, `limite`, `nome-em-uso`, `nome-invalido`, `expirado`, `assinatura-invalida`, `pedido-invalido`, `pausada`, `encerrada` (409), `movida` (409), `midia-invalida`, `indisponivel`, `termos-pendentes`, `em-andamento` (409) and `nao-encontrado` (404). `em-andamento` answers a resend of the same (member, nonce, body) while the first write is still in flight: no answer yet, resend later. An unknown outcome is HTTP 202 `{ uncertain: true }`.
- A signed read whose nonce was minted before the service started answers `expirado` (read nonces are spent in memory only, so a captured read cannot be replayed after a restart); the client asks again with a fresh nonce. The reads (`dm-list`, `dm-thread`, `dm-legacy-read`, `notices`, `group-read`) re-check the role after their work and answer `sem-permissao` when it was lost mid-request; nothing is sealed or filed for the removed admin.
- The spent-nonce table of the ACL holds at most 4096 rows for 600 s. At the cap a commit evicts the oldest rows (by expiry, never the rows being added) instead of failing. An evicted nonce loses replay protection inside its TTL; the nonce record of the diary still answers a resend of the same request with the stored seq.
- `status` answers `engagement` capped at the newest 4096 liked and 4096 reposted refs, with `engagement.truncated` true when it left some out.
- A signed read (`list`, `status`, `follows`, `notices`, `terms`, `group-read`, `dm-list`, `dm-thread`, `dm-legacy-read`) is retried with a FRESH nonce (a new `start`), never the same one: the same nonce with another body, or the same read again, is `expirado`.
- The owner cancels an offer with the signed `page-offer` whose `to` and `exp` are `null`; the unsigned `offer-cancel` route answers `pedido-invalido`.
- A `delete` never targets a `page-*` record (`pedido-invalido`), and every page fold (state, lifecycle, cuts, origin) reads raw entries, never entries after deletes.
- A `delete` whose target is a `page-*` record (`page-roster`, `page-accept`, `page-offer`, `page-close`, `page-moved`, `page-origin`, `page-link`, `page-delegate`) has no effect anywhere. The deletion fold (`packages/core/lib/deletion.js`) ignores a tombstone whose target block it holds and reads as a `page-*` record, so readers keep that block. A seeder, a core follower and a browser follower never clear a `page-*` block: every clear reads the target block first and keeps it when it is a `page-*` record or cannot be read. When a caller holds the raw blocks, `buildFeed` reads and folds the close or move cut from them, so the cut never depends on the caller's fold. The service refuses to append such a delete itself (above), so honest pages are unaffected.
- The seeder name index sets the page hint only from a `page-roster`, `page-close` or `page-moved` that verifies (countersigned, revoked issuers applied) for that log key. A follower checks each `page-close` and `page-moved` as it scans and keeps only the first one that verifies, so unverifiable padding cannot hide a later genuine move. A move whose K2 is not readable yet is retried on later passes, a bounded number of times.
- An offer to someone who is not yet an admin is refused `limite` when the page has 16 admins; only the newest `page-offer` counts, and the service enforces its expiry before it countersigns.

### Service answers: GIF search (`apps/cli/lib/account/gif-routes.js`)

The account service proxies GIF search so the member's device never talks to the GIF provider. It is off unless the operator sets both `VARANDA_GIF_API_URL` (https, a hostname, no user, query or IP literal) and `VARANDA_GIF_API_KEY`; one without the other stops the service at start. `VARANDA_GIF_MEDIA_HOSTS` (optional, exact hostnames separated by commas) lists the hosts besides the API host that may serve the images. No value is ever printed or logged.

- `GET /account/gif/policy` -> 200 `{ enabled }`, also when off (`{ enabled: false }`), so the app can hide the GIF button. While off, every other `/account/gif/*` route answers 404 `{ error: "feature-off" }`.
- Session: `POST /account/gif/session/start { member }` -> `{ nonce }` for a linked member that is not on the moderation blocklist (`nao-vinculado` or `sem-permissao` otherwise); `POST /account/gif/session/finish { member, nonce, signature }` -> `{ token, expiresAt }`. The signature is `invite.sign` with action `summary` over that nonce (`docs/rpc.md` 5.30, `packages/core/lib/invite.js`), a read-only statement the node already signs. Member keys are public, so start stores and counts nothing (only the service-wide request rate bounds it): the nonce is stateless and bound to the member, 32 bytes (43 base64url characters) made of 12 random bytes, a 4-byte big-endian expiry in unix seconds (600 s ahead) and the first 16 bytes of HMAC-SHA256 under a 32-byte key that lives only in the service's memory (a restart invalidates every outstanding nonce) over `varanda/gif-nonce/v1`, the 32-byte member key, the random bytes and the expiry. The MAC domain keeps it from ever being an invite nonce. Finish checks, in order: the nonce (canonical spelling, MAC compared in constant time, not expired; else 404 `expirado`), the signature (400 `assinatura-invalida`), the spent table (a nonce already spent answers 404 `expirado`; a table full of entries (10000) answers 503 `indisponivel` rather than forget one, which would allow a replay), the current Terms and the member's standing (below), and only then its per-member limit (10 per 10 minutes, 429 `limite-gif`). So nobody without the member's key can lock the member out, a replayed or refused finish never spends the member's window, and a member over the limit keeps the nonce until it expires. Only then is the nonce spent and the token issued. A spent nonce stays in the table until 600 s past its own expiry, so a wall clock stepped back by up to one nonce lifetime cannot make it usable again. The token lives 12 h in memory only (a restart forgets it) and travels as `Authorization: Bearer <token>` (scheme in any case, exactly one space), never in a URL or another header. A missing, malformed or unknown token answers 401 `nao-autorizado`.
- Re-check: every search and image request checks again that the token's member is still linked and not blocked, with the same check as start and finish, at most 60 s stale (cached per session; a clock that reads earlier than the last check re-checks at once). A member who fails it answers 401 `nao-autorizado` and the session is deleted (logged as the count `sessao-revogada`).
- Why a body and `Authorization`: Caddy's default logger writes the URI and the headers of a request that fails upstream and redacts only `Cookie`, `Set-Cookie`, `Authorization` and `Proxy-Authorization`, and it never logs a body. So the search text never rides in a URL and the token never in a custom header.
- `POST /account/gif/search` with a JSON body of exactly `{ q }` or `{ q, cursor }` (the same Origin and content-type rules and 4096-byte body cap as the other JSON routes of the service; a larger body answers 413) -> `{ items: [{ id, width, height, bytes }], next }`. `q` is a string of 1..64 code points with no control characters, not blank; `cursor` is absent, empty or a canonical decimal string up to 4999; any other key is `pedido-invalido`. `GET /account/gif/search` no longer exists and answers 404 `nao-encontrado` like any unknown GIF route; `next` is the next cursor or `null`. The answer never carries a provider URL or host: the service keeps the URLs of recent results in memory (one hour, at most 5000 ids) and the member names a GIF only by `id`. Items with a bad id, a non-https URL, a host outside the allowed set or a size that is not an integer in range are dropped.
- `GET /account/gif/image/<id>?size=tiny|full` (same `Authorization: Bearer` token; no member data in the URL) -> 200 `image/gif`, only for an id from a recent search. The body is checked to start with `GIF87a` or `GIF89a` and to stay under 512 KiB (`tiny`) or 8 MiB (`full`) before anything is sent, with `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Cache-Control: private, max-age=3600`, `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; sandbox` and `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`. An unknown or expired id is `nao-encontrado`.
- Limits per member per day: 120 searches, 60 full images, 2000 tiny images (429 `limite-gif`), in memory, reset on restart. At most 16 image fetches run at once (503 `indisponivel` above it, checked before the daily count is taken). A search or image is counted before the provider call, so concurrent requests stay bounded, and given back when the provider fetch fails (503 `indisponivel`).
- Provider: each request has a 10 s deadline for the whole exchange, follows at most 3 redirects, each re-checked against the same host rules, and resolves the host in the socket's own lookup, refusing loopback, private, link-local, CGNAT, multicast, reserved and documentation addresses, IPv6 ULA, link-local and site-local (`fec0::/10`), and every IPv6 form that carries an IPv4 address: IPv4-mapped `::ffff:0:0/96` (unless the IPv4 address is public), IPv4-compatible `::/96`, IPv4-translated `::ffff:0:0:0/96`, NAT64 `64:ff9b::/96` and `64:ff9b:1::/48`, 6to4 `2002::/16` and Teredo `2001::/32`. A provider failure (network, status, deadline, size, bad JSON, bytes that are not a GIF) answers 503 `indisponivel`.
- What reaches the provider: the search text, the operator's key and the association service's own network address. Never the member's address, key, session token, cookie or any identifier: requests carry only a fixed `User-Agent` and `Accept`, with no cookie, `Authorization`, `Referer` or forwarded address. The query text, the session token, the key and provider URLs never reach a log line; logs carry only `{ event: "gif", reason }` counts (`sessao`, `sessao-revogada`, `busca`, `miniatura`, `imagem`, `indisponivel`).

### Service answers: link preview (`apps/cli/lib/account/preview-routes.js`)

The account service fetches a link's preview so the member's device never opens a page it did not tap, and the site sees the service's address, never the member's. It needs no third-party key, so it is on by default; the operator turns it off with `VARANDA_PREVIEW=0` in the `varanda account` environment (server option `preview: false`; any value other than unset, empty, `0` or `1` stops the service from starting). The address checks and the guarded fetch are shared with GIF search (`apps/cli/lib/account/safe-fetch.js`), and so is the session design (`apps/cli/lib/account/member-session.js`).

- `GET /account/preview/policy` -> 200 `{ enabled }`, also when off. While off, every other `/account/preview/*` route answers 404 `{ error: "feature-off" }`.
- Session: `POST /account/preview/session/start { member }` and `POST /account/preview/session/finish { member, nonce, signature }`, exactly as the GIF session above (stateless member-bound nonce, `invite.sign` action `summary`, the same finish order with the limit of 10 per 10 minutes counted only after the signature, the spent table, the Terms and the standing pass, single-use spent table that refuses when full and keeps an entry 600 s past its expiry, current Terms required, 12 h token as `Authorization: Bearer <token>`, standing re-checked at most 60 s stale), with three differences: the MAC domain is `varanda/preview-nonce/v1`, the HMAC key is its own (32 random bytes in memory, not the GIF key) and the token table is its own. A GIF nonce or token never works here and a preview one never works at the GIF routes. The finish limit answers 429 `limite-previa`; logs say `{ event: "preview", reason }`.
- `POST /account/preview` with a JSON body of exactly `{ url }` and the session token -> `{ url, host, title, desc, image }`. `url` is a string of at most 300 characters with no control characters or surrounding spaces, `https:` on the default port, a dotted hostname (never an IP literal or a single label), no user or password, and still at most 300 characters once normalized, and that normalized form must pass the link card URL rule (`cardUrlHost` of `@varanda/rpc`, section 2, "Link cards": no brace, pipe or other character outside the canonical set, the fragment kept); anything else, or any other key, is 400 `pedido-invalido`, before any fetch. The answer's `url` is the final URL the page came from after redirects (the normalized link when there was none) and `host` that URL's hostname, never the link sent when a redirect moved to another page, so a card never pairs one site's name with another site's text; a final URL that fails `cardUrlHost` answers 503 `indisponivel` (logged as `destino`, not given back). `title` and `desc` strings, `''` when the page has none (an older service answered `null`, which the app reads as `''`; the app makes no card when `title` is empty), `image` `{ mime, bytes }` (base64) or `null`.
- Fetch: the page is fetched with one 5 s deadline, at most 3 redirects, each re-checked (https, port 443, a dotted hostname), a 512 KiB body cap (a larger `Content-Length` is refused before reading), `Content-Type` `text/html` only and no `Content-Encoding` other than identity. The host resolves in the socket's own lookup and every address must be public by the GIF rules above (loopback, private, link-local, CGNAT, multicast, reserved, documentation, IPv6 ULA, link-local, site-local and every IPv6 form that carries an IPv4 address are refused), so a redirect to a private target never opens a socket. Requests carry only `User-Agent: varanda-preview/1` and `Accept`: no cookie, `Authorization`, `Referer`, forwarded address or member identity.
- Reading: `parseOg` of `@varanda/rpc` (`lib/og-parse.js`), the reader the app uses too, over the first 512 KiB of the bytes as UTF-8: one linear scan that never throws, nothing run, framed or loaded except the image below. Tags end at the first `>` outside a quoted attribute value, attributes read as HTML reads them. Comments, bogus comments (`<!x>`, `<?x>`) and the content of `<script>`, `<style>`, `<noscript>`, `<textarea>` and `<template>` (nested templates counted) are skipped; an unclosed one skips to the end. `<title>` is RCDATA: everything up to `</title>` is title text, tags included. `title` is `og:title`, else `<title>`; `desc` is `og:description`, else `<meta name="description">`; each key keeps its first usable value, so a blank, whitespace-only or control-only value, or an `og:image` that is not a usable URL, never blocks a later one. Entities are decoded once (every decimal and hexadecimal reference, a reference to NUL, a surrogate or past U+10FFFF as U+FFFD, and `&amp;` `&lt;` `&gt;` `&quot;` `&apos;` `&nbsp;` in any case), then zero-width and directional marks (U+200B to U+200F, U+2060, U+FEFF) are removed and the card text rule of `@varanda/rpc` applies (`cardText`: every character the post text rule forbids that is not whitespace removed, bidi controls included, a lone surrogate as U+FFFD, whitespace collapsed, cut to 80 (`title`) and 160 (`desc`) code points), so a character a reference spells goes like a literal one and the answer always passes `validLinkCard`.
- Image: `og:image`, its entities decoded, resolved against the final page URL, its fragment dropped and kept only when `cardUrlHost` accepts it, is fetched under the same guards with its own 5 s deadline, a 256 KiB cap and `Content-Type` `image/png`, `image/jpeg`, `image/gif` or `image/webp`; the answer's `mime` comes from the leading bytes (PNG, JPEG, GIF, WebP signatures), so SVG or a mislabelled body is `null`. A failed image never fails the preview.
- Limits: 60 previews per member per day (429 `limite-previa`), in memory, reset on restart. At most 16 previews run at once, and at most 2 per member (503 `indisponivel` for either, checked before the daily count), so one member cannot hold every slot with a host that never answers. A preview is counted before the fetch. The count is given back only when this service refused the target before any socket opened (every address of the name refused by the address check, with no earlier hop admitted); any other failure of the target the member chose (network, status, deadline, size, type, encoding, a redirect that fails later) stays counted (503 `indisponivel`).
- Nothing is stored: the service keeps no URL, title or image, and no log line carries a URL, host, title or token; logs carry only `{ event: "preview", reason }` counts (`sessao`, `sessao-revogada`, `previa`, `previa-imagem`, `indisponivel`, `destino`), plus one `ligada` or `desligada` line when `varanda account` starts.

### Residual risks

- Custody: the service can publish, rename or message as any page. A leak of the page key file with the data directory is the issuer-key class (section 3, "Operator response to a stolen issuer key").
- Posts are not co-signed by the acting admin, so readers cannot tell which admin wrote; the service keeps a private audit row.
- The service holds the page Ed25519 key, so it can sign any digest: it can forge a page message, and it can publish a rogue `dm-key`. Admin devices compare the published `dm-key` with the box they hold and warn when they differ.
- Legacy `gen` 1 boxes were known to the service, and so is every message sealed to one of them. A leak of the page key file with the data directory still exposes those and the stored thread rows from before the migration.
- The service learns from `dm-sign` that some admin sent a page message at that time, though not what or to whom.
- A page message waits in the pool for an admin device: after `DM_RETENTION_MS` (7 days) with no admin device pulling, it expires unread.
- The service reads a page's notices.
- The thread does not say which admin wrote a message; the service keeps a private audit row.
- A leaked K1 feeds old readers until `mod block`; a paused K1 gets only the detached statement over HTTPS.
- Close is final and burns the @nome; old readers keep showing tombstoned posts.
- `page.feed` is a 64-key peek, not a full sync. The core session has none.
- A core delegate has no @nome and is invited by key.
- Public admin lists and page keys are personal data.
- A page paused and closed without a block has no cut in its log: old and new readers keep showing it until `mod block` (R5). A closed page with `pushed: false` may not reach readers until a seeder has the close.
- R6 and R6b are closed for every replica that holds the `page-*` block: a tombstone of it has no effect and no clear path removes it. Page diaries fetch tombstone targets before excluding them: for a diary whose block 1 or 2 is a `page-roster` (or whose block 2 is not held yet), the seeder, the core follower and the browser follower keep an unheld tombstone target in the download until they have read it once (`downloadExceptPages`, `pageTargets` in `packages/core/lib/deletion.js`), and `page.feed` asks for the targets in its window (a target the seeder does not advertise is waited for 1 s at most and then counts as deleted). A target read as anything but a `page-*` record is then cleared and left out for good; personal diaries still never fetch a deleted block other than block 2. A tombstone over an unheld block 2 no longer settles "personal" at once (a leaked page key may delete the roster and the close together): the full-log readers ask block 2 ALONE from a peer that advertises it, within a 2 s probe. Only block 2 actually read settles the type: a `page-roster` makes the diary a page and its targets are fetched, anything else makes it personal and block 2 is cleared at once. The probe is not sticky: one that times out (only hostile or absent peers) or finds no peer connected (it returns at once) decides nothing, and block 2 is probed again on the next update, when a peer connects, or by the retry a timed-out probe arms for itself (so a stable peer set with no update still settles it); at most one probe runs at a time and one that timed out waits 30 s before the next, so a personal diary whose block 2 is gone for good costs almost nothing. No other target is fetched while the type is unknown, every target excluded meanwhile. The seeder does not wait for the probe while it prepares a diary: the type settles later and the download is told. There is no give-up: Hypercore asks only a peer that advertises a block, so a target nobody serves costs no request. What remains is only a replica whose peers never serve the block at all: it cannot tell the target's type, so it keeps every target out and misses the roster, close or move. A replica that cleared such a block before this rule is in the same position. Remedy: `mod block` of the page key. The present length steps over an unheld tombstone target, so a follower's scan for `page-close` and `page-moved` (the key move) can pass the hole before the block lands; a held `page-close`, `page-moved` or `page-origin` that lands below the scan cursor is scanned then (other blocks are not remembered; at most 64, past that the cursor rewinds, and a scan running meanwhile keeps the rewound cursor and runs again), so a `page-moved` a leaked key tombstoned still moves the follower to K2 without a restart.
- CLOSED except "the seeder withholds", G-F3 (R6c): `page.feed` no longer depends on the peek window for the close and move cut: it reads the terminal seq from the seeder name index (`/index/page-end`) and fetches and verifies that block itself, also when a leaked key tombstoned block 2 and the close; a pointer it cannot verify makes the diary incomplete with no rows above the head, and the unverified block is never shown. What remains: a seeder that withholds the pointer (or has not indexed the close yet) leaves the window alone to decide, so a close further back than 8 tail blocks is missed if a leaked page key appended after it; and for the full-log readers, a page whose roster and close are withheld by every peer (the block 2 probe never reads it, so the close is never fetched). Remedy: `mod block` of the page key. The browser follower's head phase also asks for an unheld tombstoned target of a settled page (block 2 read as a `page-roster`) in its newest window (`pageGuard.excludable`), instead of leaving it to the linear body; while the type is not settled every deleted seq stays out of the head, and the body fetches the target once the type settles.
- R6d is closed: the feed readers (core `feedRead`, web `member-ops` feed) pass the raw blocks with the folded entries, and the fold keeps a held `page-close` or `page-moved` anyway. A reader that never received the terminal block has no cut, as in R6 above.
- R6e is closed: the seeder keeps `page-*` blocks out of its own deleted bookkeeping. A tombstone whose target reads as a `page-*` record changes nothing at the seeder (not its indexes, not `postBlock`, not its readers' fold), and one learned before its target arrived is taken back once the block is read, so a leaked-key tombstone of a `page-close`, `page-moved` or `page-roster` keeps the end pointer (`/index/page-end`) and the closed or moved hint, on a fresh seeder and after an index rebuild. What remains is the case above: every peer withholds both the roster and the close.
- Page Conversas do not follow a key move: K2 starts an empty inbox.
- The notices rate window (8 per minute per page) and the lifecycle window (2 per owner per 24 h) are in memory and reset on restart.

## 13. Blobs anônimos (`packages/core/lib/blob.js`, `blob-net.js`, `blob-pool.js`, `apps/cli/lib/seed-ws.js`)

A mídia de uma mensagem direta (section 11) vai para o semeador em pedaços de tamanho fixo, cada um com o seu selo, num conjunto comum de blobs. O cliente cifra o objeto inteiro com `secretbox` sob uma chave e um nonce aleatórios antes de cortar; a chave e o nonce só viajam dentro da mensagem direta selada, no descritor, nunca neste canal, então o semeador guarda texto cifrado que não abre. Nada do que vai ao semeador ou volta dele nomeia um diário.

### Id e descritor

- `id`: 32 bytes aleatórios (`newBlobId`), escolhido pelo cliente; não deriva do conteúdo nem da chave.
- Pedaço (chunk) de `BLOB_CHUNK_BYTES = 32768` bytes; no máximo `BLOB_MAX_CHUNKS = 257` pedaços (8 MiB de texto claro mais o MAC de 16 bytes do `secretbox`).
- Descritor dentro da mensagem selada, chaves exatas `{ id, key, nonce, chunks, bytes, sha256 }`: `id`, `key` e `sha256` em hex de 64 caracteres, `nonce` em hex de 48 (24 bytes), `bytes` o tamanho do TEXTO CIFRADO (inteiro maior que 16 e até `257 * 32768`), `chunks` exatamente `ceil(bytes / 32768)` (`validateBlobDescriptor`), `sha256` o hash do texto cifrado inteiro.

### Quadro (frame) `VBL1`, exatamente `BLOB_FRAME_BYTES = 33137` bytes

| Campo | Bytes | Posição |
| --- | --- | --- |
| magic `VBL1` (ASCII) | 4 | 0..4 |
| version (`BLOB_VERSION = 1`) | 1 | 4 |
| id | 32 | 5..37 |
| index (uint16 big-endian) | 2 | 37..39 |
| count (uint16 big-endian) | 2 | 39..41 |
| kid (da chave de selo de blob) | 8 | 41..49 |
| sig (assinatura cega) | 256 | 49..305 |
| token (do selo) | 32 | 305..337 |
| check | 32 | 337..369 |
| bytes (o último pedaço é completado com zeros) | 32768 | 369..33137 |

`decodeChunk` nunca lança e devolve `null` para quadro de outro tamanho (o de um layout anterior inclusive), magic ou version errados, `count` fora de `1..257`, `index` fora de `count` ou um `check` que não confere. O base64 do quadro tem 44184 caracteres, abaixo de `BLOB_MAX_MESSAGE = 65536`. A última parte é cortada pelo cliente com o `bytes` do descritor.

### Selo, token e check

- O selo é `(kid, sig, token)`, uma assinatura RSA cega (a mesma construção de section 11, "Stamps") sobre `token` sob a chave de selo de blob do semeador, com o domínio FDH `varanda/blob-stamp-fdh/v1`.
- `token` é um valor aleatório de 32 bytes, como o de uma mensagem direta (`newToken(n, 'blob')` em `dm-postage.js`), sorteado de novo até o representante FDH ser menor que o módulo `n` do semeador. Nunca deriva do pedaço. O cliente o cega (`blind`, tipo `'blob'`), pede o selo no canal de selos (section 11, tipo `blob`) com antecedência, tira a cegueira (`unblind`) e o guarda numa carteira de selos; qualquer selo assinado paga um pedaço qualquer, e um token gasto é recusado para qualquer outro pedaço. O quadro se monta com `encodeChunk({ id, index, count, kid, sig, token, bytes })`.
- `check = BLAKE2b-256(utf8('varanda/blob-chunk/v1\n') || id || index (uint16 be) || count (uint16 be) || bytes preenchidos)`, recalculado por `decodeChunk`. Ele cobre o lugar do pedaço e os seus bytes, não o selo: um quadro cujo `check` não confere é recusado. `kid`, `sig` e `token` são julgados pela conferência do selo, e o conjunto grava o token e o hash do quadro de cada pedaço guardado, então um `token`, `kid` ou `sig` trocado num arquivo guardado é pego (blob quebrado).
- Um selo de DM nunca paga um pedaço, e um de blob nunca paga um envelope.
- Carteira do cliente do navegador (`packages/web-member/lib/blob.js`): só a compra em segundo plano enche a carteira até 300 selos de blob, e ela a reabastece quando restam menos de 150. Os selos são comprados no link identificado de selos (tipo `blob`) e gastos depois no link anônimo de blob. Só quando a carteira não tem selos para um envio o cliente força uma compra logo antes daquele `put`, e ela compra apenas o que falta mais 8 (`FORCE_MARGIN`), nunca mais do que um enchimento completo. A contagem de selos usáveis considera só as chaves aceitas: numa rotação de chave, os selos sob o `kid` descartado não contam.
- Chave do semeador e compra em vários links (cliente do navegador): com a carteira cheia, o cliente ainda pergunta a chave de blob do semeador quando a última conferência tem mais de 12 horas (`KEY_CHECK_MS`). Uma chave com `kid` novo compra pelo menos um selo e só então vira a atual; os selos fora do par atual/anterior são descartados. Uma compra continua num novo link de selos quando o link fecha ou chega ao prazo de 180 s depois de ter comprado pelo menos um selo, e para ao atingir a meta, no `teto`, numa resposta ruim, num link que não compra nada ou numa troca de `kid`.
- `put` do cliente do navegador (primeira tentativa ou nova tentativa do usuário): envia todos os quadros em ordem, cada um uma vez, e só tem sucesso quando todos foram confirmados naquela chamada. `cheio`, `ritmo` ou um link perdido para o envio na hora (link perdido ou `ack` ausente é incerto). Um quadro recusado como `invalido` é limpo e os quadros restantes ainda são enviados; depois o `put` falha, e o envio seguinte carimba de novo os quadros limpos com selos da carteira (selos novos). Assim, uma nova tentativa enquanto o blob ainda existe o completa (os pedaços guardados respondem como repetidos, sem selo novo); depois da varredura do incompleto, os quadros de selo gasto são recusados, e o envio seguinte os carimba de novo e guarda o blob outra vez sob o mesmo id.
- Leitura (`fetchBlob`): os limites de imagem são conferidos pela assinatura dos bytes (JPEG, PNG, GIF, WebP), seja qual for o mime (comparado sem diferenciar maiúsculas de minúsculas); o corpo de um quadro nunca é guardado em memória além de `BLOB_FRAME_BYTES`; uma resposta sem fluxo legível e sem `Content-Length` é recusada.

### Canal `'varanda/blob/1'` (`packages/core/lib/blob-net.js`)

`BLOB_PROTOCOL = 'varanda/blob/1'`, em canal protomux sem desafio e sem prova: nenhuma chave de diário viaja. O membro o abre num link próprio, com uma chave Noise efêmera nova. JSON UTF-8 de chaves exatas, no máximo `BLOB_MAX_MESSAGE = 65536` bytes por mensagem.

- membro para semeador: `{ "type": "put", "chunk": <base64 canônico de exatamente 33137 bytes> }`.
- semeador para membro: `{ "type": "ack", "ok": <boolean>, "reason": null | "cheio" | "invalido" | "ritmo" }`.
- Um `put` em andamento por socket (não por canal): um segundo `put` com o primeiro sem resposta é mensagem ruim, também num canal que o membro reabriu. Mais de `MAX_BAD_BLOB = 5` mensagens ruins derrubam o socket; um quadro acima do limite, falha de JSON ou de forma, `chunk` que não é o base64 canônico de 33137 bytes, resposta não pedida e mensagem do outro papel são mensagens ruins.
- Cada mensagem tira uma ficha do balde do canal (`BLOB_MESSAGE_BURST = 64`, `BLOB_MESSAGE_RATE = 24` por segundo); sem ficha, o par é derrubado. Os `put` têm balde próprio (`BLOB_PUT_BURST = 32`, `BLOB_PUT_RATE = 8` por segundo): acima dele a resposta é `ritmo` e nada é guardado.
- `invalido`: quadro de tamanho certo que o conjunto recusa (magic ruim, token que não confere, selo ruim ou gasto, repetição com bytes diferentes). `cheio`: sem espaço no teto de bytes. Uma falha do próprio semeador (disco, arquivo de chave) nunca é `invalido`: o `put` lança, o canal fecha sem `ack` e o membro mantém o envio como incerto.
- O membro tem 15 s (`BLOB_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS = 15000`) para a resposta; passado isso, o canal fecha e o envio fica incerto. O nó nunca repete um `put` por conta própria: só a pessoa pode mandar de novo, e o conjunto responde a um quadro idêntico como sucesso repetido.
- Toda linha de registro ligada a blob (canal, conjunto, semeador e o tipo de selo) leva só um evento fixo e um motivo ou uma contagem, nunca id, token, chave, endereço ou diário. Os eventos são `blob` (motivos `completo`, `cheio`, `invalido`, `postage`), `blob-stamp`, `peer-dropped` (com o motivo), `blob-put-failed`, `blob-pool-torn`, `blob-pool-broken`, `blob-pool-remove-failed`, `blob-pool-sweep-failed`, `dm-stamp` (motivo `blob-postage`) e `warning` (motivo `blob-sweep-failed`).

### Conjunto de blobs (`packages/core/lib/blob-pool.js`)

- Fica em `<seeder dir>/blobs/`: `<id hex>/meta.json` (`{ v: 1, count, receivedAt }`, escrito de forma atômica antes do primeiro pedaço), `<id hex>/<index>` (o quadro inteiro) e `tokens.json` (`{ v: 1, tokens: [{ h, at, kid }] }`), modo 0600 nos arquivos e 0700 nos diretórios. Cada pedaço é escrito num nome temporário, recebe `fsync` e é renomeado, e só então vem o `ack`.
- Retenção `BLOB_RETENTION_MS = 7 * 86400000` a partir do PRIMEIRO pedaço, pelo relógio do semeador, e nunca renovada (nem por pedaço posterior, nem por `put` repetido). Um blob incompleto é varrido 1 hora (`BLOB_PARTIAL_MS`) depois do primeiro pedaço; um blob completo segue os 7 dias. A varredura roda a cada 60 s junto com a do conjunto de mensagens diretas. Não há apagamento a pedido. Um `receivedAt` mais de 1 dia à frente do relógio é lido como a hora da carga.
- Teto de bytes do conjunto: `BLOB_POOL_BYTES = 2 GiB` por padrão, mudado por `seed --blob-bytes`. Não há reserva adiantada: o conjunto conta os pedaços que de fato guarda (32768 bytes cada, os de blobs vencidos que ainda estão no disco inclusive), e o pedaço que passaria do teto recebe `cheio` sem nada escrito, depois das conferências do selo e do token gasto, então um pedaço recusado mantém o seu selo. Isso pode acontecer no meio de um blob, cujos pedaços já guardados ficam até a varredura do incompleto.
